# Progress

> ⚠️ **Read before every `uv run pytest`**: test runs against the running Docker Compose stack delete its real data if `TEST_POSTGRES_DSN` does not explicitly point to an isolated throwaway database (every service's `conftest.py` truncates its tables, by default against the same Postgres instance that the stack also uses). At P5-S2 this caused all previously existing documents to be irretrievably lost. Since **P5c-S1** every `conftest.py` additionally enforces `DMS_POSTGRES_DSN = TEST_POSTGRES_DSN`, so that `TestClient(app)` tests no longer unnoticedly read/write the live DB past `TEST_POSTGRES_DSN` (this had led to a real incident at P5b-S6) — however, the basic rule "without an explicitly set `TEST_POSTGRES_DSN`, everything points to the same DB as the stack" still applies unchanged. Details/rule: see "Tooling & Testing" below.

**Last completed:** Phase 30 (configurable email templates by use case/recipient domain — all five sessions, see below under "Post-Roadmap: Phase 30"). **Phase 27+ is thereby fully complete** — all four phases (27–30) done. Since then: a round of user-reported bugfixes (see "Post-Roadmap: bugfix round after Phase 30" below) and the Phase 31 planning pass (feature-gap analysis against a government-sector reference system, see `docs/egov-feature-gap-analysis.md`).

**Next session:** Phase 31 (eGov feature gap closure) — see `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` "Phase 31", start with P31-S1 (deletion reason codes, smallest/lowest-risk item) or any other session in that phase; only P31-S10/S11 have a hard dependency (on P31-S9).

Phases 0–26 (the original 107-session roadmap plus the post-triage Phase 18–26 continuation) are fully complete — see below under "Phase 26 — Helm charts for k8s/OCP" for that milestone's own summary. After Phase 26 completed, the user requested three new, mostly independent features (PDF export, direct links, configurable email templates), grounded via Explore/Plan agents against the real codebase and broken into **Phase 27–30** in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`.

After the MVP milestone, the user tested the UIs in the browser themselves for the first time and gave substantial feedback on layout/scope of functionality (folder management, metadata, 3-column explorer, admin dashboard navigation, multi-installation, standalone process designer, theming). The plan was extended accordingly with P4-S4/S5/S6 and P6-S6 (see `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`) — all three ran **before** P5-S1, so that the UI foundation was in place before further Phase-5 functionality (processing: scan, rendering, OCR, search) was layered on top. Before P5-S1, Phase 5 was additionally planned in more depth against the spec (see `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`), in order to find concept subtleties in advance rather than only during implementation. P5-S1 (virus scan), P5-S2 (rendering/renditions), P5-S3 (OCR) and P5-S4 (search) are now implemented — **Phase 5 fully completed**. Immediately afterward, another user request following the same pattern as P4-S4/S5/S6: six additional sessions (**Phase 5b**, see `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`) were inserted before Phase 6 begins — concerning extensions to already-completed services (object-type-service, folder-service, document-service, storage-service, ocr-service) as well as both frontends. After Phase 5b was completed, the items accumulated under "Open Decisions" were consolidated once and presented to the user with decision proposals — this produced **Phase 5c** (two more sessions, see `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`): P5c-S1 (test-DB isolation gap, implemented immediately) and P5c-S2 (storage rebalancing/storage device swap correction, pulled forward from the Phase-10 backlog) — **Phase 5c fully completed**. Immediately afterward, renewed user feedback from actual use (same pattern as the trigger for Phase 5b): missing preview for text-based formats (`.txt`/`.json`), missing configurability of OCR triggering/allowed upload formats, unchecked client content type, upload UX (no pop-up, no drag & drop) — this produced **Phase 5d** (two more sessions, see `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`) — **Phase 5d fully completed**. During the P5d-S2 planning, another, thematically independent user request: a configurable reference-number generator (file reference number) per document class, with details clarified in advance via follow-up questions (year-based counter reset, global display toggle with object-type override, changes only by privileged users) — this produced **Phase 5e** (three more sessions, see `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`), still open. In addition, the concept document (`Business__DMS-Konzept.md`, now version 0.18) was extended to cover two areas not previously covered, likewise a user request rather than an implementation-experience finding: **2.5 Area structure** (alongside the configurable document root there are separate special areas — inbox/outbox including the mail room role, trash/personal trash/classified-documents trash including new deletion-administration roles (4.6), quarantine, contacts, records-disposal access during the transition period, templates for structural scaffolding) as well as an extension of **8 (UI layer)** to add a dockable, VS-Code-like flexible workspace as a supplement to the previous fixed three-column layout (which remains the factory default). Both extensions are placed at the end of the roadmap as **Phase 15** (six sessions) and **Phase 16** (one session) (see `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` for the reasoning behind the placement) — pure concept/plan extension this round, no implementation.

Immediately afterward the first session of Phase 5e: **P5e-S1** extends `ObjectType` (Object-Type Service) with two fields permitted only for `applies_to="document"` — `kennzeichen_format` (a format string with the placeholders `{YYYY}`/`{YY}`/`{MM}`/`{DD}`/`{Laufende_Nummer}`, must contain `{Laufende_Nummer}` and must not reference unknown placeholders, otherwise `422`) and `kennzeichen_display_override` (tri-state `null`/`true`/`false`, here only stored, not yet evaluated — follows with P5e-S2/S3). New table `object_type_sequence` (`{object_type_id, jahr}` → `naechste_nummer`) implements the year-based, per-object-type-independent counter reset established during planning. New endpoint `POST /object-types/{id}/next-kennzeichen` returns the fully rendered string (e.g. `2026-001`) and is **atomically secured against concurrency**: `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` creates the counter row if needed (collision-free on simultaneous first calls), the subsequent `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` locks it for the rest of the transaction and thereby serializes concurrent calls. Verified via an `asyncio.gather` test with five simultaneous calls (guarantees `001`–`005`, no duplicates/gaps), additionally checked live against the built container. **63 tests** (previously 48, 15 new: format/override validation, counter independence per object type, 404 cases, concurrency test, API equivalents), `ruff check`/`ruff format` clean. `docs/services/object-type-service.md` updated (new endpoint, new table, own "reference-number generator" section). Pure backend session with no frontend touch — no browser test needed. `graphify dms/ --update` deliberately deferred (not a phase completion, follows at the latest with P5e-S3). Next step (historical): **P5e-S2** (Document Service: actual reference-number assignment on document creation + privileged change via the new `dms-admin` role).

Immediately afterward **P5e-S2**: `POST /documents` queries `POST /object-types/{id}/next-kennzeichen` server-side when `object_type_id` is set (`ObjectTypeClient.next_kennzeichen()`, `404` = no generator configured) and writes the result under the reserved attribute key `Kennzeichen` in `attributes` — a value sent by the client for this key is always discarded, regardless of whether a generator is configured at all. `PATCH /documents/{id}` detects a change to `attributes["Kennzeichen"]` by old/new comparison (`attributes` is a full replace, not a merge — so even silently omitting the key counts as a change) and rejects it with `403` unless the `X-DMS-Roles` header injected by the gateway contains the role from the new settings field `kennzeichen_admin_role` (default `"dms-admin"`) — **the first place in the entire system where this header, injected since P4-S1 but never evaluated until now, is actually checked** (see "Authorization" below). **Auth Service**: `ensure_realm_and_client` idempotently creates the realm role `dms-admin` (`create_realm_role(..., skip_exists=True)`, analogous to the existing theme-attribute bootstrap). **Planning error corrected during implementation**: the original Phase-5e planning assumed an "existing role management" for assigning roles to users (`/users`, admin UI) — inspection of the real code showed this does not exist (`/users` only manages accounts, no role assignment, no `assign_realm_roles` call anywhere in the system). Pragmatically accepted for the current solo/test usage (assignment manually via the Keycloak Admin Console), documented as a real gap in `docs/services/auth-service.md`/`docs/services/document-service.md` "Open Points" instead of silently glossed over — no new role-management API was built without being asked (scope fidelity to the session). New tests: Object-Type Service unchanged (63, see above); **Document Service 66 tests** (previously 56, 10 new: client reference number is discarded, 403/200 cases including wrong role, unchanged attributes need no role, removing a set reference number via replace counts as a change, plus three real integration tests against an object type configured with `kennzeichen_format` on the running Object-Type Service); **Auth Service 19 tests** (previously 18, 1 new: bootstrap creates the role idempotently). `ruff check`/`ruff format` clean for both services. Verified live against the built stack (object type with generator created → document created with a forged client `Kennzeichen` → server-generated `2026-001` wins; `PATCH` without role/with wrong role → `403`; with `dms-admin` → `200`; test data then deleted, all containers subsequently stopped). `docs/services/document-service.md` (new "reference-number generator" section) and `docs/services/auth-service.md` updated. `graphify dms/ --update` still deliberately deferred (follows with the phase completion in P5e-S3). Next step: **P5e-S3** (Admin UI: format-string/display-override editor on the object type + global display setting; User UI: `Kennzeichen` before the file name in the explorer list/tab title, `MetadataPanel` field editable only for `dms-admin`) — **last session of Phase 5e**.

Immediately afterward the last session of Phase 5e: **P5e-S3** carries the reference-number generator through into both frontends. **Admin UI**: `ObjectTypeEditor` gets a format-string field (`kennzeichen_format`) and a tri-state selection field for `kennzeichen_display_override` when `applies_to="document"`, forced client-side to `null` for `applies_to="folder"` (analogous to the existing `icon` field); new page `/kennzeichen-settings/` (`KennzeichenSettings`, new Object-Type-Service endpoint `GET`/`PUT /kennzeichen-config`, singleton table `kennzeichen_config` with `show_before_filename`, default `true` — same single-row configuration pattern as `OcrConfig`/`UploadConfig`, but this time in the Object-Type Service, since the object-type override already lives there). **User UI**: new `lib/kennzeichen.ts` with the pure functions `resolveKennzeichenDisplay()`/`formatDocumentTitle()` (override-vs-global-default resolution, testable via Vitest without rendering); `ExplorerPane` loads all document classes plus the global default once (same pattern as the existing folder-icon fetch) and applies the helper to list rows **and** tab titles, `FolderTree` gets both values passed through for its document leaves; `MetadataPanel` shows its own `Kennzeichen` field outside the layout-driven attribute grid (the key is not a regular object-type attribute), **disabled for all users without the `dms-admin` role** (`user.realm_roles` from `GET /me`) — the first UI-side role check in the entire system, a purely client-side anticipation of the `403` rule already enforced server-side since P5e-S2. **Deliberately not built during implementation**: no central "resolved display" endpoint in the Object-Type Service — both frontends resolve override-vs-default client-side themselves (documented as an open point for a possible third consumer). Tests: **Object-Type Service 67 tests** (previously 63, 4 new: `kennzeichen-config` repository/API tests for default and persistence), **Admin UI 64 tests** (previously 58, 6 new: `ObjectTypeEditor` visibility/submit/loading of the new fields, `KennzeichenSettings` load/save/empty state), **User UI 49 tests** (previously 37, 12 new: 7 pure `kennzeichen.ts` function tests, 1 API-client test for `getKennzeichenConfig`, 4 `DocumentWorkspace` integration tests for display resolution + role check in the metadata panel). All three `ruff`/`tsc`/`eslint` gates clean. **Fully verified live against the built stack** (real login through the gateway, object type with generator created, document created with a forged client reference number — server value `2026-001` wins, `PATCH` without `dms-admin` role → `403`, `GET`/`PUT /kennzeichen-config` through the gateway), test data/test users then deleted, all containers subsequently stopped. Admin UI/User UI still **not visually verified in the browser** (no Chrome/Chromium in this environment, as with every previous frontend session) — only via Vitest component tests plus curl-confirmed correctness of the underlying gateway calls. `docs/services/object-type-service.md`, `docs/services/document-service.md` (open-points correction), `docs/services/admin-ui.md`, `docs/services/auth-service.md` and `docs/services/user-ui.md` updated. **`graphify dms/ --update` run afterward (phase completion, mandatory)**, see "Tooling & Testing" below for the result. → **Phase 5e thus fully completed** (all three sessions done). Next step: **P6-S1** (Workflow Engine scaffolding).

Immediately afterward the first session of Phase 6: **P6-S1** builds the workflow engine scaffolding (7.1) as a new, standalone `workflow-service` — deliberately with no dependency on any other backend service (no `dms-auth-client`, no HTTP clients), since this session explicitly delivers only BPMN import/execution with manual/automatic tasks; RBAC/four-eyes approval is assigned to P6-S4, case-service integration to P6-S3, SLA/notification to P6-S2, signature tasks to P6-S5, the process designer to P6-S6. Licensing question already clarified in advance ([ADR 0018](docs/adr/0018-spiffworkflow-lgpl-license.md)). **Researched via plan mode before implementation instead of assumed**: two Explore agents (repo conventions against real code, SpiffWorkflow API against the official documentation) plus a Plan agent for the file-precise build plan, afterward every SpiffWorkflow API assumption (`BpmnParser`, `BpmnWorkflow`, `BpmnWorkflowSerializer`, `TaskState`) verified against version **3.1.2** via `help()`/`inspect` after the actual `uv add` installation, **not** taken blindly from the documentation — in the process a real API pitfall was found empirically and documented: `BpmnWorkflow.set_data()` does NOT, contrary to documentation intuition, make process variables visible to subsequent tasks; data must instead be set directly on the ready start task before the first run (`spiff_adapter.set_initial_data()`). **User follow-up question on script-task security**: SpiffWorkflow's default scripting directly executes Python code embedded in the BPMN XML with no role check — a real concern in this session for imported third-party processes (7.1 names BPMN import from external tools as a core feature) — user decided in favor of activation as documented (fits the current internal test/development operation), referred as an open point to P6-S4 instead of a stub or an accelerated role check. **Architecture**: `spiff_adapter.py` isolates the entire SpiffWorkflow API surface in one file (limits the blast radius of a future version bump to a single module); process-instance state is persisted as a full serialized SpiffWorkflow blob, not a normalized task table ([ADR 0019](docs/adr/0019-workflow-full-state-serialization.md)) — ready tasks are deserialized live on every read access. Endpoints: `POST`/`GET`/`DELETE /process-definitions` (BPMN XML upload, name collision → `409`, unparsable/ambiguous process → `422`, deletion with existing instances → `409`), `POST /process-definitions/{id}/instances` (executes automatic tasks immediately, status `"completed"` on a fully automatic run, otherwise `"running"`), `GET /instances`/`GET .../tasks`/`POST .../tasks/{id}/complete` (`409` if the task is not (or no longer) ready). Deliberately **no** authorization (neither `X-DMS-Roles` check nor `permission-service` call) — `completed_by`/`created_by` are plain, unchecked strings like elsewhere in the repo before their respective enforcement. Events: pure producer (`workflow.instance.started`/`.completed`, `workflow.task.completed` on a new stream `workflow`), no consumer — Audit Service extended with `workflow.>` (same immediate-extension pattern as for every previous new producer stream). **44 tests, all green** (`test_spiff_adapter.py` isolates the SpiffWorkflow wrapper against real BPMN test fixtures taken from the official SpiffWorkflow GitHub repo instead of hand-written XML, including lane extraction with/without BPMN lanes; `test_repository.py`/`test_api.py` cover all error paths), `ruff check`/`ruff format` clean. `docker compose build workflow-service` ran through without additional system packages (no `apt-get` needed for `lxml`, manylinux wheel). **Fully verified live against the built stack**: BPMN file uploaded, instance started, ready manual task found, completed, instance status `"completed"` confirmed, `409`/`422` cases checked live, test data then removed via `TRUNCATE`, all containers stopped. No browser test needed (pure backend session, not on the UI sessions list). `docs/services/workflow-service.md` (new, including a detailed "Open Points" with references to P6-S2–S6), `docs/architecture.md` (new `WorkflowService` node, in the process also retroactively adding the previously missing Phase-5e read-hint line — Phase 5e likewise introduced no new node but was never noted in the diagram), `infra/docker-compose.yml`/`.env.example` (port `8014`) updated. `graphify dms/ --update` deliberately deferred (Phase 6 has six sessions, not a phase completion). Next step: **P6-S2** (SLA time monitoring per step + Notification Service).

Immediately afterward the second session of Phase 6: **P6-S2** delivers SLA time monitoring per process step (timer/boundary events) plus a new, standalone `notification-service` (7.1). **Researched via plan mode before implementation instead of assumed**: `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s P6-S2 line is a pure title with no detail, concept 7.1 was used for the precise shaping (maximum processing duration per step, configurable escalation, Notification Service with the channels email/in-app/webhook); the SpiffWorkflow timer API (`refresh_waiting_tasks()`, `BoundaryEvent` detection via `isinstance`) was verified against the installed version 3.1.2 via `inspect` and **both BPMN semantics were actually tested against a fixture loaded from the official SpiffWorkflow repo**: a non-interrupting boundary timer (`cancelActivity="false"`) lets the original task keep running, a locally modified variant with `cancelActivity="true"` additionally confirmed the interrupting cancellation — both entirely SpiffWorkflow's own semantics, `workflow-service` did not need to write its own cancel/routing logic for this. **Architecture/scope decisions** (see [ADR 0020](docs/adr/0020-sla-timer-polling.md)): SLA timers are detected via an asyncio poll loop (`_sla_poll_loop`, default 30s, `DMS_SLA_POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS`) instead of push-based — no distributed lock across multiple `workflow-service` replicas, precision tied to the poll interval; recipient resolution deliberately **without an RBAC call**, instead transported as an opaque `escalation_email` process datum via `initial_data` (same pattern as `business_key` since P6-S1) — `auth-service` has no role lookup for users anyway; existing "logs only" alerting spots (including `storage-service`) were deliberately **not** hooked up to the new service, since P6-S2's title mentions only SLA time monitoring (same scope discipline as the still-unconnected OCR `needs_review` since P6-S1). New `notification-service`: all three concept channels actually implemented (email via `aiosmtplib` against a new `mailpit` dev container, in-app as pure persistence, webhook via `httpx`), delivery synchronous without retry (`status`: `sent`/`failed` directly in the notification record). First service in the project with **both event-bus roles simultaneously**: consumes specifically `workflow.task.escalated` (not `workflow.>` — the other workflow events have no notification semantics) and publishes its own `notification.sent`/`.failed` events on a new stream `notification`; `audit-service` extended with `notification.>` (same immediate-extension pattern as for every previous new producer stream). New route in `workflow-service`: none — internal only (`spiff_adapter.check_timers()`, `repository.advance_timers()`, background task in `main.py`'s `lifespan`). Tests: `workflow-service` extended with timer/escalation tests in `test_spiff_adapter.py`/`test_repository.py`; `notification-service` with a fully new test suite (`test_delivery.py` really against `mailpit` as well as a local `http.server` started within the suite itself for the webhook success case, `test_repository.py`, `test_api.py`, `test_consumer.py` — simulated event directly to the handler, no real NATS) — all green, `ruff check`/`ruff format` clean for both services. `docker compose build workflow-service notification-service` ran through without additional system packages. **Fully verified live against the built stack**: BPMN file with boundary timer + `escalation_email` uploaded and started, after a short `DMS_SLA_POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS` confirmed via `notification-service` that in-app and email notification were delivered with `status="sent"` (email additionally verified via Mailpit's web UI), original task then completed normally, instance status `"completed"` confirmed, test data then deleted, all containers stopped. No browser test needed (pure backend session). `docs/services/workflow-service.md` (timer section, updated events/open-points tables), `docs/services/notification-service.md` (new), `docs/architecture.md` (new `Notification` node + `Mailpit` infra node, updated event-bus-roles read hints), `docs/adr/0020-sla-timer-polling.md` (new), `infra/docker-compose.yml`/`.env.example` (port `8015`, `mailpit`) updated. `graphify dms/ --update` still deliberately deferred (Phase 6 not yet complete). Next step: **P6-S3** (Case Service: circulation folders).

Immediately afterward the third session of Phase 6: **P6-S3** delivers the new, standalone `case-service` for circulation folders (references to case documents, 2.3) as well as an extension of `document-service` with process-specific working copies. **Researched via plan mode before implementation instead of assumed**: an Explore agent against the real code of `workflow-service`/`notification-service`/`document-service`/`folder-service`/`permission-service`/`object-type-service`/`gateway-service` confirmed, among other things, that gateway routing is fully dynamic (no code change needed for a new service), that **no** existing service checks permissions synchronously (only `search-service` filters search results via batch check), and that `permission-service`'s resource tree structure is built exclusively via `folder.>` events — individual documents are not registered as their own node in it at all. **Architecture decisions** (see `docs/services/case-service.md`): (1) working copies deliberately live **not** in `case-service`, but as three additional, nullable provenance fields (`derived_from_document_id`/`derived_from_version_number`/`originating_case_id`) on `document-service`'s existing `POST /documents` — no new route, full reuse of the already existing versioning/audit/virus-scan pipeline; confirms the direction already suspected in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`. (2) `case-service` deliberately registers **no** own `resource.created`/`.moved`/`.deleted` events in `permission-service` — no precedent for non-folder objects, same (known) gap as with documents. (3) A circulation folder starts a process instance in `workflow-service` on creation with `business_key = case_id` — `case-service` is thereby the **first consumer** of `workflow.instance.completed` (previously a pure producer), the handler matches exclusively on this `business_key` and ignores instances with no matching circulation folder. **Two-tier reference model (2.3) actually implemented**: `GET /cases/{id}/documents` resolves `current_version_number`/`deleted_at` live via `document-service` while the circulation folder is open; upon reaching the BPMN end state, `close_case()` fixes the then-current version in `snapshot_version_number` for every still-active reference — later version changes to the original demonstrably no longer take effect afterward (verified live, see below). References are soft-deleted on removal (`removed_by`/`removed_at`), not hard-removed. **Known, documented race condition** (not fixed, see `docs/services/case-service.md` "Open Points"): if a circulation folder starts a fully automated process with no manual task, `workflow.instance.completed` may already be published before the service's own `Case` row is committed — the live verification therefore deliberately used a process with a manual task. Schema name `case` is a reserved SQL keyword (`CASE WHEN`) — automatically correctly quoted by SQLAlchemy in ORM-generated DDL (verified), raw SQL strings in `main.py`/`tests/conftest.py` quote it manually as `"case"`; the table for circulation folders is deliberately called `cases` (plural), so as not to additionally need this quoting obligation on the table name too. New port **8016**. Events: producer on new stream `case` (`case.created`/`.document.added`/`.removed`/`.closed`), consumer of `workflow.instance.completed` — `audit-service` extended with `case.>` (same immediate-extension pattern as for every previous new producer stream). **97 tests, all green** (case-service: 26 new — `test_repository.py` purely at the DB level with no HTTP calls, `test_consumer.py` with a simulated event directly to the handler like `notification-service`, `test_api.py` as real integration tests against locally running `workflow-service`/`document-service`/`object-type-service` instances; document-service: **71 tests** (previously 66, 5 new for the working-copy fields including validation error cases)), `ruff check`/`ruff format` clean for both services. `docker compose build document-service case-service` ran through without additional system packages. **Fully verified live against the built stack**: BPMN process with manual task uploaded, circulation folder created (`process_instance_id` set), two documents referenced, a new version of a referenced document checked in (dynamic reference confirmed: `current_version_number` in the reference list immediately jumped to the new state), manual task completed, waited briefly — completion confirmed via the real NATS event (`status="closed"`, `snapshot_version_number` fixed), a further version change on the original demonstrably no longer changed the fixed snapshot, a working copy created via `POST /documents` with the three new fields (independent document, correct provenance fields), all 400/404/409 error cases checked live, test data then removed via `TRUNCATE`, all containers stopped. No browser test needed (pure backend session, not on the UI sessions list). `docs/services/case-service.md` (new), `docs/services/document-service.md` ("working copies" section), `docs/services/workflow-service.md` (consumer addition), `docs/architecture.md` (new `Case` node, HTTP/event edges, event-bus-roles bullet), `infra/docker-compose.yml`/`.env.example` (port 8016) updated. `graphify dms/ --update` deliberately deferred (Phase 6 not yet complete). Next step: **P6-S4** (generic four-eyes approval mechanism + retrofit family, see roadmap look-ahead below).

Immediately afterward the fourth session of Phase 6: **P6-S4** delivers the generic four-eyes approval mechanism (4.3) plus the retrofits for the two cases named by 4.3 itself as examples: Document-Service force-unlock (ungated since P3-S2) and Permission-Service scope locks (ungated since P3-S4). **Researched via plan mode before implementation instead of assumed**: an Explore agent against the real code of `permission-service` (scope-lock endpoints, `structure_consumer.py`, event publishing), `document-service`'s force-unlock endpoint, and the existing client/singleton config patterns confirmed that there was no approval/two-phase logic whatsoever anywhere in the repo yet, and that `document-service` so far has **zero** NATS consumers (pure producer). **Architecture decision** (see [ADR 0022](docs/adr/0022-four-eyes-approval-via-events.md)): the generic mechanism lives in `permission-service` (new tables `approval_action_config`/`approval_request`, endpoints `GET`/`PUT /approval-config[/{action_type}]`, `POST /approval-requests`, `GET /approval-requests[/{id}]`, `POST .../approve|reject`) — `POST .../approve` does **not** perform the actual action itself, but only marks the request as approved and publishes `permission.approval.approved` (including `action_type` + original `payload`). Execution is taken over by the respectively responsible service as a consumer of this event: `document-service` gets its first-ever consumer (`consumer.py`, second `NatsEventBusClient` with `ensure_stream=False`, same two-client principle as `notification-service`/`case-service`), `permission-service` consumes **its own event itself** for its own scope-lock actions — deliberately no special-case dispatch in the `approve` handler, so that the mechanism demonstrably works identically for "own" and "foreign" action types. **Core rule from 4.3 implemented**: `approve` rejects with `403` if `approved_by == initiated_by`; `reject` deliberately has no such restriction (withdrawing one's own request is unproblematic). **Default remains ungated**: `requires_approval` is `false` per action type until an admin explicitly sets `PUT /approval-config/{action_type} {"requires_approval": true}` — literal implementation of "configurable per action type, not globally enforced", no breaking change for ongoing internal test operation. Both retrofitted endpoints (`POST /documents/{id}/lock/force-release`, `POST`/`DELETE /scope-locks`) got a result wrapper for this (`status: "released"/"created"/"pending_approval"` + optional `approval_request_id`, same pattern as the already-existing `CheckinResult`). **Not part of this session** (see ADR 0022 "Consequences" and PROGRESS.md "Authorization" below): no execution feedback channel (an externally failed completion remains logged at the executing service, `ApprovalRequest.status` stays `"approved"`), no notification of approvers, permission/role changes (`POST /role-assignments`) do not yet use the mechanism. Tests: `permission-service` **64 tests** (new: approval-config default/upsert, approval-request lifecycle including the `403` initiator case and `409` double-decision case in `test_repository.py`, approval endpoints + gated scope-lock paths in `test_api.py`, new `test_approval_consumer.py` with a simulated event directly at the handler), `document-service` **76 tests** (new: gated force-unlock path in `test_api.py`, new `test_consumer.py` for the first own consumer) — all green, `ruff check`/`ruff format` clean for both services. `docker compose build permission-service document-service` ran through without additional system packages. **Two real bugs found and fixed during the first live run** (exactly the reason this step is never skipped): (1) `infra/docker-compose.yml` was missing `DMS_PERMISSION_SERVICE_BASE_URL` for `document-service` — with the default `http://localhost:8004`, the container pointed to itself instead of `permission-service`, every force-unlock call failed with `500`/`ConnectError`; added (env var + `depends_on: permission-service`). (2) Both new consumers (`document_service/consumer.py`, `permission_service/approval_consumer.py`) accessed the nested event payload with `payload["schlüssel"]` instead of `.get(...)` — a `permission.approval.approved` event actually published during this session's test runs over the shared NATS server with a mismatched payload shape (from `permission-service`'s own `test_approval_request_lifecycle_via_api`, which uses the same `action_type` with a test payload `{"x": 1}`) landed on the running `document-service` container and crashed its handler with `KeyError` — since the message was thereby never acknowledged, JetStream would have redelivered it endlessly. Fixed via defensive `.get(...)` + logging instead of crashing in both consumers (one new regression test each: `test_approved_event_without_document_id_is_logged_not_raised`, `test_scope_lock_create_with_missing_keys_is_logged_not_raised`) — **important lesson**: NATS is shared infrastructure across all services/test runs, independent of the Postgres test database isolated via `TEST_POSTGRES_DSN`; a test run of one service can publish real events that a concurrently running live container of another service actually consumes. **Fully verified live against the built stack** (after both fixes): force-unlock without configuration continued to go through immediately (regression confirmed); after `PUT /approval-config/document.force_unlock {"requires_approval": true}` the same call returned `pending_approval` while the lock remained active, `POST .../approve` with `approved_by == initiated_by` returned `403`, with a different person `200` — the lock was afterward actually lifted via the real NATS event path and `document.lock.force_released` published; the same flow confirmed for `POST /scope-locks` with `permission.scope_lock.create` (self-consumption also confirmed live). Test data then removed via `TRUNCATE`, all containers stopped. No browser test needed (pure backend session). `docs/adr/0022-four-eyes-approval-via-events.md` (new), `docs/services/permission-service.md` (new "four-eyes approval mechanism" section, events/open-points tables updated), `docs/services/document-service.md` (force-unlock section, new "consumes" entry), `docs/architecture.md` (new HTTP/event edges, event-bus-roles bullet, changelog line) updated. `graphify dms/ --update` deliberately deferred (Phase 6 not yet complete, P6-S5/S6 still to follow). Next step: **P6-S5** (superuser break-glass + domain-separated admin roles).

Immediately afterward the fifth session of Phase 6: **P6-S5** delivers superuser break-glass + domain-separated admin roles (4.6) plus the retrofits for Auth-Service `/users` and Admin-UI role checking. **Important follow-up question before implementation**: the original plan assumption (domain-admin roles as Keycloak realm roles, analogous to `dms-admin`) was explicitly corrected by the user via a follow-up question — the roles should be **system-native** (not in Keycloak), with automatically created **technical user accounts** per domain (default password = username, to be changed by the operator), user and role as separate concepts, user firmly assigned to the role. Additional, deliberately not-implemented context: these accounts should later also serve to verify that services dialing into the cluster are not forgeries. **Architecture decision** (see [ADR 0023](docs/adr/0023-superuser-breakglass-and-domain-admin-accounts.md)): the domain-admin "roles" live as `Role` rows in the already-existing RBAC system of the Permission Service (since P2-S2), separate from Keycloak, instead of in a third permission system — `permission-service` idempotently seeds 8 roles at every start (the 7 named in 4.6's domains plus `breakglass-approver`), checked via the already-existing `GET /effective-permissions/.../root`. `auth-service` only actually creates a technical account for the "user/permission management" domain (`users-admin`/`users-admin`) and assigns it the role via HTTP against `permission-service` (`permission_client.py`, best-effort at bootstrap — `permission-service` might not yet be reachable, no retry loop). The remaining 6 domains deliberately remain without an account (no attack-surface growth without functional benefit, since e.g. license management/query console do not yet exist as services). **Generic extension of the P6-S4 mechanism**: `ApprovalActionConfig` gets `required_permission` — if set, both initiator and approver must hold this capability (in addition to the initiator≠approver rule from 4.3), hard-preset to `breakglass.approve` for `auth.superuser.activate` — satisfies 4.6's "two different members of a permission group" more strictly than the mere "any second person" from 4.3, without special-case dispatch in the generic `approve` handler. **Superuser break-glass**: a single, disabled-by-default Keycloak account (`superuser`), reactivated exclusively via the (now role-bound) generic approval flow; `auth-service` gets its **first event-bus connection at all** for this (first consumer AND first producer, stream `auth`) and activates the account after approval (`enabled=True` + `dms_superuser_expires_at` attribute). **Deliberate simplification**: state lives as a Keycloak user attribute instead of a new Auth-Service database (same pattern as the theme preference, ADR 0009), and a single absolute expiry timestamp (default 30 min) instead of separate total-duration/10-minute-inactivity timers, enforced via a poll loop (`_superuser_poll_loop`, exactly the ADR-0020 pattern from workflow-service). `notification-service` additionally consumes `auth.superuser.activated` for an optional security notification, `audit-service` consumes `auth.>`. **Retrofit Auth-Service `/users`**: `GET`/`POST`/`DELETE` now check `admin.user_management` via a real `permission-service` call (`403` otherwise) — deliberately *not* via `X-DMS-Roles`, but the new, more generic pattern (the existing `dms-admin` reference-number check in `document-service`, P5e-S2, remains unchanged on the old pattern, documented divergence). **Retrofit Admin-UI**: `auth-context.tsx` additionally loads `permissions` after login (`GET /effective-permissions/{sub}/root`), `AdminSidebar` hides `/users/` without the capability, new `RequireCapability` component additionally protects the route server-independently, new `/superuser/` page (show status, request/approve activation — thin caller of the existing generic approval endpoints, no new UI concept). Tests: `permission-service` **77 tests** (previously 64, new: `required_permission` enforcement on create/approve, role-seeding idempotency), `auth-service` **35 tests** (new: domain-admin/superuser account bootstrap, `/users` gating including `403` without capability, `PermissionServiceClient` against a real running `permission-service`, consumer/poll-loop tests), `notification-service` **18 tests** (previously 17, new branch for `auth.superuser.activated`), `audit-service` unchanged **12 tests** (pure config line) — all green. Admin-UI **71 Vitest tests** (previously 64, new: `SuperuserBreakGlass`, `AdminSidebar` capability gating, `AuthProvider` `permissions`), `tsc`/`eslint` clean. **Two real bugs found and fixed during the first live/Docker run**: (1) Keycloak's name validation rejected `lastName: "Nutzer-/Rechteverwaltung"` because of the slash (`error-person-name-invalid-character`) — simplified to `"Nutzerverwaltung"`; (2) `auth-service`'s `pyproject.toml` did not declare `dms-eventbus-client` as a dependency — local tests ran anyway (workspace already fully synced), but the freshly built Docker container crashed at startup with `ModuleNotFoundError`, since `uv sync --frozen --package auth-service` only installs declared dependencies; fixed by adding it in `pyproject.toml` + `uv lock`. After both fixes, fully rebuilt and retested, all green. **A third, important observation made during the actual live smoke test** (no correction needed, but documented, see "Tooling & Testing" below): the superuser account was already `enabled=true` before the smoke test even began, because earlier test runs of `permission-service`/`auth-service` in this session had already published real `permission.approval.approved` events over the shared NATS server, which the live-running `auth-service` container actually consumed and thereupon activated the real Keycloak account, including 10 real security notifications to `notification-service` — Keycloak is thus, just like NATS (P6-S4), shared infrastructure without test isolation. The actual end-to-end flow was confirmed fully live independent of that: `users-admin` login → `GET /users` `200`, `effective-permissions` shows `admin.user_management`; unprivileged user → `403`; break-glass request by Alice without approval → `pending`; Alice tries to approve her own request → `403`; Bob (a second `breakglass-approver`) approves → `200`, `GET /superuser/status` shows the new, extended expiry time (event path thus actually traversed, not just the API response); `POST /superuser/deactivate` → immediately `active:false`; `notification-service` and `audit-service` (`auth.>`) both show real events. Afterward all test users/role assignments deleted, superuser account confirmed deactivated, all containers stopped. `docs/adr/0023-superuser-breakglass-and-domain-admin-accounts.md` (new), `docs/services/{auth,permission,notification,audit,admin-ui}-service.md` resp. `.md`, `docs/architecture.md` (new HTTP/event edges, event-bus-roles bullet, changelog line) updated. **Additionally at user request, independent of P6-S5's core mandate**: new `scripts/run-tests.sh` coordinates test runs across all services (bring up the Docker stack, ensure `dms_test`, always explicitly set `TEST_POSTGRES_DSN`, stop/start NATS-consumer containers selectively, `ruff`, summary table) — see "Tooling & Testing" below; uncovered real, pre-existing lint drift in 8 files on the first full run, fixed immediately. `graphify dms/ --update` deliberately deferred (Phase 6 not yet complete, P6-S6 still to follow). Next step: **P6-S6** (emergency shutdown, 4.8, builds on the break-glass activation from P6-S5).

Immediately afterward the sixth session of Phase 6: **P6-S6** delivers emergency shutdown (4.8: system-wide emergency lockdown + maintenance mode) plus the retrofits for Workflow-Service and Notification-Service authorization, narrowed in scope by the user via a follow-up question. **Important follow-up question before implementation**: since neither a lane-to-role resolution nor service-to-service authentication exists, the user chose the narrower, recommended option "gate admin actions, leave everyday usage open" — Workflow-Service process definitions (including script-task upload) behind `admin.object_config` (new technical account `config-admin`, symmetric to `users-admin`), instance start/task completion remain open to every authenticated principal and instead respect the new maintenance mode; Notification Service only gets a recipient existence check against real `auth-service` accounts, but itself remains reachable during maintenance mode (needed for security alerting). **Researched via plan mode before implementation instead of assumed**: an Explore agent against the real code confirmed that the gateway (`proxy()`) is the single pass-through point for every proxied request (already with a `public_routes` allow-list mechanism, directly extendable for a maintenance-mode blocker), that the P6-S4 four-eyes mechanism generalizes to a new `action_type` with no core changes (only a new `ApprovalActionConfig` row), that `permission-service` did not yet have `httpx` as a dependency (never needed before, since no cross-service call had ever originated there — proactively added, to avoid repeating the P6-S5 Docker-build error), and that neither `workflow-service` nor `notification-service` had any identity/permission checking. **Architecture decisions** (see [ADR 0024](docs/adr/0024-not-shutdown-gateway-enforced.md)): maintenance-mode state lives as a new singleton table `SystemMaintenanceMode` in the Permission Service (same pattern as `OcrConfig`/`GuardConfig`); who may trigger it is configurable via a new, seeded role `domain-admin-emergency` (capability `system.not_shutdown.trigger`), deliberately **without** an automatic account (like `breakglass-approver` — an emergency lockdown remains tied to a real person). `POST /maintenance-mode/trigger` **always first** checks a baseline capability (new `repository.require_capability()` function extracted from `_require_permission_if_configured` — a real difference from the previous four-eyes cases, which had no permission check at all outside the approval flow) and then runs either directly or via the existing four-eyes mechanism (default `requires_approval=false`, unlike break-glass **not** hard-preset to `true`). `POST /maintenance-mode/lift` is exclusively reserved for the currently active superuser — Permission Service for this **queries the Auth Service for the first time** (`GET /superuser/status`, extended by `principal_id`), the **first cross-service call of this service in this direction** (Auth Service has already been calling Permission Service since P6-S5). This new bidirectional dependency is not a runtime cycle (independent, non-nested, request-time HTTP calls), but a real Docker-Compose `depends_on` cycle risk — solved by `permission-service` only getting the new environment variable, but deliberately **no** `depends_on: auth-service` (the call happens only at `POST /maintenance-mode/lift`, not at its own startup), verified via `docker compose config --quiet`. **Gateway as central enforcement point + header-broadcast pattern** (new architecture decision): a new `MaintenanceStateClient` (mirrored on `InstanceResolver`, registry resolution instead of a fixed URL, short caching) blocks proxied requests outside an allow list with `503`, but fails open if the Permission Service is unreachable; every request let through additionally gets an `X-DMS-Maintenance-Active` header, so backend services (Auth Service `/login`, Workflow Service instance start/task completion) can read the state directly instead of each building their own polling connection to the Permission Service — only the SLA poll loop (no incoming request) gets its own thin `permission_client.py`. **Deliberately limited, honestly documented omissions** (federation/plugin instances do not exist, no system-wide write ban beyond the gateway, no hard stop of already-running executions, no increased audit priority) — same discipline as every previous session of this family. Tests: `permission-service` **92 tests** (previously 77, new: baseline capability enforcement, maintenance-mode activation/lifting including idempotency/reset-on-reactivation, trigger endpoint direct/via four-eyes, lift without active superuser → `403` as a real cross-service test against the live-running `auth-service`, approval-consumer branch), `auth-service` **41 tests** (previously 35, new: `principal_id` in superuser status, login rejection during maintenance mode except for the superuser, `config-admin` bootstrap), `gateway-service` **14 tests** (previously 12, new: blocking/allow-list via a swapped `app.state.maintenance_state`, same test pattern as the existing rate-limiter swap), `workflow-service` **52 tests** (previously 46, new: `403` without `admin.object_config` when creating/deleting a process definition via a real call against the live-running Permission Service, `503` during active maintenance mode for instance start/task completion), `notification-service` **22 tests** (previously 18, new: `400` for an unknown recipient for `email`/`in_app`, success case against a user actually created via `auth-service`, security notification on `permission.maintenance_mode.activated`) — all green, `ruff check`/`ruff format` clean. Admin-UI **77 Vitest tests** (previously 71, new: emergency-shutdown section in `SuperuserBreakGlass` including visibility by capability/active-superuser principle, new `MaintenanceBanner` component), User-UI **52 Vitest tests** (previously 49, new: `MaintenanceBanner`), `tsc`/`eslint` clean for both frontends. **Real bugs found and fixed during implementation** (before the live run, discovered during self-testing): (1) `_require_object_config` called `has_permission("", "admin.object_config")` with an empty principal when no `X-DMS-Principal` header was set — led to a malformed path (`/effective-permissions//root`) and a `404` instead of a clean `403`; fixed by a short-circuit before the client call. (2) Notification Service's `AuthServiceClient.recipient_exists` initially called `GET /users` without an auth header — gated since P6-S5, every call failed with `401`; fixed by logging in as `users-admin` on every call (no token caching, accepted latency trade-off). (3) A `depends_on: auth-service` on `permission-service` would have created a Docker-Compose cycle with its existing `depends_on: permission-service` (since P6-S5) — removed, since the cross-service call is request-time (see above). `docker compose config --quiet` confirms cycle-free. **Fully verified live against the built stack** (two real test users `p6s6-alice`/`p6s6-bob` created via `users-admin`, `alice` got `domain-admin-emergency`): `bob` (without capability) → `POST /maintenance-mode/trigger` `403`; `alice` (with capability) → `201`/`status:"activated"` (no four-eyes configured); gateway afterward blocks regular proxied requests (`GET /api/folder-service/...`) with `503`, still lets the allow list (`GET /api/permission-service/maintenance-mode`) through with `200`; login for `bob` during the lockdown → `503` with the expected message; login **attempt** for the username `superuser` is correctly **not** blocked by the maintenance-mode gate (no `503`, reaches Keycloak) — but fails there with `401`/"Account is not fully set up", see below. `POST /notifications` directly through the gateway during the lockdown → `503` (deliberately not on the allow list, see ADR 0024), while the **internal** NATS alerting path worked independently: `permission.maintenance_mode.activated` really generated a delivered email to `security@dms.local` (confirmed via `GET /notifications` directly at `notification-service`). `POST /maintenance-mode/lift` with `alice` as `lifted_by` (not the superuser) → `403`; with the `principal_id` of the **already-active** superuser (activated by an earlier test run in this session series, see "Tooling & Testing" precedent from P6-S5) as `lifted_by` → `200`, afterward normal access confirmed possible again after the 5s gateway cache (`maintenance_cache_ttl_seconds`) expired. `config-admin` login → process definition with script task `201`, `bob` (without capability) → `403`; independent of that, `bob` was still able to start an instance and complete its manual task outside maintenance mode, without any role. `POST /notifications` with unknown recipient → `400`, with `bob`'s real username → `201`/`sent`. **A regression bug found and fixed during the first full test run**: `case-service`'s test suite creates its own test process definition directly against `workflow-service`, without the new `X-DMS-Principal` header — after the `admin.object_config` gating, this failed with `403` (6 test failures). Fixed via a `case-service`-own fixture `_grant_config_admin_permission`, identical to `workflow-service`'s pattern (own test principal `case-service-test-config-admin`, real, idempotent call against the live-running Permission Service) — confirms exactly the kind of cross-service side effect that `scripts/run-tests.sh --build` as a full run is meant to guard against. **A real, previously undiscovered bug found, deliberately not fixed in this session** (see `docs/services/auth-service.md` "Open Points"): the superuser account (`enable_superuser_account`, P6-S5) has neither `firstName` nor `lastName` nor `email` set in the live environment — but Keycloak's Declarative User Profile requires these fields for the "user" context and rejects the direct login with `invalid_grant`/"Account is not fully set up", independent of the new maintenance-mode code. Likely cause: the account was once created by an early `auth-service` test run during P6-S5 development with an at-the-time incomplete payload, `ensure_superuser_account`'s `if exists: return` idempotency guard has never since backfilled it with the complete fields. The actual break-glass activation flow itself is unaffected by this (it runs via the approval mechanism, not via `/login`) — only an actual interactive login as superuser is. Not fixed, since it is P6-S5 code, not P6-S6 scope; this live test was the first to actually attempt a real superuser login. Afterward test data/role assignments removed (test users, role assignment), maintenance mode safely deactivated (confirmed `active:false`), all containers stopped. `docs/adr/0024-not-shutdown-gateway-enforced.md` (new), `docs/services/{permission,auth,gateway,workflow,notification,admin-ui,user-ui}-service.md` resp. `.md`, `docs/architecture.md` (new HTTP/event edges, event-bus-roles bullet, changelog line) updated. `graphify dms/ --update` deliberately deferred (the original P6-S5/S6 continue as standalone, future P6-S7/S8 sessions per the roadmap look-ahead — not a phase completion). Next step: **P6-S7** (Signature Service, 3.10).

Immediately afterward the seventh session of Phase 6: **P6-S7** delivers the new `signature-service` (3.10, eIDAS-compliant electronic signature) — recommended per `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` as "build scaffolding + connector abstraction, actually implement/test only SES/AES with system-internal/self-signed keys", QES deliberately excluded (no accredited external QTSP available). **Researched via plan mode before implementation instead of assumed**: three Explore agents confirmed that the storage-backend pattern (`id`-not-`type` keying, factory dispatch, ADR 0017) transfers 1:1 to signature-provider connectors, that `spiff_adapter.py` uses a plain `BpmnParser` (no `task_type` switch, dispatch only via `task_spec.manual`), and that the least invasive path to a "signature task" is a switch to `SpiffWorkflow.camunda.parser.CamundaParser` (populates `task_spec.extensions` from `camunda:properties`, without changing `manualTask`→`ManualTask`), as well as that no PKI/certificate code exists anywhere in the repo (`signature-service` is the first component to issue its own certificates). **Architecture decisions** (see [ADR 0025](docs/adr/0025-signature-service-internal-ca-and-connector-plugin.md)): `SignatureProviderConnector`(ABC) + factory (`connectors/__init__.py`), configured via `DMS_SIGNATURE_PROVIDERS` (default seed: one `internal` connector for SES/AES); `type: "qtsp"` is provided for in the schema but not implemented. A new singleton table `internal_ca` (same pattern as `SystemMaintenanceMode`/`OcrConfig`) holds a self-signed root CA generated via `cryptography` on first start (RSA 2048, 20 years); `InternalSelfSignedConnector.sign()` issues a leaf certificate per signing operation (SES: generic `CN=DMS System (SES)`, AES: personally identifiable with display name/email from a real `auth-service` account check) and embeds it into the PDF bytes via **pyHanko** (PAdES-B-B). Signing creates a **new document version** at document-service (`POST .../versions`, `expected_base_version_number` = the signed source version) instead of replacing the original version — the PAdES signature inevitably changes the PDF bytes, the unsigned version remains accessible untouched (2.1a). `object-type-service` got an additive column `required_signature_level` (only for `applies_to="document"`, same validation as `kennzeichen_format`), enforced at the signing operation itself. `workflow-service`'s parser switch to `CamundaParser` had to be accompanied by a matching serializer config (`SpiffWorkflow.camunda.serializer.config.CAMUNDA_CONFIG` instead of `BpmnWorkflowSerializer.configure()`) — otherwise JSON serialization of already-existing `userTask` fixtures (the P6-S2 boundary-timer test) fails, since `CamundaParser` maps `userTask` to Camunda's own spec class. **Two real API pitfalls found empirically**: pyHanko's `sign_pdf`/`validate_pdf_signature` internally call `asyncio.run()` and collide with the FastAPI handler's already-running event loop (`RuntimeError`) — fixed by using the coroutine-native `async_sign_pdf`/`async_validate_pdf_signature`, verified against the actually installed version (pyHanko 0.36.2), not just taken from documentation. **Signature task in Workflow Service** (7.1): a `<bpmn:manualTask>` with `camunda:properties` `taskType=signature`/`requiredLevel=...` technically remains an ordinary manual task, but `POST .../tasks/{id}/complete` now requires a `signature_id`, checked via a new `signature_client.py` (pattern like `permission_client.py` from P6-S6) against document match and minimum level. No process-designer palette entry for this yet (follows with P6-S8) — extension attributes must be set into the BPMN XML by hand. **Frontends**: Admin-UI `ObjectTypeEditor` got a conditional dropdown "minimum signature level"; User-UI got a new `SignaturesPanel` component below the metadata form (list + level selection SES/AES + "sign now" + on-demand "verify" with valid/invalid badge, QES visible but disabled). Tests: `signature-service` **11 tests** (new service, a real test PDF generated via `pypdf`, real pyHanko signing/verification, real calls against document-service/object-type-service/auth-service), `object-type-service` **71 tests** (previously 67, new: `required_signature_level` validation), `workflow-service` **60 tests** (previously 52, new: extensions parsing, signature-task gate with real signatures against the live-running `signature-service`) — all green, `ruff check`/`ruff format` clean. Admin-UI still **77 tests** (existing `ObjectTypeEditor` assertions extended with the new field, no new test case needed), User-UI **55 tests** (previously 52, new: `SignaturesPanel` empty state/list+verify/sign). `tsc`/`eslint` clean for both frontends. **Fully verified live against the built stack over the real gateway** (real test user `p6s7-alice` created via `users-admin`): real PDF uploaded → `POST /signatures` (SES) → `201`, new version 2 visible at document-service (431→8307 bytes, `comment: "Elektronische Signatur (SES)"`) → `GET .../verify` → `valid:true`. Object type with `required_signature_level=aes` created, document of this class assigned → SES attempt `400` ("requires at least signature level 'aes'") → AES attempt `201` with correct personally identifiable certificate subject (`CN=Alice Seven`, email as `1.2.840.113549.1.9.1` OID). BPMN with signature task uploaded via `config-admin`, instance started with `initial_data.document_id`, `GET .../tasks` correctly showed `extensions: {taskType: "signature", requiredLevel: "aes"}` → task-complete without `signature_id` → `400`; with a signature belonging to a different document → `400` (document mismatch correctly detected); with the matching AES signature → `200`/`status: "completed"`. Afterward fully cleaned up (test user, both test documents soft-deleted, test object type deleted, test process-definition delete attempt expectedly returned `409` due to the still-existing instance — same accepted behavior as in every previous session), all containers stopped. `docs/adr/0025-signature-service-internal-ca-and-connector-plugin.md` (new), `docs/services/signature-service.md` (new), `docs/services/{object-type,workflow,audit,admin-ui,user-ui}-service.md` resp. `.md`, `docs/architecture.md` (new node, new HTTP/event edges, event-bus-roles bullet, changelog paragraph) updated. `graphify dms/ --update` deliberately deferred (not a phase completion, Phase 6 only ends with P6-S8). Next step: **P6-S8** (Process Designer, standalone frontend application with `bpmn-js`, ADR 0021).

Immediately afterward the eighth session of Phase 6: **P6-S8** delivers the Process Designer as a new, standalone frontend application `apps/process-designer` (port 3002, concept 7.1/8) — graphical BPMN 2.0 modeling against the existing Workflow Service. **Plan follow-up question before implementation**: the first plan draft (only BPMN modeling + signature-task properties panel) was rejected by the user at plan approval and extended with two requirements — real process versioning immediately, as well as cross-installation external swimlanes/handover (Federation Hub, 7.4), while explicitly still **no** multi-installation of the designer itself. Split via `AskUserQuestion` (see "Process Designer / process versioning" above): versioning was implemented still in this session, Federation Hub as a new session **P6-S9** inserted into the roadmap (pulled forward from P13-S3/S4). **`bpmn-js-spiffworkflow` discarded during actual research** (WebSearch/WebFetch against npm/GitHub, not taken from training data): not published on npm since 2022 (only obtainable via a `github:...#main` branch pin, reproducibility risk), plus a license inconsistency between npm metadata (MIT) and the current GitHub `package.json` (LGPL) — see [ADR 0026](docs/adr/0026-process-designer-bpmn-js-without-spiffworkflow-addon.md), deviating from the ADR-0021 assumption. Instead `bpmn-js` + `bpmn-js-properties-panel` + `@bpmn-io/properties-panel` + `camunda-bpmn-moddle`, with an **own** `SignatureTaskPropertiesProvider` (checkbox "signature required" + level selection SES/AES/QES on `bpmn:ManualTask` elements, reads/writes the same `camunda:properties` that `CamundaParser` has expected since P6-S7) — the provider/group/entry registration pattern (`propertiesPanel.registerProvider`, `bpmnFactory.create`, `commandStack.execute('element.updateModdleProperties', ...)`) was traced against the actually installed, bundled source of `bpmn-js-properties-panel`, not assumed from documentation. **Process versioning in the Workflow Service** (see [ADR 0027](docs/adr/0027-workflow-process-definition-versioning.md)): `name` changes from a globally unique identifier to a process-family key, new column `version` (server-side automatically `max(version per name)+1`), uniqueness now over `(name, version)`. `POST /process-definitions` under an existing name automatically creates the next version — the previous `409` duplicate path is removed without replacement. `GET /process-definitions` by default returns only the newest version per family (Postgres `DISTINCT ON`), `?name=` returns the full history. Migration idempotent via `DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS` (old unique constraint on `name` alone, name empirically confirmed against the running dev stack via `\d`) + `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` (Postgres has no `ADD CONSTRAINT IF NOT EXISTS`). **Single-installation like User-UI** (global `dms.tokens` key), but with admin-ui-like `permissions` loading (`getEffectivePermissions`) for the capability gating: reading/opening remains open to every authenticated principal, saving/deleting are button-gated behind `admin.object_config` (P6-S6). `BpmnDesigner` mounts via `next/dynamic`/`{ssr:false}` (bpmn-js manipulates the DOM directly, incompatible with `output:"export"`'s build-time render pass) and exposes `exportXml`/`importXml` via an `onReady` callback prop instead of `forwardRef`/`useImperativeHandle` (avoids uncertainty about ref forwarding through `next/dynamic`). **A real build error found and fixed on the first `next build`**: `useService` is re-exported by `bpmn-js-properties-panel`, not by `@bpmn-io/properties-panel` (the original import failed with "not exported"). Tests: **workflow-service 64 tests** (previously 60, 4 new: version assignment instead of 409, `GET` list filtering with/without `?name=`), **process-designer 22 tests** (new app: `AuthProvider`/`ThemeProvider` copies, `ProcessDefinitionList` including version history/capability gating/backend error messages, `BpmnDesigner` with a mocked `bpmn-js`, `SignatureTaskPropertiesProvider`'s pure `getSignatureLevel`/`isSignatureRequired` read functions against a moddle-element double with no DOM) — `tsc`/`eslint`/`next build` clean (including a justified `no-explicit-any` ESLint exception, since `bpmn-js` itself types `ModdleElement`/`Moddle` as `any`). `case-service` (26 tests) re-verified without regression after container stop/start (known NATS durable-consumer collision with a running container). `infra/docker-compose.yml` (new `process-designer` block, port `${PROCESS_DESIGNER_PORT:-3002}`), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`/`PROGRESS.md` (new session P6-S9, P13-S3/S4 back-reference), `docs/adr/0026-*.md`/`0027-*.md` (new), `docs/services/process-designer.md` (new), `docs/services/workflow-service.md`, `docs/architecture.md` (new `ProcessDesigner` node, changelog paragraph) updated. `graphify dms/ --update` deliberately deferred (Phase 6 only ends with P6-S9). Next step: **P6-S9** (Federation Hub scaffolding, pulled forward from P13-S3/S4).

Immediately afterward the ninth and last session of Phase 6: **P6-S9** delivers the Federation Hub scaffolding (7.4) — pulled forward from P13-S3/S4 after the P6-S8 follow-up question. Before plan approval, two `AskUserQuestion` follow-ups: (1) actually implement end-to-end encryption now instead of only with P13-S4 (user chose the recommended option — every installation generates its own RSA-2048 key pair, see below), (2) smoke test via self-loopback instead of a second simulated installation (recommended option likewise chosen). New, standalone `services/federation-hub-service/` (standard service template, port 8018, schema `federation`) — deliberately **not** an internal service of this installation: no registry self-registration, no `depends_on: gateway-service`, no event-bus producer/consumer, only shipped in `docker-compose.yml` for local-development convenience. Address book (`Installation`: `id`, `callback_base_url`, `public_key_pem`, `api_key_hash`, version/compatibility range) + control center (`Handover`: metadata only, no payload field, synchronous delivery within the same request). **Trust model refined during implementation**: the design sketched in the plan (hub delivers deliveries using the target installation's plaintext API key known to it) would have forced the hub to store foreign secrets in plaintext — instead the hub got its own RSA-2048 signing key pair (`hub_identity`, singleton like `signature-service`'s `InternalCa`) and signs every delivery itself (RSA-PSS, `X-Federation-Hub-Signature`); installations verify using the hub's public key retrieved once via `GET /public-key` (trust-on-first-use). API keys are only SHA-256-hashed installation-side (deliberately no bcrypt/argon2 — the key is already a high-entropy random value, not a human-chosen password). Documented in new [ADR 0028](docs/adr/0028-federation-hub-trust-and-encryption-model.md). **Workflow Service**: two new manual-task special forms (`taskType=federated`/`federated_return`, `camunda:properties`, no new BPMN element — same pattern as the signature task) are automatically dispatched as soon as ready (`_dispatch_pending_federation_tasks`, called after instance start/task completion/SLA poll tick) — a direct `.../complete` call on such a task returns `409`. New tables `federation_identity` (own key pair, API key received from the hub, cached hub public key) and `federation_task` (links a local task/instance with a hub `handover_id`, prevents double dispatching). New public endpoints `POST /federation/inbound`/`.../inbound-result` (no `X-DMS-Principal`, authentication via the hub signature, still respect maintenance mode — deliberately **not** in `gateway-service`'s `maintenance_mode_allowed_routes`, since federated processing is everyday processing, not an admin action) as well as `GET /federation/installations` (ungated proxy onto the hub's address book, basis for the designer selection). `gateway-service`'s `public_routes` extended with both inbound paths. **Process Designer**: new properties-panel group "Federation (7.4)" on `bpmn:ManualTask` elements (`FederatedStepPropertiesProvider.tsx`, deliberately duplicated rather than shared with the signature-task provider), installation list loaded once before modeler creation and injected as a static didi value — the entire group stays hidden if the hub knows no installations (satisfies concept 7.1 literally). Deliberately **no** real swimlane editing in bpmn-js (no established provider pattern for it, significantly higher risk) — the target installation remains a property of the process step, not of a lane. **Notification Service**: new fourth consumer branch for `workflow.federation.inbound_received`, same `notify_email` pattern as `escalation_email` for the SLA timer. **Real bug found only during the test run**: the new subject shares the `"workflow"` stream with the already-existing `workflow.task.escalated` — a durable JetStream consumer name is unique per stream, not per subject; a second `subscribe()` call with the same durable name for a different filter subject on the same stream reproducibly failed with "consumer is already bound to a subscription"; fixed by a separate second durable name only for the new subject (no redelivery of the existing history of the three existing subjects). **Two further real bugs found only during the self-loopback smoke test itself** (see ADR 0028 "addendum" for the full analysis): the hub originally committed the handover row only after the delivery attempt (in the self-loopback the target installation calls back synchronously before the row was visible, `404`) and originally generated `handover_id` itself (circular dependency, since the sending installation's own local bookkeeping needs this ID already before the nested callback) — fixed by committing before delivery in the hub as well as a protocol change (the sending installation generates `handover_id` itself and commits its own `FederationTask` row before the hub call), accompanied by a loosening of `FederationTask`'s uniqueness to `(handover_id, direction)`. Tests: **federation-hub-service 21 tests** (new service: registration/update with/without API key, version-compatibility matrix, real signed HTTP delivery to an in-process stub via `httpx.ASGITransport`, deterministic `delivery_failed` case via `httpx.MockTransport`), **workflow-service 79 tests** (previously 64, 15 new: crypto round trip, real self-registration against the running hub, signature rejection, `409` guard, dispatch with a real but unreachable target, maintenance-mode guard, `(handover_id, direction)` disambiguation — a full end-to-end round trip is not representable with `TestClient`, deliberately left to the live smoke test like the SLA poll loop), **notification-service** two new consumer tests, **process-designer 28 tests** (previously 22, 6 new: `FederatedStepPropertiesProvider`'s pure read functions, `BpmnDesigner` now with six instead of four modules) — `tsc`/`eslint`/`next build` clean. Live smoke test: `federation-hub-service` + newly built `workflow-service` started, real self-registration confirmed (`GET /installations` shows its own entry with a real public key), self-loopback round trip (sender process with a `federated` task targeting its own `installation_id`, recipient process with a `federated_return` task linked via `DMS_FEDERATION_PROCESS_TYPE_MAP`) completed successfully, `notify_email` confirmed via Mailpit, direct `.../complete` attempt on the `federated` task rejected with `409`. `infra/docker-compose.yml` (new `federation-hub-service` block, port `${FEDERATION_HUB_SERVICE_PORT:-8018}`, `workflow-service` connected to it by default), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`/`PROGRESS.md` (P6-S9 done, P13-S3/S4 adjusted accordingly — P13-S4's scope shrinks to mTLS/key rotation, since E2E encryption was already implemented here), `docs/adr/0028-*.md` (new), `docs/services/federation-hub-service.md` (new), `docs/services/workflow-service.md`/`process-designer.md`/`notification-service.md`, `docs/architecture.md` (new `FederationHub` node, deliberately placed outside the backend subgraph) updated. **Phase 6 thereby completed** (P6-S1–S9). Next step: **P7-S0** (phase kickoff planning for Phase 7).

**Roadmap look-ahead planning after P6-S2** (user request, no code, no own P6-Sx line): the remaining roadmap (36 open sessions at the time) was planned ahead, so that future sessions can start faster. An Explore agent read all concept sections of the remaining Phase-6 sessions in full (2.3, 4.3/4.6/4.7/4.8, 3.10, 7.1/8) as well as the entire rest of `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s structure; afterward the licensing questions this raised were actually checked via WebSearch/WebFetch instead of assumed. **Most important finding**: `bpmn-js`/`bpmn-js-spiffworkflow` (for the Process Designer) are not under MIT/Apache, but under their own "bpmn.io License" — free commercial use, but a hard-coded, non-removable watermark (link to bpmn.io) on every rendered diagram, no purchase option found to remove it. Decided via follow-up question: accept the watermark (same pattern as ADR 0018 for SpiffWorkflow), see new [ADR 0021](docs/adr/0021-bpmn-io-license-watermark.md). **Second finding**: the original `P6-S4` bundled three independent concept topics (4.3 four-eyes, 4.6 break-glass, 4.8 emergency shutdown) AND was simultaneously the planned catch-up slot for six already-known, deliberately unsecured spots from earlier sessions (see "Authorization" below) — too much for one session. Decided via follow-up question: now split into `P6-S4`/`P6-S5`/`P6-S6` (assignment of the six legacy cases by thematic proximity, **provisional**, to be confirmed/adjusted at the respective session start), the original `P6-S5` (Signature Service, pyHanko is MIT-licensed, no comparable licensing question) and `P6-S6` (Process Designer) move up accordingly to `P6-S7`/`P6-S8` — see `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` Phase 6 for the full new breakdown including architecture/scope notes per session. **Third point (research depth)**: for Phases 7–16 deliberately **no** deep research was done now (library/version status would age by the time of actual implementation months later anyway) — instead, starting with Phase 7, **every phase gets a new first session `PN-S0`** ("phase kickoff planning"), which at session start takes on exactly the role this round just took on for Phase 6. This grows the roadmap from 67 to **79 sessions** in total (+2 from the P6-S4 split, +10 from the ten new `PN-S0` sessions in Phases 7–16), of the originally 36 open sessions **48** remain open. Cross-references to the old P6-S5/P6-S6 numbers in already-existing documents (`docs/architecture.md`, ADR 0018/0019, `docs/services/workflow-service.md`, `services/workflow-service/README.md`) were updated as well — earlier mentions of "P6-S4"/"P6-S5"/"P6-S6" in the **dated changelog paragraphs above** (e.g. in the P6-S1 paragraph) still refer to the old numbering before this restructuring and were deliberately not changed retroactively (changelog stays a log). The next actual implementation session remains unchanged **P6-S3**.

Details per session: see [`IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`](IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md).

## Open Decisions

Consolidated at the MVP milestone (P4-S3) — sorted by topic instead of chronologically, resolved items removed (e.g. the provisional structure event contract Permission↔Folder, confirmed since P3-S3). See also [`docs/architecture.md`](docs/architecture.md) for the big picture.

### Fundamental decisions still to be made
- Currently no open fundamental decisions. **SpiffWorkflow license** (concept 13, was due before P6-S1): decided — LGPLv3 accepted as an unmodified dependency, see [ADR 0018](docs/adr/0018-spiffworkflow-lgpl-license.md). No longer a blocker for P6-S1.

### Authorization — a consistent, known pattern
All previous "sensitive" endpoints share the same gap: the gateway (since P4-S1) only checks whether a valid bearer token is present (**authentication**), never whether the principal is authorized for the specific action (**authorization**). Closing this gap would require the respective backend service to evaluate the identity headers passed through by the gateway (`X-DMS-Principal`/`X-DMS-Username`/`X-DMS-Roles`) — until now **no** service evaluated them. **Broken for exactly one case since P5e-S2**: Document Service checks `X-DMS-Roles` against `dms-admin` before `attributes["Kennzeichen"]` may be changed (see "Reference-Number Generator" below) — deliberately pulled forward as a single case, since the user explicitly wanted immediate enforcement here. **Planning correction after P6-S4**: the original consolidation decision (after P5b-S6) assumed P6-S4 would "introduce a real role/permission check" — what was actually implemented was an **optional** four-eyes mechanism (4.3, see below) that involves a second person in the flow, but is **not** an `X-DMS-Roles` evaluation and by default remains ungated until an action type is explicitly activated. The actual "who is even allowed to initiate/approve" role check for force-unlock/scope locks remains open — only the four-eyes *addition* to it now exists. **A second, real pattern established since P6-S5**: for new checks, `X-DMS-Roles` is no longer evaluated, but a real `permission-service` call against system-native domain-admin roles separate from Keycloak (4.6) — so far for exactly one case (Auth-Service `/users` + Admin-UI, capability `admin.user_management`), see [ADR 0023](docs/adr/0023-superuser-breakglass-and-domain-admin-accounts.md). The two patterns (P5e-S2's `X-DMS-Roles` string comparison vs. P6-S5's `permission-service` check) deliberately coexist unharmonized. **Known gap of the P5e-S2 implementation**: `dms-admin` is only idempotently *created*, the *assignment* to specific users has no API/UI yet (see `docs/services/auth-service.md` "Open Points") — must for now be done via the Keycloak Admin Console.
- **Document Service force-unlock** (4.3): still no role check; since P6-S4 an optional four-eyes intermediate step can be activated (`PUT /approval-config/document.force_unlock`, off by default), see [ADR 0022](docs/adr/0022-four-eyes-approval-via-events.md).
- **Permission Service scope locks** (`POST/DELETE /scope-locks`, 4.7): still no role check; since P6-S4 optionally the same four-eyes mechanism (`permission.scope_lock.create`/`.release`, off by default).
- **Auth Service user management** (`/users`): enforced since **P6-S5** — requires the capability `admin.user_management` (403 otherwise), see `docs/services/auth-service.md`.
- **Admin-UI overall**: enforced for `/users/` since **P6-S5** (nav hiding + `RequireCapability`); all other admin areas (object-type editor, registry, installations, OCR/upload/reference-number settings, storage guard) remain ungated, every logged-in user still sees them fully.
