- **Superuser break-glass** (4.6, since P6-S5): account disabled by default, reactivation only via the generic approval mechanism with an additional role binding (`required_permission="breakglass.approve"`) — see "Superuser Break-Glass" in `docs/services/auth-service.md` and ADR 0023. **Discovered during the P6-S6 live verification**: the superuser account cannot log in interactively in the current environment (missing `firstName`/`lastName`/`email` on the Keycloak account, Declarative User Profile rejection) — the activation flow itself is unaffected, only the `/login` attempt fails; not fixed (P6-S5 code, outside the P6-S6 scope), see `docs/services/auth-service.md` "Open Points".
- **5 of the 7 domain-admin roles from 4.6 without account/enforcement** (since P6-S5/S6): "user/permission management" (P6-S5) and, since **P6-S6**, "workflow configuration" (`config-admin`, Workflow Service) are enforced, the rest are only predefined in `permission-service` as a `Role` row — follows with the respective future retrofit session of that domain.
- **Workflow Service**: gated for process definitions since **P6-S6** (create/delete, capability `admin.object_config`) — instance start/task completion remain **deliberately** open to every authenticated principal (user decision: "gate admin actions, leave everyday usage open"), but respect the new system-wide maintenance mode since P6-S6 (4.8). Since script code enters the system exclusively via the now-gated BPMN upload, this effectively secures script-task execution as well, see "Workflow Engine" below.
- **Notification Service**: since **P6-S6**, `POST /notifications` checks the recipient's existence against real `auth-service` accounts (`400` otherwise, for `email`/`in_app`) — the *caller* itself remains ungated, every authenticated principal can trigger a notification for any known recipient, see "Workflow Engine / Notification Service" below.
- **Case Service** (since P6-S3): ungated — creating circulation folders, adding/removing document references is possible for every authenticated principal, see `docs/services/case-service.md` "Open Points".
- **Emergency shutdown (4.8, since P6-S6)**: who may trigger the system-wide emergency lockdown is configurable via the new, seeded role `domain-admin-emergency` (capability `system.not_shutdown.trigger`), deliberately with no automatic account (like `breakglass-approver`). Lifting it is reserved exclusively for the currently active superuser (cross-service check against `auth-service`). The gateway blocks every proxied request during an active lockdown except a small allow list, independent of RBAC — see [ADR 0024](docs/adr/0024-not-shutdown-gateway-enforced.md). Federation (7.4)/plugin instances (3.8) do not exist, a system-wide write ban beyond the gateway is not implemented, no increased audit priority.
- **Signature Service** (3.10, since P6-S7): `POST /signatures` itself is **ungated** — every authenticated principal can create a signature for any PDF document, as long as `signer_principal_id` is a real `auth-service` account. No new domain-admin role. `POST /signatures` is additionally not on the gateway allow list, so it is automatically blocked as well during the Not-Shutdown maintenance mode (4.8, default deny). The only real enforcement is the minimum signature level configurable per object type, see `docs/services/signature-service.md`.

### Auth / Identity (4.4)
- **AD group → internal role mapping**: still not implemented. Permission Service does not resolve group memberships — a `principal_id` of an assignment must exactly match the queried principal.
- **Issuer hostname consistency Keycloak**: currently uncritical (only internal password grant); for a future browser-based redirect flow, the hostname configuration will need to be reconsidered.

### Storage (3.6)
- **Redundancy limited to two targets** (ADR 0004): `local`+`s3` instead of a generic target set.
- **No automatic periodic execution** of fixity checks/replication retry queue — on-demand endpoints for a future external scheduler (at the earliest Plugin Orchestration Service, Phase 10).
- **Rebalancing on newly added target**: implemented since **P5c-S2** — the restart already required for a target-set change automatically triggers `repository.seed_pending_copies_for_new_target` via the storage-device-swap guard's first-start bootstrap (marks all already-existing objects with no copy on the new target as `pending`, the existing retry queue then catches them up). Verified live against the real stack (223 real objects automatically flagged, a subset successfully replicated, then fully cleaned up). Removing a target from the target set remains open (a related but different question — orphaned `object_copy` rows), see `docs/services/storage-service.md` "Open Points".
- **Storage-device identity + arbitrarily many devices of the same kind**: implemented since **P5b-S6** (ADR 0017) — the target set is now a real `Settings.targets` list (key `id`, not `type` → arbitrarily many instances of the same kind possible), each target gets a device ID (marker object under a reserved key, reference value additionally in the shared DB), start refusal on mismatch, DB-backed admin override (`GET`/`PUT /guard-config`, Admin-UI page `/storage-guard/`) for a degraded start with automatic re-replication flagging. **Since P5c-S2**: `POST /guard-status/{target_id}/reidentify` accepts an intended storage-device swap at runtime, without a restart (Admin-UI button "accept storage-device swap") — replaces the previously necessary direct DB correction. Both ADR-0017 follow-up points thereby resolved.

### Object Model & Layouts (2.2/2.2a/2.2b, new since Phase 5b)
- **Enforced object hierarchy (`allowedParentTypes`) and class icons**: implemented since **P5b-S1** (ADR 0013) — Object-Type Service (schema+validation), Folder Service (create *and* move), Document Service (create). Admin-UI handling (checkboxes for `allowedParentTypes`, curated icon selection field) since **P5b-S3**, User-UI display of class icons before folder names (list + tree) since **P5b-S4** — thereby the original concept-8 scope ("class icon before every name") is fully implemented. Still open (see ADR 0013 "Consequences"): no retroactive check when `allowedParentTypes` is tightened after the fact, no cycle detection across multiple classes; icon provenance/format (concept 13) is pragmatically answered with a curated, hard-wired set rather than definitively.
- **Form layouts** (display/search/upload from a shared, GUI-generated JSON representation): fully implemented — data model + smart-layout generation since **P5b-S2** (ADR 0014), Admin-UI editor/layout designer since **P5b-S3**, User-UI consumption in `MetadataPanel`/`SearchPane`/`UploadForm` since **P5b-S4** (shared `LayoutFormFields` component, responsive breakpoint measured against window width). Still open: no retroactive check when an object type's attribute list changes after the fact while a custom layout is already saved (see ADR 0014); breakpoint measures window width instead of the actual (freely resizable via `Splitter`) panel width.
- **Explorer tree view**: implemented since **P5b-S4** (`FolderTree`, a user-togglable alternative to the list view, remembered only locally in `localStorage` rather than across devices like the color scheme) — the breadcrumb path on clicking a tree node is reconstructed client-side from the already-expanded ancestors instead of via a new backend endpoint (see ADR 0015).

### OCR (3.9)
- **Configurability**: implemented since **P5b-S5** (ADR 0016) — `ocrEnabled` as a Docker Compose profile opt-out (`profiles: ["ocr"]`, no Admin-UI switch, since a non-deployed container cannot accept a request), maximum word ceiling and processing batch size as a DB-backed, live-editable `OcrConfig` row (`GET`/`PUT /config`, new Admin-UI page `/ocr-settings/`). Word ceiling is estimated (`page count × 250`, no exact count), batch size limits concurrency via a new optional `max_concurrency` parameter of `NatsEventBusClient.subscribe()` (default remains strictly sequential, unchanged for all other consumers). Still open (see ADR 0016 "Consequences"): word-ceiling estimation is coarse, no resource accounting per concurrently running engine call.

### Content-Type Governance & Upload UX (new since Phase 5d, user feedback after Phase 5c completion)
- **Content-type sniffing + format whitelist**: implemented since **P5d-S1** — Document Service determines `content_type` server-side via magic bytes (`python-magic`) instead of adopting the client header, admin-editable `UploadConfig` whitelist (`GET`/`PUT /upload-config`) rejects unlisted formats with `400`.
- **OCR triggering restrictable by content type**: implemented since **P5d-S1** — `OcrConfig.allowed_content_types` (positive list, empty = no restriction) applies in addition to the technical `select_engine()` selection.
- **Missing preview for text-based formats (`.txt`/`.json`)**: implemented since **P5d-S2** — client-side direct display for `text/*`/`application/json` in `PreviewPane` (`<pre>` block, truncation hint from 200,000 characters), no new rendering-service renderer.
- **Upload UX (pop-up, drag & drop)**: implemented since **P5d-S2** — `UploadForm` is a real modal dialog, accepts drag-and-drop over the entire dialog surface. → **Phase 5d thereby fully completed** (both sessions done).

### Reference-Number Generator / File Reference Number (Phase 5e, user request during P5d-S2 planning — **fully implemented, P5e-S1–S3**)
Three design decisions clarified in advance via follow-up questions, all implemented:
- **Counter reset**: per year, a separate counter per object type (`{objekttyp_id, jahr}` → `object_type_sequence` table) — not per day, not globally sequential. Implemented since **P5e-S1** (Object-Type Service, `POST /object-types/{id}/next-kennzeichen`, atomic via `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` + `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`), see `docs/services/object-type-service.md`.
- **Display-location configuration**: deliberately **no** catalog of several independent display points (tab title, list prefix, ... individually toggleable) — instead a single global default switch ("show reference number before file name"), which individual document classes can override via a tri-state field (`null`/`true`/`false`). Simpler than originally up for discussion (several fixed display points each on/off). Field since P5e-S1 (`kennzeichen_display_override`), global default (`kennzeichen_config`) + actual display in both frontends (explorer list, tree view, tab title) since **P5e-S3**.
- **Editability**: only the new realm role `dms-admin` may subsequently change an already-assigned reference number (`PATCH /documents/{id}`, `403` otherwise) — read-only for all other users. Implemented server-side since **P5e-S2** — this is the **first real role check in the entire system**: the `X-DMS-Roles` header injected by the gateway since P4-S1 had not been evaluated by any service until then (see "Authorization — a consistent, known pattern" above). Since **P5e-S3** additionally anticipated client-side in the User-UI metadata panel (field disabled without the `dms-admin` role) — the first UI-side role check in the entire system, purely cosmetic, the actual safeguard remains server-side. Deliberately **not** deferred to P6-S4, since the user explicitly wanted immediate enforcement here, unlike the previously deliberately bundled cases (force-unlock, scope locks, user management). **Planning correction**: the assumed "existing role management" for assigning `dms-admin` to users does not exist — see `docs/services/auth-service.md` "Open Points", still unresolved.

### Workflow Engine / Notification Service (7.1, new since Phase 6, P6-S1/P6-S2)
- **Authorization only for process definitions since P6-S6**: `POST/DELETE /process-definitions` now requires the capability `admin.object_config` (a real `permission-service` call, `403` otherwise) — starting instances and completing tasks remain **deliberately** open to every authenticated principal (user decision "gate admin actions, leave everyday usage open"), instead respecting the new system-wide maintenance mode (4.8, `503` during an active emergency lockdown). `notification-service`'s `POST /notifications` only got a recipient existence check since P6-S6, no caller role check — see [ADR 0024](docs/adr/0024-not-shutdown-gateway-enforced.md).
- **Script-task security**: SpiffWorkflow's default scripting directly executes Python code embedded in the BPMN XML server-side. Clarified via follow-up question in P6-S1: activated for the current internal test/development operation. **Effectively secured since P6-S6**: script code enters the system exclusively via the now-gated BPMN upload (no separate execution endpoint), so the `admin.object_config` gating automatically covers this case as well without needing its own check. **Still to be revisited before productive multi-user operation with BPMN import by domain admins who are not fully trusted**, see `docs/services/workflow-service.md` "Open Points".
- **State persistence**: full serialized SpiffWorkflow blob per instance instead of a normalized task table, see [ADR 0019](docs/adr/0019-workflow-full-state-serialization.md).
- **SLA time monitoring is polling-based, not push** (since P6-S2): an asyncio loop in `workflow-service` checks every running instance for due boundary timers every `sla_poll_interval_seconds` (default 30s). Precision tied to the poll interval, **no distributed lock** across multiple `workflow-service` replicas (would publish the same escalation multiple times) — see [ADR 0020](docs/adr/0020-sla-timer-polling.md).
- **Recipient resolution without RBAC** (since P6-S2, **still not implemented despite P6-S6**): `escalation_email` is an opaque, unvalidated process datum (convention like `business_key`), no resolution of a BPMN lane/"supervisor role" against real user accounts — `auth-service` currently also has no role lookup for users that would enable this. P6-S6's retrofit scope was deliberately limited by user follow-up to a pure existence check, no role resolution — remains open for a later session.
- **Notification delivery without retry** (since P6-S2): a failed email/webhook send remains permanently `status="failed"`, no automatic retry, no dead-letter handling.
- **No retrofit of existing "logs only" alerting spots** (since P6-S2): `storage-service` (comment "as long as no Notification Service exists (comes with Phase 6)") and other future consumers mentioned in the concept (force-unlock, break-glass, deletion-deadline pre-notice, license expiry, report dispatch, monitoring escalation) were **not** hooked up to `notification-service` in this session — P6-S2's title names only SLA time monitoring. Every future consumer registers its own subject into `notification-service`'s `subjects` list when actually connected.
- **`workflow.instance.completed` has had a first consumer since P6-S3**: `case-service` (see its own section below) — workflow-service itself did not need to be changed for this.
- **Signature task implemented since P6-S7** (3.10): BPMN parser switched to `CamundaParser`, so that `bpmn:extensionElements/camunda:properties` (`taskType=signature`/`requiredLevel=...`) become visible — technically it remains an ordinary manual task. `POST .../tasks/{id}/complete` requires a valid `signature_id` for a task so marked, checked against the new `signature-service` (document match + minimum level). **A real properties-panel entry has existed for this in the Process Designer since P6-S8** (checkbox + level selection on `bpmn:ManualTask` elements, `SignatureTaskPropertiesProvider`) — extension attributes no longer need to be set into the BPMN XML by hand.
- **Real process-definition versioning since P6-S8** (see [ADR 0027](docs/adr/0027-workflow-process-definition-versioning.md)): `name` is a process-family key instead of a globally unique identifier, `POST /process-definitions` under an existing name automatically creates the next version (the previous `409` duplicate path is removed without replacement), `GET /process-definitions` by default returns only the newest version per family (`?name=` for the full history). No race-condition lock on version assignment, no rollback endpoint, no deleting entire families — see ADR 0027 "Consequences".

### Case Service (2.3, new since P6-S3)
- **No authorization**: like every service before its respective enforcement — deliberately deferred to the P6-S4/S5/S6 family, see "Authorization" above.
- **No resource-tree entry in `permission-service`**: unlike `folder-service`, `case-service` registers no `resource.created`/`.moved`/`.deleted` events — no precedent for non-folder objects in the tree, `document-service` likewise does not register its documents individually.
- **Known race condition for fully automated processes with no manual task**: if `workflow-service` publishes `workflow.instance.completed` before the service's own `Case` row is committed, the event is lost — the circulation folder would incorrectly remain `"open"`. Not fixed (outside the core scope of this session), see `docs/services/case-service.md` "Open Points".
- **Working copies (2.3) deliberately live in `document-service`, not in `case-service`**: three additional, nullable provenance fields (`derived_from_document_id`/`derived_from_version_number`/`originating_case_id`) on its existing `POST /documents` — no new route, full reuse of the versioning/audit/virus-scan pipeline, see `docs/services/document-service.md` "Working Copies".

### Signature Service (3.10, new since P6-S7)
- **QES fully unimplemented**: neither a real QTSP connector nor a test case for it — a signing attempt with `level="qes"` fails with `400` regardless of the object type ("no connector configured"). Needs an external business relationship with an accredited trust-service provider, see [ADR 0025](docs/adr/0025-signature-service-internal-ca-and-connector-plugin.md).
- **Only PAdES-B-B, no PAdES-B-LTA**: no timestamp-authority countersigning — 3.10 explicitly names B-LTA for long-term archiving/records disposal (5.6). A future retrofit would need a real timestamp authority.
- **No OCSP/CRL revocation check**: `GET /signatures/{id}/verify` only checks integrity and certificate validity period — the only honest verification depth for a self-signed internal CA without real revocation-list infrastructure.
- **Signing creates a new document version** instead of replacing the signed version (2.1a) — the PAdES signature inevitably changes the PDF bytes, the unsigned original version remains accessible untouched.
- **Only PDF documents can be signed** — PAdES is PDF-specific (mandated by pyHanko itself).
- **Process-Designer properties-panel entry for signature tasks present since P6-S8** — see "Workflow Engine" above.
- **The technical account `users-admin` is reused a third time for a foreign domain** (signer existence check) — as with notification-service (P6-S6), already noted in ADR 0024 as a pattern worth revisiting.

### Federation Hub (7.4, new since P6-S9)
- **Real end-to-end encryption already implemented now**, contrary to the original roadmap assumption (P13-S4 was supposed to deliver this once the installation-ID foundations from P13-S1 exist) — user decision via `AskUserQuestion` at session start. Every installation generates its own RSA-2048 key pair, the hub never sees plaintext, see [ADR 0028](docs/adr/0028-federation-hub-trust-and-encryption-model.md).
- **Trust model refined during implementation**: the original plan foresaw the hub delivering inbound deliveries using the target installation's API key known to it — that would have forced the hub to store installations' API keys in plaintext. Instead the hub got its own signing key pair and signs every delivery itself (`X-Federation-Hub-Signature`) — installations only store hashed API keys for the reverse direction. No shared secret anywhere in plaintext.
- **Self-loopback instead of a real two-installation test**: this dev environment has only one running `workflow-service` installation — the live smoke test lets it hand off a federated step to itself (its own `installation_id` as the target). A complete round trip (encryption, hub mediation, signature verification, receipt, new local instance, reply) actually runs through this way. A real test with two separate installations is still outstanding.
- **Two real bugs found only in this process, invisible to unit tests** (see ADR 0028 "addendum"): (1) the hub originally committed the handover row only after the delivery attempt — in the self-loopback the target installation calls back synchronously into the hub before the row was visible (`404`), fix: commit before delivery. (2) the hub originally generated `handover_id` itself — but in the self-loopback the sending installation's own local bookkeeping needs this ID already before the nested callback can look it up there (circular dependency), fix: protocol change, the sending installation generates `handover_id` itself and commits its own row before the hub call. `FederationTask`'s uniqueness had to be loosened from `handover_id` alone to `(handover_id, direction)` for this.
- **`federation-hub-service` deliberately not an internal service**: no registry self-registration, no `depends_on: gateway-service`, no event-bus producer/consumer — shipped in the local `docker-compose.yml` anyway (dev-only convenience). An operator would run it completely separately in production.
- **No new BPMN element**: federated steps are, like the signature task (P6-S7), manual-task special forms (`taskType=federated`/`federated_return`, `camunda:properties`) — automatic dispatch, no manual completion possible (`409` on a direct attempt).
- **Deliberately no real swimlane editing in the Process Designer** — the roadmap wording "external swimlanes" has remained purely UX framing; technically the target remains a property of the individual process step (new properties-panel group, visible only when the hub knows at least one installation).
- **Open points**: no enforcement of declared `supported_process_types`/`supported_document_types` on the hub side, no retry queue for a temporarily unreachable target, process-type mapping on the recipient side purely configuration-based. The trust-model hardening topic then noted as "no mTLS/no real installation identity" was closed in P13-S4 (RSA-PSS request signing instead of API key, key rotation/revocation — see [ADR 0039](docs/adr/0039-federation-trust-hardening-request-signing-over-mtls.md)).
- **Real bug found and fixed in the notification-service consumer**: `workflow.federation.inbound_received` shares the `"workflow"` stream with the already-existing `workflow.task.escalated` (P6-S2) — a durable JetStream consumer name is unique per stream, not per subject; a second `subscribe()` call with the same durable name for a different filter subject on the same stream reproducibly failed with `"consumer is already bound to a subscription"`. Discovered only during the actual test run (not apparent when writing the code) — fix: a separate second durable name (`"notification-service-federation"`) only for the new subject, see `docs/services/notification-service.md`.

### Bugfixing outside the session roadmap (after P7-S0, before P7-S1)
User feedback on already-shipped features (3.7/3.9/3.10/2.2), not a regular `PN-SN` session — nevertheless fixed with full test/live verification effort:
- **`.ods` preview did not work at all**: there simply was no renderer for it (only `.docx`/`.pptx` had a text-extraction rendition, 2.4). New `OdsTextExtractionRenderer` (`odfpy`, Apache-2.0, no licensing question) in `rendering-service`, same `substitute_text` pattern as Word/PowerPoint.
- **`.docx`/`.pptx` preview did not work despite an existing `substitute_text` rendition**: `PreviewPane.tsx` only knew about `thumbnail` renditions and the client-side direct-text path (P5d-S2) — the `substitute_text` rendition that `rendering-service` had long been generating was never queried by the frontend. Fix: a new branch checks `listRenditions` for a `ready` `substitute_text` rendition before the OCR/thumbnail path and displays its content like client-side text.
- **PDF preview always showed only page 1**: the root cause lay deep in `ocr-service` itself, not the frontend — both `NativeTextLayerEngine` and `TesseractEngine` in `extract()` processed only `doc[0]`, regardless of the actual page count, and the pipeline persisted a fixed single `page-1.png`. Fix: both engines now raster/recognize all pages (`OcrExtractionResult.page_images: list[bytes]` instead of a single `bytes | None`), the pipeline stores one image per page (`page_image_storage_key` is now a prefix, no longer a full object key), `GET /ocr-results/{id}/page-image` got a `page_number` query parameter (default 1, `404` outside the valid range). `PreviewPane.tsx` got its own page selector (analogous to the existing version selector) that reloads only the page image specifically instead of triggering the full preview reload. Verified via a real live test against the running stack (three-page PDF uploaded, all three page images actually different, page 4 returns `404`).
- **Signing failed with `pyhanko.sign.general.SigningError: ... hybrid cross-reference sections ...`**: real PDFs (e.g. from LibreOffice) often have a hybrid cross-reference table (classic xref table + `/XRefStm` for older readers) — pyHanko rejects this in the default strict mode (protection against so-called "shadow attacks" during *validation* of foreign PDFs). For *signing* a document that was just uploaded, one's own, this is too common a legitimate case to reject outright — `IncrementalPdfFileWriter`/`PdfFileReader` in `signature_service/connectors/internal.py` now run with `strict=False`, with the reasoning commented in the code (a deliberate trade-off, not an oversight).
- **The signing form was offered for every document, even though signing is server-side only permitted for `application/pdf` anyway**: `SignaturesPanel.tsx` now checks the `content_type` of the current version (same `listDocumentVersions` pattern as `PreviewPane`) and hides the signing form for anything except PDF, replacing it with an explanatory note instead of a button doomed to fail.
- **When creating a folder, no folder class (object type with `applies_to="folder"`) was selectable**: the backend (`folder-service`) has supported `object_type_id` since P3-S3 already, only `ExplorerPane.tsx`/`DocumentWorkspace.tsx`/`lib/api.ts`'s `createFolder()` never passed it through. Fix: new selection field in the "new folder" form (visible only if at least one folder class exists — same pattern as the document-class selection during upload).
- All fixes verified with real tests (no additional mocking of the actually-fixed logic): `rendering-service` 30, `ocr-service` 36 (including a new live integration test against the real `document-service`/`storage-service`), `signature-service` 11, `user-ui` 60 Vitest tests plus `typecheck`/`lint`/`build` clean. Additionally a live round trip against the newly built stack (ODS upload → `substitute_text` rendition with correct cell content; three-page PDF upload → three actually different page images, `404` for page 4), test data then deleted.

### Compliance & Records Disposal (Phase 7, P7-S0)
- **P7-S0 was a pure research/planning session, no implementation code** — the first `PN-S0` kickoff session actually carried out under the convention introduced starting Phase 7 (Phase 6 had no such session retroactively, see "Roadmap look-ahead planning after P6-S2" below).
- **Retention/legal hold/deletion register (5.2/5.2a) start from essentially zero**: `document-service` only has a raw `deleted_at` with no restoration period, no retention fields on object type/folder, no legal-hold concept — a `grep` across the entire code returned zero hits for "legal_hold"/"hold"/"retention". P7-S1 therefore does not start by extending a partial concept, but builds trash/legal-hold/forced-deletion entirely from scratch.
- **Object-lock/WORM is completely missing in the Storage Service** — not planned in any session so far, but explicitly named by 5.2a as a prerequisite for the controlled forced-deletion exception (governance vs. compliance mode). Added as extra prep work in P7-S1 (see IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md); to be checked at P7-S1 session start whether that still fits into one session.
- **`audit-service` needs a retrofit before P7-S2 (reporting/forensic trace) is sensibly buildable**: the event chain is already hash-chained/immutable (`AuditEvent.prev_hash`/`hash`), but the acting user is only ad hoc in `payload` (no first-class field), and `GET /events` only knows `limit` — no filtering by subject/user/time range/action type. For 5.4b ("all actions by user X"/"all users on document Y") the raw append-only log is not enough without doing this retrofit first.
- **`rendering-service`'s `PdfArchiveRenderer` is only best-effort** (its own docstring: no ISO-19005 proof, no Ghostscript/veraPDF available) — for the "mandatory" archive conversion required by 5.6, to be checked at P7-S3 session start whether this suffices or a real conformance engine needs to be added.
- **Migration process (7.2) does not exist yet** — 5.6 describes the records-disposal workflow as "technically closely related" to it, but P7-S3 cannot copy a 7.2 blueprint; it must build its own audited/resumable workflow pattern directly on the Workflow Engine (P6-S1).
- **Already done and directly reusable**: the circulation-folder closure snapshot logic (2.3, `case-service`, `snapshot_version_number`) as well as the Storage Service's multi-backend support (ADR 0017) for 5.6/5.2a.
- **XDOMEA/KDBX licensing question clarified, see [ADR 0029](docs/adr/0029-aussonderung-xdomea-eigenimplementierung-kdbx-plugin.md)**: no maintained Python library exists for XDOMEA (only scattered state-archive test toolkits) — P7-S3 serializes/validates itself against the official XDOMEA 3.0.0 XSD schemas via `lxml`. For KDBX, `pykeepass` is the only established library, but it is under **GPL-3.0** (not LGPL like SpiffWorkflow, ADR 0018) — GPL-3.0 has no exemption for unmodified use as a dependency, a bundled `pykeepass` in the distributed Docker image would subject the whole service to copyleft obligations. **Decided via `AskUserQuestion` at session start**: KDBX support is implemented as an optional plugin not bundled by default (analogous to the storage-backend plugin principle, ADR 0017) instead of as a normal dependency or a full in-house reimplementation.

### Retention & Forced Deletion (5.2/5.2a, since P7-S1)
- **Two `AskUserQuestion` follow-ups before plan approval, both decided in favor of the larger scope**: (1) storage-level WORM/object-lock is built **now**, not deferred to a later session — even though this means a break in the `StorageBackend` interface and S3 Object Lock cannot be enabled retroactively on an existing bucket; (2) documents **and** folders were originally to be covered together in this session, not only documents.
- **Resulting split into P7-S1/P7-S1b** (own decision made during planning, analogous to the P6-S4→S4/S5/S6 and P6-S8→+P6-S9 precedent): both answers together produce a scope that noticeably exceeds every previous session (storage-interface break + two complete object-type variants). **P7-S1 itself was limited to the storage WORM foundation + the full functionality for documents**; the new **P7-S1b**, inserted directly afterward, carries over the same pattern (trash, legal hold, forced deletion, deletion register) to folders, which for this get their very first soft-delete concept in P7-S1b (`document-service` already had a raw `deleted_at`, `folder-service` currently has **no** soft delete at all).
- **Architecture — no new BPMN/timer service**: `workflow-service` has no generic "callback after N days" mechanism, only real process instances with timer/boundary events (confirmed at P7-S0). Instead `document-service` got its own `_retention_poll_loop` — exactly the same idiom as `workflow-service`'s `_sla_poll_loop` (ADR 0020).
- **One shared field pair for 5.2 and 5.2a**: `Document.retention_until` + `full_deletion` instead of separate configurations — when due, `full_deletion` decides whether it becomes a regular trash entry or a direct physical forced deletion (legal hold blocks both equally).
- **Legal hold + deletion register live in `document-service` itself**, not in a new compliance service — deliberately, to avoid a premature extraction before the need across more than one object type is established (to be reassessed once, e.g., circulation folders should also need retention, Phase 15).
- **Four-eyes principle for forced deletion reuses the existing force-unlock precedent 1:1** (`document_service/approval_client.py`, `consumer.py`, since P6-S4) via a new action type `document.force_delete` — no change to `permission-service` needed except the configuration row.
- **Storage-level WORM: governance instead of compliance mode** ([ADR 0030](docs/adr/0030-storage-object-lock-governance-mode.md)) — compliance mode would make the forced-deletion exception explicitly required by 5.2a technically impossible. Application-layer guard (`retention_guard.py`, portable for any backend type) plus additional real S3 Object Lock hardening for `type="s3"` targets.
- **A real bug found during live verification against MinIO**: a `delete_object()` without an explicit `VersionId` only removes a delete marker on a versioned bucket, the locked version remained really in place — `S3Backend.delete()` therefore first reads the current `VersionId` via `head_object` when Object Lock is active and passes it explicitly to `delete_object`. Found only through real integration tests against live-running MinIO, not through mocking.
- **Two real bugs found while testing `document-service`**: (1) `hard_delete_document` initially violated an FK constraint (`legal_hold_document_id_fkey`), since SQLAlchemy's unit-of-work does not automatically order raw-FK-column deletes without a declared `relationship()` — fixed by an explicit `session.flush()` between deleting dependent rows (versions/lock/holds) and deleting the `Document` row itself. (2) A singleton-config race: the new poll loop fires immediately on every fresh app start (`TestClient(app)` in every test) and competed with API-side get-or-create calls for the same `RetentionConfig`/`TrashConfig` row (`UniqueViolationError`) — fixed by explicitly seeding both singleton rows in the lifespan routine, **before** starting the poll loop and before accepting any requests.
- **A third real bug, found only during the live smoke test against the real stack** (not via `pytest`, since existing tests never reopened the poll-loop session across multiple real ticks): `_execute_or_defer_forced_deletion` only flushed `Document.force_delete_approval_requested_at` (`await session.flush()`), instead of committing it. Since the poll loop opens a **fresh** session per tick and closes it without an explicit commit (rollback on close), the lock was lost on every tick — with the four-eyes principle activated, **every further tick** created another `document.force_delete` approval request for the same document (observed live: 21 duplicates for a single test document, one more every 8 seconds). Fix: `await session.commit()` instead of `await session.flush()`. New regression test `test_force_delete_approval_requested_only_once_across_poll_ticks` (`test_retention_actions.py`) deliberately opens its own, fresh session per simulated tick (like the real poll loop) — a single reused session would have masked the bug via the identity map without actually committing it (this was itself once done wrong while writing the test and would otherwise not have caught the bug). **113 tests** (previously 112).
- **An incident during live test-data cleanup**: while attempting to clean up two own test documents via the trash-expiry path, `TrashConfig.restore_period_days` was set to `0` for testing — an **installation-wide**, not test-folder-scoped setting. The next poll tick thereby physically co-deleted **15 documents already sitting in the trash from earlier, unrelated test sessions** (all already soft-deleted beforehand, no active/production documents affected). User informed via follow-up question and asked for approval — answer: uncritical for the development environment ("test documents I would have deleted anyway"). Configuration immediately reset to `30`. Recorded as a lesson: installation-wide config changes (even "just for testing") can have effects far beyond one's own test area when a background poll loop is involved — future cleanups should target the individual document (e.g. via an already-scheduled forced deletion) rather than a global configuration lowering.
- **Deletion register not backup-differentiated** (5.2a requires "stored separately, independent of the regular backup cycle") — not fully satisfiable, since Phase 11 (backup/restore) does not yet exist. Compensated by every deletion-register row additionally being published as a regular event, which the existing hash-chained `audit-service` chain also records.
- **Deliberately open points of this foundation**: no legal-hold role check (any logged-in user can set/release a hold), the `local` storage backend still has no real WORM (only the application-layer check), `replication.py`'s re-replication propagates `retention_until` to `record_copy`, but not `lock_until` to the actual backend write call.
- Tests: `object-type-service` **76** (previously 71), `storage-service` **100** (previously 86), `document-service` **113** (roughly 43 new — the largest single change of this session, including 3 new integration tests against a live-running `storage-service` in `test_retention_actions.py`, one of which is the live-bug regression test described above), `notification-service` **26** (previously 22), `admin-ui` **86** (previously 77, including 2 new test files `RetentionSettings`/`DeletionRegister`), `user-ui` **64** (previously 60, including a new `retention-panel.test.tsx`) — all `ruff check`/`format`/`tsc`/`eslint`/`next build` clean. Full backend regression across all 18 services subsequently green as well.
- **Live smoke test against the newly built stack** (poll interval temporarily lowered to 8s): regular retention → trash → `POST /restore` within the period succeeded; legal hold demonstrably blocked a due `retention_until` deadline, after `POST .../release` the next tick took effect immediately; `full_deletion=true` with the four-eyes principle disabled deleted immediately and physically including a deletion-register entry; with the four-eyes principle activated the deletion remained pending until `POST /approval-requests/{id}/approve` (finding and fixing the bug described above in the process); object lock against an additional, purely test-purpose S3 target (fresh MinIO bucket, `object_lock_mode=governance`, mounted as a primary target to avoid the re-replication on-demand trap) — `DELETE` without bypass `403`, with `bypass_governance=true` + `dms-admin` role `204` and actually (not just a delete marker) removed. Test target/bucket/DB rows as well as test users/folders subsequently fully removed, the real, productively used `local` storage target left untouched.
- **Scope follow-up question at P7-S1b plan approval**: user raised two new ideas — (1) a context-menu entry "request deletion" for regular users, including the four-eyes principle even for deletion admins (no going it alone), analogous to the existing `document.force_delete` pattern, but as its own, separately configurable action type; (2) a favorites/bookmark-list feature for cases and folders for quick retrieval. Decided via `AskUserQuestion`: **both remain outside P7-S1b** and were inserted as new, standalone sessions **P7-S1c** (deletion-request workflow) and **P7-S1d** (favorites) in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` — P7-S1b itself remains strictly limited to the folder counterpart of P7-S1.

### P7-S1b: the same pattern for folders

- **Own tables instead of reuse**: `folder-service` got its own `legal_hold`/`deletion_register_entry`/`retention_config`/`trash_config` tables in the `folder` schema (structurally identical to the `document-service` counterparts), no cross-schema reuse — an installation operator can thereby configure different requirements (restoration period, deletion-reason requirement) for folders than for documents. `ObjectType.default_retention_days`/`deletion_reason_required_override` (already built object-type-wide in P7-S1) only needed to be consumed.
- **Cascading trash, synchronous instead of event-based**: `POST /folders/{id}/trash`/`.../restore` recursively moves/restores the entire active subtree — subfolders directly in `folder-service` itself, contained documents via a new, **synchronous** REST call to `document-service` (`document_client.py` ↔ new `POST /documents/cascade-trash`/`cascade-restore`/`count-active` endpoints, new `Document.deleted_via_folder_id` field). Synchronous rather than event-based chosen deliberately, so that e.g. an immediate `GET /documents/deleted` after the deletion is already consistent. Already independently deleted subfolders/documents remain untouched while cascading (their cascade provenance is not overwritten), otherwise a later restore of the parent folder would incorrectly bring them back too.
- **No automatic cascading of forced deletion**: before the `_retention_poll_loop` physically removes a folder with `full_deletion=true`, it checks via `document_client.count_active()` plus its own subtree query whether active subfolders/documents still exist — if so, the forced deletion is skipped for this tick (logged, next attempt on the next tick). Deliberate, conservative design decision: automatically extending physical deletions to an entire subtree would have been a significantly bigger risk than the accepted "hangs until manually cleared/via trash expiry".
- **Four-eyes principle and deletion reminder**: new action type `folder.force_delete` (exact copy-paste pattern of `document.force_delete`, own `approval_client.py`/`consumer.py` in `folder-service`), `folder.deletion.reminder` event (new `notification-service` consumer, a 1:1 copy of the `document.deletion.reminder` consumer).
- Tests: `document-service` **118** (previously 113, 5 new: 3 repository + 2 API tests for the cascade endpoints), `folder-service` **67** (entirely new for retention/legal-hold/trash/poll-loop/consumer, previously 29 pure CRUD tests, including a regression test for the live bug described below), `notification-service` **27** (previously 26, 1 new for `folder.deletion.reminder`), `admin-ui` **87** (previously 86 — `RetentionSettings` split into two independent sections, `DeletionRegister` merges both registers), `user-ui` **69** (previously 64 — new `folder-retention-modal.test.tsx` plus folder-trash/retention-modal tests in `document-workspace.test.tsx`) — all `ruff check`/`format`/`tsc`/`eslint`/`next build` clean. Full backend regression across all 18 services subsequently green as well (`scripts/run-tests.sh --build`).
- **Real bug found only during the live smoke test** (not via `pytest`, since existing tests never combined an already soft-deleted subfolder AND a due forced deletion of the parent folder against a real Postgres FK constraint): the not-empty check before a folder forced deletion used `list_active_subtree_ids` (hides already soft-deleted subfolders) and incorrectly considered a folder with only one *already-trashed* subfolder to be "empty". `hard_delete_folder` thereby failed live with `ForeignKeyViolationError` (`folder_parent_id_fkey`) — the still-present-as-a-DB-row but soft-deleted subfolder still referenced the parent folder via `parent_id`. Since the exception was thrown out of the poll-loop's iteration over all due folders, the entire remaining tick (including the trash-expiry phase for all other folders) got stuck on every further run as well. **Fix**: a new check `has_any_child_folder_row` (deliberately WITHOUT a `deleted_at` filter) blocks the physical parent-folder deletion as long as any subfolder row exists, deleted or not — the already-trashed subfolder after all still has its own, independent restoration period and is deliberately not automatically cleaned up along with it. New regression test `test_execute_or_defer_forced_deletion_skips_when_child_row_already_soft_deleted`.
- **Live smoke test against the newly built stack** (poll interval temporarily lowered to 8s, a disposable test user `p7s1b-smoketest` with the `dms-admin` role created via the Keycloak Admin REST API): (1) folder with a subfolder + two documents moved to the trash → both documents cascaded soft-deleted, `POST .../restore` restored the folder + subfolder + both documents, a third document previously independently deleted in the same folder demonstrably stayed in the trash; (2) legal hold demonstrably blocked a due `retention_until` deadline (`deleted_at` remained `null` over several ticks), after `POST .../release` the next tick took effect immediately (correctly cascading again onto subfolders + documents); (3) `full_deletion=true` on an already-empty folder with the four-eyes principle disabled deleted immediately and physically including a deletion-register entry; (4) `full_deletion=true` on a non-empty folder remained untouched over several ticks (finding the bug described above, live via log traceback), after specifically forcing the expiry only of the single test subfolder (`deleted_at` backdated by 40 days, **not** via the installation-wide `TrashConfig` — lesson from P7-S1 taken to heart), the subfolder was regularly cleaned up via trash expiry and the parent folder was then correctly force-deleted; (5) four-eyes activated for `folder.force_delete`, expiry repeated → deletion remained pending until `POST /approval-requests/{id}/approve`, then completed. All test folders/documents/storage objects as well as the test user subsequently fully removed (targeted by ID, no global config change), four-eyes configuration reset, poll interval reset to the default (3600s).

### P7-S1c: deletion-request workflow for regular users via context menu

- **Two follow-up questions at plan approval**: (1) How far does "request deletion" reach — only actual forced deletion, or already the regular move-to-trash? Resolved by design instead of by user decision: a new, own action type `document.delete`/`folder.delete` gates precisely the regular trash action, independent of `document.force_delete`/`folder.force_delete` (retention-triggered forced deletion), default `requires_approval=false` changes nothing about existing behavior. (2) **Missing approval inbox recognized**: the existing four-eyes mechanism (since P6-S4) had no operating interface anywhere — only `SuperuserBreakGlass`'s free-text field for a single, hardcoded request ID (`auth.superuser.activate`), no list, no reject path. User agreed to include a minimal approval inbox ("will later be folded into the administrative trash").
- **New action types instead of extending `force_delete`**: `document.delete`/`folder.delete` gate the manual, user-triggered regular deletion; `document.force_delete`/`folder.force_delete` remain unchanged for the retention-triggered, poll-loop-triggered forced deletion. Both independently configurable via `PUT /approval-config/{action_type}`.
- **Gate check directly in the endpoint**, exact pattern of the already-existing `force_release_lock` (4.2, P6-S4): new `POST /documents/{id}/trash` (the old `DELETE /documents/{id}?deleted_by=` remains unchanged — no frontend ever called it, documents so far had **no** deletion UI at all), existing `POST /folders/{id}/trash` extended in place (breaking change of the response to `TrashResult{status, folder|document, approval_request_id}`, migrated existing tests/frontend callers along with it). Enforced server-side, independent of the UI path.
- **No new self-approval logic needed**: `permission-service` already generically prevented (`NotInitiatorAllowedError`) initiator and approver from being identical — satisfies "even a deletion admin may not delete alone" without any new role concept.
- **New generic `ContextMenu.tsx`** in the User-UI (positioned at the click point, closes on outside click/Escape) instead of yet another icon button — deliberately built so that P7-S1d (favorites/"mark") can directly reuse the same mechanism with a further entry. Replaces the previous 🗑 button on folders, gives documents a deletion option in the UI for the first time ever.
- **New minimal approval inbox `ApprovalsPane`** (new `IconRail` entry "approvals") lives in the User-UI rather than the Admin-UI, since regular users approve each other here — not an administrative process. Deliberately filtered to only `document.delete`/`folder.delete` (no generic all-action-types inbox), approve button disabled for self-submitted requests.
- **No Admin-UI toggle for the approval requirement** — an identical, already-existing gap as for `document.force_delete`/`folder.force_delete`/`document.force_unlock`, deliberately left consistent rather than an island solution just for the two new action types.
- Tests: `document-service` **123** (previously 118, 5 new: 2 API tests for `POST .../trash` immediate/`pending_approval`, 3 consumer tests for `document.delete`), `folder-service` **71** (previously 67, 4 new: 1 API test for the `pending_approval` branch, 3 consumer tests for `folder.delete`), `user-ui` **78** (previously 69 — new `context-menu.test.tsx` and `approvals-pane.test.tsx`, plus converted/new tests in `document-workspace.test.tsx`) — all `ruff check`/`format`/`tsc`/`eslint`/`next build` clean. Full backend regression across all 18 services subsequently green as well.

### P7-S1d: Favorites/Bookmark List

- **Scope follow-up question at plan approval**: the roadmap line literally spoke of "favorites/bookmark list for **cases** and folders". In the code, however, "Akte" (case) is its own precise term (`case-service`, circulation folders, concept 2.3) — and this service so far has **no** UI at all in `user-ui` (only `SuperuserBreakGlass`-like free-text fields exist elsewhere, nothing at all for circulation folders). Three options presented: only documents+folders (lean, "Akte" meant colloquially), only circulation folders+folders (literal reading, additionally requires a first minimal circulation-folder list view), or all three. User chose **documents + folders** — no circulation-folder UI in this session.
- **New, standalone `favorite-service`** instead of extending an existing service: a purely user-related bookmark concept belongs to none of the existing services (neither document- nor folder-service knows "user" as its own concept), but fits well with the established one-service-per-concept pattern (`case-service` for circulation folders, `permission-service` for approvals). Schema `favorite`, table `favorite` (`user_id`/`object_type`/`object_id`/`created_at`, unique constraint on all three business fields), four endpoints (`POST`/`GET`/`DELETE /favorites`, `GET /healthz`), events `favorite.added`/`favorite.removed` (stream `favorite`) — `audit-service` immediately extended with `favorite.>`, same pattern as for every previous new producer stream. Port `8019`, no own NATS consumer (pure producer, not needed in `CONSUMER_SERVICES` of `scripts/run-tests.sh`).
- **Deliberately no referential check** against document-/folder-service when creating a favorite — unlike `case-service`'s document references (business data with a traceability requirement), a favorite is a low-stakes personal bookmark; an orphaned reference after deletion of the original causes no harm. The resolution logic (including 404 tolerance) deliberately lives in the calling UI (`FavoritesPane`), not in the service itself — keeps `favorite-service` as the only service in the system completely without a cross-service HTTP client.
- **Context-menu reuse as intended in P7-S1c**: the generic `ContextMenu.tsx` deliberately built there got a second entry ("add to favorites"/"remove from favorites") next to the existing deletion entry with no change to the component itself — exactly the use case it was prepared for in P7-S1c.
- **"Open" navigation with no new backend endpoint**: for documents the already-existing `openDocumentTab` suffices (independent of the currently displayed folder). For folders, a new, purely client-side loop climbs the `parent_id` chain via the already-existing `GET /folders/{id}` up to the root (`"root"`), to reconstruct the breadcrumb path for the existing (since P5b-S4) `navigateToFolder` — no new path-resolution endpoint needed in `folder-service`.
- Tests: `favorite-service` new, **12 tests**, `user-ui` **86** (previously 78 — new `favorites-pane.test.tsx` with 5 tests, a new multi-entry test in `context-menu.test.tsx`, two new/integrated tests in `document-workspace.test.tsx` for favoriting via the context menu and opening a favorited folder including the reconstructed breadcrumb path) — all `ruff check`/`format`/`tsc`/`eslint`/`next build` clean. Full backend regression across now **19 services** (new: `favorite-service`) green.
- **Live smoke test**: two disposable Keycloak users created, as user A a test folder + a test document created and both favorited (`POST /favorites` → `201`), duplicate correctly rejected with `409`, user isolation confirmed (user B sees none of user A's favorites), filtering by `object_type` works, removal (`DELETE /favorites`) works and returns `404` on a repeated attempt, audit trail shows `favorite.added`/`favorite.removed` with correct payload (`GET /events/verify` still `ok: true`, 10818 entries checked). **Side finding**: real `favorite.added`/`.removed` entries already found in the same audit trail for the users "alice"/"bob" — the user had already tried out the new context menu/`FavoritesPane` in parallel in the browser. Test data (users, folder/document rows, storage object) subsequently fully removed; the session's own audit entries deliberately left as a historical record (like the deletion register), the entries generated by the user themselves left untouched.

### P7-S2: Audit-Service Retrofit

- **Scope check before planning**: the P7-S0 requirement ("prerequisite is a retrofit of `audit-service` — first-class actor/user field … plus a filter API") turned out to already be a full session before Reporting Service/Forensic Trace could even begin themselves. A `grep` across all services found **71 `publish_event(...)` call sites in 13 services**, all of which would need to populate a first-class actor field. Decision (own, plan-justified design decision, no `AskUserQuestion` needed — analogous to P7-S1→S1/S1b): P7-S2 covers **exclusively** the retrofit, Reporting Service (5.4a) and Forensic Trace (5.4b) were inserted as new **P7-S2b**/**P7-S2c**.
- **Actor field on the shared `Event` envelope** (`libs/dms-eventbus-client/src/dms_eventbus_client/envelope.py`), not just on `AuditEvent` — the envelope is the only place known to *every* producer; a field only on `AuditEvent` would have merely shifted the criticized ad-hoc convention by one more conversion step. `actor: str | None = None`, backward-compatible.
- **Convention for the actor value per call site**: human username where a local variable already provides it (`created_by`, `deleted_by`, `initiated_by`, `approved_by`, `set_by`, `released_by`, ...); `"system:<component>"` for background-/system-triggered events (poll loops, automatic pipelines like OCR/rendering/virus scan) — reuses the `"system:retention-poll"` convention that already existed before this session in `document-service`/`folder-service`. **New sub-pattern**: a consumer that itself publishes something again in reaction to a foreign event (`case-service` on `workflow.instance.completed`, `auth-service` on `permission.approval.approved`) passes through `event.actor` of the triggering event instead of inventing a new value — the same causal action, the same acting person. For a few call sites with no available action identity at all (e.g. `document.metadata.updated`, `folder.resource.moved`/`.deleted`, `document.restored`, `document.retention.updated`), `actor` deliberately remained `None` — this session only made already-existing information first-class, no new fields were added to foreign schemas (would be its own, much bigger cut).
- **Cutover versioning, so as not to retroactively break the existing hash chain**: new singleton row `AuditMeta.actor_field_cutover_id` (same "one row, `id=1`" pattern as `KennzeichenConfig`/`RetentionConfig`), set once to `MAX(id)` of the already-existing rows on first start after the migration. `_hashable_fields()` includes `actor` in the canonical hash JSON only for `id > cutover_id` — both when appending new rows and when recomputing in `verify_chain`. Without this mechanism, merely adding a new field to the canonical JSON would have retroactively broken **every** historical chain check, even with `actor: None` (the canonical JSON already differs due to the extra key). Live smoke test confirmed: `GET /events/verify` remained `ok: true` with an identical checked row count before/after the migration.
- **Filter API additive on the existing `GET /events`**: `actor`/`subject` exact match, `event_type` exact or NATS wildcard convention (`"document.>"` → SQL `LIKE`, same notation as `Settings.subjects`), `since`/`until` on `occurred_at`. No new endpoint, no existing caller affected (no frontend consumes `GET /events` so far).
- **No new test per call site** (deliberate trade-off) — 71 dedicated tests would have been disproportionate; instead targeted new actor verification tests in the three services with the most call sites (`document-service`, `folder-service`, `permission-service`, one test each that monkeypatches `app.state.event_bus`/`app.state.publisher` and decodes the published `Event.actor`), plus the actual new `audit-service` tests for cutover behavior/filters. Existing tests that mock `publish_event` (`fake_publish`, `EventRecorder`) needed an additional `actor=None` default argument everywhere, to stay compatible with the new keyword parameter — purely mechanical, no behavior change.
- Tests: `audit-service` **21** (previously 12, 9 new), `document-service` **124** (previously 123, 1 new), `folder-service` **72** (previously 71, 1 new), `permission-service` **93** (previously 92, 1 new) — all remaining 9 retrofitted services unchanged in test count (only existing mocks adjusted). Full backend regression across all **19 services** green (a single, isolated, non-reproducible `permission-service` test failure on the first `--build` run — already-documented NATS cross-test trap, see "Tooling & Testing" below — green on the repeat run).
- **Live smoke test**: `GET /events/verify` state noted before the migration, checked again after deployment — `ok: true`, identical row count for the legacy history. A real action (create a folder) triggered and found via `GET /events?actor=<username>`; `GET /events?event_type=folder.>&since=...` returned the expected hits; system-triggered events showed `actor="system:<component>"` as expected (e.g. `system:ocr-service`, `system:federation-hub` from running background activity).
- **A real, previously undiscovered pre-existing bug found during the smoke test**: a newly created folder did not show up anywhere in the audit trail despite successful creation (`GET /events?subject=<folder_id>` empty). Cause: `"folder.>"` was completely missing from `audit_service.settings.Settings.subjects` — `folder-service` has had its own event stream since **P7-S1b**, but was never added to this list (unlike every other new producer stream since then, all of which followed the established "immediate extension pattern"). Fix: `"folder.>"` added, `audit-service` rebuilt/redeployed. **Positive side effect**: JetStream's default durable-consumer behavior (`deliver_policy` delivers the entire existing stream history on a new subject) thereby automatically backfilled **the entire prior folder event history** retroactively (old entries correctly with `actor: null`, since published before the P7-S2 retrofit) — with no restart of another service, no manual backfill script. `GET /events/verify` remained `ok: true` throughout. No data loss, a pure gap closure.

### P7-S2b: Reporting Service

- **4 of 5 concept example reports built, license utilization deliberately excluded**: 5.4 lists the reports explicitly as examples ("e.g. ..."), not a definitive list — a License Service simply does not exist yet (concept 3.2b/Phase 9, `P9-S0` still outstanding), a license-utilization report would have had no real data source. Own, plan-justified design decision, no `AskUserQuestion` needed.
- **Mixed data source per report instead of a single pattern**: concept 3.1 explicitly allows both ("via events/read models **or** a dedicated reporting service with its own replicated view"). Only **document volume** got a real read model (new `document_created_event` table, fed by consuming `document.created`) — the only report where its own time-series aggregation actually creates new value. **Open workflow tasks**/**user activity**/**storage consumption per backend**, by contrast, are synchronous live queries against `workflow-service`/`audit-service`/`storage-service` — no reason to duplicate data that is already well queryable there, and no event marks a workflow task as "newly due" (only start/completion are published), a read model for it would inherently go stale.
- **First real consumer of the P7-S2 audit filter API**: the user-activity report calls `GET /events?actor=&since=&until=` — the filter API built in the predecessor session had had no actual caller until then.
- **`document.created` payload extended with `folder_id`** (`document-service`, one line in `event_payload`): without this field the new read model could not have grouped by folder without synchronously querying the Document Service on every event — exactly the live join 3.1 wants to avoid. No existing test checks the exact payload key set, no test adjustment needed.
- **New `GET /storage/usage` endpoint** (`storage-service`, `GROUP BY backend` over the existing `object_metadata` table) instead of its own read model just for a single metric that the Storage Service already has on hand anyway.
- **Schedulable dispatch without an attachment**: `notification-service`'s `NotificationCreate` has no attachment field, retrofitting it would have been its own, unplanned cut into a foreign service. Instead a new `_report_schedule_poll_loop` (exact poll idiom like `document-service`'s `_retention_poll_loop`/`workflow-service`'s `_sla_poll_loop`) generates the due report, uploads it to `storage-service` under `reports/{schedule_id}/{run_id}.{format}` (`ReportRun` row), and has `notification-service` send a plain-text email with a download link pointing to a new `GET /report-runs/{id}/download` proxy. Ad-hoc exports are deliberately **not** persisted — only scheduled/dispatched runs need a storage location an email link can point to.
- **`gateway_base_url` deliberately separated from `self_address`**: `self_address` (registry self-registration) is an internal Docker DNS name, unreachable from a real email client outside the Docker network — discovered during implementation, before it could have led to a broken download link in a real email.
- **NATS JetStream backfill confirmed again** (same phenomenon as `folder.>` in P7-S2): the `reporting-service`'s new `document.>` subscription retroactively delivered the entire historical `document.created` event history on first start — the read model was thereby immediately filled with real historical data (confirmed: `GET /reports/document-volume` showed entries from 2026-07-26 onward).
- **Async test trap in the poll-loop test**: `TestClient(app)` runs the ASGI lifespan (and thereby the DB engine in `app.state.session_factory`) on its own event loop — a direct `await` on this engine from a pytest-asyncio test coroutine (a *different* loop) fails with `RuntimeError: ... attached to a different loop`. Fixed by a dedicated `poll_env` fixture that builds its own `session_factory` from the `engine` fixture (already bound to the test's own event loop) and manually sets `app.state.storage_client`/`app.state.notification_client` as `AsyncMock()` — bypasses `TestClient`/lifespan entirely for the two poll-tick tests, since `_run_due_schedules` doesn't need it.
- **`CONSUMER_SERVICES` gap in `scripts/run-tests.sh` found**: the new `reporting-service`, like every service with its own NATS consumer, has a `durable="reporting-service"` name — without adding it to the test script's `CONSUMER_SERVICES` list, `pytest`'s `TestClient(app)` lifespan collided with the parallel-running Docker container over the same consumer (`nats.js.errors.Error: consumer is already bound to a subscription`, 11 API test failures on the first full `--build` regression). Fix: `reporting-service` added to the list (same pattern as `document-service`/`audit-service`/... before) — green on the repeat run.
- Tests: `reporting-service` **37 tests** (new), `document-service` **124** (unchanged, no test checks the exact `document.created` payload key set), `storage-service` **101** (previously 100, 1 new aggregation test — delta-based rather than absolute, since `test_api.py`'s `client` fixture is not cleaned up between tests and previous tests had already uploaded objects). Admin-UI **96 tests** (previously 87, 9 new in `reports-view.test.tsx`), `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` clean. Full backend regression across all **20 services** green (after the `CONSUMER_SERVICES` fix).
- **Real, previously undiscovered pre-existing bug found in the P7-S2 filter API**: `audit_service.repository.list_events` sorted by `AuditEvent.id` **ascending** before the `LIMIT` — for a non-specific query without a tight time/action filter (exactly the case the new user-activity report triggers when first opened with no filter), this returned the **oldest** 5000 rows instead of the most recent ones out of over 18,000 existing events — a just-performed real action (`document.created` as `users-admin`) simply did not show up in the report, even though `GET /events?actor=users-admin` found it correctly. Discovered only during the live smoke test (P7-S2 itself had no real caller yet for such a broad, unfiltered query). Fix: `order_by(AuditEvent.id.desc())` — no existing test pins down a particular ordering, `audit-service` **21/21 tests** still green, `GET /events/verify` still `ok: true` after rebuild/redeploy (15935 rows checked, no chain broken).
- **`notification-service`'s P6-S6 recipient validation triggered for the first time by another service**: the first planning poll-tick test run with a made-up test address (`smoketest@example.com`) failed with `400` — correct, already-existing behavior since P6-S6 (`recipient` must be a real `auth-service` account for `channel="email"`, see `docs/services/notification-service.md`), not a new bug. With a real account email (`users-admin@system.local`) the complete cycle ran through: report generated, uploaded to `storage-service`, `ReportRun` created, email delivered via Mailpit, `next_run_at` correctly advanced by one day. For the Admin-UI this means: `recipient_email` in the scheduling form must be a real user's email address, not an arbitrary external address — not validated/communicated anywhere, see "Open Points" in `docs/services/reporting-service.md`.
- **`infra/docker-compose.yml`**: `DMS_REPORT_POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS: ${REPORTING_SERVICE_POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS:-3600}` added (same env-override pattern as `DOCUMENT_SERVICE_RETENTION_POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS`/`WORKFLOW_SLA_POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS`/`FOLDER_SERVICE_RETENTION_POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS`) — originally needed for the live smoke test with a short interval, kept as a regular configuration option.
- **Live Docker verification**: container built+started, `/healthz`, `/reports/document-volume` (real, backfilled historical data including correct `folder_id`), `/reports/storage-usage` (real backend sizes), `/reports/open-workflow-tasks` (correctly empty with no open instances), CSV and PDF export of one report each (`text/csv`/`application/pdf`, PDF starts with `%PDF`) checked against the real running stack. **Full scheduling cycle** verified with the poll interval lowered to 15s: schedule created (`next_run_at` immediately due) → poll tick generated the report, uploaded it, sent the email via Mailpit (`Subject: DMS-Bericht: storage_usage`, correct download link) → download link successfully retrieved through the gateway proxy (real CSV content) → `next_run_at` advanced by one day. Test data (document, schedule) subsequently fully removed, poll interval reset to the default.
- **`graphify . --update` successfully run afterward**: 36 changed/new files detected (28 code, 8 documents), 1 reported deletion (`CLAUDE.md`) — again the same known gitignore false alarm as in P5b-S4 (file exists unchanged, but is on the git exclusion list), deliberately not pruned. AST extraction produced 525 nodes/1108 edges, a subagent semantically extracted the 8 changed documents (32 nodes/49 edges/3 hyperedges, including own nodes for the reporting-service architecture decisions and the two edge cases found at P7-S2b). Graph afterward **6994 nodes/12207 edges in 664 communities** (previously ca. 2357/3959, state as of P5b-S4 plus several since-then not individually logged interim updates) — no shrink-guard trigger. **In the process, an already longer-existing data-quality problem not caused by this session was discovered**: `graph.json` contains, for the same `source_file`, sometimes with a `dms/` prefix, sometimes without (depending on whether `graphify` was called in an earlier session from the parent directory or directly from within `dms/`) — this led to a node-ID collision for `docs/services/audit-service.md`, where per the warning message the newly extracted node was discarded in favor of a stale duplicate. Not fixed (would require normalizing all historical `source_file` paths in the existing graph, outside the scope of this session) — **open point for a future session**, ideally as part of the next full rebuild. 14 of the 664 communities given content-appropriate readable names (the rest, mostly already unnamed "Community N" placeholders before this session, remained unchanged, matching the already-existing practice of not renaming every community on every `--update`). Health check: no dangling/missing/collapsed edges, a single self-loop (uncritical). `graph.html`/`GRAPH_REPORT.md`/`manifest.json`/`cost.json` updated (cumulative 1,671,933 input tokens over 12 runs).

### P7-S2c: Forensic Trace

- **Second function of the existing `reporting-service` instead of a third new service**: concept 5.4 describes standard reports (a) and forensic trace (b) explicitly as two use cases of the *same* Reporting Service — the `AuditClient` already built in P7-S2b and the CSV/PDF export functions were reused directly.
- **Real research gap found before planning**: `document-service` had until then published **no** event for read access (neither `GET /documents/{id}` nor either of the two content downloads) — but the concept explicitly names "viewed, changed, downloaded, or deleted documents" as the core of the trace for the central use case (compromised account, exfiltration). Clarified via `AskUserQuestion`, since this is a real volume/value trade-off (not resolvable by an "e.g." concept wording as is otherwise usual in this project): the user's answer demanded **both** actions (view *and* download) **and** a configurable logging depth instead of a simple yes/no — "base audit trail, overridable by roles, more or less. Default: everything."
- **New configuration model "base + role override"** in `document-service`: `AuditTraceConfig` (singleton, `log_viewed`/`log_downloaded`, default both `true`, same single-row pattern as `UploadConfig`) + `AuditTraceRoleOverride` (PK `role`, free text with no existence check — same existing gap as for every other role name in the system, nullable fields = "base applies"). **Conflict rule for multiple assigned roles with contradicting overrides**: "log" wins — a role that demands more logging cannot be silently undermined by another role of the same user (own, plan-justified security-first decision, since the user's own requirement specified no tie-break rule). Roles come from the `X-DMS-Roles` header already injected by the gateway (same pattern as `_has_kennzeichen_admin_role`), the actor from `X-DMS-Username`. Held locally in `document-service` rather than centrally — avoids a synchronous cross-service config fetch on the hot read path (every document click/download).
- **`document.viewed` deliberately only on single fetch, not on listing**: `GET /documents?folder_id=` potentially returns dozens of documents at once — an event per listed document would have triggered a flood on every folder opening, saying nothing about actually individually viewed documents. Only `GET /documents/{id}` (explicit single fetch) counts as a "view".
- **Categorization with no new taxonomy to maintain**: `reporting_service.forensic.categorize_event_type()` derives `view`/`download`/`change`/`delete` purely from the `event_type` suffix (`.viewed`/`.downloaded`/`.deleted`|`.force_deleted`|`.trash_purged`, remainder category `change`) — no new database field, `audit-service` needed no code change for the two new event types (fall under the already-subscribed `document.>` wildcard).
- **Self-audit via the regular event mechanism**: `reporting-service` got its own producer bus (stream `reporting`, identical dual-bus pattern as `document-service`) and publishes `reporting.forensic_trace.queried` unconditionally on every `GET /forensic-trace` query (including export) with the filters used as payload — `audit-service` had already subscribed to `reporting.>` since P7-S2b (added proactively back then, with no effect), now the first actual producer. `queried_by` is a mandatory, not optional, parameter — without an indication of the caller, the self-audit mechanism would make no sense.
- **Anomaly hints kept minimal**: exactly one rule type ("more than N downloads by the same actor within M minutes", sliding-window/two-pointer over the hits already contained in the trace result, no additional query) instead of a generic rule engine — the concept explicitly names this as optional/"initially exclusively via configurable static thresholds", AI/ML anomaly detection deliberately out of scope.
- **Trace object type "role" deliberately not implemented**: `permission-service` currently publishes no events for role/role-assignment CRUD (only approval/scope-lock/maintenance-mode, all with `subject=None`) — role tracking would require its own retrofit there. Concept text names role only as an example ("e.g. a role"), not a definitive list — same omission pattern as the license utilization in P7-S2b.
- **Two new Admin-UI pages under the existing "security" group** (previously only Superuser Break-Glass): `/forensic-trace/` (`ForensicTraceView` — filters, results table, anomaly banner, export) and `/audit-trace-settings/` (`AuditTraceSettings` — base checkboxes + role-override table with tri-state selects). **Discovered during testing**: the tri-state selects in the override creation form initially carried the same `aria-label` text as the base configuration checkboxes ("log read accesses (metadata view)") — led to `findByLabelText` in the test hitting the wrong checkbox/select and a checkbox-toggle test failing (assertion got `(true, true)` instead of `(false, true)`). Fix: own, distinguishable `aria-label` texts for the override selects (`overrideLogViewedLabel`/`overrideLogDownloadedLabel`) — this simultaneously fixes a real accessibility problem (identical accessible names in two places on the same page are also confusing for screen-reader users), not just a test artifact.
- Tests: `document-service` **141** (previously 124, 17 new: config CRUD, `resolve_should_log` including the multi-role conflict case, event-publish behavior of the three read endpoints depending on config/role), `reporting-service` **54** (previously 37, 17 new: `forensic.py` categorization/anomaly detection, forensic-trace API including self-audit event content), `audit-service` **21** (unchanged, only `settings.subjects` extended with `reporting.>`). Admin-UI **107 tests** (previously 96, 11 new: `forensic-trace-view.test.tsx` + `audit-trace-settings.test.tsx`), `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` clean. Full backend regression across all **20 services** green.
- **Live Docker verification**: document created, viewed, and downloaded — `document.viewed`/`document.downloaded` correctly in the audit trail with `actor="users-admin"` (from `X-DMS-Username`). **Side finding**: the internal rendering-pipeline access to the same document content (immediately after creation, `rendering-service` automatically generates a preview) also generated a `document.viewed`/`document.downloaded` pair with `actor: null` — confirms that the new instrumentation is complete as intended, including internal service-to-service accesses (which naturally carry no `X-DMS-Username` header). `GET /forensic-trace?subject=<document_id>` and `?category=download` returned the expected, correctly categorized hits. Base configuration set to `log_viewed=false` → no new `document.viewed` (counter unchanged); two contradictory role overrides created (`quiet-role`: `log_viewed=false`, `loud-role`: `log_viewed=true`) and viewed with both roles simultaneously (`X-DMS-Roles: quiet-role,loud-role`) → event was still published (conflict rule "log wins" confirmed live). 20 rapid downloads triggered → `anomalies` in the trace response correctly reported "25 downloads within 5 minutes (threshold: 20)". Every trace query demonstrably generated a `reporting.forensic_trace.queried` self-audit event (`GET /events?event_type=reporting.>`), including the queries from the automated test run itself. CSV/PDF export actually downloaded (`%PDF` signature, CSV with full payload-JSON columns). `GET /events/verify` remained `ok: true` throughout (16908 rows checked). Test data (document, role overrides) fully removed, base configuration reset to default (both `true`).
- **`graphify . --update` successfully run afterward**: 30 changed/new files detected (24 code, 6 documents), 1 reported deletion (`CLAUDE.md`) — again the same known gitignore false alarm, deliberately not pruned. AST extraction produced 615 nodes/1415 edges, a subagent semantically extracted the 6 changed documents (36 nodes/68 edges/3 hyperedges, including own nodes for forensic trace as the reporting-service's second function, the base+role-override model, and the "log wins" conflict rule). Graph afterward **7136 nodes/12532 edges in 686 communities** (previously 6994/12207) — no shrink-guard trigger. **The same already-documented `dms/`-prefix collision for `docs/services/audit-service.md` occurred again** (see the P7-S2b entry above) — still not fixed, unchanged open point for the next full rebuild. 11 of the 686 communities given content-appropriate readable names. Health check: no dangling/missing/collapsed edges, a single self-loop (uncritical). `graph.html`/`GRAPH_REPORT.md`/`manifest.json`/`cost.json` updated (cumulative 1,818,857 input tokens over 13 runs).

### P7-S3: Records Disposal & Long-Term Archiving

- **Plan follow-up question at approval, decided in favor of the larger conversion scope**: the first plan foresaw "PDF/A where possible, otherwise archive the original format" (based on the P5-S2 note "LibreOffice headless not reliably available here").
