User rejected this: *"the conversion boundary must be overcome. It is essential that all document types — including pptx, docx, xlsx, odt, ods, txt etc. — can be converted. If a PDF/A conversion is not possible, falling back to PDF is okay"* — PDF/A preferred, plain PDF as an acceptable fallback, but no silent original-format fallback for unconverted formats. The old assumption was checked live and was outdated: LibreOffice is in fact installed on this host (`/opt/libreoffice26.2/program/soffice`, just not on `PATH`) and was verified live against a real `.txt`→PDF conversion. The revised plan was then approved.
- **`rendering-service`'s `PdfArchiveRenderer` extended universally**: a new `renderers/_libreoffice.py` helper calls `soffice --headless --convert-to pdf:writer_pdf_Export:{"SelectPdfVersion":{"type":"long","value":1}}` (PDF/A-1b export filter) via subprocess, with an isolated `-env:UserInstallation=file://<tmp-profile>` per call (avoids "another instance running" lock conflicts on parallel calls) and binary-path resolution first via `PATH`, otherwise known absolute paths. Raster images instead run directly through Pillow (`Image.save(..., format="PDF")`, faster than a LibreOffice subprocess). **Surprising test finding**: LibreOffice is astonishingly permissive when parsing corrupted input — a test that was guaranteed to expect a `ConversionError` for garbage data with a `.docx` extension failed, because LibreOffice uncomplainingly "converted" the garbage as text. Replaced with a more reliable failure-case test (missing binary path via `monkeypatch`).
- **New archive target role in `storage-service`** instead of a dedicated storage system: `BackendTargetConfig.role: "archive" | None` — `resolve_targets()` (regular upload replication) filters out archive targets, new `PUT`/`GET /objects/{key}/archive-copy`, `.../archive-copy/verify`, `DELETE /objects/{key}/live-copies` endpoints address them specifically. Reuses ~90% of the existing multi-backend/fixity infrastructure (ADR 0017).
- **A real bug found and fixed while building, not just a test-fixture problem**: `PUT /objects/{key:path}` (the general upload) was registered in the source code **before** `PUT /objects/{key:path}/archive-copy` — since `{key:path}` is a greedy converter and Starlette matches routes in registration order, the generic route caught every `.../archive-copy` call (the key became `"xxx/archive-copy"` instead of `"xxx"` + suffix route). Symptom during the test run: a test expecting `503` with no archive target configured got `201` instead (the generic upload rejects nothing); a round-trip test got `404` on download, since the upload had landed at the wrong backend/key. Fix: the generic `PUT /objects/{key:path}` route in the source code moved after all more specific suffix routes (`.../copies`, `.../archive-copy`, `.../archive-copy/verify`, `.../live-copies`) — the same ordering rule the existing `GET`/`DELETE` routes already followed, only the new `PUT` route had violated it.
- **New `archival-service`** (port 8021, schema `archival`): poll loop + status-field state machine (`pending → locked → copied → verified → released → dehydrated` + `failed`), no new BPMN process (no 7.2 blueprint, `workflow-service` has no generic "step by step" API) — same idiom as `document-service`'s `_retention_poll_loop`. Thin HTTP clients to `document-service`/`rendering-service`/`storage-service`/`object-type-service`, no NATS consumer/producer (purely poll/HTTP-based). `document.archived`/`document.dehydrated`/`document.rehydrated` are published by `document-service` itself (domain-owner principle) when `archival-service` calls its internal callback endpoints — not by `archival-service` itself.
- **`KeyStore` plugin interface** (ADR 0029): only the interface + a trivial `EnvKeyStore` (a single key from `DMS_ARCHIVE_ENCRYPTION_KEY`) are shipped, real KDBX integration remains a separate plugin outside the standard image. **Deliberately no fallback to a randomly generated key** when none is configured — that would change on every restart and permanently render already-encrypted archive copies undecryptable; `EnvKeyStore.get_key()` instead throws `KeyNotFoundError`.
- **"Dehydration" instead of physical deletion**: the `Document` row remains fully intact, only the live storage copy is removed after `dehydration_delay_days` (default 30) — legal hold blocks only this step, not the archiving itself. Retrieval (`POST /archival-transfers/{id}/retrieve`) requires `archive_retrieval_role` (default `dms-admin`) in the `X-DMS-Roles` header and resets the transfer to `released` with a newly set `released_at` — the transition period until the next dehydration deliberately starts anew instead of being immediately due again.
- **`DocumentVersionOut` extended with `storage_object_key`** (document-service) — `archival-service` needs the exact live key for dehydration/retrieval, this field was previously only internally visible. Additive, backward-compatible schema extension.
- Tests: `rendering-service` **34** (new: `_libreoffice.py` binary-path resolution including failure case, extended `PdfArchiveRenderer` format coverage, four existing tests adjusted for the extended renderer responsibility), `object-type-service` **81** (previously 76, 5 new: `default_archive_after_days`/`archive_encryption_enabled`), `document-service` **151** (previously 141, 10 new: disposal-field resolution, seven new endpoints, legal-hold blocking of dehydration), `storage-service` **110** (previously 100, 10 new: archive target role, `write_to_targets`/`delete_from_targets`, archive-copy API round trip), `archival-service` **31 new** (first test run of this new service, entirely green). Admin-UI **116 tests** (previously 107, 9 new: `archival-transfers.test.tsx` + three new `ObjectTypeEditor` tests), `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` clean. Full backend regression across all **22 services** green (new: `archival-service` picked up automatically by `scripts/run-tests.sh`'s service discovery, no manual entry needed — pure directory-based detection).
- **`docs/services/archival-service.md` newly created**, `rendering-service.md`/`object-type-service.md`/`document-service.md`/`storage-service.md`/`admin-ui.md` updated. `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`: P7-S3 ✅, new **P7-S3b** (XDOMEA for circulation folders) inserted — same splitting precedent as P7-S1→S1/S1b, P7-S2→S2/S2b/S2c.
- **Live Docker verification** (`archival-service` restarted with `DMS_ARCHIVAL_POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS=5`/`DMS_DEHYDRATION_DELAY_DAYS=0`, then reset back to the defaults 3600/30): object type with `default_archive_after_days=0` created, three documents (a real PDF, a `.docx` via `python-docx`, a `.png` via Pillow) all fully passed through `pending → locked → copied → verified → released → dehydrated` within a few seconds, all with `archive_format="pdf_a"` (the `.docx`/`.png` conversion ran via LibreOffice resp. Pillow in `rendering-service`, not via an original-format fallback). `GET .../content` afterward returned `404`, `GET /objects/{key}/archive-copy/verify` confirmed the archive copy still intact (`ok: true`, checksum identical). `document.archived`/`document.dehydrated` both correctly with `actor="system:archival-service"` in the audit trail. **Legal-hold blocking confirmed**: a fourth document got a legal hold set immediately after creation (narrowly won the race against the 5-second poll tick) — the transfer archived normally up to `released`, but remained stuck there over several further ticks (`dehydrated_at: null`), live content still retrievable via `GET .../content`; after `POST /legal-holds/{id}/release` the next tick dehydrated it immediately as expected. **Encrypted retrieval confirmed**: a fifth document archived under an object type with `archive_encryption_enabled=true` (archive key correctly with a `.pdf.enc` suffix, `encrypted: true`), `POST /archival-transfers/{id}/retrieve` without an `X-DMS-Roles` header returned `403`, with `dms-admin` `200` — content correctly decrypted and restored under the same live storage key (`GET .../content` afterward returned a valid `%PDF` again). **A behavior discovered that follows from the test configuration itself, not an application error**: with `dehydration_delay_days=0`, the due-check (`released_at <= now - 0 days`) makes every `released` transfer *immediately* due again — a retrieval under this test configuration was thereby immediately dehydrated again by the next tick (5s later), even though `retrieve` had correctly reset `released_at`. With a realistic period (e.g. 30 days, the production default), the restored content stays reachable for the full transition period as intended — re-verified with `DEHYDRATION_DELAY_DAYS=30`: the same transfer remained `released` over several further ticks after a second retrieval, content still retrievable. All five test documents and both test object types subsequently fully removed (documents soft-deleted via `DELETE /documents/{id}`, object types via `DELETE /object-types/{id}`), `archival-service` reset to the production defaults.

### P7-S3b: XDOMEA Records Disposal for Circulation Folders

- **Plan follow-up question at session start, decided in favor of the current XDOMEA version**: ADR 0029 (P7-S0) had named XDOMEA 3.0.0 as the target version. Researching the actual schema procurement (before any implementation) showed: 3.0.0 was, per the official KoSIT registration (queried via the `xrepository.de` API), set to expire in a few weeks at session time and was only fully findable via a GPL-3.0-licensed third-party mirror (GitHub, Thuringia state archive) — the actual KoSIT sources (`xdomea.de/schemata/300/*.xsd`) returned `404` (only the `targetNamespace` URI, no real download address). XDOMEA 4.0.0, by contrast, was cleanly obtainable via the official KoSIT infrastructure at this point in time (`schema.kdo.de` for the actual `xdomea-*.xsd` files, `xoev.de` directly for the three shared XÖV base modules). Presented via `AskUserQuestion` with exactly these facts — user chose **4.0.0**. ADR 0029 updated via an addendum (the core argument "XDOMEA as an open standard with no licensing question" remains valid unchanged, only the version number changes).
- **Schema procurement actually verified before implementation, not just assumed**: all 7 schema files needed for the `Aussonderung.Aussonderung.0503` message (`xdomea-Baukasten.xsd`, `xdomea-Datentypen.xsd`, `xdomea-Nachrichten-AussonderungDurchfuehren.xsd`, `xdomea-Typen-AussonderungDurchfuehren.xsd`, `xoev-code.xsd`, `xoev-basisnachricht-unqualified-g2g_1.1.xsd`, `din-norm-91379-datatypes.xsd`) were downloaded and **successfully compiled against `lxml.etree.XMLSchema`**, before starting message construction at all. Two transitive external dependencies discovered in the process that were not obvious from the original research (`xoev-code.xsd` for code-list base types, the G2G base-message module for UUID/contact types) — both likewise cleanly obtained directly from `xoev.de`, no further GPL contact.
- **Iteratively building a minimally valid message directly against the real, compiled schema** (not built speculatively from documentation): several validation errors found and corrected live along the way — `nachrichtentyp` is itself a structured `Code` type (`<code>0503</code>`), not plain text; three fields in `Identifikation.NachrichtType` are explicitly `form="unqualified"` (no `xdomea:` namespace prefix), the rest of the message otherwise qualified; `Importbestaetigung` with `fixed="1"` requires exactly the literal `"1"`, not `"true"` (XSD fixed comparison is lexical, not semantic); `FormatType` additionally requires a **mandatory** field `Primaerdokument/Dateiname` beyond `Name`/`SonstigerName`, whose value must follow a strict regex pattern (starting with a UUID) — the originally assumed free `{document_id}_{version}` naming would have violated the schema.
- **`Case` → `xdomea:Vorgang` with no `Akte` wrapper**: structurally confirmed against the real schema (`Schriftgutobjekt` is an `xs:choice` between `Akte`/`Vorgang`, a bare `Vorgang` at the top level is valid) — fits case-service's flat data model with no artificial intermediate level.
- **No per-object-type disposal field for circulation folders**: `ObjectType.applies_to` is strictly `"document"`|`"folder"` (verified in the code), Cases only use `object_type_id` loosely for attribute validation. Instead of extending the `applies_to` enum (a larger intervention in object-type-service not justified here), `case-service` got an **installation-wide** `CaseArchivalConfig` (singleton, same pattern as `RetentionConfig`), resolved in `close_case()` at closure time.
- **No `dehydrated` status for `CaseArchivalTransfer`** (unlike documents) — a circulation folder has no live content of its own, only references to documents with their own, independent P7-S3 lifecycle.
- **Only the 0503 message, no full bilateral XDOMEA flow** (0501 offer directory → 0502 assessment directory → 0504–0507 confirmations) — the full flow presupposes an actually responding second system (an archive system) that does not exist here. Same scoping principle as P7-S3's "no 7.2 BPMN blueprint to reproduce".
- **Package format**: a ZIP file (`zipfile`, Python standard library) with `aussonderung.xml` + `dokumente/{uuid}.{ext}` per referenced, active (not soft-deleted) document reference — the same filename is referenced in the XML under `Primaerdokument/Dateiname`.
- **Retrieval as a pure download, no rewrite mutation** (unlike documents): `GET /case-archival-transfers/{id}/package` instead of `POST .../retrieve` — a circulation folder has no live storage location to write back to.
- Tests: `archival-service` **50** (previously 31, 19 new: `test_xdomea.py` validates the generated message against the real vendored schema — the most valuable test of this session —, `test_case_pipeline.py` for the full phase cascade including ZIP-content check/encryption/exclusion of soft-deleted references, API extension for `/case-archival-transfers`), `case-service` **39** (previously 26, 13 new: `archive_after` resolution at closure, `CaseArchivalConfig` CRUD, `due-for-archival` filter, `request_archive` including `CaseNotClosedError`, `mark_archived`, API endpoints). Admin-UI **120 tests** (previously 116, 4 new: `CaseArchivalSection` in `archival-transfers.test.tsx`), `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` clean. Full backend regression across all **22 services** green.
- **`services/archival-service/src/archival_service/xdomea_schema/`** new — 7 vendored `.xsd` files + `README.md` with the exact source URLs (provenance record, no GPL mirror). Verified: `uv build --wheel` automatically embeds all 7 files as package data (hatchling's default behavior for non-Python files in the package directory, no manual `MANIFEST.in`/`force-include` needed).
- `docs/services/archival-service.md` (new "XDOMEA Records Disposal for Circulation Folders" section), `case-service.md` (new "records disposal" section), `admin-ui.md` updated. ADR 0029 updated via an addendum (see above). `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`: P7-S3b ✅ — **Phase 7 thereby fully completed**.
- **Live Docker verification** (`archival-service` restarted with `ARCHIVAL_SERVICE_POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS=8`, then reset back to the default 3600): `CaseArchivalConfig.default_archive_after_days_closed=0` set, a circulation folder with two real documents created and closed via a real BPMN process (manual task) — `status="closed"`, `archive_after` correctly resolved from `closed_at` (0-day delay). The `CaseArchivalTransfer` fully automatically passed through `pending → locked → packaged → verified → released` within ~2s, all four phase timestamps within the same second. `GET .../package` returned a valid ZIP with `aussonderung.xml` + two `dokumente/{uuid}.txt` files; the content of `aussonderung.xml` was manually inspected (correct `xdomeaUUID`s, correct `Betreff`, correct `Dateiname`/`DateinameOriginal` mapping, `Format/Name="100"`/"Sonstiges" as documented) and **additionally re-validated via a standalone `lxml` call outside the pytest suite against the vendored 4.0.0 schema — `assertValid` succeeded, an independent second confirmation of schema conformance**. **Encryption confirmed**: `archive_encryption_enabled=true` set, a second circulation folder with two further documents disposed of — the transfer showed `encrypted=true`, storage key correctly with a `.zip.enc` suffix; `GET .../package` without an `X-DMS-Roles` header returned `403`, with a non-matching role likewise `403`, with `dms-admin` `200` — content correctly decrypted, ZIP with the same two files readable. All four test documents subsequently soft-deleted (`DELETE /documents/{id}`). The two test circulation folders and the test process definition were deliberately left in place: `case-service` has **no** case-deletion endpoint (per 5.6, cases are permanently discoverable metadata, never to be deleted — the same design decision already justified above for the state-machine layout), and `workflow-service` refuses to delete a process definition with existing instances (`"has instances - deletion rejected"`, no instance-deletion endpoint provided) — both consistent, intended audit-trail behavior, not actual unaccounted-for test artifacts.

### Diagnostic Tools (Phase 8, P8-S0–P8-S3)
- **P8-S0 was a pure research/planning session, no implementation code** — second `PN-S0` kickoff session after P7-S0, same convention.
- **Concept 6.1 (central query & trace console)/6.2 (CLI tool) implementable unchanged as described** — no overlooked prep work in other services discovered, unlike P7-S0 (where retention/legal-hold/audit-actor-field had to be built entirely from scratch). Session breakdown P8-S1/S2/S3 therefore remains **unchanged**, no split needed like P6-S4→S4/S5/S6 or P7-S1→S1/S1b/S1c/S1d.
- **Central finding: `pglast` GPL-3.0 licensing question, structurally identical to the ADR 0029 KDBX finding**: concept 1a/6.1 names `pglast` (Python binding to the real PostgreSQL parser via `libpg_query`, including AST visitor/printer infrastructure for the DMS-specific trace/hierarchy grammar extensions) as the "decided" library. Actual checking showed: **`pglast` is GPL-3.0-or-later** (WebSearch/WebFetch against PyPI/GitHub, not taken from training data — currently v8.4 from 2026-07-22, actively maintained, PG16 here covered via the older `v6` branch). `libpg_query` itself (the underlying C library) is, by contrast, unproblematic BSD-3-Clause — the conflict arises exclusively from `pglast`'s own Python wrapper, not from the parsed PostgreSQL grammar itself.
- **Checked alternatives, both discarded**: `pgparse` (BSD-3-Clause, PyPI, but only 74 commits/2 stars — a very small project, per its own docs only parsing/normalization/fingerprinting, **no** AST manipulation/prettifier infrastructure, which 6.1 explicitly needs for the grammar extension) and `psqlparse` (BSD, but effectively abandoned — last stable release 2016, last pre-release 2019, no indication of PG16/17/18 support).
- **Decided via `AskUserQuestion` at session start**: the same plugin architecture as KDBX in ADR 0029 — query-service (P8-S1) defines a lean parser-extension interface, `pglast` itself is not bundled into the Docker image by default, but documented as an optional plugin the operator installs themselves. This retains the full `pglast` functionality actually "decided" in the concept (instead of a functionally weaker alternative or a discarded in-house reimplementation, which 6.1 itself excludes: "deliberately no own parser written from scratch"). See [ADR 0031](docs/adr/0031-query-konsole-pglast-plugin.md).
- **Open for P8-S1 session start**: the sensible functional scope of the Query & Trace Service **without** an installed `pglast` plugin (e.g. pure structured read-model queries with no full psql dialect vs. no console function at all until the plugin is installed) — deliberately not yet settled here, no technical obstacle.
- `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`: new phase preamble Phase 8, P8-S0 ✅, P8-S1 line extended with the plugin note. `docs/adr/0031-query-konsole-pglast-plugin.md` newly created.
- **P8-S1 (Query & Trace Service, read access)**: new, fully stateless `services/query-service/` (port 8022, no own Postgres schema — same pattern as `gateway-service`, logging exclusively via the event bus instead of a local copy).
- **Research before implementation uncovered a real security gap, not just an assumption**: `audit-service`'s `GET /events` is completely unauthenticated/unfiltered (no `X-DMS-*` header check), and `reporting-service`'s existing forensic-trace endpoint (`GET /forensic-trace`, P7-S2c) has the exact same gap — `queried_by` is a plain free-text parameter, no real permission check of the result rows, even though the page already looks user-facing. Concept 6.1, however, literally demands: "a query can never see ... more than the executing person would be entitled to anyway". `query-service` closes this gap for its own read access — a retrofit of `reporting-service` itself was deliberately **not** part of this session (too large a side quest), documented as an open point.
- **`ParserPlugin` interface exactly following the ADR-0029 KDBX pattern**: `query_service/parser.py` only defines the interface (`ParserPlugin.parse`, `load_parser_plugin` via `importlib`) — **no `pglast` code in the repo**, not even as an "optional" directory. Tested via a test-only fake plugin (`tests/fixtures/fake_parser_plugin.py`, a trivial `SELECT * FROM events WHERE k='v'` pattern via `sys.path` injection in `conftest.py`, since `--import-mode=importlib` knows no `tests/__init__.py` packages) — clearly marked as a test double, no real SQL grammar.
- **Two-track query language instead of all-or-nothing**: `GET /query/events` (structured filter parameters) delivers the full core benefit immediately with no plugin; `POST /query` (free text) returns `501` without a configured `DMS_QUERY_PARSER_PLUGIN_MODULE`. Unlike KDBX (ADR 0029, a niche export format), a completely functionless console here would be disproportionate until plugin installation — the console is a visible core feature.
- **Real RBAC/scope-lock filtering of result rows** (`filtering.py`): a folder resource is resolved per event — `document-service` events carry the `document_id` as `subject`, resolved via `GET /documents/{id}` → `folder_id`; `folder-service` events already carry the `resource_id` directly. All other categories (workflow/case/auth/signature/notification/registry/...) are **not resolvable** and are excluded fail-closed, except for the activated superuser (4.6) — a deliberate, documented scope boundary rather than a nonexistent generic object permission for every conceivable domain. Resolutions deduplicated + parallelized (`asyncio.gather`), `permission-service`'s `POST /check/batch` already combines RBAC and scope locks (4.7) in a single response (1:1 reuse from `search-service`).
- **`admin.query_console` role already existed, but unused** — `permission-service`'s `DOMAIN_ADMIN_ROLES` catalog had the entry `domain-admin-query-console` since the original role seeding, without any service ever having actually checked it. `query-service` is the first real consumer (`_require_query_console`, 1:1 gate pattern from `workflow-service._require_object_config`).
- **Superuser exception with no header shortcut**: `AuthServiceClient.get_active_superuser()` (1:1 pattern from `permission-service`) — only whoever is themselves the currently active superuser principal gets the special rights, not every caller while some activation is running (specifically tested).
- **`audit-service`'s subjects list extended with `"query.>"`** (proactively, not only after a live-test finding like the `"folder.>"` pre-existing bug in P7-S2) — without this addition `query.executed` would never have reached the audit trail.
- **A real, small bug found and fixed during testing**: `filter_events_by_permission` originally called `permission_client.check_batch(...)` even when not a single event had a folder resource (empty `resource_ids`) — an unnecessary network round trip. A test uncovered this (the real `check_batch` implementation already has this short-circuit itself, but the caller should not need to hide behind it); fixed via an explicit `if not unique_resource_ids: return []` guard directly in `filtering.py`.
- **Admin-UI**: new, standalone `QueryConsoleView` (`/query-console/`) — filter form + results table + transparent "N of M events visible" hint (makes the server-side RBAC filtering visible to users instead of rows silently disappearing) resp. its own superuser hint. **No** free-text SQL input field in this session — the `POST /query` path remains unused anyway without an installed plugin, a UI for it would be wasted effort. New sidebar group "diagnostic tools", visible only with the `admin.query_console` capability (identical gating pattern to existing entries).
- Tests: `query-service` **24 new** (first test run of this new service, entirely green: `test_parser.py`, `test_filtering.py`, `test_api.py`), `audit-service` still **21** (only the subjects list changed, no new tests needed). Admin-UI **127 tests** (previously 120, 7 new: `query-console-view.test.tsx` + two `admin-sidebar.test.tsx` extensions), `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` clean. Full backend regression across all **22 services** green (`query-service` newly auto-detected by `scripts/run-tests.sh`, pure directory-based detection like every new service).
- **`docs/services/query-service.md` newly created**, `admin-ui.md`/`audit-service.md` updated. **`docs/architecture.md` deliberately NOT updated** — the diagram has been frozen since P6-S9 (Phase 7 is entirely missing: `archival-service`/`reporting-service`/`favorite-service` are also nowhere in the diagram), adding only `query-service` would have created an inconsistent, arbitrary-looking picture instead of a real update — documented as an open point, not a silent omission.
- **Live Docker verification** (a real stack freshly rebuilt via `scripts/run-tests.sh --build`, `query-service`/`audit-service` already running with this session's changes): `domain-admin-query-console` role assigned to a test principal, test document created and viewed/downloaded (generates real `document.viewed`/`document.downloaded` events, P7-S2c). `GET /query/events?subject=<doc_id>` **without** an `X-DMS-Principal` header → `403`; with a principal that lacks the console role → `403`; with the role but **without** `document.read` on the resolved folder resource (`folder_id=null` → `"root"`) → `200` with `total_before_filter=7`/`total_after_filter=0` (all rows correctly filtered, not merely empty for lack of data). After granting a `document.read` test role on `root` → `total_after_filter=7` of `8` (the eighth, non-resolvable `rendering.completed` event stays hidden as intended). As an already-active superuser (from a running break-glass session of this same session) → `superuser: true`, all 9 events visible, **including** the otherwise-hidden `rendering.completed` — role gate fully bypassed. `POST /query` without the role → `403`; with the role but with no plugin configured → `501` with the expected ADR-0031 reference text. After the `"query.>"` addition confirmed: `GET /events?event_type=query.>` on `audit-service` shows real `query.executed` entries with correct `actor`/`payload` (including entries already generated during the pytest suite against the real NATS — made visible retroactively by the JetStream backfill mechanism, no data loss). Test data subsequently removed: document soft-deleted, both role assignments deleted (`DELETE /role-assignments/{id}`, `204`); the specially created test role itself (`query-smoketest-reader`) could **not** be deleted — `permission-service` offers no `DELETE` endpoint for `Role` (only `GET`/`POST /roles`), roles are apparently permanent/non-deletable in this system, the same design line as with cases/process definitions with instances (P7-S3b) — the orphaned, now assignment-free role remains as an inert artifact, no technical omission of this session.

- **P8-S2 (Manipulation mode + safeguard levels, backend)**: delivers the write side of the console (concept 6.1, points 1–5). **Central scope finding before planning, confirmed via `AskUserQuestion`**: concept 6.1's own example ("reset attribute Y on all documents of type Z with condition B") presupposes a filter-based bulk write — no owner service (document-/object-type-/folder-/case-service) has even a single endpoint that accepts a server-evaluated filter condition instead of an explicit ID (`grep` across all four services confirms: only explicit ID lists). A real generic SQL manipulation system would be its own, multi-session, distributed write-execution-engine project. **User chose the recommended option**: a small, code-defined catalog of structured, curated manipulation actions (analogous to the `action_type` pattern of the four-eyes infrastructure, ADR 0022) instead of free SQL text — each targets a single object by ID via an already-existing owner-service endpoint.
- **Three concrete actions**: `document.attribute_reset` (non-critical, `PATCH /documents/{id}`, closest in content to the concept example, but honestly limited to one document), `permission.role_assignment.delete` (critical, `DELETE /role-assignments/{id}`, covers concept 6.1's example category "permission/role tables" literally), `object_type.update` (critical, `PUT /object-types/{id}`, only the fields `naming_constraints`/`conditions` — "object-type/constraint definitions"). "License data" (the third concept category) does not exist — no License Service before Phase 9, no third example of this category buildable.
- **Critical marking hardcoded in Python, not configurable** — literal implementation of point 4 ("cannot be circumvented via a divergent general configuration") — safest when there is no configuration knob for it at all.
- **Protection switch as its own, lighter mechanism instead of reusing the superuser break-glass itself** — concept 6.1 calls it only "comparable" to 4.6. `query-service` got its **first own Postgres schema** (`query`, table `manipulation_mode_status`) — genuinely own state, no duplication of foreign read models, does not reverse the P8-S1 decision "no own data storage" (which was only aimed against duplicating foreign data). Lazy expiry check with no poll loop (an expired protection switch only blocks the next write attempt, nothing to clean up — unlike break-glass). New, fine-grained permission `admin.query_console.manipulate`, separate from the read permission — concept 6.1 explicitly names "no manipulation" as a separately assignable restriction. The activated superuser fully bypasses the protection switch, does not need to activate it separately.
- **Dry run deliberately stateless** — an HMAC-signed, short-lived token (`dry_run_tokens.py`, new `DMS_DRY_RUN_SECRET`) itself transports `action_type`/`params`/`principal_id`/expiry time; `/manipulate/execute` only checks the signature, no second table needed. Mandatory for **all** callers, including the superuser (the concept names no exception here).
- **Four-eyes fully via the existing ADR-0022 infrastructure, no parallel structure**: for non-critical actions, `execute` queries `GET /approval-config/{action_type}` (an installation configures it via the already-existing `PUT /approval-config/{action_type}`, no permission-service code change needed except the new role-catalog entry); critical actions always force an approval request, **even for the activated superuser** — the only place in the entire console where the superuser is not allowed to act "unrestricted" (concept point 4 literally), specifically tested (`test_critical_action_always_requires_approval_even_for_superuser`, the most important security test of this session). Execution after approval via a **new NATS consumer** in `query-service` (a producer only until P8-S2) on `permission.approval.approved`, identical pattern to `document-service`/`auth-service` (payload read defensively, `.get()`).
- **New role `domain-admin-query-console-manipulate`** in `permission-service`'s `DOMAIN_ADMIN_ROLES` catalog (now 9 instead of 8 rows) — no own technical Keycloak account needed, `query-service` checks the role assignment directly, as already for `domain-admin-query-console` (P8-S1). In the process, the documentation line in `docs/services/permission-service.md`, which had silently become stale since P8-S1, was also corrected (it had incorrectly claimed that only `domain-admin-users`/`domain-admin-config` had real enforcement).
- **A real bug found and fixed in `scripts/run-tests.sh`**: the `CONSUMER_SERVICES` list (services with their own NATS consumer, which must be stopped as containers before their own test run) did not yet know about `query-service` — this session's new consumer collided with the just-freshly-started live container on the first `--build` regression run (`nats: JetStream.Error consumer is already bound to a subscription`, 21 errors). Fixed by adding `query-service` to the list — exactly the same error class as the already-known live-container collision, but here in the test-runner script itself instead of just a one-off manual test session.
- **Admin-UI integration deliberately not part of this session** — own scope decision made during planning (no `AskUserQuestion` needed, analogous to P7-S2→S2b/S2c): protection-switch toggle, dry-run preview, confirm flow, approval display are their own, substantial frontend cut on top of an already-large backend topic. New, directly inserted session **P8-S2b**.
- Tests: `query-service` **54** (previously 24, 30 new: `test_dry_run_tokens.py`, `test_manipulation_mode.py`, `test_manipulation.py`, `test_consumer.py`, `test_api.py` extended by 9 manipulation-endpoint tests), `permission-service` still **93** (the new role-catalog entry is already covered by the existing generic seeding tests, based on `len(DOMAIN_ADMIN_ROLES)`, no new tests needed). `ruff check`/`ruff format` clean for both services. Full backend regression across all **22 services** green (after the `run-tests.sh` correction above).
- **`docs/services/query-service.md`** extended with architecture decisions, new API lines, a new "manipulation mode" section with an action table, data model, events, updated tests/open-points sections. **`docs/services/permission-service.md`**: new catalog line + correction of the stale enforcement claim, new consumer note.
- **Live Docker verification** (a real stack freshly rebuilt via `scripts/run-tests.sh`): `domain-admin-query-console-manipulate` role assigned to a test principal, test document with two attributes created. `POST /manipulate/dry-run` **before** protection-switch activation → `403`. After activation: dry run for `document.attribute_reset` returns the correct preview ("would reset attribute 'notiz' ... from 'wichtig' to null") + token; `POST /manipulate/execute` with no four-eyes configured → immediate execution, `GET /documents/{id}` confirms the attribute actually removed. After `PUT /approval-config/document.attribute_reset {requires_approval:true}`: the same flow now returns `pending_approval`; a second person approves (`POST /approval-requests/{id}/approve`) → `query-service`'s new consumer executes the action within 2s, a second document attribute actually removed, `query.manipulation.executed` with correct `actor`/`payload` in the audit trail (`GET /events?event_type=query.manipulation.>`). **Critical action tested with an active superuser** (`permission.role_assignment.delete` on a specially created test role assignment): dry run as superuser worked without the protection switch active (bypass confirmed), **but** `execute` still returned `pending_approval` instead of immediate execution — the role assignment remained actually unchanged until approval (`GET /role-assignments` confirms), disappearing only after approval by a second person. **The central security proof of this session actually confirmed against the live stack, not just simulated in tests.** Protection switch deactivated → `POST /manipulate/dry-run` for the same non-critical action as a non-superuser → `403`. Test data subsequently removed: document soft-deleted, both role assignments deleted, `approval-config` reset to the default (`requires_approval: false`); the role `domain-admin-query-console-manipulate` itself remains (no `DELETE /roles` endpoint, see above), likewise both approval-request records (permanent audit record, not a technical omission).

- **P8-S2b (Admin-UI integration of manipulation mode)**: delivers the frontend side split off during P8-S2 planning. **Research before planning instead of assumption**: `apps/admin-ui/src/lib/api.ts` already had a fully generic four-eyes client function `approveApprovalRequest(token, requestId, approvedBy)` from the P6-S5 break-glass integration — no superuser-specific code in it, directly reusable unchanged. `GET /approval-requests` on `permission-service` only filters `action_type` as a single string, not as a list — for the three query-service action types, `?status=pending` is queried once and filtered client-side (saves two unnecessary extra calls compared to the three-separate-calls pattern of the break-glass page). `ObjectTypeEditor.tsx` has **no** existing rich-editor component for `naming_constraints`/`conditions` (it only preserves them on edit, no UI for them) — no pattern to reuse, a raw JSON text field for the `value` parameter of `object_type.update` is the pragmatic, honest choice for this low-level diagnostic tool.
- **No new page slug**: new "Manipulation" section within the existing `QueryConsoleView`/`/query-console/` page, `QueryConsoleView` itself was split into two subcomponents for this (`QueryEventsSection`/`ManipulationSection`, same splitting pattern as `ArchivalTransfersView`'s `DocumentArchivalSection`/`CaseArchivalSection` from P7-S3b).
- **Two-step UI enforces the same dry-run discipline the backend enforces anyway**: "simulate" shows preview text + critical badge and keeps the `dry_run_token` in component state; "execute" only appears afterward. Every form change (action-type or parameter change) discards the stored token via a shared `resetDryRun()` call in every `onChange` handler — prevents an execution based on stale parameters no longer matching the current form values.
- **Fine-grained double gating**: the manipulation section additionally checks the new `admin.query_console.manipulate` capability locally in the component, on top of the already-existing sidebar gating (`admin.query_console`, P8-S1) — pure defense-in-depth/UX (no section for users without the role), actual enforcement remains fully server-side in `query-service` (`403` without the role/without an active protection switch).
- **Dynamic parameter form without generic rendering**: deliberately three directly written-out form branches instead of an abstraction over a generic field schema — for only three fixed, known actions, a form engine would be premature complexity.
- **A real test stumbling block found and fixed**: `userEvent.type()` interprets `{`/`}` as special syntax for keyboard modifiers (e.g. `{shift}`) — a test that wanted to type invalid JSON like `"{not valid json"` failed with a parse error of the test library itself, not the actual code. Fixed via the documented escape convention (`{{` for a literal `{`).
- Tests: Admin-UI **135 tests** (previously 127, 8 new in `query-console-view.test.tsx`: visibility behind the manipulate capability, activate/deactivate protection switch, dry-run→execute for a non-critical action, critical badge + pending-approval result for a critical action, JSON parse error, render approval table + approve, empty state), `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` clean.
- `docs/services/admin-ui.md` (query-console section extended with the manipulation part, new API line, test counts), `docs/services/query-service.md` ("open points" cleared of the now-resolved Admin-UI point, new point "no reject button").
- **Live verification, honestly limited and documented**: no browser automation tool is available in this environment — an actual interactive click-through of `QueryConsoleView` in the browser was technically not possible (checked via `ToolSearch`, no Playwright/Puppeteer-like tool found). Instead: **(1)** gateway routing for the new paths actually confirmed against the running stack — `GET http://localhost:8009/api/query-service/manipulation-mode/status` and `.../api/permission-service/approval-requests?status=pending` correctly return `401` without a token (not `404`), i.e. `gateway-service`'s registry-based routing already correctly resolves both service types, no gateway code change needed (confirms the pattern known from P8-S1). **(2)** every endpoint called by the UI was already confirmed directly against the real stack in P8-S2's live verification (protection switch, dry run, execute, four-eyes, approval) — the UI calls exactly the same endpoints with the same parameters, only via the gateway path instead of directly. **(3)** the 13 `query-console-view.test.tsx` tests render the real component (React Testing Library, no shallow rendering) and simulate real user interaction (`userEvent.click`/`.type`/`.selectOptions`) for the complete protection-switch, dry-run→execute, and approval flow. This combination does not replace a real browser test, but covers both the routing and interaction layers — documented as a deliberate limitation, not a silently skipped verification.

- **P8-S3 (CLI tool, 6.2)**: closes Phase 8. **Research before planning**: concept 6.2 lists a broad functional scope (configuration import/export 7.3, query & trace console 6.1, migration/transfer operations 7.2, object types/constraints/workflows, registry/plugin-orchestration status 3.2/3.8, license status 9.3, user/role management, backup/restore 10.4, delta/comparison runs 7.5) — comparison against `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` showed that 7.3/9.3/3.8/10.4/7.5 belong to later phases (9/10/11/12/14) and simply have no backend today. Instead of building dummies or silently shrinking the scope: honestly limited to real endpoints, the rest documented in tabular form with a phase reference in `docs/tools/cli.md` — same pattern as P7-S3's handling of the then-missing 7.2 blueprint. For 7.2 (migration/transfer operations), `dms archival ...` covers the closest real equivalent: `archival-service`'s records-disposal transfers (5.6, P7-S3/S3b) — thematically related but not factually the same.
- **No new service** — `tools/cli/` does not follow the `docs/service-template.md` pattern (no `main.py`/FastAPI/Postgres schema, no entry in `infra/docker-compose.yml`: no health endpoint, no long-running "up" state). New `tools/*` in `[tool.uv.workspace].members` (root `pyproject.toml`, previously only `libs/*`/`services/*`). Python + `typer`, since it fits the existing `uv` monorepo stack, no new ecosystem needed.
- **Gateway client exactly like the web UIs**: `GatewayClient` (`client.py`) talks to `{gateway_url}/api/{service_type}/{path}` with a bearer token — 1:1 the same pattern as `apps/*-ui/src/lib/api.ts`'s `request()` helper, no direct backend addresses. This means the CLI is subject to the same permissions/safeguard levels as the web UIs, with no own enforcement logic.
- **A real gap in the web UIs discovered while designing the auth**: `refreshToken()` has long been defined in `apps/admin-ui`/`apps/user-ui`, but is **called nowhere** (0 call sites checked) — inconsequential so far for a short-lived browser tab. For a CLI whose access token expires across many short process invocations spread over hours, that would force constant re-login. `GatewayClient` therefore implements a transparent single-attempt refresh+retry on `401` — a real gap of the web UIs, closed here for the first time (still open there, outside this session's scope).
- **Service-account auth (concept 6.2, CI/CD pipelines) deliberately not built**: `auth-service`'s Keycloak client has `serviceAccountsEnabled: False` (`bootstrap.py`) — only password and refresh-token grants exist. A clean service account per pipeline would need its own confidential Keycloak clients (client management API), a standalone, multi-session feature. As a stopgap: `DMS_TOKEN`/`DMS_GATEWAY_URL` env vars override the locally stored `~/.dms/credentials.json` (chmod 600) — a pipeline injects a token obtained elsewhere, with the CLI writing nothing to disk.
- **Seven domain modules** (`commands/{auth,config,query,object_type,user,role,registry,workflow,archival}.py`), each with its own Typer sub-app — fulfills concept 6.2's "modular ... structured into functional sub-commands" structurally (self-contained readable/testable/extensible); a literal split into separately shippable individual binaries remains a later packaging decision.
- **`dms query manipulate`/`dms query approvals` talk to the same endpoints as `QueryConsoleView`'s `ManipulationSection` (P8-S2b)**, including the same hardcoded list of the three curated action types — deliberately duplicated in the CLI code instead of an import from `query-service`, since service isolation (`CONTRIBUTING.md`: services communicate only via APIs, not shared code) applies equally to tools talking to them. Concept 6.1 literally requires that "the same query language and the same safeguard levels" apply in the UI and the CLI.
- **Output format**: a dependency-free table as the default, `--output json`/`-o json` global (must come before the subcommand) — satisfies "scriptable output formats such as JSON" without introducing a rendering package like `rich`.
- Tests: `tools/cli/tests/` **70 new** (first test run of this new package) — `GatewayClient` (401 refresh-retry including a failed refresh, error paths, all HTTP methods), credential storage (env-var override, file permissions `0600`), output formatting, as well as representative tests per domain module via `typer.testing.CliRunner` + `httpx.MockTransport` (no real network, `GatewayClient.__init__` globally patched instead of each command module individually). `ruff check`/`ruff format` clean.
- `docs/tools/cli.md` newly created (scope table, architecture decisions, command overview, open points). `docs/services/query-service.md`/`docs/services/permission-service.md` extended with short "also usable via CLI" notes, no other content changes needed (CLI only uses existing endpoints).
- **Live Docker verification, this time without the P8-S2b limitation** (no browser tool needed — a CLI is fully genuinely operable directly via the bash shell): `dms login` as the existing `users-admin` account against the running stack, `dms whoami` confirms real Keycloak claims. `dms user create` creates a real test principal (`dms-cli-testuser`), `dms role list` finds the real `domain-admin-query-console`/`-manipulate` IDs, two `dms role assignment create` grant both on `root`. Logged in as the test principal: `dms query events list` correctly returns `0/5` (RBAC filtering fail-closed with no resource right, as verified in P8-S1), `dms registry status`/`dms object-type list`/`dms workflow definitions list`/`dms workflow instances list --status running`/`dms archival transfers list` return real data from the running stack. `dms query text "..."` → correctly `501` with the ADR-0031 reference text (no parser plugin installed). `dms query manipulate dry-run` **before** protection-switch activation → correctly `403`; after `dms query manipulation-mode activate --minutes 10` → dry run returns a real preview + token for a test document created via a `curl` multipart upload, `dms query manipulate execute` actually removes the attribute (`GET /documents/{id}` confirms `attributes: {}`). After `PUT /approval-config/document.attribute_reset {requires_approval:true}` (directly via `curl`, no CLI command for it, as already in P8-S2/S2b): the same flow returns `pending_approval`; **switch back to the `users-admin` credential set** (second person, own `DMS_CLI_HOME`), `dms query approvals list` shows the real pending request, `dms query approvals approve <id>` (with no `--approved-by`, correctly defaulting to `users-admin` from the stored credentials) → `query-service`'s consumer executes the action within a few seconds, a second document attribute actually removed. `dms object-type create -f`/`update -f`/`delete` against a real test object type — `update` in the process hit a real, correct `422` server validation error (`icon` only allowed for `applies_to=folder`, own test-data mistake, not a CLI bug), simultaneously showing that server error messages are cleanly passed through with exit code 1; `delete` succeeded. Test data fully removed: both test documents deleted via `DELETE /documents/{id}?deleted_by=...` (`dms object-type delete` covers no query-parameter case like this, done directly via `curl`), `approval-config` reset to `requires_approval:false`, both role assignments removed via `dms role assignment delete`, test principal deleted via `dms user delete` and confirmed removed via `dms user list`. The one surviving artifact is, as in P8-S1/S2, the permanent approval-request record (no `DELETE` endpoint for it, intended audit behavior).

### Licensing System (Phase 9, P9-S0–P9-S2)

**P9-S0 was a pure research/planning session, no implementation code** — third `PN-S0` kickoff session after P7-S0/P8-S0, same convention. Concept 9.1–9.3 verified against the current code status, no contradictions found against what was already built, but several concrete anchor points and one real gap identified — actual code inspection instead of assumption.

- **Signature scheme clarified ([ADR 0032](docs/adr/0032-lizenzdatei-signaturverfahren.md))**: JWT/JWS in compact format, **RS256** — deliberately no new library/format, but reuse of `python-jose[cryptography]>=3.3`, already present in every service container via `libs/dms-auth-client`'s `TokenValidator` (Keycloak JWT verification, 4.4). Checked: the installed `jose` build supports `RS256`/`ES256`/... but **no** `EdDSA`/Ed25519 — RSA is thereby the only asymmetric option already proven in the system, and additionally exactly the scheme Keycloak itself uses for signed access tokens (no second curve/key format in the same system). The private key is generated and managed **offline**, outside every Docker image/repo, by the licensor — no license-issuance tool is part of this repo. Every installation only gets the public key statically embedded (settings/mounted file) — no JWKS endpoint, no rotation in this first expansion stage (installations can be operated air-gapped, there is no online service they could query for it), documented as an open point.
- **Three usage dimensions (9.1) checked against the existing data — two are already fully or partly representable with existing building blocks, one has a real gap**:
  - **Storage volume**: `reporting-service`'s `GET /reports/storage-usage` (P7-S2b) already queries live aggregated consumption per backend against `storage-service` — the same client call is directly reusable for the license check, no new aggregation logic needed.
  - **Concurrent users**: `auth-service`'s already-existing `KeycloakAdmin` wrapper (`python-keycloak`) has a ready-made method `get_client_sessions_stats()` (current session count per client in the realm, official Keycloak admin API) — no new session tracking needed, only a new thin `auth-service` endpoint passing this already-available number through.
  - **Document count — real gap**: `document-service`'s only counting endpoint is `POST /documents/count-active` (P7-S1b, for the folder-forced-deletion check), **folder-tree-scoped** (`folder_ids` parameter required), no endpoint for the installation-wide total of active documents. `reporting-service`'s `GET /reports/document-volume` only returns a time series ("volume over time"), no running total. P9-S1 must either extend `count_active_by_folder_ids` with a "no filter = all" mode or add a dedicated total-count endpoint — real prep work, not just a call to an existing endpoint.
  - **Application components**: concept examples (CMIS connector, Migration Service, workflow automation) suggest that **not every** service is separately licensable, but only selected, "premium"/optional-marked modules — which specific services in this system that means is to be clarified at P9-S1 session start, no pre-decision made here.
- **Registry mediation (9.2/9.3, "b) license mediation" in the concept text on 3.2) requires a capability that does not exist anywhere yet**: "affected services react (e.g. switch to demo mode) without a restart" means an already-running service must react to a license-status change without restarting and without a synchronous extra call on every request. No service does this today for any state — the closest blueprint is `gateway-service`'s Not-Shutdown mechanism (4.8, ADR 0024): a central `MaintenanceStateClient` with a TTL cache polls `permission-service` and attaches the current state as an `X-DMS-Maintenance-Active` header to every passed-through request — the same cache-poll-and-broadcast pattern is a real, already-proven blueprint for an `X-DMS-License-Status`-like solution, but not a pre-decision of this session (P9-S2 decides whether demo mode/lockout is enforced at the gateway or per service — demo mode, unlike Not-Shutdown, is nuanced per component ("read-only only" and the like), a pure gateway blanket block like 4.8 likely does not fit 1:1).
- `registry-service`'s `InstanceOut`/`POST /instances` response currently has **no** license-status field (`instance_id`/`service_type`/`version`/`capabilities`/`health_endpoint`/`address`/`registered_at`/`last_heartbeat_at`/`healthy`) — concept 9.3 requires "Registry Service supplies its component's license status to every registering service", so it must extend the registration response and get a `license-service` client (P9-S2, real prep work, not a pure display session).
- **Installation ID (9.2: "each installation registers itself with the License Service ... with its own installation ID")**: no existing "identity of this installation itself" concept found — `federation-hub-service`'s `installation_id` describes exclusively foreign, federated installations in its own address book, no self-identity. For a single installation (the standard case in this repo, same scope boundary as Federation Hub 7.4 "optional"), a simple, once-generated/persisted ID suffices — no blocker, a short note for P9-S1 session start.
- **Deliberately confirmed outside the Phase 9 scope**: the higher-level, cross-installation Fleet/License Management Service and a central License Service for multiple installations (concept, mentioned directly after 9.2) are explicitly marked as an "optional, separate building block" — not part of P9-S1/S2, same boundary as Federation Hub. The license check before placement in the Plugin Orchestration Service (3.8) belongs to Phase 10, not here. Delta/comparison runs (7.5) explicitly exclude license status from the comparison (already noted this way in the concept text) — no action needed.
- **Session breakdown P9-S1/S2 confirmed, no split needed** — the two real prep items found above (document-count endpoint, registry-registration extension) fit cleanly into the already-planned split (S1 = License Service itself including usage check, S2 = mediation/demo-mode/Admin-UI), no additional intermediate session needed.
- No code, no tests, no `graphify update` (no change relevant to code/document counters except this ADR + the roadmap files) — next update with P9-S1.

- **P9-S1 (License Service, 9.1/9.2)**: new `services/license-service/` (port 8023, schema `license`, singleton table `installed_license` — genuinely own state, same pattern as `query-service`'s `manipulation_mode_status`, P8-S2). Directly implements the signature decision found in P9-S0: `license_verifier.py` uses `python-jose[cryptography]` (already a dependency of `dms-auth-client`) for RS256 verification against a statically embedded, hardcoded-in-`settings.py` dev key ([ADR 0032](docs/adr/0032-lizenzdatei-signaturverfahren.md)) — the corresponding private key exists **only** in `tests/fixtures/dev_private_key.pem`, never in the Docker image/Compose file. `POST /license` deliberately only rejects on an invalid signature (`400`); a signature-valid but expired license is still stored and shown as invalid via `GET /license/status` (design decision: reflects the real situation instead of a special case on upload).
- **Two of the three P9-S0 prep items were real, necessary extensions of existing services**: `document-service` got `GET /documents/count-active-total` (new `repository.count_active_total`, deliberately **not** reusing `count_active_by_folder_ids([])`, since an empty list there carries the safety meaning "no folders checked", not "no filter"). `auth-service` got `GET /sessions/count` (`KeycloakAdmin.get_client_sessions_stats()`, filtered to its own OIDC client, `active` field is a **string**, not int, in the real Keycloak response — verified live against real Keycloak) and `GET /users/count` — both deliberately **ungated**, since `GET /users` requires a real Keycloak bearer token via `Depends(get_current_user)`, which no service possesses (P9-S0 finding confirmed).
- **Usage-data sources deliberately direct, not via reporting-service**: `storage-service`'s `GET /storage/usage` called directly (sum `total_size_bytes`) instead of via `reporting-service`'s report endpoint, which internally makes the same call — service isolation, no unnecessary extra hop through a pure reporting layer.
- **Poll loop with edge detection instead of event spam**: `InstalledLicense.last_status_snapshot` (JSON) holds the last-reported state (invalid/soon-expiring/exceeded per dimension) — the three events (`license.limit_exceeded`/`license.expiring_soon`/`license.invalid`) are only published on an actual state change, specifically tested (`test_recovering_from_exceeded_allows_new_event_later`: limit exceeded → event; back within limit → no event, snapshot reset; exceeded again → event again, not permanently suppressed). A new installation resets the snapshot (fresh assessment instead of inherited "already reported" flags from the previous license).
- **A real bug found and fixed while wiring up `notification-service`**: the three new `license.*` subjects all share the same new `"license"` stream — a second/third `subscribe()` call with the standard durable name `"notification-service"` for a respectively different filter subject on the same stream fails with "consumer is already bound to a subscription", the same limitation that `workflow.federation.inbound_received` (P6-S9) already required a second durable name for, but here for the first time with **three** rather than two subjects on a shared stream. Discovered on the first `scripts/run-tests.sh notification-service` run (8 failures), fixed via a small `_SHARED_STREAM_DURABLE_OVERRIDES` mapping table instead of the previous two-case if/else branching — all three license subjects get their own durable name, for symmetry reasons also the first of the three (no technical necessity for that, but more readable than a default+two-exceptions pattern).
- **`admin.license` role enforced for the first time** (long seeded, but unused) — exact gate pattern from `query-service` (superuser bypass via `auth-service`'s `GET /superuser/status`, otherwise a `permission-service` check against `admin.license`).
- Tests: `license-service` **25 new** (first test run of this new service: `test_license_verifier.py`, `test_usage.py`, `test_poll_loop.py`, `test_api.py`), `document-service` **153** (previously 151, 2 new: repository and API level for `count_active_total`), `auth-service` **45** (previously 41, 4 new: `test_license_usage_endpoints.py`, real integration tests against real dev Keycloak, no mocking — matching this service's already-established test pattern), `notification-service` **30** (previously 22, 8 new: 3 new consumer tests + the 5 existing `test_api.py` tests made runnable again by the durable fix), `audit-service` still **21** (only a settings change). Full backend regression across all **24 services** green (`license-service` automatically captured via directory detection, no `CONSUMER_SERVICES` entry needed — the service itself has no own NATS consumer, only a producer).
- **Live Docker verification** (a real stack freshly rebuilt via `scripts/run-tests.sh --build`): test principal with the `domain-admin-license` role created, `POST /license` with a license actually signed via `tests/fixtures/license_factory.py` (`document_limit=5`, deliberately below the real document count) → `201`, `GET /license/status` shows real live usage (34 real active documents from the entire session history, 52 real concurrent Keycloak sessions, real storage consumption) and correctly `documents` as exceeded. Without the role → `403`. `GET /license/status` through the gateway with no token → `401` (expected, no `public_routes` entry) — directly within the Docker network (simulating the future `registry-service` caller path from P9-S2) → `200`, ungated as intended. Container restart forces an immediate first poll tick (production interval 3600s, no waiting needed in the verification): `license.limit_exceeded` really lands in the audit trail (`GET /events?event_type=license.limit_exceeded`, correct `actor="system:license-poll"`) **and** as a real email in Mailpit ("license usage limit exceeded" to `license-admin@dms.local"`). A second upload with an expired test license (`exp` in the past) → `valid:false`, `invalid_reason:"license expired"`, after a restart a real `license.invalid` event in the audit trail. Test data removed: role assignment and test principal deleted; the most recently uploaded (expired) test license itself remains as the installation state (no `DELETE /license` endpoint provided, analogous to other non-deletable artifacts like roles/approval requests in earlier sessions) — a harmless remaining state, no data risk.

- **P9-S2 (license mediation/demo mode/limit blocking/Admin-UI, 9.3)**: **two scope follow-up questions at session start** (`AskUserQuestion`): (1) concept 9.3's third behavior ("exceeding a usage limit ... prevents new creations") was missing from the roadmap short version — user decided to build it anyway. (2) demo mode/lockout behavior is demonstrated exclusively in `workflow-service` (the only real, currently existing licensable component from 9.1 — CMIS connector/Migration Service only come in Phase 12), no premature stub connector.
- **`registry-service` gets its first own NATS consumer** (`consumer.py`, subject `"license.>"`, durable `registry-service`) — reacts to every `license.*` event only with a cache invalidation (no payload parsing needed), plus a TTL cache (60s) as a fallback. New `licensing.ComponentLicenseCache.status_for(service_type)`: only `service_type` values in the new settings `licensable_components` (default `{"workflow-service": "demo"}`) are licensable at all — every other service stays "core" and always gets `"licensed"`, without even querying `license-service` for it. `InstanceOut` (register/heartbeat/listing responses) now carries `license_status`, new ungated `GET /license-status/{service_type}` for poll clients of already-running services.
- **`workflow-service` enforces demo mode/lockout** via a new `license_client.LicenseStatusClient` (TTL cache 15s against `registry-service`'s new endpoint, fail-open to `"licensed"`) and a new `Depends()` gate dependency (`_license_gate`) — deliberately a real FastAPI dependency instead of manually in the function body like maintenance mode, since an async cross-service call is needed here rather than a simple header read. `"unlicensed"` also blocks reads, `"demo"` only blocks writes (concept example "read-only only" literally). `/federation/*` endpoints deliberately excluded (a standalone topic, Phase 13).
- **`document-service` blocks new creations when the document limit is exceeded** — new `license_client.LicenseLimitClient` (TTL cache 30s against `license-service`'s `GET /license/status`, fail-open to "not exceeded"), checked as the first action in `POST /documents` (`403`). Direct call to `license-service`, not via the registry — that only mediates component status, not usage limits. Existing documents/versioning/restoration deliberately untouched (concept 9.3 literally: "does not block retroactively").
- **A real cross-service problem found and fixed before it reached the tests**: the obvious production default (`licensable_components={"workflow-service": "demo"}`) would have permanently put `workflow-service` into demo mode in the bundled dev/test stack (no license-issuance CLI, ADR 0032 — a valid license is never automatically installed here) and broken `case-service`'s integration tests, which really call `POST /process-definitions` against the live-running `workflow-service` container (actually failed with `403`/`Forbidden` on the first full `scripts/run-tests.sh --build` run, 7 failures + 1 fail). Fixed via an explicit `DMS_LICENSABLE_COMPONENTS: ${REGISTRY_SERVICE_LICENSABLE_COMPONENTS:-{}}` override in `infra/docker-compose.yml` (only for this stack, the code default remains the production value) — the same pattern already needed in P9-S1 for its own unit tests as a `_default_licensed`/`_default_no_license_limit_exceeded` autouse fixture (every test infrastructure carried over from P9-S1 with real cross-service calls against `workflow-service`/`document-service` would otherwise have broken by default due to the new license check).
- Tests: `registry-service` **28** (previously 12 across test_api/test_repository/test_events, now additionally `test_licensing.py`/`test_consumer.py`/API extensions — 16 new total), `workflow-service` **85** (previously 61, 24 new: `test_license_client.py`, `test_license_gate.py`, plus the new `_default_licensed` autouse fixture in `conftest.py`), `document-service` **158** (previously 153, 5 new: `test_license_limit.py`). `admin-ui` **144 tests across 26 files** (previously 135/24 — 2 new files `license-status-view.test.tsx`/`license-status-banner.test.tsx`, 9 new), `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` clean (`/license` route builds/exports cleanly as a static page). Full backend regression across all **23 services** green, `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` repo-wide clean.
- **Live Docker verification, this time with targeted temporary overrides instead of only additive uploads** (since the Compose default is `licensable_components={}`, `registry-service` had to be specifically restarted with `REGISTRY_SERVICE_LICENSABLE_COMPONENTS='{"workflow-service":"demo"}'` resp. `"lock"` for the demo/lockout verification — reset to the Compose default afterward): test principal with the `domain-admin-license` role created. A real license **not** covering `workflow-service` uploaded → `GET /license-status/workflow-service` shows `"demo"` **with no restart of workflow-service or registry-service** (event-triggered cache invalidation actually confirmed, not just the TTL fallback) — `GET /process-definitions` still `200`, `POST .../instances` actually `403` ("demo mode active - read-only access only."). After restoring coverage in a new license → status actually back to `"licensed"`, the same `POST` returns `201` again. With policy `"lock"` → status `"unlicensed"`, now `GET /process-definitions` also actually `403` ("license required..."). Document limit: a real license with `document_limit=50` (below the real document count 112) uploaded → a real `POST /documents` upload returns `403` ("license document limit exceeded"), a previously existing document remains retrievable via `GET /documents/{id}` `200`. A forced poll tick (container restart) confirms a real `license.limit_exceeded` event (`dimension: documents`) in the audit trail **and** a real email in Mailpit to `license-admin@dms.local`. Gateway routing confirmed: `GET /api/license-service/license/status` with no token → `401` (expected). Finally a generous license covering all components/limits installed (deliberately **not**, unlike P9-S1, an intentionally exceeding/expired remaining state — a tight remaining state would have permanently blocked other services' write paths). Test data fully removed: role assignment and test principal deleted, `registry-service` reset to the Compose default.

### Orchestration & Rolling Updates (Phase 10, P10-S0–P10-S3)
- **P10-S0 was a pure research/planning session, no implementation code** — fourth `PN-S0` kickoff session after P7-S0/P8-S0/P9-S0, same convention.
- **Deployment landscape checked, finding: only Docker Compose actually present.** `infra/k8s/README.md` has remained an unchanged placeholder since its creation ("Kubernetes manifests only become relevant once the Plugin Orchestration Service requires a platform scheduler"), no Swarm manifests anywhere in the repo, `.github/workflows/` contains only `ci.yml` (tests, no deploy). Concept 3.8's "platform scheduler preferred, FFD as fallback" thus has only one path that actually ever runs against real infrastructure in this project — the FFD fallback.
- **Follow-up question at session start** (`AskUserQuestion`) on the platform-scheduler branch: three options presented (a) clean interface, only mock-tested, (b) set up an actual Docker Swarm test target additionally, (c) leave the branch out for now. **User chose (a)** — interface + a concrete adapter are built, but only tested against a mocked scheduler API (same pattern as other cross-service clients in the project, `httpx.MockTransport`), honestly documented as "untested against a real cluster" until a real target system is on the agenda. No additional infrastructure effort, analogous to the P8-S3 precedent (CLI tool: features with no backend are documented instead of blindly built or silently skipped).
- **Central structural finding: the drain mechanism belongs after P10-S2, not — as originally worded in the roadmap short version — only after P10-S3.** Concept 3.8's own text assigns the drain mechanism ("redistribution of existing instances (rebalancing) happens in a controlled and gentle manner... drain behavior") explicitly to the time-profile-aware-placement paragraph (P10-S2's topic); 10.5 (rolling updates, P10-S3) explicitly describes reusing "the same drain mechanism that the Plugin Orchestration Service (3.8) already uses for gentle redistribution under load spikes", just for a different occasion — "no second, parallel-maintained concept". P10-S3 would thus have incorrectly had to rebuild it, even though P10-S2 needs it first. Corrected breakdown: **P10-S2 builds** the drain state (new `status` field `active`/`draining`, analogous pattern to `InstanceOut.license_status` from P9-S2 — response enrichment, no additional persisted field needed unless `ServiceInstance` needs it anyway) and adjusts `gateway-service`'s already-existing `InstanceResolver`/`pick()` (currently `random.choice()` over all `healthy` instances in `services/gateway-service/src/gateway_service/upstream.py` — `draining` instances must drop out of the selection for *new* requests, running requests are never affected in this synchronous reverse-proxy model anyway). **P10-S3 uses** the same mechanism only for the update occasion, plus the expand/contract tooling for schema changes.
- **Side finding while tracing this mechanism**: `libs/dms-registry-client` is pure self-registration (register/heartbeat/deregister) and has **no** discovery/resolve function — the actual dynamic instance resolution happens exclusively in `gateway-service`'s own `InstanceResolver` (with its own 5s TTL cache, fail-open to the last known state). Good for 10.5: the gateway queries the registry again anyway on every (cached) resolution, so a new instance status arrives automatically with no gateway restart, as soon as `resolve()` knows the `draining` filter.
- **Bootstrap gap found in the resource data**: 3.8's cold-start placement (P10-S1) and the implicit profile formation from historical load (P10-S2) both reference "the same sensor infrastructure as described in 10.1" — but 10.1 (monitoring & observability) is **Phase 11**, thus comes *after* Phase 10 in roadmap order. Since Docker Compose (see above) ships no metrics server, the Plugin Orchestration Service in P10-S1/S2 cannot rely on a platform as in the concept's normal case (Kubernetes/OpenShift metrics server). 3.8 itself already anticipates this case ("only if the platform does not supply this data does the orchestrator collect it via its own mechanism") — P10-S1/S2 therefore build a deliberately minimal internal resource sample (e.g. periodic reading of container stats), no reordering of the phase sequence needed. Phase 11 later builds the full sensor layer; the orchestrator then switches over to it (3.8's own preference rule: "where the platform supplies it, the orchestrator preferentially uses that").
- No new external library/licensing question in this session (unlike P7-S0/P8-S0) — hence no new ADR, the three findings above are pure session-breakdown/architecture clarifications and already fully appear in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (the Phase-10 preamble).
- Session breakdown P10-S1/S2/S3 thereby **refined in content, but not split** (no additional session like earlier phase splits).

- **P10-S1 (Plugin Orchestration Service scaffolding, 3.8)**: **follow-up question at session start** (`AskUserQuestion`) on the depth of automation — concept 3.8 literally requires "automatic, with no manual assignment", but the repo has no container-lifecycle automation whatsoever (no Docker SDK, no Docker-socket access anywhere). Three options presented: (a) decision/recommendation engine with no Docker socket, (b) real automation via the Docker Engine API, (c) two-track/deferred. **User chose (a)** — the service computes and audits placement decisions, but does not itself start containers. Actual starting remains external (a human/deploy script), same justification as the P10-S0 decision on the platform-scheduler branch (P8-S3 precedent: a feature with no real backend is documented rather than faked).
- New `plugin-orchestration-service` (port 8024, schema `orchestration`, structurally modeled on `license-service` — its own poll/sampler loop, event publishing, no NATS consumer). `PluginManifest` (upsert per `plugin_type`, no version coexistence): `scaling_type` (`stateless_horizontal`/`singleton`), optional `resource_cpu_cores`/`resource_ram_mb`, `load_profile` (not yet evaluated, follows with P10-S2), `dependencies`.
- **Resource estimation solved without a Docker API via self-reporting instead of external measurement**: plugin instances actively report their own, self-measured resource usage via `POST /plugins/{plugin_type}/resource-usage` (`instance_id`/`cpu_cores`/`ram_mb`) — an exact analogue to the registry heartbeat principle (3.2a: "everyone reports themselves"), avoids Docker-socket access entirely. The median of the most recently reported, still fresh (`resource_report_stale_after_seconds`, default 60s) values per `plugin_type` is the basis for concept 3.8's cold-start fallback. No real plugin calls this today (none exists yet) — covered synthetically by 24 new tests, same pattern as `registry-service` before P9-S2.
- **Three estimation sources with their own `source` field** (`PlacementDecisionOut`): `"manifest"` (static values declared), `"observed_median"` (median of fresh reports), `"default_fallback"` (documented minimal default 0.5 cores/256 MB if neither manifest nor observation exists — deliberately its own value, to distinguish this "completely cold" case from 3.8's real median case).
- **Own minimal resource sample** (`ClusterNode`, `node_id="self"`): a periodic `psutil` sampler (same poll-loop idiom as `license-service`) measures exclusively its own host — in the Docker Compose environment there is only this one node anyway (P10-S0 finding: the full 10.1 sensor infrastructure only exists starting Phase 11). Capacity check: available cores = `cpu_cores * (1 - cpu_usage_percent/100)`.
- **Singleton conflict detection**: `scaling_type="singleton"` + a fresh resource report from another instance of the same type → `POST /placements` returns `409` instead of a second placement.
- **Every placement decision is audited, including rejected ones** (concept 3.8: "the Plugin Orchestration Service itself is also a service whose decisions are audited") — locally in `placement_decision` (read model `GET /placements`) AND as an `orchestration.placement.decided` event; `audit-service`'s subjects list got `"orchestration.>"`.
- **Dependency check informative, not blocking**: the `dependencies` listed in the manifest are checked against `registry-service`'s `GET /instances/{type}` (new small `RegistryClient`, TTL cache 15s, fail-open) and returned as `dependency_status`, but do not prevent a placement (the concept does not explicitly require this).
- **New domain-separated admin role `domain-admin-orchestration` (`admin.orchestration`)** — comes into being with the actual feature, like `admin.license` (P9-S1), concept 4.6 names its domain list only as examples. Gate on `POST /plugins/{type}`/`POST /placements`, 1:1 gate pattern from `license-service` (superuser bypass + `PermissionServiceClient`). The existing `test_ensure_domain_admin_roles_seeds_all_expected_roles` test in `permission-service` derives its expectation dynamically from `DOMAIN_ADMIN_ROLES` — automatically covers the new role, no test change needed.
- **A real concurrency issue found and fixed, actually occurring during test development**: the obvious get-then-create upsert for `ClusterNode` (modeled on `registry_service.repository.register`) led to a real `UniqueViolationError`, because the background sampler loop and a test seeding the same node with its own values could concurrently create the same row. Fixed via an atomic `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` (`sampler.upsert_node`) — a deliberate, documented deviation from the get-then-create pattern otherwise usual in the project, since here (unlike e.g. registry instance registration) two concurrent writers can actually hit the same row. A second, related problem (direct access to `app.state.session_factory` from a test with its own event loop → "attached to a different loop") was fixed by using the already-existing `session` fixture (its own engine bound to the test loop) instead of the app's own factory.
- Tests: `plugin-orchestration-service` **24 new** (`test_placement.py`: manifest source/median fallback/default fallback/staleness/singleton conflict/capacity check/dependency status; `test_sampler.py`: `psutil` plausibility, upsert idempotency; `test_api.py`: gate, manifest CRUD, resource usage, placement including `409`, `GET /nodes`/`GET /placements`). Full backend regression across all **25 services** green, `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` repo-wide clean.
- **Live Docker verification** (a real stack freshly rebuilt via `scripts/run-tests.sh --build`): test principal `test-orchestration-admin` directly linked with `domain-admin-orchestration` via `permission-service`'s ungated `POST /role-assignments` (role already automatically seeded at `permission-service` startup, `GET /roles` confirms `admin.orchestration`). Without a principal/with the wrong principal → real `403`. `GET /nodes` shows the real `psutil` values of the test machine (24 cores, ~62 GB RAM) instead of fake data. Manifest with no static resource values + `POST /placements` → `source="default_fallback"` (0.5 cores/256 MB). Two real `POST .../resource-usage` reports (1.0/3.0 cores, 300/500 MB) + a repeated placement → `source="observed_median"` (2.0 cores/400 MB) — median fallback confirmed live. Singleton manifest + one resource report + a second placement request → real `409`.
