Manifest with an absurdly high resource requirement (9999 cores) → the decision is still persisted (`placement_allowed=false`, `node_id=null`, `reason` set) rather than discarded — audit obligation for rejected requests confirmed too. Unknown manifest → real `404`. `GET /events?event_type=orchestration.placement.decided` at `audit-service` shows all four real events in the hash chain. Test data: role assignment deleted; the created test manifests/placements/reports themselves remain (no `DELETE` endpoint provided, analogous to other non-deletable artifacts of earlier sessions) — a harmless remaining state in a brand-new service with no real users, no risk.

- **P10-S2 (time-profile-aware placement, drain mechanism, FFD fallback, platform-scheduler detection, 3.8)**: implements both P10-S0 decisions directly — platform scheduler only as a mock-tested interface, the orchestrator remains a pure decision/recommendation engine (therefore deliberately triggers **no** automatic drain at the registry — the roadmap line for this session explicitly named only the state/its enforcement, not an automatic coupling, which absent a real plugin would have been an unfounded assumption about shared `instance_id`s between registry registration and resource reporting anyway).
- **Drain mechanism in `registry-service`**: new field `ServiceInstance.status` (`"active"`/`"draining"`), new `POST /instances/{id}/drain` (ungated like every other registry endpoint — WHEN draining happens is decided by an external deploy tool/P10-S3, not the registry itself). A **new** registration always starts `"active"`; a re-registration of the same `instance_id` (self-healing after `404`, not a real restart) does **not** reset an existing `"draining"` status — only `/drain` itself sets it.
- **`gateway-service`'s `InstanceResolver` excludes draining instances from new routing**: `resolve()` additionally filters on `status == "active"` in addition to `healthy` — a draining instance remains visible in the registry and keeps running untouched (no kill, running requests unaffected), but drops out of the selection pool for new requests. The only place in the code that actually selects instances for live traffic (P10-S0 finding: `dms-registry-client` itself has no resolve function).
- **First-fit-decreasing across multiple nodes in `plugin-orchestration-service`**: new `POST /nodes/{node_id}` (admin-gated like manifests) for declaring additional node capacity — symmetric to the P10-S1 precedent of resource self-reporting (no real second host calls this today in the Docker Compose environment, but the same self-reporting logic a future second host would use). Placement selects, across all `ClusterNode` rows in ascending `node_id` order, the first node with sufficient free capacity.
- **Time-profile-aware node selection ahead of the pure capacity check** (concept 3.8 literally: time profile "as an additional sort/grouping criterion before the pure resource-size sorting"): capacity-capable nodes are sorted by a complementarity score of their currently "living" plugin types (living = a fresh `PluginResourceReport` + the most recent allowed `PlacementDecision` pointing to this node) — score = number of types with a different `load_profile` minus the number with the same. With no `load_profile` (the default case today), it stays pure first-fit. Implicit profile derivation from historical observation (the concept explicitly names it optional) deliberately not built.
- **Platform-scheduler detection**: new `platform_scheduler.py` with a `SchedulerAdapter` protocol, `NullSchedulerAdapter` (the real state in this project — always `None`, FFD fallback applies), `detect_platform_scheduler()` (informational, checks `KUBERNETES_SERVICE_HOST`, currently with no associated adapter — a hit only produces a warning log line). New `PlacementDecision.placement_method` field (`"platform_scheduler"`/`"ffd"`) makes the concept-3.8 distinction auditable.
- **A real schema problem found and fixed, twice, same cause**: `create_all` creates missing tables, but does not alter existing ones — the new columns `service_instance.status` and `placement_decision.placement_method` did not exist in the test database already running from P10-S1, actually failing with `UndefinedColumnError` on the first full test run (25 resp. 15 failures). Fixed via the same additive `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` migration that `document-service` et al. had already established for exactly this case (no Alembic in this phase) — the first session in Phase 10 that extends an existing table in an already-running service, not just creates new tables.
- Tests: `registry-service` **36** (previously 28, 8 new: `test_new_instance_registers_as_active`, `test_drain_sets_status_to_draining`, `test_drain_unknown_instance_returns_404`, `test_draining_instance_still_listed`, `test_reregistering_same_instance_does_not_reset_draining` in `test_api.py` + three `mark_draining` tests in `test_repository.py`), `gateway-service` **15** (previously 14, 1 new: `test_draining_instance_is_excluded_from_routing`, real registry integration like the existing blueprint), `plugin-orchestration-service` **33** (previously 24, 9 new: multi-node first-fit, time-profile ranking, platform-scheduler delegation/fallback in `test_placement.py`, node-upsert gate in `test_api.py`). Full backend regression across all **25 services** green, `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` repo-wide clean.
- **Live Docker verification** (a real test user created via the Keycloak admin API, a real login via `auth-service` for a real gateway bearer token; test principal `test-orchestration-admin` again linked with `domain-admin-orchestration`): a real instance of a test `service_type` registered, reached via the gateway (`200`), then really drained (`POST /instances/{id}/drain`, response shows `status:"draining"`) → the **first** gateway call right afterward still returned `200` (the `InstanceResolver`'s 5s TTL cache still held the pre-drain instance as active — real, expected cache behavior, not a malfunction), after the TTL expired the same call returned a real `503`. `GET /instances/{type}` continues to show the instance with `status:"draining"` throughout (not deregistered). FFD: two real nodes of different capacity (`node-small`/`node-large`) created + a manifest with a high resource requirement → placement really skipped `node-small` and landed on `node-large`. Time-profile ranking: two equally sized nodes (`node-a`/`node-b`) created, an `"interactive"` plugin really placed on `node-a` and made "living" via a resource report, `node-a` temporarily set to `cpu_usage_percent=100` to force a `"nightly"` plugin onto `node-b` (then `node-a` reset to `0` again) — a third, new `"nightly"` manifest afterward really preferred `node-a` (complementary `"interactive"` neighbor) over `node-b` (competing `"nightly"` neighbor). `GET /events?event_type=orchestration.placement.decided` at `audit-service` shows `placement_method="ffd"` for all these real decisions (no platform scheduler in this stack). Test data removed: role assignment, Keycloak test user, test registry instance; test manifests/nodes/placements in `plugin-orchestration-service` remain as in P10-S1 (no `DELETE` endpoint, harmless).

- **P10-S3 (rolling updates, reuse of the drain mechanism, expand/contract, 10.5)**: **follow-up question at session start** (`AskUserQuestion`) on the depth of automation — a real, working script instead of just documentation, including a real live demonstration on an uncritical service in the running dev stack (justifiable, since the architecture is consistently stateless throughout, guiding principle 1/6 — a container is replaceable at any time).
- **Finding during planning: a rollback gap from P10-S2**. Concept 10.5 explicitly requires that a rollback remain possible "as long as the drain... has not yet fully completed" — but P10-S2 only built `POST /instances/{id}/drain`, no reversal. With no way to reactivate a draining instance, there would be no real rollback path. New, symmetric `POST /instances/{instance_id}/activate` in `registry-service` (resets `status` back to `"active"`) closes this gap.
- **New `scripts/rolling-update.sh <service>`**: a real, working choreography against the running Docker Compose stack — query instance IDs before the update, build a new image, start a temporary canary container **with no host port publish** (`docker compose run -d --no-deps`, no conflict with the running, port-bound Compose service; `DMS_SELF_ADDRESS` is overridden to the container's own DNS name, since `docker compose run` containers do not automatically get the shared service network alias), poll for its real healthy status via the registry (health/readiness check from 10.5 — reuses the already-existing heartbeat computation, no new protocol), set old instance(s) to draining, a grace period (configurable, default 30s — an honestly documented simplification, since there is no generic "running operations completed" signal across arbitrary services), stop/remove the old container, freshly start a regular instance (finally replacing the canary), wait for readiness again, set the canary to draining/stop it. At every point in time, at least one active instance remains reachable.
- **A real detail discovered and correctly handled during the build**: `docker compose run` containers do NOT automatically get the shared network alias of the Compose service (unlike with `--scale`) — without explicitly overriding `DMS_SELF_ADDRESS`, the canary would have incorrectly registered under the address of the already-running old instance. Solved via `-e DMS_SELF_ADDRESS=http://<canary-name>:8000` on the `docker compose run` call.
- **Consumer services deliberately excluded**: the script aborts with a clear error message if the target service is on the same `CONSUMER_SERVICES` list as `scripts/run-tests.sh` (duplicated, with a reference comment) — a second container with the same exclusive NATS durable name would fail to subscribe with "consumer is already bound to a subscription", the exact same, already-known limitation. A real solution (queue groups instead of exclusive durable names) is a NATS architecture change, not part of this session.
- **Self-deregistration on stop proved already sufficient**: `docker compose stop`/`docker stop` send `SIGTERM`, whereby the existing lifespan shutdown logic of every service (`dms-registry-client`'s `RegistryRegistration.stop()`, since P4-S1) already self-deregisters via `DELETE /instances/{id}` — the script did not need its own explicit call for this, confirmed by the live run (no orphaned rows after completion).
- **Expand/contract formalized** (no new code) in a new `docs/operations/rolling-updates.md` — the first document of this category in the project (previously only `docs/services/*.md`/`docs/adr/*.md`/`docs/tools/*.md`). The additive `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS ... DEFAULT ...` migration that `document-service` has been using since P7-S1 and `registry-service`/`plugin-orchestration-service` since P10-S2 IS already the "expand" step — here named for the first time and recorded as a convention in `CONTRIBUTING.md`. "Contract" (removing no-longer-needed old columns, only after a fully completed rollout) remains documented as a named, but never-yet-used second step.
- Tests: `registry-service` **40** (previously 36, 4 new: `test_activate_resets_status_to_active`/`test_activate_unknown_instance_raises` in `test_repository.py`, `test_activate_resets_draining_to_active`/`test_activate_unknown_instance_returns_404` in `test_api.py`). Full backend regression across all **25 services** green, `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` repo-wide clean.
- **Live Docker verification, this time a real, complete rolling-update run instead of just individual API calls**: `scripts/rolling-update.sh favorite-service --drain-grace-seconds 15 --readiness-timeout-seconds 60` run against the real, running stack (no consumer, uncritical) — **during the entire run, 45 real gateway health checks with a real bearer token in parallel every 2 seconds (90s total duration): 0 of 45 failed**, real proof of zero downtime. Script flow really confirmed: old instance `favorite-service-5470d0ba` → canary `favorite-service-e57bc1d8` reported healthy → old instance drained → old container stopped/removed (self-deregisters) → regular new instance `favorite-service-7bf97af6` started and found ready → canary drained/removed. `GET /instances/favorite-service` afterward shows exactly one `"active"` instance, `docker ps` no longer shows a canary container. Abort path: `scripts/rolling-update.sh audit-service` (a consumer service) → immediate, expected failure with an explanatory message, no container change (confirmed via `docker ps`). Rollback round trip individually verified: `POST /instances/{id}/drain` → real `503` through the gateway (after the 5s resolver cache expires) → `POST /instances/{id}/activate` → real `200` again. Test data (Keycloak test user) removed.

### Monitoring & Backup/Restore (Phase 11, P11-S0–P11-S4)
- **P11-S0 was a pure research/planning session, no implementation code** — fifth `PN-S0` kickoff session after P7-S0/P8-S0/P9-S0/P10-S0, same convention.
- **Infrastructure status checked, finding: still only Docker Compose, no monitoring connection whatsoever present.** `infra/docker-compose.yml` (39 Compose services) contains no Prometheus, Grafana, or CheckMK container; a repo-wide search for `/metrics` endpoints and `prometheus` across all 25 services returned zero hits. P11-S1/S2 thus find no existing connection they would only need to "check" (as the roadmap short version suggested), but must add Prometheus/Grafana/CheckMK themselves as new Compose services for the first time.
- **Sensor registry belongs in the existing `registry-service`**: concept 10.1's own text places it there ("The Registry Service, in addition to service discovery (3.2), also maintains a sensor registry: which services offer which sensors, which are currently active"), analogous to the P9-S2 precedent (license mediation was likewise appended to the existing registry, not a new service). A possible new `monitoring-service` is thereby only responsible for aggregation/dashboard configuration, not the sensor catalog itself — avoids a second, parallel discovery mechanism.
- **Full retrofit of all services deliberately excluded**: 10.1 taken literally ("every service exposes its active sensors via a unified metrics endpoint") would require a full sensor catalog + `/metrics` endpoint for all 25 services in a single session. This contradicts the established precedent from P6-S2 (the notification retrofit deliberately not done comprehensively — "every future consumer registers its own subject when actually connected") and P8-S3 (CLI scope honestly limited to real backends instead of building dummies). **Decision**: P11-S1 builds the pattern + a shared lib `libs/dms-metrics-client` (analogous to `dms-registry-client`) + pilot sensors on two representative services (`registry-service` itself as the most trivial case, `document-service` as a high-throughput case) instead of instrumenting all 25 services. Further services get their sensors on actual demand in later sessions — documented as a deliberate gap, not silently claimed as complete.
- **Backward dependency on 7.3 found** (same pattern as the P10-S0 finding on 10.1's dependency on 10.1 sensor infrastructure): 10.1 explicitly refers to "part of the JSON configuration export (7.3)" for the sensor on/off configuration — but the configuration import/export service only exists starting **P12-S3**, thus comes after Phase 11. P11-S1 therefore cannot yet ex-/import sensor configuration via 7.3, but persists and audits it independently for now (own table in `registry-service`) and hooks it up once P12-S3 exists. No reordering of the phase sequence needed — 7.3 deliberately bundles with the connectors from Phase 12, not pulled forward.
- **10.4 building blocks partly already really present, partly deliberately not to be rebuilt**: `document_service.models.DeletionRegisterEntry` (P7-S1) is already exactly the deletion register that the mandatory deletion reconciliation after restore (10.4) needs — the associated gap ("not backup-differentiated... since Phase 11 does not yet exist") already stands as a known open item further up in the Phase-9 section; P11-S4 now concretely closes it. Real object versioning (one of the two storage-backup options named equally by the concept), however, does not exist in `storage-service` — only the different WORM/governance mode from P7-S1 (ADR 0030), which prevents deletion/overwriting *during* retention, but retains no history of overwritten versions. P11-S3 therefore takes the second path named equally by the concept itself ("separate backup target outside the regular storage target set") instead of rebuilding an own storage-versioning layer as a side effect of the backup session — that would have been a significantly bigger, standalone storage-interface intervention.
- **Additional decision on the P10-S0 promise of the orchestrator switch-over**: P10-S0/P10-S1 had held out the prospect that the Plugin Orchestration Service would later "switch over to the full sensor layer" (3.8's own preference rule: "where the platform supplies it, the orchestrator preferentially uses that"). This switch-over is deliberately **not** followed up on in Phase 11: in the real, existing Docker Compose environment there is only this one host anyway, the existing `psutil` sampler (P10-S1) and a new Prometheus source would deliver identical values — a switch-over would bring no measurable value before real multi-node infrastructure exists, only additional code detour. Remains documented follow-up work, relevant only once real multi-node operation exists, not a silently dropped point.
- No new external library/licensing question in this session — hence no new ADR, the findings above are pure session-breakdown/architecture clarifications and already fully appear in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (the Phase-11 preamble).
- Session breakdown P11-S1–S4 thereby **refined in content, but not split** (no additional session like earlier phase splits) — same result as P10-S0.

- **P11-S1 (sensor concept + Monitoring Service + Prometheus exposition + sensor registry, 10.1)**: **follow-up question at session start** (`AskUserQuestion`) on the scrape topology — user explicitly decided against "Prometheus scrapes every domain service directly" and in favor of a **pull/proxy model via a new `monitoring-service`**: domain services each keep their own `/metrics` endpoint, `monitoring-service` knows all active instances via `registry-service`'s existing `GET /instances`, fetches their `/metrics` itself live and in parallel, and merges them into its own response — Prometheus scrapes exclusively this one service. This corrects the P11-S0 finding (2), which had still placed the sensor registry in `registry-service` — it now consistently lives in `monitoring-service` (monitoring domain, not discovery domain), as the roadmap short version ("sensor concept + **Monitoring Service**, Prometheus exposition, sensor registry") named it from the start.
- **New lib `libs/dms-metrics-client`**: `SensorSpec` (name/group/cost/description), `SensorRegistry` builds `GuardedCounter`/`GuardedGauge`/`GuardedHistogram` from it, wrapping `prometheus_client` objects — every call (`inc()`/`observe()`/`set()`) first checks the activation status and does not touch the underlying metric object at all when "off" (10.1 literally: "no creation, no buffering", not just invisible in the export). `run_gauge_sampler_loop()` is a generic poll loop for "current state" gauges (same idiom as the existing sampler loops) that only runs the underlying (potentially expensive) computation for active sensors. `SensorConfigClient` polls `monitoring-service`'s `GET /sensor-config` (TTL 15s, fail-open to "everything active") — deliberately simpler than the `ComponentLicenseCache` blueprint (P9-S2, no NATS invalidation), a scope decision given the overall size of this session.
- **`registry-service` only got a pass-through `sensors` field** (`RegisterRequest`/`ServiceInstance`/`InstanceOut`, additive `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS sensors JSON DEFAULT '[]'::json`) — no new business logic, no new gate, minimal footprint. Registers itself with two sensors (`registry.instances.active_total`, `registry.service.heartbeat.miss` — the latter literally the concept-10.1 example name, here implemented as a live count of currently missed heartbeats) and gets its own `GET /metrics`.
- **New `monitoring-service`** (port 8026, schema `monitoring`, table `sensor_config` with a special key `__global__` for the base setting + arbitrary sensor-specific overrides, default "everything active"): `GET /metrics` (scrape proxy, see below), `GET /sensors` (aggregated catalog from all active instances, ungated), `GET /sensor-config` (resolved configuration, ungated, poll target for `SensorConfigClient`), `PUT /sensor-config/global`/`PUT /sensor-config/{name}` (new domain-admin role `admin.monitoring`, same gate pattern as `admin.orchestration`/`admin.license`, audits `monitoring.sensor.config_changed`). **Real federation pattern for the scrape proxy**: `scraper.merge_metric_families` parses each scraped response with `prometheus_client.parser.text_string_to_metric_families`, groups by metric name, and attaches an `instance` label to each sample — plain text concatenation would have violated the exposition format's grouping rule for same-named metrics across multiple instances (HELP/TYPE must be contiguous). Live, not cached — every `GET /metrics` call scrapes fresh, no background poll loop needed. An always-active, non-configurable counter `monitoring_scrape_failures_total` (own `CollectorRegistry`, separate from the scraped metrics) counts failed scrape attempts — deliberately outside the configurable sensor catalog, since a self-reference (monitoring-service asking itself via HTTP whether its own sensor is active) would be unnecessary complexity with no real benefit. Deliberately no circular `depends_on` in `infra/docker-compose.yml` (only `monitoring-service depends_on registry-service`, not the other way around) — same fail-open pattern as `license-service`/`registry-service` since P9-S2.
- **`document-service` is the second pilot**: `document.upload.duration` (histogram, `performance`, `expensive` — literally the concept-10.1 example name, the timer is only started when the sensor is active, measured around `POST /documents`) and `document.count.active_total` (gauge, `capacity`, `cheap`, uses the same `repository.count_active_total` query as `GET /documents/count-active-total` from P9-S1, periodically sampled via `run_gauge_sampler_loop`).
- **`permission-service`** got the new domain-admin role `domain-admin-monitoring` (`admin.monitoring`) — an existing dynamic role test automatically covers this (same pattern as `admin.orchestration` in P10-S1). **`audit-service`** got `"monitoring.>"`.
- **A real problem found and fixed during live verification**: the scrape proxy initially tried to fetch `/metrics` against ALL currently active, healthy instances (23 actually registered services), even though only the two pilots have a `/metrics` endpoint at all — the other 21 services returned structurally expected `404`s, massively flooding `monitoring_scrape_failures_total` (69,844 failed attempts after a few minutes of runtime, since every Prometheus scrape cycle re-queried all 23 targets). Fixed via filtering in `RegistryClient.list_active_instances()` to instances with at least one declared sensor (`i.get("sensors")`) — after the fix `monitoring_scrape_failures_total 0.0`, only the two real pilots are scraped now. A new dedicated `test_clients.py` now covers this filtering behavior.
- Tests: `libs/dms-metrics-client` **10 new** (guard behavior active/inactive, sampler loop skips inactive sensors, `SensorConfigClient` TTL/fail-open), `libs/dms-registry-client` **+1** (sensor-declaration round trip against the real, live-running registry), `registry-service` **43** (previously 40, 3 new: sensor-field round trip in repository/API, `/metrics` format), `monitoring-service` **18 new** (repository config resolution, scraper merge including federation grouping, API gate/`/metrics`/`/sensors`, `RegistryClient` sensor filter), `document-service` **161** (previously 158, 3 new: `/metrics` format, guard behavior active/inactive on upload). Full backend regression across all **26 services** green (`scripts/run-tests.sh --build`), `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` repo-wide clean.
- **Live Docker verification, consistently against the real, newly built stack**: `GET /sensors` at `monitoring-service` correctly shows both pilot catalogs merged (four sensors, all `active:true`). `GET /metrics` shows real, live-scraped values of both pilots with `instance` labels (including `document_upload_duration_bucket{instance="document-service-f27f9d83",...}`). Gate really checked: without a principal/with an unknown principal → real `403`; a real role assignment (`domain-admin-monitoring`) via `permission-service`'s ungated `POST /role-assignments` → `PUT /sensor-config/document.upload.duration` with `{"enabled": false}` → real `200`. **Real end-to-end proof of "no recording when deactivated"**: after deactivation, a real upload directly against `document-service` (`POST /documents`) — `document_upload_duration_count` remained unchanged at `32.0`, even though the upload actually went through; after removing the override (`{"enabled": null}`, falls back to the active base setting) and the 15s poll interval elapsing, a second real upload → counter really rose to `33.0`. `curl http://localhost:9090/api/v1/targets` confirms: Prometheus knows exactly one target (`monitoring-service:8000`), status `"health":"up"`. `GET /events?event_type=monitoring.sensor.config_changed` at `audit-service` shows all real configuration changes with correct `actor` in the hash chain. Test data removed: role assignment deleted, both test uploads (`SensorTest`/`SensorTest2`) regularly soft-deleted.
- **`graphify dms/ --update` run afterward** (not a phase completion, but substantial code growth — new service + new lib, CONTRIBUTING.md criterion): **7158 nodes, 12947 edges, 586 communities** (previously 6924/12542) — health check clean (no dangling/missing/collapsed edges). Same benign CLAUDE.md path-alias false report as in every previous session confirmed (file exists unchanged at both mount points, merge reported "already clean", no data loss). HTML export automatically aggregated to community view (7158 nodes above the 5000-node direct-render threshold).

- **P11-S2 (Grafana dashboard templates, 10.1)**: **follow-up question at session start** on the CheckMK scope — three options presented (a real container + partial automation via the REST API, a real container with purely manually documented setup, or no container/documentation only), each after prior research into the actual automatability (the official `checkmk/check-mk-raw` image exists and starts with no manual `omd create`, but the Prometheus special-agent rule likely cannot be fully automated via the REST API without a one-time manual GUI step, for lack of an independently documented `value_raw` raw format for this ruleset type). **User explicitly decided against CheckMK in this session** ("I want Grafana, not CheckMK") — neither a container nor documentation, deliberately noted as an open point for a possible later session (see `docs/operations/monitoring.md`), not silently marked done.
- **Grafana fully declaratively provisioned** (concept 10.1: "supplies sensible default dashboard definitions as exportable JSON templates... a starting point, not mandatory use"): new `grafana` Compose service (`grafana/grafana:11.3.1`, port `3003`, default login `admin`/`admin` — pure dev convenience like other default credentials in this stack), `infra/grafana/provisioning/datasources/prometheus.yml` wires up the already-running `prometheus` instance with a fixed `uid: prometheus` (so dashboard JSONs can reference it without needing Grafana's template-variable data-source-import mapping mechanism), `infra/grafana/provisioning/dashboards/dashboards.yml` automatically loads every JSON file from `infra/grafana/dashboards/` — no manual setup in the UI needed.
- **New `infra/grafana/dashboards/dms-sensor-overview.json`**: seven panels for the four P11-S1 pilot sensors (`registry.instances.active_total`, `registry.service.heartbeat.miss`, `document.upload.duration` as average duration via `rate(_sum)/rate(_count)`, `document.count.active_total`) plus the always-active `monitoring_scrape_failures_total` counter and `up{job="monitoring-service"}` as a target-status indicator. Remains a pure JSON file, independently exportable/importable from the Compose stack (concept requirement literally satisfied) — automatically grows as further services get scraped via `monitoring-service`, with no need to change the file itself (panel queries already aggregate with `sum(...)` across all instances).
- **Live Docker verification**: `docker logs dms-grafana-1` confirms real provisioning ("inserting datasource from configuration", "finished to provision dashboards"). `GET /api/search` (basic auth `admin`/`admin`) shows the real dashboard in the `DMS` folder, `GET /api/datasources` shows the real Prometheus data source with `uid=prometheus`. **Real proof that the panels actually work, not just syntactically valid JSON**: `GET /api/datasources/proxy/uid/prometheus/api/v1/query` (the same path Grafana's panels use internally) really returns `23` for `sum(registry_instances_active_total)`, really `33` for `sum(document_upload_duration_count)` (identical state to the end of P11-S1), and really `1` for `up{job="monitoring-service"}`. No Python code changed in this session — pure infra/doc session, `graphify update` deliberately deferred (not a phase completion, no substantial code growth in the sense of the CONTRIBUTING.md criterion).

- **P11-S3 (backup/restore mechanism, 10.4)**: **two follow-up questions at session start** — (1) orchestrator form: operational scripts (`scripts/backup.sh`/`scripts/restore.sh`) instead of a new permanently running service with standing privileged Postgres/storage credentials, user chose the recommended script variant (same pattern as `scripts/rolling-update.sh`, P10-S3); (2) confirmation of the scope split to P11-S4 already laid out in the roadmap ("deletion reconciliation after restore + automated restore tests") — this session delivers only the mechanical backup/restore mechanism (10.4 steps 1-3/5-6), not the deletion reconciliation (step 4) or the search-index rebuild (step 7).
- **Research before planning**: confirmed that neither WAL archiving nor `pg_basebackup`/`pg_dump` existed anywhere in the repo (Postgres ran with stock `postgres:16-alpine` defaults), that the existing maintenance mode (4.8, P6-S6: `permission-service` singleton `SystemMaintenanceMode` + enforcement in `gateway-service`) is real and functional, that `storage-service` has no bulk enumeration interface for its objects (only `storage.object_metadata`/`storage.object_copy` directly queryable via SQL), and that practically the entire runtime configuration already lives in Postgres singleton tables.
- **Plan correction during implementation: no maintenance mode during backup.** The original planning foresaw briefly activating the existing maintenance mode (4.8) to force a "consistency anchor" for backup. On closer reading of concept 10.4, it turned out that maintenance mode is textually linked there only to **restore** ("automatically trigger a maintenance mode on restore... remains locked until the deletion reconciliation completes"), not to backup — and a WAL-based backup (`pg_basebackup` + continuous archiving) is inherently a system-level online/hot backup anyway, whose consistency arises through WAL replay at restore time, not through freezing writes during the backup. Additionally it turned out that `POST /maintenance-mode/lift` mandatorily requires the **currently activated** superuser (4.8, whose activation itself goes through a real four-eyes break-glass procedure per 4.6) — inappropriately heavyweight for a routine backup operation. Since `restore.sh` in this session works exclusively against an isolated scratch environment (see below), it does not touch the running stack anyway and consequently does not need maintenance mode either — documented as an open follow-up point for a real production restore (P11-S4 context).
- **New WAL archiving in `infra/docker-compose.yml`**: the `postgres` service got `archive_mode=on`/`wal_level=replica`/`archive_command='test ! -f /wal-archive/%f && cp %p /wal-archive/%f'`/`max_wal_senders=3`, new volume `postgres-wal-archive`. **A real problem found and fixed on the first start**: a freshly created Docker volume initially belongs to `root`, the `postgres` process (user `postgres`) could not write into it (`cp: can't create '/wal-archive/...': Permission denied`, confirmed via `docker logs`). Fixed via a new, one-off `postgres-wal-archive-init` Compose service (`chown -R postgres:postgres /wal-archive` as root), which `postgres` now waits for (`depends_on: condition: service_completed_successfully`) — reproducible on every `docker compose up`, no manual step needed for new developer environments. After the fix, really confirmed: `pg_switch_wal()` produces real WAL segment files under `/wal-archive`, taken over by the archive process.
- **`scripts/backup.sh`**: reads `DMS_TARGETS` from the running `storage-service` container, backs up every target with `role != "archive"` (the archive role serves records disposal, 5.6, not backup protection against logical errors — deliberately untouched) via `docker exec ... tar czf - -C <base_path> .` — only `type="local"` implemented/verified (the type actually active in the stack), `type="s3"` is recognized and skipped as a documented gap instead of shipping unverified code. Afterward `pg_basebackup` (online, `-Fp -Xs -P`) against the running Postgres container, the current WAL LSN noted as the "consistency anchor", `manifest.json` written (the single source of truth for which artifacts belong together — exactly the main error source 10.4 names for asynchronous backups). The `config_export: null` placeholder field honestly documents the 7.3 backward dependency (configuration export only exists starting P12-S3) instead of concealing it or building a dummy.
- **`scripts/restore.sh`**: works **deliberately against an isolated scratch environment**, not the running 26-service dev stack — actually overwriting the single shared Postgres instance of all services would have been destructive to the running session. Builds a temporary second Postgres container (`dms-postgres-restore-test`, own scratch volume, no `dms-net`, no host port) from the basebackup, with `recovery.signal` + `restore_command`/`recovery_target_time`/`recovery_target_action=promote` against the (read-only mounted) WAL archive volume — real point-in-time recovery, not a dry run. Storage tarballs are extracted into a scratch directory, checksums are checked against the **restored** `storage.object_metadata` table (proves DB and storage restore consistency toward each other). Cleans up scratch containers/volumes/directories in every case (`trap cleanup EXIT`). At the end, explicitly outputs what per 10.4 is still missing (deletion reconciliation/search index/automated tests, referencing P11-S4) — no silent "done".
- **Live Docker verification, with a real point-in-time-recovery proof**: a real test document created (`PITR-Testdokument-VOR-Backup`), `scripts/backup.sh` run against the real, running stack (backup directory, basebackup ~94 MB, storage tarball ~48 MB, `manifest.json` fully checked). Afterward the title of the test document really changed via `PATCH` (`PITR-Testdokument-NACH-Backup-VERAENDERT`), a `pg_switch_wal()` forced the immediate archiving of the relevant WAL segment. `scripts/restore.sh <backup-dir> --recovery-target-time "<time between backup and change>"` run — **the restored scratch database really showed the old title `PITR-Testdokument-VOR-Backup`**, not the changed one (directly cross-checked via `docker exec dms-postgres-restore-test psql`, for this the cleanup trap was temporarily commented out once and then restored). Storage checksum comparison, really: **8712 of 8723 objects checked, 0 discrepancies** (11 missing objects are plausibly attributable to the not-perfectly-atomic temporal separation of the storage tar and the DB basebackup LSN — exactly the imprecision 10.4 names, documented by `manifest.json`, not an error in the check logic itself). Test document then regularly soft-deleted, scratch containers/volumes/backup test directory fully removed, the running stack remained reachable throughout (gateway/document-service/registry-service `200` confirmed after completion). No Python code changed — pure infra/script/doc session, `graphify update` deliberately deferred (not a phase completion, no code growth).

- **P11-S4 (deletion reconciliation after restore + automated restore tests, 10.4) — closes Phase 11**: **follow-up question at session start** on the depth of the automated restore test — full shadow application stack (document-service+storage-service against the scratch data from P11-S3) vs. a shared approach (detection really against the scratch DB, the actual deletion separately really against the live stack). **User chose the shared approach** (the recommendation) — less new infrastructure, both parts still really tested, just not chained into a single continuous run.
- **Research before planning**: confirmed `document_service.retention_actions.execute_forced_deletion()`/`folder_service`'s structurally identical counterpart as the reusable "physical deletion" function (storage bypass + `DeletionRegisterEntry` + hard delete), that both services publish the same `document.force_deleted`/`folder.force_deleted` event type, that `permission-service`'s `ApprovalActionConfig` returns `requires_approval=False` with no explicit row (i.e. `document.force_delete` is ungated in this stack — important for the later live test), and that the repo so far has no Python test idiom whatsoever for "spawn a real Docker container from within a test" — only `scripts/backup.sh`/`restore.sh` (bash) do that.
- **Deletion-register ledger, independent of the DB restore cycle**: new `audit_service/deletion_ledger.py` — the existing NATS consumer (`consumer.py`) appends a JSON line to `/deletion-ledger/deletion-register.jsonl` on `document.force_deleted`/`folder.force_deleted` events (deliberately NOT on `trash_expiry` — lower concept consequence), on a **new, own** Docker volume (`deletion-ledger-data`) — technically fully separate from the shared, restorable Postgres instance. Every line carries an `entry_id` (the `event_id` of the underlying `Event`, not the Postgres row ID of `DeletionRegisterEntry` — deliberately, so the reference still holds even if the original row itself had disappeared again due to a restore). Concretely closes the gap known since P7-S1 (deletion register introduced) and P9-S2/P11-S0 (documented as an open gap: "not backup-differentiated").
- **New, structurally identical `POST .../reconcile-restore-deletion` endpoints** in document-service and folder-service: call `execute_forced_deletion()` directly with `triggered_by="system:restore-reconciliation"` and publish the same event type with an additional payload field `reconciliation_of_entry_id` — "the same mechanism as for the original forced deletion" (10.4 literally), no second, potentially divergent implementation. Gate: `X-DMS-Roles` must contain `dms-admin` (same simple pattern as document-service's existing reference-number gate, folder-service got a new, analogous `admin_role` setting for this) — deliberately no new `PermissionServiceClient` for such a rare, purely operational endpoint. **A real detail corrected during the build**: `execute_forced_deletion` creates the `DeletionRegisterEntry` BEFORE hard-deleting the row — without an upfront existence check, an unknown `document_id`/`folder_id` would have left an orphaned register entry before the `404` kicked in; both endpoints therefore explicitly check first.
- **`scripts/restore.sh` now got the step so far only documented as "TODO P11-S4" for real**: after the point-in-time recovery, reads the ledger from the running `audit-service` container, filters entries between the backup time (`manifest.json`) and the actual, real moment of the restore run (not `recovery_target_time` — that is the point in the past being rolled back to, the resurrection case arises precisely from this difference), checks each candidate via an SQL query against the restored scratch DB for whether the object "resurrects" there, and prints the finished `curl` call for the later live-connected system on hits. **A real security gap found and fixed during the build**: the first version passed the raw ledger file content to Python via an *unquoted* bash heredoc — free-text fields like `reason`/`triggered_by` could have contained `$()`/backticks and been executed by the shell before Python ever saw them. Fixed via a temporary file + quoted heredoc (`<<'PYEOF'`), which excludes any shell interpolation of the ledger content.
- **New `scripts/test-restore.sh`**: a self-verifying, repeatable restore test with a real exit code (not just log output) — part 1 really rebuilds the complete resurrection scenario (create a test document, take a backup, backdate `retention_until` into the past + `full_deletion=true`, `docker compose restart document-service` forces an immediate first poll tick instead of waiting for the 3600s interval — same technique as P9-S1 —, real forced deletion, run `restore.sh` targeting a point before that, check detection via an assertion); part 2 separately and really verifies the reconcile endpoint against a second, active test document (403 without the role, 204 with the role, document then really gone, `DeletionRegisterEntry` with correct `triggered_by`). **A real formatting problem found and fixed on the first run**: `manifest.json` stores timestamps ISO-8601 (`.../T.../Z`), but Postgres' `recovery_target_time` only accepts its own format (`... +00`) — the first test run really failed with `invalid value for parameter "recovery_target_time"`, fixed via a small format conversion in the script. Also discovered for real: `PUT /documents/{id}/retention` with `retention_until: null` is never recognized as "due" by the poll loop (`list_due_for_retention_action` requires `retention_until <= now`) — `retention_until` must be set to a real past date, not `null`.
- Tests: `audit-service` **26** (previously 21, 5 new: `deletion_ledger.py` function tests including folder/`trash_expiry` exclusion, a real NATS round-trip test), `document-service` **164** (previously 161, 3 new: gate, real deletion, `404` for an unknown document), `folder-service` **75** (previously 72, 3 new, structurally identical). Full backend regression across all **26 services** green (`scripts/run-tests.sh --build`), `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` repo-wide clean.
- **Live Docker verification, `scripts/test-restore.sh` twice in a row fully successful (exit 0)**: part 1 — a real test document created, a real backup taken, `retention_until` really backdated into the past, `document-service` restarted (immediate poll tick), document really force-deleted (real `404` confirmed), `restore.sh` run with `recovery-target-time` before the deletion — really reported `RECONCILIATION_NEEDED_COUNT=1` with the correct document plus a ready-made `curl` call. Part 2 — reconcile endpoint without the role → real `403`, with `X-DMS-Roles: dms-admin` → real `204`, document then really gone, `GET /deletion-register` shows a real entry with `triggered_by="system:restore-reconciliation"`. Both runs fully cleaned up after themselves (no orphaned scratch containers/volumes/backup directories), the running stack reachable throughout (gateway/document-service `200` after completion). Deletion-register ledger in the `audit-service` container really confirmed (`docker exec ... cat /deletion-ledger/deletion-register.jsonl`).
- **`graphify dms/ --update` run afterward (phase completion, mandatory per CONTRIBUTING.md)**: **7250 nodes, 13038 edges, 598 communities** (previously 7158/12947) — health check clean. Same benign CLAUDE.md path-alias false report as in every previous session confirmed (no data loss). A harmless node-ID collision reported and automatically resolved (two docs referenced ADR 0020 with the same generated node name `adr_0020_sla_timer_polling` — graphify's own collision protection discarded the second, no loss of actual content). HTML export automatically aggregated to community view (598 community nodes, 921 edges).

### Bugfixing outside the session roadmap (after P11-S4, before P12-S0)
User feedback on document preview (2.4/3.7), not a regular `PN-SN` session — nevertheless fixed with full test/live verification effort: "Somehow I always only get the rendition (plain text)" — images should appear as images, PDFs as PDFs, HTML rendered, text documents formatted Word-like (A4 page).
- **Root-cause research before implementation showed: three of the four gaps needed no new code at all, just connecting unused, already-existing building blocks.** `rendering-service`'s `PdfArchiveRenderer` has, since P7-S3, already automatically converted every office/text format into a real, paginated PDF via LibreOffice (`pdf_archive` rendition) — but it was purely intended as an archive copy and was never consumed by the frontend as a preview. Raw image/PDF bytes could be shown directly natively in the browser via the long-existing `downloadDocumentVersion()`, entirely without a rendition detour. Only `text/html` needed an actual frontend novelty: `PreviewPane.tsx`'s `isTextPreviewable()` matches `text/html` via its `text/*` prefix check, so HTML uploads landed as raw, escaped markup in a `<pre>` block instead of being rendered.
- **`PreviewPane.tsx` re-prioritized** (`PreviewKind` extended with `"pdf"`/`"html"`): `text/html` checked first (before the `isTextPreviewable()` branch) → `<iframe sandbox="" referrerPolicy="no-referrer" srcDoc={...}>`; then unchanged the existing direct-text branch; for everything else first `listRenditions()`, in it the new `pdf_archive` preference **before** `substitute_text` (image/PDF sources excluded from this — `PdfArchiveRenderer` also converts images to PDF, but here "image as image" should appear); then unchanged the existing OCR branch (preference for the interactive word overlay retained); as new final fallbacks a native `<embed type="application/pdf">` for `application/pdf` resp. full image resolution for `image/*`, both via `downloadDocumentVersion()`. The old `thumbnail` rendition query at the end was completely removed, since `ThumbnailRenderer` in rendering-service only supports image sources anyway and images are now intercepted beforehand via their own raw-byte branch — dead leftover code with no remaining call path.
- **A design error found and corrected during implementation itself**: the first version let `application/pdf` jump immediately and unconditionally (before any OCR check) to the native `<embed>` — this would have made the existing, valuable OCR page-image-with-word-overlay feature unreachable for scanned/digital PDFs (visible in seven really-failing existing tests in `document-workspace.test.tsx` that check exactly this feature). Correction: the native PDF embed is now a fallback **after** the OCR check, not its replacement — a PDF with an existing OCR result still gets the page image with text overlay, only a PDF *without* a (completed) OCR result (the actual case reported by the user: "PDFs have no preview at all") now gets the real embedded PDF instead of previously nothing.
- **Security decision HTML sandbox**: `content_type` comes from purely magic-byte-based sniffing with no allow-/denylist (`document-service/content_type_sniffer.py`) — any file whose bytes look like HTML is trusted as `text/html`, regardless of the filename. Since this is the first untrusted-HTML rendering in the entire codebase (no prior iframe/`dangerouslySetInnerHTML` precedent), the `<iframe>` runs with an empty `sandbox=""` (no `allow-scripts`/`allow-same-origin`/`allow-forms`/`allow-popups`/`allow-top-navigation`) — prevents script execution from an uploaded file against the session of whoever later views it (stored XSS). Confirmed live against the real stack: an uploaded `<script>` tag remains in the raw, unmodified file content (`document-service` sanitizes nothing server-side), the sandbox is the sole, but per component test sufficient, safeguard.
- Tests: new standalone `apps/user-ui/tests/PreviewPane.test.tsx` (7 tests, renders `PreviewPane` directly instead of via `DocumentWorkspace`), two adjusted `document-workspace.test.tsx` fixtures (a previously unrealistic `application/pdf`+`thumbnail`-rendition test scenario that `rendering-service` never actually produces, switched to a real `image/jpeg` document) — `apps/user-ui` overall **93 tests** (previously 86), `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` clean (one already-existing, unchanged `no-img-element` hint).
- **Live Docker verification against the running stack** (`docker compose build user-ui && docker compose up -d user-ui`, the remaining 26-service stack already/newly started): `.txt`/`.html`/`.pdf`/`.jpg`/`.docx` test documents really uploaded — server-side sniffing produced the expected `content_type` for all five; `.docx` automatically got a `ready` `pdf_archive` rendition (verified via `pypdf`: a real, single-page, LibreOffice-generated PDF with the correct original text) as well as still a `ready` `substitute_text` rendition; `GET .../content` returned byte-identical raw data with the correct content-type header for PDF/JPG, for HTML the untouched original content including the script tag. **No browser available in this environment** (as already documented in the original bugfixing round after P7-S0) — the actual DOM rendering is therefore only secured via the Vitest component tests (jsdom), not visually; a human should cross-check in a real browser before production use.
- `docs/services/user-ui.md` updated (preview section restructured, "open points" entry on the `pdf_archive` gap closed, new entry on the deliberately remaining limit of the HTML sandbox — no blocking of external subresource requests, only `referrerPolicy="no-referrer"` as a mitigation).

**Immediately afterward a second, thematically related user-feedback fix on OCR** (still outside the roadmap): the user suspected OCR was creating a new, text-only version of the document — **research disproved this** (`ocr-service` had never created a `document-service` version, only its own `OcrResult` rows; the actual cause of the preview gap was the `PreviewPane.tsx` fix described above). But the actual wish behind this was a real, missing feature: "a PDF with no text layer becomes a PDF with a text layer", for images a separate text extract instead of a format change, and OCR by default only for PDFs instead of for everything.
- **Three follow-up questions at session start, all decided with the recommendation**: (1) storage location of the searchable PDF as a new document version instead of a new rendition — deliberately the same pattern as `signature-service` (ADR 0025), which already server-side creates a new version when it changes PDF bytes; (2) the existing word overlay (P5-S3) is **not removed**, but demoted to a real fallback for PDFs (a second, specific follow-up question exactly on this consequence, user explicitly chose "keep as fallback"); (3) for images the image remains the main display, the text extract is added purely additively, no mixing with the overlay.
- **`services/ocr-service/src/ocr_service/text_layer.py`** (new): `embed_text_layer()` embeds Tesseract's recognized words as an invisible text layer (`page.insert_text(..., render_mode=3)` — the same PDF standard mechanism `ocrmypdf` also uses) at the correct positions, converted from pixel to PDF-point coordinates, into a copy of the original PDF bytes — visible appearance stays unchanged, only searchability changes.
- **`document_client.py` got `create_version()`**, 1:1 following the pattern of `signature_service.document_client.checkin_signed_version()`; **`pipeline.py`** calls it after successful Tesseract extraction for PDF sources (`created_by="system:ocr-service"`), non-blocking (a failure must not undo an already-successful OCR finding). **`repository.py`'s `get_config()`**: default changed from `allowed_content_types=[]` to `["application/pdf"]` — OCR now by default only runs for PDFs, images require a deliberate admin opt-in (only for freshly created config rows, existing installations keep their stored value).
- **Two real bugs found while testing, both fixed before the live rollout** (details/reasoning see `docs/services/ocr-service.md` "searchable PDF"): (1) a page with no recognized words at all still got a pointless new version with an empty text layer — discovered because exactly such an empty test fixture PDF in `signature-service`'s own test suite (`tests/fixtures/sample.pdf`, a completely white 200×200 test page) threw off its version-number assumptions, since the `ocr-service` container keeps running live during **other** services' test runs and processes every uploaded PDF (`signature-service`'s own test suite really failed with `assert 4 == (1 + 1)`). Fix: embedding only when at least one word was recognized. (2) **missing termination of the reprocessing cycle for short recognized text**: the newly created version itself triggers `document.version.created` again; the original assumption that `_native_text_available()`'s 20-character threshold reliably ends this after one pass did not hold for a single short test word — Tesseract, on re-rastering (the invisibly embedded words do not change pixels), recognized the same visible word again and a second copy was embedded, until the threshold was only then exceeded (really observed: 3 instead of 2 versions). Fix: `VersionMetadata` now carries `created_by`; the embedding does not run if the processed version already originates from `system:ocr-service` itself — an explicit lock instead of relying on an incidental character-threshold coincidence.
- **`PreviewPane.tsx`**: the PDF branch now switches based on `ocrResult.engine` instead of just "a result exists" — `engine === "tesseract"` (a real scan with no text layer) still shows the word overlay, `engine === "native_text_layer"` (always present, or embedded by OCR) shows the native `<embed>` view. The image branch got a purely additive, expandable "recognized text (OCR)" block (`ocrResult.full_text`, the field had always existed server-side, but was never shown as its own view in the frontend) — with no change to the existing overlay.
- **Known, accepted edge-case limitation**: for very short recognized text (below `min_native_text_chars`'s 20 characters), the new, technically already-searchable version stays at `engine="tesseract"` — `PreviewPane`'s engine switch therefore still shows the overlay instead of the native view. Affects practically no real multi-line scans, deliberately not eliminated with additional complexity.
- Tests: new `services/ocr-service/tests/test_text_layer.py` (4 tests, pure `fitz` function tests with no Tesseract/document-service dependency), four new Tesseract-gated pipeline tests (a scan with text → new version; an empty scan → no new version; the service's own generated version is not embedded twice — regression test for bug 2; a raster image → no version creation), repository/API default tests updated — `ocr-service` overall **48 tests** (previously 40, of which 8 are Tesseract-gated and skipped on this host, verified live in the container). Full backend regression across all **26 services** green (including `signature-service`, whose failure due to bug 1 was actually reproduced in the meantime and actually fixed by the fix), `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean. `apps/user-ui`: `PreviewPane.test.tsx` extended to 9 tests, two `document-workspace.test.tsx` fixtures corrected (`engine: "native_text_layer"` → `"tesseract"`, since they would otherwise have represented an inconsistent state relative to the new engine switch) — **95 tests** (previously 93), `tsc`/`eslint` clean.
- **Live Docker verification**: a synthetic scan PDF (an image with text, no native text layer) really uploaded — the `tesseract` engine recognized the word, exactly one new version really came into being (`created_by="system:ocr-service"`), whose content really contained extractable text per `fitz`, visible pixels unchanged. The real malfunction that actually occurred on the first attempt (3 instead of 2 versions due to bug 2) was really re-verified after the fix — exactly 2 versions. An image upload was correctly skipped with the new default (`status="skipped"`, reason visible in the audit trail); after explicit admin opt-in via `PUT /config`, OCR really ran for it, with no new version created. `user-ui` rebuilt (`--no-cache`, since a regular build incorrectly served the changed layer from the cache) and restarted.
- `docs/services/ocr-service.md` (new "searchable PDF instead of a plain scan" section, configurability/backend-connection/tests/open-points sections updated), `docs/services/user-ui.md` (engine switch + image text extract added), `docs/services/document-service.md` (new note that `POST .../versions` is now also called by `ocr-service`) updated. `graphify update` skipped for this pass at user request.

**Immediately afterward a third, smaller user-feedback fix**: "with auth I currently get too-many-requests very quickly" — actually investigated instead of assumed. `auth-service` itself has no rate limiting, Keycloak's brute-force protection is configured nowhere, and neither `user-ui` nor `admin-ui` have a retry loop that would explain this (all three ruled out via research). Root cause: `gateway-service`'s `RateLimiter` (ADR 0005, in-process sliding window, key = the `sub` claim for authenticated requests, otherwise client IP) has had, unchanged since P4-S1, a default of **120 requests/60s** — too tight for this three-column SPA: a single page load already fires a good dozen parallel calls (folders, documents, favorites, approval configuration, reference-number config, object types, `auth-service:me`/`me/preferences`), plus `MaintenanceBanner`'s 30-second poll in both frontends — normal active clicking trivially exceeds 120/minute for a single logged-in user. Confirmed live in the gateway log: a real burst showed `429` simultaneously on `document-service`/`folder-service`/`object-type-service`/`permission-service`/`auth-service` routes — auth was just the most obvious spot where the user noticed it, not the actual cause. Fix: default raised to **600/60s** (`services/gateway-service/src/gateway_service/settings.py`, `infra/.env`/`.env.example`, `infra/docker-compose.yml`'s fallback) — significantly more room for real usage, while remaining a genuine safety net. `gateway-service` still **15 tests** green, `ruff` clean, live confirmed via over 150 rapid login requests in a row (previously this would long since have triggered `429` at 120, now consistently `401` for wrong test credentials, not a single `429`). `docs/services/gateway-service.md` updated (new section on the default raise with reasoning).

### Connectors & Migration (Phase 12, P12-S0)
- **P12-S0 was a pure research/planning session, no implementation code** — sixth `PN-S0` kickoff session after P7-S0/P8-S0/P9-S0/P10-S0/P11-S0, same convention.
- **No existing connector scaffolding**: a repo-wide search for "connector"/"CMIS"/"WebDAV" returned only prose references ("comes only in Phase 12") plus an incidental `"cmis-connector"` example string in `plugin-orchestration-service` tests (a generic `PluginManifest` placeholder, not a real component) — a clean starting point, no legacy baggage.
- **`registry-service` needs no schema change for a connector**: `RegisterRequest`/`InstanceOut` already have `service_type: str` (free, no enum), `capabilities: list[str]` (free), and `version: str` — the latter already generically satisfies concept 3.3's requirement for "versioning of the connector protocol ... via the API version in the registration handshake" for every service, not just connectors. A connector can register with the existing `libs/dms-registry-client` with no extension at all.
- **The `licensable_components` extension point from P9-S2 directly reusable**: lives in `registry-service/src/registry_service/settings.py` (`{"workflow-service": "demo"}`), not in `license-service` itself. A connector as a licensable component (concept 3.3: "e.g. CMIS connector as its own licensable component") only needs another dict entry + possibly the same dev-stack override as for `workflow-service` (empty `DMS_LICENSABLE_COMPONENTS` in `infra/docker-compose.yml`, so the bundled dev stack doesn't permanently end up in demo mode).
- **A real finding that affects P12-S2, not P12-S1**: `workflow-service` still has no plumbing at all for "an automatic/service task triggers a connector call" (concept 7.1) — automatic tasks so far run exclusively via SpiffWorkflow's built-in Python scripting, no `bpmn:serviceTask` support, no delegate/connector registration. The already-established pattern for new task subtypes (`signature`/`federated`/`federated_return` as `taskType` values on `bpmn:manualTask` via `camunda:properties`, see P6-S5/P6-S9) is the natural blueprint for a future `connector` `taskType`, but genuinely new work — relevant for **P12-S2** (migration/transfer, per 7.2, literally runs "itself as an auditable, resumable workflow via the Workflow Engine"), not for P12-S1 itself.
- **CMIS-vs-WebDAV reference-connector decision actually researched instead of assumed** (WebSearch/WebFetch against PyPI): `cmislib` — the only notable Python CMIS client (Apache Chemistry, Apache-2.0 licensed, no license conflict) — released its last version (0.7.0) in June 2022, before that a five-year gap since 0.6.0 (2017), and officially only declares Python 3.4–3.6 support (this project runs on 3.12) — real maintenance/compatibility risks, similar to the PaddleOCR situation from ADR 0011, only this time with no license problem, but an aging problem instead. WebDAV, protocol-wise, is only an HTTP extension (PROPFIND/MKCOL/COPY/MOVE/LOCK) rather than CMIS' full navigation/object/versioning/ACL service model with its own type definitions — fits the pattern already established in the project of "thin client instead of a heavy/stale library" (`gateway-service` already passes through arbitrary HTTP methods via `httpx`, `lxml` is already vendored via `archival-service`'s XDOMEA work). An actively maintained modern alternative found: `webdav4` (MIT, released February 2026).
- **Follow-up question at session start, user chose the recommendation with an extension**: WebDAV as the reference connector for P12-S1 (instead of CMIS) — **additionally** the user wanted CMIS not just deferred as an open point, but added as its own, new session **P12-S4** into the roadmap (CMIS connector as a second reference connector, using the same SDK built in P12-S1). `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` updated accordingly (P12-S1 refined, P12-S2 extended with the connector-call finding, new P12-S4 appended — no renumbering of existing sessions).
- A pure research/planning session, no code growth — `graphify update` deliberately not run (no implementation code changed, as with every previous `PN-S0` session).

### Connectors & Migration (Phase 12, P12-S1)
- **Direction decision before implementation**: concept 3.3/3.7/3.8/9/12 are ambiguous about whether a connector opens the DMS to external programs, or conversely taps into an external repository. Two concrete text passages clarify this: concept 4.2 ("if a user opens a document from within an application, e.g. Word via WebDAV/CMIS integration, for editing...") and ADR 0002 ("...with which the document is addressed anyway via external applications") — both describe the DMS as the system being addressed. `webdav-connector` accordingly implements a WebDAV **server**, not a client.
- **New shared lib `libs/dms-connector-sdk`** (concept 3.3 literally requires a reusable SDK, not just a connector): `DmsTreeClient` translates path/folder/document operations into `folder-service`/`document-service` HTTP calls, `ConnectorCapability`/`ConnectorDescriptor` map the capability description required by 3.3 (`read`/`write`/`metadata`/`locking`/`versioning`) — uses `dms-registry-client`'s already-existing free `capabilities: list[str]` field, no schema change needed (P12-S0 finding). **Deliberately synchronous** (`httpx.Client`, not `AsyncClient`): the first connector builds on `wsgidav`, whose `DAVProvider` interface is itself synchronous (WSGI) — an async variant would have needed an `asgiref.async_to_sync` bridge across several nested thread/loop boundaries, a known fragile pattern. A future FastAPI-based connector (CMIS, P12-S4) can still safely use the lib (FastAPI automatically runs normal `def` endpoints in its own thread pool).
- **New service `services/webdav-connector`** (port 8027, no own Postgres/NATS schema): `wsgidav` (MIT, an actively maintained reference implementation for Python WebDAV servers) instead of its own protocol layer, mounted under `/webdav` into a normal FastAPI app via `asgiref.wsgi.WsgiToAsgi` — the service stays externally consistent with the standard service template (`/healthz`, registry self-registration, license check). `DmsAuthDomainController` maps WebDAV basic auth onto `auth-service`'s existing `POST /login` — no second user store. WebDAV LOCK/UNLOCK runs directly via `document-service`'s existing lock endpoints (4.2) — no second, competing lock system. Licensing per the P9-S2 pattern: `registry-service.licensable_components` got `"webdav-connector": "demo"`.
- **A real, previously unknown bug found and fixed in `document-service`**: no way existed to move a document between folders (`folder-service`'s `PATCH /folders/{id}` has supported `parent_id` changes since P3-S3, the document counterpart was completely missing) — WebDAV MOVE is a mandatory method for documents (renaming **and** moving is standard usage in Explorer/Finder). `DocumentUpdate` got a new optional `folder_id` field, `PATCH /documents/{id}` moves on a deviation from the current value, with the same target-existence/object-hierarchy validation as on creation.
- **Three real implementation bugs found during live verification** (automated tests against mocks would have shown none of them — all three only showed up on the real WSGI/HTTP request path):
  1. **wsgidav `mount_path` missing**: `WsgiToAsgi` mounts the app under `/webdav`, but wsgidav itself knows nothing of this prefix and returned hrefs relative to its own root (`/Ordner-abc123/` instead of `/webdav/Ordner-abc123/`) — the `webdav4` test client (which determines resource names by stripping the mount prefix from the href) thereby received **mangled rather than erroring** names, a particularly insidious silent failure. Fix: a `"mount_path"` key in the `WsgiDAVApp` configuration dict.
  2. **`BytesIO.close()` before `end_write()`**: wsgidav's real `do_PUT` handler calls `fileobj.close()` before calling `res.end_write()` — a `getvalue()` there raised `ValueError: I/O operation on closed file` (→ HTTP 500). A direct Python reproduction with no real HTTP/WSGI path did not show the bug, since it never called `close()` — a textbook example of how a library-internals assumption ("the buffer stays open until I read it") would have gone undetected with no real end-to-end request test. Fix: `_CapturingBuffer` (a `BytesIO` subclass) that captures the content on `close()`.
  3. **File metadata incorrectly read from `DocumentOut` instead of `DocumentVersionOut`**: `document-service`'s `DocumentOut` carries no size/content-type/checksum — those live exclusively on the respective current `DocumentVersionOut`. `DmsTreeClient` originally read these fields with fallback defaults (`0`/`None`/`""`) from the wrong body — an empty string as an ETag caused wsgidav's own validation (`checked_etag`) to fail with HTTP 500 (only `None` or a non-empty string are valid). Fix: an additional `GET /documents/{id}/versions/{version_number}` call on every `TreeDocument` construction (`_fetch_current_version`) — an additional round trip per document in a directory listing, deliberately accepted for a reference implementation.
- **Tests**: `dms-connector-sdk` (12, really against running `folder-service`/`document-service` instances), `webdav-connector` (12, a real round trip via the `webdav4` test client: PROPFIND/PUT/GET/MKCOL/MOVE/LOCK/DELETE — no protocol mocking), `document-service` (169, including the three new move tests). Full backend regression (`scripts/run-tests.sh --build`, all 26 services) green, `ruff check`/`ruff format` clean.
- **Deliberate boundary**: real Windows Explorer/macOS Finder compatibility could not be tested in this environment (no GUI client available) — `wsgidav` is the engine actually used/tested for this in practice, a human should really mount it once before production use.
- **`docs/architecture.md` deliberately not touched**: the diagram explicitly documents itself as a frozen snapshot "as of the MVP milestone" up to P6-S9 and was not updated for any of Phases 7–11 (no `archival-service`/`license-service`/`monitoring-service`/etc. node) — a single new `WebdavConnector` node would be inconsistent with this document's stated scope.
- New docs: `docs/services/webdav-connector.md`, `libs/dms-connector-sdk/README.md`; updated: `docs/services/document-service.md` (`folder_id` move), `docs/services/registry-service.md` (new licensable component).
- **`graphify update` deliberately not yet run** — user request: only after P12-S4 (CMIS connector) completes, not after every individual Phase-12 sub-session.

### Connectors & Migration (Phase 12, P12-S2)
- **Direction clarification via follow-up question**: unlike 7.4 (Federation Hub), 7.2 names no mediating instance — user chose a **direct installation pair with an API key** (admin manually registers the target installation) instead of hub mediation, since 7.2 describes a one-off, standalone transfer, not ongoing federated operation (details/reasoning see ADR 0034).
- **Generic `connector_call` service-task plumbing in `workflow-service`** (the finding from P12-S0, but deliberately built not migration-specific): a `bpmn:serviceTask` with `camunda:properties` `taskType=connector_call`/`serviceUrl=...` is mapped, via SpiffWorkflow's own `OVERRIDE_PARSER_CLASSES` extension point, onto a new `ConnectorServiceTask` spec class that synchronously fires an HTTP `POST` to `serviceUrl` and merges the response back into the process data. `serviceUrl` supports `{placeholder}` substitution from process data (e.g. `{transfer_id}`). New, likewise generic endpoint `POST /instances/{id}/retry` (resumability via SpiffWorkflow's `reset_branch()`), plus a caller-determined `instance_id` (`ProcessInstanceCreate.instance_id`, same pattern as `federation-hub-service`'s `handover_id`, ADR 0028).
- **Three real SpiffWorkflow quirks found while building** (not apparent from documentation, clarified via direct source-code inspection + reproduction): `ServiceTask` is not registered in any default serializer configuration (serialization otherwise fails with "not JSON serializable"); `_execute()` must return `True` to actually complete the task (a falsy return value leaves it permanently stuck in `STARTED`); only `CamundaTaskParser` (not the base `TaskParser`) reads `camunda:properties` at all. Additionally really verified: a timer (`bpmn:intermediateCatchEvent`) can reference a process variable instead of a static ISO literal (`DurationTimerEventDefinition.has_fired()` evaluates via the script engine) — the basis for the configurable deletion period.
- **New service `services/migration-service`** (port 8028, own Postgres schema `migration`): lock (`permission-service`'s existing scope locks, 4.7, instead of an own locking system) → copy (recursive tree traversal via `dms-connector-sdk`, also transfers role assignments by role name rather than local `role_id`) → verify (checksum comparison) → release at the target system → deletion at the source system (`folder-service`'s existing cascading `POST /folders/{id}/trash`) after a transition period governed by a real BPMN timer. Dry run as its own, shorter second BPMN definition instead of a gateway in the main process. Four-eyes (4.3) exactly per the P6-S4 pattern (`ApprovalClient`/NATS consumer for `permission.approval.approved`). Licensing per the P9-S2 pattern (`"migration-service": "demo"`, concept 9.1 literally names Migration Service as an example).
- **Four real implementation bugs found and fixed during live verification** (as with P12-S1: automated unit tests against pure logic would have shown none of them):
  1. **Missing `session.commit()` in all five step endpoints**: every step only updated the `transfer` row via `session.flush()`, never `commit()` — `Depends(get_session)`'s context manager automatically rolls back a merely flushed transaction on close. The entire transfer thereby appeared to run through with no errors (every step returned 200), but **no** status change was actually persisted (the row stayed forever at `"pending"`) — found only by directly comparing workflow-service's instance timestamps (a difference of only ~80ms for the whole chain) against the unchanged `transfer` status.
  2. **Transaction isolation on self-loopback** (the same bug class already documented in ADR 0028 for `federation-hub-service`): the transfer was only flushed, not committed, before the first `connector_call` step synchronously fired back onto the same service — a new transaction/request did not yet see the row (`404`). Fix: an explicit commit right after creating the transfer, before starting the workflow.
  3. **A real deadlock on self-loopback**: `LocalDmsClient`/`PeerClient` are deliberately synchronous (like `dms-connector-sdk` itself) — a direct call in an `async def` endpoint blocks the entire event-loop thread. On self-loopback, the blocking call waits for a response from exactly the thread it itself is blocking and which would otherwise process the incoming request (`httpx.ReadTimeout`). Fix: `asyncio.to_thread()` for every `local`/`peer` call (offloading sync work from an async context — the unproblematic direction, unlike the `asgiref.async_to_sync` avoided in P12-S1).
  4. **Lost `workflow_instance_id` on an immediately failing first step**: if the very first automatic step already failed, `workflow_client.start_instance()` threw an exception **before** `set_workflow_instance()` was reached — the (actually really created in workflow-service) instance remained forever unaddressable, `POST /instances/{id}/retry` had no ID to address. Fix: `ProcessInstanceCreate.instance_id` (new, optional) lets the caller determine its own reference and persist it **before** the start (identical pattern to `federation-hub-service`'s `handover_id`, ADR 0028).
- **Additionally found for real**: `scope_lock.id` at `permission-service` is an autoincrement integer PK, not a UUID string — `asyncpg` rejected writing the unconverted int into a `String` column; a `_fail_transfer()` error path accessed a session attribute after the session was already in a `PendingRollbackError` state due to a prior flush failure (masking the real error message with a second exception) — both fixed.
- **Deliberate boundaries** (documented in `docs/services/migration-service.md` and ADR 0034): no historical timestamps for migrated versions (`document-service`'s check-in sets them server-side), `principal_id` remains opaque for copied permissions (no identity matching between the two installations' user populations), only the current document version is migrated (no full version history), migrated folders always land in the root of the target installation, `dry-run-check` only checks reachability/existence of the target folder (no full object-type/constraint compatibility analysis), self-loopback test instead of a real two-installation test (the same boundary already established for `federation-hub-service`).
- **Tests**: `workflow-service` (97, +7 vs. P12-S1: connector service-task mechanics, `serviceUrl` templating, `/retry` resumability, caller-determined `instance_id`, variable-based timer), `migration-service` (8, really against the running container including a self-loopback full run with `retention_days=0`, dry run, four-eyes path). Full backend regression (`scripts/run-tests.sh --build`, all 27 services including `migration-service`) green, `ruff check`/`ruff format` clean. Additionally verified live (beyond the automated test scope): a complete four-eyes flow (release request → approval by a second person → automatic start via the NATS consumer), retry against an actually unreachable target installation (transfer correctly remains at `"locked"`, no silent progress).
- New docs: `docs/services/migration-service.md`, `services/migration-service/README.md`, [ADR 0034](docs/adr/0034-migration-service-direct-pairing-and-generic-connector-service-tasks.md); updated: `docs/services/workflow-service.md` (new "connector service tasks" section), `docs/services/registry-service.md` (new licensable component), `libs/dms-connector-sdk/README.md` (new `get_folder`/`get_document` methods, second consumer).
- **`graphify update` still deliberately not run** — user request applies to the entire Phase 12, only after P12-S4 (CMIS connector).

### Connectors & Migration (Phase 12, P12-S3)
- **New service `services/config-service`** (port 8029, **no own Postgres schema** — a pure stateless orchestrator like `webdav-connector`): five real categories are read/written directly at their respective owner service — `object_types` (object-type-service, layouts only if `is_custom=true`, 2.2b — a computed smart-layout default is never exported), `workflows` (workflow-service, only the newest version per family — the target re-versions automatically on import, ADR 0027), `roles` (permission-service, role templates by name, not resource-bound `role_assignment` rows), `approval_config` (four-eyes configuration, 4.3), and `sensor_config` (monitoring-service, 10.1). "UI customizations"/AD-group mapping (literally named in 7.3) exist nowhere in the code and were deliberately **not** invented as fictitious, empty categories.
- **Upsert-by-natural-key instead of duplicating**: object types/roles are matched by `name` (already unique in their respective DB schema) — a repeated import (staging→production after another change) updates instead of duplicating. `approval_config`/`sensor_config` use their owner services' already-existing upsert endpoints directly, workflows always count as `created` (workflow-service re-versions automatically anyway). Every entry runs individually in a `try`/`except` — one faulty entry lands in `CategoryResult.errors`, but does not abort the whole import.
- **Resolves the backward-dependency finding from P11-S0**: `monitoring-service`'s sensor configuration had been deliberately persisted independently since P11-S1 ("configuration export only exists starting P12-S3") — `sensor_config` is now a regular export/import category like any other.
