- **A real, previously unknown gap found and fixed**: `permission-service` only supported `POST` (create) and `GET` (list) for roles, no update — even though comparable resources (folders, documents, object types) have long had their update counterpart. New `PUT /roles/{role_id}` endpoint (`name` remains immutable, the natural key for the configuration comparison) closes the gap, with two new tests (95 instead of 93 in `permission-service`).
- **Gating via an existing capability instead of a new domain-admin role**: `POST /config/import` requires `admin.object_config` (the same capability as `workflow-service`'s process-definition upload) — a full configuration import is an extension of the same responsibility. Since sensor-configuration writes additionally require `admin.monitoring` (P11-S1), `config-service` self-bootstraps **both** roles at startup (`domain-admin-config` + `domain-admin-monitoring`), identical idempotent self-assignment pattern to `migration-service`'s `_ensure_config_admin_permission()` (P12-S2).
- **Schema versioning as a prepared but deliberately empty extension point** (7.3: "versioning of the configuration schema itself"): `SCHEMA_VERSION = "1.0"` (so far the only version), `migrations.py` already defines the `MIGRATIONS` registry + `upgrade_to_current()` (cycle detection, `422` on an unknown version, operates on the raw dict BEFORE Pydantic validation) — deliberately **no** invented migration for a version that does not exist yet.
- Details/reasoning see [ADR 0035](docs/adr/0035-config-service-scope-and-upsert-semantics.md).
- **Verified live** (beyond the automated test scope) against the real running stack: `GET /config/export` returns real data for all five categories (2 object types including a real `is_custom` example, 317 workflow families — accumulated test data across the entire prior conversation, 30 roles including all `domain-admin-*` roles, 7 four-eyes configurations, correctly shaped sensor configuration); `POST /config/import` verified for 403 without/with the wrong principal, a successful import with `admin.object_config`, and upsert behavior (a second import of the same role updates instead of duplicating).
- **Tests**: `config-service` (10, really against the running container: export with/without a category filter, unknown category `422`, import without/with the wrong principal `403`, unsupported `schema_version` `422`, role upsert create→update, four-eyes config upsert, a full export→reimport round trip), `permission-service` (95, +2: `PUT /roles/{id}`). Full backend regression (`scripts/run-tests.sh --build`, all 28 services including `config-service`) green, `ruff check`/`ruff format` clean.
- New docs: `docs/services/config-service.md`, `services/config-service/README.md`, [ADR 0035](docs/adr/0035-config-service-scope-and-upsert-semantics.md); updated: `docs/services/permission-service.md` (new `PUT /roles/{id}` endpoint), `docs/services/monitoring-service.md` (7.3 integration documented, the old "no integration" limitation removed).
- **`graphify update` still deliberately not run** — user request applies to the entire Phase 12, only after P12-S4 (CMIS connector).

### Connectors & Migration (Phase 12, P12-S4) — closes Phase 12
- **Follow-up question at session start, blocking research done before any implementation decision**: unlike WebDAV (`wsgidav`, actively maintained), there is **no maintained Python CMIS *server* library** — all found Python CMIS packages (`cmislib`, `cmislib3`, `cmislib-maykin`, `CMIS.PythonLib`, `drc-cmis`) are pure client libraries, the one real server framework (Apache Chemistry's OpenCMIS) is Java-only. User chose the recommended full scope (**browser binding with versioning/checkout**) instead of a smaller "read/write/navigation only" scope. Details/reasoning see [ADR 0036](docs/adr/0036-cmis-connector-from-scratch-browser-binding.md).
- **New service `services/cmis-connector`** (port 8030, no own Postgres schema — a pure translator like `webdav-connector`, same `libs/dms-connector-sdk`): the CMIS 1.1 browser binding (chapter 5 of the real OASIS specification, read directly from the PDF instead of guessed from memory) hand-implemented, ~14 endpoints — `repositoryInfo`/`children`/`object`/`content` reading, `createDocument`/`createFolder`/`update`/`move`/`delete`/`deleteTree`/`setContent`/`checkOut`/`cancelCheckOut`/`checkIn` writing. Only succinct properties (5.2.11), no CMIS type system for own object-type attributes, no CMIS query (full-text search already runs via Postgres FTS per ADR 0012 anyway).
- **No separate PWC object on checkout/checkin/cancelCheckout**: `document-service` knows no "working copy" as its own entity — the real document lock (4.2) takes on exactly the role required by the CMIS PWC mechanism, the returned "PWC" ID is deliberately identical to the original document ID rather than an invented second ID.
- **Three real findings during live verification**:
  1. **URL query `objectId` initially ignored on POST actions**: the write routes initially only read `objectId` from the form, never from the query string (5.3.4 vs. 5.4.4.3.3) — `createDocument`/`createFolder` thereby always landed in the root folder, regardless of the target folder addressed via the URL. Actually uncovered by two failing tests (a supposedly non-empty folder could be deleted, a document cascade-deleted via `deleteTree` remained unchanged). Fix: both addressing paths are read, the form control takes precedence.
  2. **`folder-service`'s hard delete only checks subfolders, never documents** (which live in another service/schema) — never previously visible, since the only prior caller (`webdav-connector`) always first recursively deletes all children. CMIS' non-cascading `delete` action, however, MUST refuse for a document-holding folder — compensated in the connector (`_tree.list_children()` pre-check), not changed in `folder-service` itself.
  3. **A pure performance finding, not a code bug**: after the `--build` full regression run, `webdav-connector`'s test suite failed with `httpx.ReadTimeout` on a PROPFIND to the root. Cause: test data accumulated over the very long conversation runtime in the shared dev root folder (68 subfolders + 74 documents directly under `root`, from multiple connectors'/services' own, never-self-cleaning test runs) — `DmsTreeClient.list_children()`'s deliberately accepted additional HTTP call per document added up to over 10 seconds for a single root request. Fixed via a one-time cleanup (`POST /folders/{id}/trash`/`DELETE /documents/{id}` for all 142 root objects) instead of a code change — reduced the same request to 76ms.
- **Two small, backward-compatible extensions to `libs/dms-connector-sdk`**: `TreeFolder`/`TreeDocument` got `created_by`/`created_at` (long present in the underlying `FolderOut`/`DocumentOut` responses, but never carried over into the dataclasses) — for `TreeFolder` deliberately optional (`| None`), since the free local root shortcut construction in `resolve_path("")` (traversed on every WebDAV request to the root) does not know these values, without risking an additional network call on the hot path (`None` = CMIS' own "value not set" state, 5.2.7, not an invented value). `write_document()` got an optional `comment` argument (basis for `cmis:checkinComment`).
- **Tests**: `cmis-connector` (17, really against the running container including a `second_real_user` fixture for a real two-actor checkout-conflict test: `document-service`'s lock check compares `locked_by`, not `session_id`, the same actor can re-acquire their own lock at any time). Full backend regression (`scripts/run-tests.sh --build`, all 29 services including `cmis-connector`) green, `ruff check`/`ruff format` clean.
- New docs: `docs/services/cmis-connector.md`, `services/cmis-connector/README.md`, [ADR 0036](docs/adr/0036-cmis-connector-from-scratch-browser-binding.md); updated: `libs/dms-connector-sdk/README.md` (new fields/argument, third consumer), `docs/services/webdav-connector.md` (reference to the now-finished second connector), `registry-service`'s `licensable_components` (`"cmis-connector": "demo"`, concept 9.1 literally names CMIS connector as an example).
- **Phase 12 (Connectors & Migration) thereby fully completed.** `graphify update` now runs (user request: only after P12-S4, applied to the entire phase).

### Market Comparison (Phase 12b, P12b-S1)

- **Interim session, user request, a pure analysis/planning session** (no implementation code) — mandate: compare a reference-system documentation against the project's own code/concept status, document gaps/discrepancies, extend the roadmap with new sessions as needed. **Additional user request at session start**: "DMS should be at least as capable as the reference, and ideally more so, like uninterrupted updates" — divergence from the reference on individual points is explicitly fine, as long as the overall level stays at least equivalent.
- **Research via six parallel background agents** (Explore type), one per thematic component group of the reference documentation, with a clearly scoped mandate (functional scope, terminology, notable points on HA/updates/multi-instance) and a response-length cap, to keep results compact and comparable.
- **Most important single finding**: the reference documentation explicitly confirms that mixed version states of several server instances against a shared database schema are **not** supported — the operations manual requires always updating all installations working against one database simultaneously, with no expand/contract concept. This confirms, with a concrete text passage, what the project's own concept had already noted as a differentiator (10.5) — and this rolling-update mechanism is not only conceptually foreseen in this project's own system, but has actually been really built and live-verified since P10-S2/S3 (drain mechanism, canary switchover, 0 failed health checks in the test run).
- **Further confirmed unique selling points** (comparison table 12.1 refined accordingly, without changing the core statement): horizontal scaling (reference: shared-database clusters with no built-in load distribution, manual DNS/reverse-proxy assignment), storage redundancy (reference: manual failover in a backup-mirroring mode, no quorum-/config-driven write behavior), permission depth (reference administration tools: a single global admin right instead of a resource hierarchy + scope locks + domain-separated roles; the reference's "four-eyes principle" there is purely admin-config control, not a generic approval mechanism for arbitrary action types like here), monitoring (reference: its own sensor database + Nagios/JMX instead of Prometheus/Grafana), deletion model (reference: two-tier split across two components, no deletion-reason requirement), configuration delta between installations (reference: no built-in tool).
- **Real, confirmed opponent strength**: native Office integration (ribbon add-in for Word/Excel/PowerPoint/Outlook — open/save with no DMS client, workflow start from within the document, inline metadata, central template library, email archiving from Outlook) — in the project's own concept so far only covered via the generic WebDAV/CMIS connector (pure file editing, no ribbon/workflow access). Added as a new 12.2 candidate (see below), deliberately not pulled into the core scope (Windows-/COM-specific, significantly more effort than the other candidates).
- **New concept extension "fleet update orchestration"** (3a) as a direct implementation of the "ideally more so" user request: closes the gap that a fleet of many installations (reseller scenario) could so far only be updated individually/manually. Core ideas: staggered rollout across named waves/groups with include/exclude, a declarative update plan per installation, a **five-valued error decision** per step (`retry_later`/`wait_external`/`manual_required`/`recoverable_failed`/`fatal_contract`) instead of binary success/failure, automatic scope locks/emergency shutdown (4.7/4.8) as safe update windows per installation, reuse of the generic four-eyes mechanism (4.3) for manual approval gates instead of a new special mechanism, automatic backup before risky steps (reusing 10.4) with a plan-fingerprint check before restore. Extends (does not replace) the existing rolling-update mechanism (10.5) with a coordination layer for many installations at once.
- **Six new 12.2 extension candidates** (deliberately still not part of the core concept, only documented gaps with prioritization): native Office ribbon add-in, DMN decision tables alongside BPMN (SpiffWorkflow already brings its own DMN support — technically cheap to add), a persistent team workspace ("Teamspace", explicitly not a circulation-folder replacement, but a SharePoint-/Teams-channel-like permanent group area), extended full-text search query language (fuzzy/proximity search, e.g. addable via `pg_trgm`), regional business calendars for SLA deadline calculation (7.1). The previously already-existing candidates (ERP connectors, native mobile clients, AI functions) remain unchanged in the list, only repositioned.
- **§13 correction**: the old open point on 10.5 ("standalone orchestration logic vs. platform primitives, canary yes/no") is already answered for a single installation by the actual P10-S2/S3 implementation (an own script + registry drain instead of Kubernetes primitives, since only Docker Compose is actually present; canary works and is live-verified) — marked done, the remaining open point is only the new fleet-wide layer.
- **Deliberately not adopted / no gap**: PXML-like bulk document migration is structurally more primitive than the project's own migration service (7.2, which already brings checksums/four-eyes/transition period) — no catch-up needed. Vertical/industry-specific connectors (DATEV, an e-government-specific transfer format, generic app-scaffolding tools) were checked and deliberately not pursued further — too narrow/platform-specific for a generic DMS concept.
- Concept changes: `Konzept.md` §3a (new "fleet update orchestration"), §12.1 (comparison table refined, two new rows: "fleet-wide update orchestration", "Office integration"), §12.2 (six new candidates), §13 (10.5 point updated). `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`: P12b-S1 marked done, new session `P13-S2b` (fleet update orchestration) inserted, total session counter 81→82.
- **`graphify update` not part of this session** — a pure concept/planning session with no code change, the next regular run comes with the first actual implementation session (P13-S0/S1).

**Second feedback round from the user** (after first review of the 12.2 candidate list): five of the six new candidates should after all go into the binding core scope, not just remain as a documented, non-binding gap — plus an additional user question on domain-separated admin roles.
- **Domain-separated admin roles (user question, no concept change)**: user wanted the ability to sort admin tasks into different roles, with the system admin as the only full-access account. **Already fully present** (4.6, really implemented since P6-S5/P6-S6): seven predefined, functionally restricted admin roles (user/permission management, object-type/workflow configuration, storage/backend management, license management, query console, deletion administration, deletion administration classified documents) plus a disabled break-glass superuser with full access, reactivatable only via the four-eyes principle. Since these roles are ordinary RBAC roles, arbitrary further combinations can be formed via the existing generic role system (4.1) with no need to change the core mechanism — no concept gap, just noted for the record.
- **Office integration significantly expanded**: instead of a pure MS-Office add-in, now explicitly required **cross-platform** — additionally LibreOffice/OpenOffice support, so Linux/Mac are covered too (MS Office itself has no native Linux version). New concept section **3.3a**: two separate, equally treated extensions instead of a Windows-centric solution — a modern **Office add-in (Office.js)** instead of the older VSTO/COM technology for MS Office (Office.js runs web-based within Office on Windows/Mac/Web, VSTO would have structurally been limited to Windows), plus a **UNO API extension** (`.oxt`) for LibreOffice/OpenOffice (genuinely cross-platform, since both programs themselves run on Windows/Linux/Mac). Both talk to the same existing connector/API path (3.3) instead of their own document logic. Deliberate boundary: under Linux, only the LibreOffice/OpenOffice path covers the integration, since MS Office does not exist there — a limitation of the Office product itself, not a gap in this concept. Two new roadmap sessions (`P14-S8`/`S9`, one per extension) instead of one, since both technology stacks are entirely different.
- **DMN decision tables** anchored in 7.1: a Business Rule Task calls a named DMN 1.3 table, edited in the same Process Designer as BPMN, ex-/importable via the configuration export (7.3), built on SpiffWorkflow's own DMN support (no additional third-party dependency). New session `P14-S4`.
- **Team workspace ("Teamspace")** anchored as a new row in the special-areas table (2.5): a self-managed, permanent group area (folders/documents/appointments/contacts), clearly distinguished from the circulation folder (2.3, sequential routing), its own access regime independent of the rest of the RBAC model. New session `P14-S6`.
- **Extended full-text search** anchored as a new concept section **3.7a**: boolean combination with clear precedence rules, phrase search, wildcards, **fuzzy search** and **proximity search** supplementing the already-planned Postgres FTS boolean/phrase search (ADR 0012) — the fuzzy component via `pg_trgm`, deliberately no external search engine (Elasticsearch/Solr) as a mandatory dependency. New session `P14-S7`.
- **Regional business calendars**: user question on whether this should be made configurable to save development effort — **recommendation accepted**: deliberately implemented as a freely configurable, named data structure (a list of non-working days per calendar, multiple calendars possible in parallel, assignable to a process/an installation) instead of a hardcoded region selection (e.g. German federal states) — saves development effort for every individual jurisdiction and does not lock the system to one region; optional prebuilt calendar templates remain ordinary, still-changeable configuration data. Anchored in 7.1. New session `P14-S5`.
- **12.2 cleaned up accordingly**: the five points now moved into the core scope removed from the candidate list — only ERP/line-of-business connectors, native mobile clients, and AI functions remain as deliberately deferred candidates (P14-S3 unchanged responsible for preparing their extension point).
- Concept changes (second round): `Konzept.md` §3.3a (new, Office integration), §2.5 (new Teamspace row), §3.7a (new, full-text search), §7.1 (DMN paragraph + business-calendar paragraph in SLA time monitoring), §12.1 (two rows refined: Office integration now "explicitly provided for", the workflow row extended with DMN), §12.2 (reduced to three remaining candidates). `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`: six new sessions `P14-S4`–`S9` inserted, total session counter 82→88.
- **`graphify update` still not part of this session** — both rounds remain pure concept/planning work with no code change.

**Third round: in-depth completeness check** (user request: "then we should probably check again whether we actually achieve that", referring to the general principle "at least equivalent to the reference, better where possible" — not only referring to uninterrupted updates, as a roadmap wording in the second round had inadvertently suggested and was corrected in this round). All six research results from the first round were once again fully re-checked point by point against the *current* (already-expanded) concept status, instead of only looking at the initially picked-out highlights.
- **Three real, previously overlooked gaps found and adopted**:
  1. **Public share link** (4.2a, new) — time-limited, unauthenticated read access to a single document. Since unauthenticated access is a real security trade-off, this was specifically raised via follow-up question instead of decided autonomously — user decision: adopt it, but **globally fully disableable**, so that when disabled the feature is not merely blocked but not even offered in the UI (same principle as the already-existing COMPOSE_PROFILES opt-out for OCR).
  2. **Delegation during absence** (4.4a, new) — time-limited, scope-limited task takeover under the delegate's own account with an on-behalf-of note in the audit trail, deliberately no identity switch/"login as", so as not to dilute accountability.
  3. **Bulk editing of metadata** (8, new) — attribute multiple selected objects together in one step, via the same constraint check as a single change, with a clearly summarized success/failure result per object.
- **A deliberate boundary newly documented instead of silently left open**: no real-time co-authoring (4.2) — the lock model deliberately remains exclusive, since an operational-transform/CRDT infrastructure would be out of all proportion to the core purpose, and the reference does not build this itself either, but passes it through to an external cloud-office integration.
- **A false alarm defused**: encryption of data at rest initially seemed to be foreseen only for records disposal (5.6/7.4) — actually already generally recorded in 10.3 as a requirement ("encryption of data at rest and transport encryption (TLS) between all services"), no gap.
- **Checked and deliberately not adopted** (consistency with already-made exclusion decisions): pick lists/controlled vocabularies as a separate attribute type (the existing, freely extensible object-type attribute system already covers this, no special case needed), e-invoice rendering (as narrow/vertical as the already-excluded DATEV/GBA/XTA connectors), various smaller UI nuances of the reference (a collective label for duplicate display names, a batch editor for scan batches, TWAIN scanner integration, "send message"/a library of prebuilt process building blocks) — all already reachable via the existing generic architecture (connector principle, free BPMN modeling, constraint engine), with no own concept line needed.
- Roadmap correction: the `P13-S2b` description worded the user's wish too narrowly when first written ("DMS should be at least equivalent **for uninterrupted updates**") — corrected to the actually general principle ("DMS should be at least equivalent, better where possible, **against the market reference generally** — here concretely applied to updates"), at both occurrences in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`.
- Concept changes (third round): `Konzept.md` §4.2 (new deliberate boundary: no real-time co-authoring), §4.2a (new, share link), §4.4a (new, delegation), §8 (bulk editing added). `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`: three new sessions `P14-S10`–`S12`, total session counter 88→91, `P13-S2b` wording corrected.
- **`graphify update` still not part of this session** — all three rounds remain pure concept/planning work with no code change.

### Multiple Installations & Federation (Phase 13, P13-S0)

- **Phase kickoff, no implementation code** — Task: verify concept sections 3a (multiple installations/fleet update orchestration) and 7.4 (federation hub) against the actual code/service state, adjust the P13-S1–S4/S2b session breakdown if needed. **Additional user request at session start**: a new Phase 17 "Preconfigured configuration packages" (concept §14, new) placed at the end of the roadmap, including an eGov configuration package, as well as a new standing rule — run `graphify update` only at the end of the entire Phase 13, not after each individual session as had previously been customary.
- **3a verification**: no service in the repository has an `installation_id`/fleet concept so far — `license-service` still checks licenses purely on a per-single-installation basis (9.1/9.2, unchanged since P9-S1/S2), no fleet/license management service exists. **P13-S1 (isolation/installation ID) and P13-S2/S2b (fleet/license management, fleet update orchestration) are thus fully open as planned** — no anticipation found through already-existing code, no adjustment to the session breakdown needed.
- **7.4 verification against `federation-hub-service` (P6-S9)**: confirms that the foundation already built there fully satisfies the concept description — the `Installation` model already carries `version`/`min_compatible_peer_version` (version compatibility range, 7.4) as well as `supported_process_types`/`supported_document_types` (opt-in per process/document type); per `crypto_utils.py`, the hub itself demonstrably never holds a decryption key (only its own signature keypair for delivery proofs). The actual end-to-end encryption, as already decided at P6-S9, lies entirely in `workflow_service.federation_crypto` (a genuine hybrid envelope scheme: a fresh AES-256-GCM key per message, RSA-OAEP-encrypted with the target installation's public key) — **confirmed to be genuinely implemented, not a dummy**.
- **P13-S4 scope thereby finally confirmed** (previously only an analytical assumption of this conversation, now verified against the code): the actual end-to-end encryption is complete, P13-S4 remains reduced, as described in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`, to hardening the deliberately simple trust model (mTLS instead of pure API-key checking, key rotation/revocation, real installation identity instead of a self-chosen `installation_id` — the latter builds on the installation-ID foundation still to be created in P13-S1).
- **P13-S3 scope unchanged**: since the fleet/installation-ID foundations from P13-S1/S2/S2b do not yet exist at the time of this kickoff, the review task noted there ("adjustment to the then-existing foundations, if gaps appear") cannot yet be conclusively answered — remains, as planned, a review point at the very start of P13-S3 itself, no meaningful anticipation possible.
- **No contradiction found between the concept text and the code state** — neither 3a nor 7.4 needed to be substantively adjusted, pure confirmation.
- Concept changes: `Konzept.md` new §14 "Preconfigured configuration packages" (14.1 basic principle, 14.2 eGov configuration package, 14.3 further conceivable packages, not part of this version) — deliberately placed as the last content addition, since a package presupposes that all the building blocks it is composed of already exist. `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`: new "Phase 17 — Preconfigured configuration packages" with four sessions `P17-S0`–`S3`, total session counter 91→95.
- **`graphify update`: new standing rule for the entire Phase 13** — will not be run after each individual session (P13-S0/S1/S2/S2b/S3/S4), but only once at the very end of the phase, after completion of the last Phase-13 session.

### Multiple Installations & Federation (Phase 13, P13-S1)

- **Task**: "Multiple-installation foundations: isolation, installation ID (3a)" — the least specified roadmap entry of the entire phase, deliberately left open for design decisions as the first genuine implementation session (P13-S0 found no reason for advance clarification).
- **Actual finding before implementation**: across the entire repository there was so far **no single shared installation identity** — three completely independent, never-connected "identifiers" ran side by side: (1) `workflow-service`'s federation identity (random `uuid.uuid4()`, only for the hub, 7.4), (2) `migration-service`'s `paired_installation` entries (foreign identifiers of other installations, no concept for its own), (3) `license-service`, which — despite ADR 0032 explicitly naming `installation_id` as a possible JWT claim — had **never actually checked it** (documented in its own "Open Points" section since P9-S0). Only `workflow-service` even knew its own, isolatedly configured `installation_display_name` — no other service did.
- **Decision: a single shared value instead of a new service** — `installation_id`/`installation_display_name` move into `dms_common.BaseServiceSettings` (new `DMS_INSTALLATION_ID`/`DMS_INSTALLATION_DISPLAY_NAME` environment variables, a single value set once for the whole installation in `infra/.env`/`docker-compose.yml`, passed through to all 29 Python services) instead of a new, DB-backed identity singleton (such as `HubIdentity`/`InternalCa`) — significantly simpler, no new moving component, matches 3a's own wording ("Each installation registers itself ... with its own installation ID" — a property of the installation as a whole, not something a single service invents for itself).
- **`registry-service` gets `GET /installation`** (`{id, display_name}`, ungated, no DB access — pure configuration information) as the sole, HTTP-queryable information point for tools/admin UI/the future fleet management layer (P13-S2), analogous to `federation-hub-service`'s `/public-key`.
- **`license-service` ties license checking to this field** (`usage.compute_usage()` got a new `local_installation_id` parameter): if `installation_id` is set in the license token, it must match the locally configured `installation_id` exactly, otherwise the license is deemed invalid (`invalid_reason="License was issued for a different installation"`) — prevents an unaltered copy of a license file issued for installation A, whose signature remains valid, from being placed on installation B. **Backward compatible**: if the claim is missing (older license files/test fixtures), nothing is checked, no forced reissue needed. `license-service` needs no network call to `registry-service` for this — `installation_id` is already available locally via the same `BaseServiceSettings` base.
- **`workflow-service`'s own, service-specific `installation_display_name` setting removed** — now uses the shared `settings.installation_display_name` when reporting to the Federation Hub. The randomly generated hub `installation_id` itself remains **deliberately independent** of the new fleet/license `installation_id`: federation partners (7.4, opt-in, potentially foreign organizations) should not automatically learn an installation's internal fleet identity just because they participate in a federated process — two separate identifiers for two different circles of trust, no accidental identity linkage.
- **Isolation (3a, first bullet list) verified, not newly built**: a dedicated DB/event bus/registry instance/storage target set per installation is already structurally given by the Docker Compose architecture (one complete, independent stack per installation) — no service code assumes foreign access across installation boundaries. No additional safeguard built (e.g. a runtime check against accidentally misconfigured registry/DB URLs), since 3a does not require this and it would be a pure operator configuration risk, not a code gap.
- **Mechanical but necessary Docker Compose change**: `DMS_INSTALLATION_ID`/`DMS_INSTALLATION_DISPLAY_NAME` had to be added to all 29 service `environment` blocks (no `env_file` passthrough in this Compose setup — every service lists its `DMS_*` variables individually) — without this, an operator could have set the new variables in `.env` without any effect (a silent dud). A real merge conflict was found and fixed in the process: `workflow-service` already had its own `DMS_INSTALLATION_DISPLAY_NAME: ${WORKFLOW_SERVICE_INSTALLATION_DISPLAY_NAME:-DMS-Dev-Installation}` line (the old, now-removed service-own setting) — undetected, this would have led to a duplicate YAML key (Docker Compose rejects this hard, `docker compose config` aborts with "mapping key already defined"), found via a duplicate-key check against the real YAML parser before the test run, not only at live startup.
- **Verified live**: `curl http://localhost:8001/installation` returns the default (`{"id":"local-dev","display_name":"DMS Installation (Development)"}`), `docker compose exec workflow-service printenv` confirms the variables actually arriving in the container, `GET /federation/installations` on the running `workflow-service` shows the new, shared `display_name` in the installation row updated at the hub after a restart.
- **Tests**: `libs/dms-common` (+2, shared defaults/env override), `registry-service` (+1, `GET /installation`), `license-service` (+6: `test_usage.py` missing/matching/differing claim, `test_poll_loop.py` edge detection for the new invalidity reason, `test_api.py` end-to-end upload). Full backend regression (`scripts/run-tests.sh --build`, all 29 services) green, `ruff check`/`ruff format` clean.
- Concept changes: none — 3a/7.4 were already accurate, this session merely followed up on the still-missing implementation. Documentation changes: `docs/services/registry-service.md` (new endpoint + section), `docs/services/license-service.md` (installation binding, open-point line closed), `docs/services/workflow-service.md` (settings consolidation), `libs/dms-common/README.md`, [ADR 0032](docs/adr/0032-lizenzdatei-signaturverfahren.md) (addendum instead of a new ADR number — direct continuation of the same decision already named there).

### Multiple Installations & Federation (Phase 13, P13-S2)

- **Task**: "Fleet/license management service (superordinate management layer)" — concept 3a names three specific capabilities: license issuance/renewal, basic health overview, central provisioning of new installations from a configuration template, explicitly without access to document contents.
- **Architecture decision: new, installation-independent service** `fleet-management-service` (port 8031, own Postgres schema `fleet`) — not part of a single installation, same pattern as `federation-hub-service` (ADR 0028): an operator with multiple installations deploys it separately; it is only included in the bundled dev/test stack for convenience.
- **Central design question: how does a service external to an installation authenticate against an installation?** RBAC/Keycloak is structurally ruled out (each installation has its own, independent realm, 3a: "fully isolated"). Chosen solution: a new, installation-wide, optional `DMS_FLEET_AGENT_API_KEY` (`dms_common.BaseServiceSettings`, a direct continuation of the P13-S1 pattern) — `license-service`'s `POST /license` and `config-service`'s `POST /config/import` additionally accept, alongside the existing RBAC path, an `Authorization: Bearer <key>` bypass (`_is_fleet_agent()`). The same API-key-instead-of-RBAC line as `federation-hub-service`/`migration-service` (ADR 0028/0034) — the third occurrence of the same, already established pattern, no new mechanism. Details/rationale see [ADR 0037](docs/adr/0037-fleet-management-service-agent-key-and-gateway-public-routes.md).
- **`gateway-service.settings.public_routes` extended by four entries** (`registry-service:installation`, `license-service:license/status`, `license-service:license`, `config-service:config/import`) — without this, the gateway itself would already require a Keycloak bearer token, which an external fleet service can structurally never have. Confirmed in the process: the gateway passes an `Authorization` header already present on the original request through unchanged all the way to the downstream on a public route (`filter_headers()` only removes hop-by-hop headers) — the fleet agent key thus arrives unchanged at the respective target service, without the gateway itself needing to know it.
- **Reaches every managed installation exclusively via its gateway** (never an internal container address, same principle as `workflow-service`'s `callback_base_url` to the Federation Hub) — and exclusively `registry-service`/`license-service`/`config-service`, never `document-service`/`folder-service` (3a verbatim: "no access to document contents").
- **`fleet_agent_api_key` stored in plaintext**, not hashed — `fleet-management-service` must present it on every outgoing call, never verify it itself (identical rationale to `migration-service`'s `PairedInstallation`, ADR 0034 — already extensively documented there, directly reused here rather than re-derived).
- **`POST /installations/{id}/provision` is a pure pass-through**, not a template library — accepts a raw 7.3 configuration document (e.g. the export of a reference installation). A curated package library is deliberately not part of this session, that is concept §14/Phase 17 ("Preconfigured configuration packages") — this session only lays the technical transmission path that Phase 17 can later populate with real, maintained packages.
- **`GET /installations/status` parallelized** (`asyncio.gather`) over all managed installations, each individual poll catches network/auth errors itself (`reachable=false` instead of an exception) — a single unreachable installation does not delay the overview of the others.
- **Verified live against the running stack (self-loopback)**: `fleet-management-service` registered as a `managed_installation` on the same stack's own `gateway-service` — `GET .../status` returned the real installation identity assigned via P13-S1 as well as the real license status already installed from an earlier test run (real user counts/storage/document counts); `POST .../provision` with a minimal configuration document actually ran through `config-service`'s import path.
- **Tests**: `fleet-management-service` (new, 13 tests, in-process `TestClient` against an ASGI stub of the target installation, same pattern as `federation-hub-service`), `license-service` (+2: fleet-key bypass valid/wrong), `config-service` (+2, against the real running container like all tests there), `gateway-service` (+1, all four new routes reachable without a bearer token), `libs/dms-common` (+1, `fleet_agent_api_key` default/override). Full backend regression (`scripts/run-tests.sh --build`, now 30 services) green, `ruff check`/`ruff format` clean.
- **The same root test-residue effect as at P12-S4 recurred, not a new bug**: this session's live verification (self-loopback, plus all previous P13-S0/S1 live checks) had again accumulated test data at the root folder (60 documents + 59 folders) — `webdav-connector`'s root PROPFIND therefore again took over 7s instead of milliseconds (`DmsTreeClient.list_children()`'s known extra HTTP call per document, see P12-S4). Same remedy as back then: root objects cleaned up (`DELETE /documents/{id}?deleted_by=...`, `POST /folders/{id}/trash` with a `deleted_by` body field), root PROPFIND back at 30ms, full regression green again.
- Concept changes: none — 3a was already accurate. Docs: new `docs/services/fleet-management-service.md`, `services/fleet-management-service/README.md`, [ADR 0037](docs/adr/0037-fleet-management-service-agent-key-and-gateway-public-routes.md); updated: `docs/services/license-service.md`/`config-service.md` (new bypass path in the API table), `docs/services/gateway-service.md` (four new `public_routes`, `Authorization` passthrough behavior documented), `docs/services/registry-service.md` (actual use by the now-existing fleet service noted).

### Multiple Installations & Federation (Phase 13, P13-S2b)

- **Task**: "Fleet update orchestration (3a extension)" — concept 3a explicitly names "as an extension of the fleet/license management service already envisaged", i.e. an extension of `fleet-management-service` (P13-S2), not a new service.
- **Central design assumption, corrected during the session**: originally planned to implement "secure update windows" (4.7/4.8) via real, remotely triggered HTTP calls from `fleet-management-service` into `permission-service`'s `POST /scope-locks`/`POST /maintenance-mode/trigger` (analogous to the P13-S2 bypass for `license-service`/`config-service`). Two real obstacles were found during the concrete implementation, before any code was committed: (1) `create_scope_lock`/`release_scope_lock` have **no** RBAC gate at all, only the implicit protection "you must already possess a valid Keycloak token of this installation" — `migration-service` also already calls them today directly and without any header; a new gate would have broken this existing, working internal call. (2) `maintenance-mode/trigger` requires a specific capability, `maintenance-mode/lift` literally requires the identity of the **currently active superuser** of that one installation — remote control would have had to either introduce a new, previously nonexistent RBAC special right into `permission-service`, or bypass its deliberately strict superuser binding. Both are independent, security-relevant fundamental decisions that this session should not have made in passing — see [ADR 0038](docs/adr/0038-fleet-update-orchestration-external-gates-not-remote-control.md) for the full derivation.
- **Corrected approach**: plan steps have exactly two types — `"verify"` (automatic, queries the already-existing `_fetch_status()` path from P13-S2) and `"gate"` (confirmed externally/manually via `POST .../mark-done` — stands for setting a scope lock/maintenance mode, performing the rolling update (10.5), taking a backup (10.4), final release). `fleet-management-service` does not trigger these actions itself, only coordinates order/timing and tracks the result — matches 3a's own wording "coordination layer ... that specifically triggers 10.5", interpreted as "determining timing/order", not as a remote-control claim. This also makes the model uniform: 10.4/10.5 were never remotely controllable anyway, owing to their script-rather-than-service nature (P10-S3/P11-S3) — the same mechanics now apply to all four "preparation/update/backup/release" step types.
- **Five-valued error decision implemented verbatim** as `InstallationRun.status`: `retry_later`/`wait_external`/`manual_required`/`recoverable_failed`/`fatal_contract`, plus the two structural states `pending`/`completed`. `mark-done` lets the reporting operator choose between `success` and the three reportable error classes (not `wait_external`/`manual_required` — these are structural states, not an externally reportable outcome). `verify` automatically evaluates `_fetch_status()`: unreachable → `retry_later`, reachable but license status invalid → `recoverable_failed`, otherwise success.
- **Four-eyes principle (4.3) as a structural rather than cryptographic principle at the fleet level**: since `fleet-management-service` (like `migration-service`'s own admin endpoints already) does not maintain its own user management, there is no reuse of `permission-service`'s real NATS consumer approval mechanism (which would be per-installation isolated anyway, not centrally subscribable — the event bus is explicitly one of the four isolated resources in 3a). Instead: a `requires_approval` step transitions to `manual_required` after `mark-done` (`proposed_by` stored), `approve` enforces `actor != proposed_by` as a structural (not cryptographic) separation of proposal and approval.
- **Staggered waves without a new "campaign" super-type**: a `Rollout` is already the smallest, independent, explicitly started unit (a plan applied to a resolved set of installations) — "no automatic chain reaction to the next wave" (3a) is thereby trivially satisfied, without needing to model a superordinate multi-wave campaign: the next wave is simply a second, separately created `Rollout`.
- **Tests**: `fleet-management-service` (+13, now 26: group membership, plan validation, rollout resolution including an empty target set, complete happy path across all three steps of a sample plan including four-eyes rejection by the same person, `recoverable_failed`→`retry`, `fatal_contract`→`acknowledge-fatal`, `verify` for an unreachable installation, rejection of an approval). A real test failure on repeated execution was found and fixed: group names without a UUID suffix violated `installation_group`'s unique constraint on a second test run against the same (not cleaned between runs) dev database — fixed with unique test names (same pattern already common elsewhere in the project). Full backend regression (`scripts/run-tests.sh --build`, still 30 services) green, `ruff check`/`ruff format` clean.
- **The same root test-residue effect observed and fixed a third time** (see P12-S4/P13-S2) — cleaned up again after this session (29 documents + 29 folders), root PROPFIND back at 30ms.
- Concept changes: none — 3a was already accurate, including for the "external gates instead of remote control" decision now made (3a describes only the coordination goal, not the mechanism). Docs: new [ADR 0038](docs/adr/0038-fleet-update-orchestration-external-gates-not-remote-control.md), `docs/services/fleet-management-service.md` (new section + data model + API table + three new "Open Points"), `services/fleet-management-service/README.md`.

### Multiple Installations & Federation (Phase 13, P13-S3)

- **Task**: "Adaptation to the then-existing multi-installation/fleet foundations from P13-S1/S2/S2b (extend contacts/version compatibility as needed), if gaps show up since P6-S9" — a review session, not a scope fixed in advance.
- **Contacts reviewed, no gap**: the obvious question of whether `federation-hub-service`'s installation identifier should now be unified with the new fleet/license `installation_id` (P13-S1) had already been deliberately answered "no" at P13-S1/ADR 0037 (separate circles of trust, federation partners should not automatically learn the internal fleet identity) — reconfirmed, no change needed.
- **Real finding regarding version compatibility**: concept 7.4 literally requires that the compatibility range be "part of the already-versioned configuration schema (7.3)". Before this session it lived exclusively in `workflow-service`'s `Settings` (`installation_version`/`installation_min_compatible_peer_version`) — changeable only via a container restart, never reachable through the regular configuration import. Closed following the same pattern as the P11-S0 finding for `sensor_config` (P12-S3): new `FederationConfig` DB row in `workflow-service` (seeded from `Settings` defaults, backward compatible), `GET`/`PUT /federation/config` (`PUT` gated behind `admin.object_config`, immediately triggers a re-registration at the hub — no more restart needed), new `config-service` category `federation_config`.
- **Real crash bug found, not just a documentation gap**: while writing a test case for the new `PUT /federation/config`, it turned out that `federation-hub-service`'s `_parse_version()` accepted any `version` string without complaint — a test value with a hex-like suffix (`f"9.{uuid4().hex[:6]}"`) was stored without complaint at registration, but crashed **a completely different, later** `POST /handovers` mediation (in an entirely different test) with an unhandled `ValueError` (`500`) — an error far removed from its actual cause. Fixed by a new, shared `version_utils.parse_version()` (used by BOTH `schemas.py` AND `repository.py`, no duplicate implementation) plus a Pydantic `field_validator` on `InstallationRegister` that already rejects the format with `422` at `POST /installations`. Two new regression tests in `federation-hub-service` now cover this.
- **Cleanup of the legacy data thereby made visible**: the bug (and several test runs before it was fixed) had polluted the real, persistent address book table of the running `federation-hub-service` container with over 60 never-cleaned `test-install-*` rows plus two corrupted version values for `workflow-service`'s own identity (among other things a visible symptom of a longer-known but never-before-acute open point: "No cleanup of old rows — grows unbounded"). Cleaned up directly via SQL (rows deleted/corrected) — a pure dev/test artifact cleanup, no production data affected.
- **Tests**: `workflow-service` (+3, now 100: `GET`/`PUT /federation/config`, admin gate, roundtrip with restoration of the prior state), `federation-hub-service` (+2, now 23: `422` for non-numeric `version`/`min_compatible_peer_version`), `config-service` (+3, now 13: new category in export default/filter, real import affects the running `workflow-service` container). Full backend regression (`scripts/run-tests.sh --build`, still 30 services) green, `ruff check`/`ruff format` clean.
- Concept changes: none — 7.4 was already accurate, this session merely followed up on the still-missing implementation. Docs: [ADR 0028](docs/adr/0028-federation-hub-trust-and-encryption-model.md) addendum, `docs/services/workflow-service.md`/`federation-hub-service.md`/`config-service.md` updated.

### Multiple Installations & Federation (Phase 13, P13-S4)

- **Task**: last session of Phase 13 — hardening the trust model deliberately kept simple at P6-S9, based on the installation-ID/PKI foundations from P13-S1: mTLS instead of pure API-key authentication, key rotation/revocation for the federated installation key pairs, real installation identity instead of a self-chosen `installation_id`.
- **Real mTLS deliberately discarded, a cryptographically equivalent mechanism built instead**: TLS/mTLS is throughout the rest of the system consistently a pure deployment matter (concept 10.3) — not a single other service in this project terminates TLS itself or verifies client certificates. An isolated real mTLS just for the Federation Hub would have introduced an entirely new certificate-management discipline that exists nowhere else, with no reuse value for the rest of the system. Instead, every write-bound installation→hub request is now signed with the same RSA-2048 key pair (RSA-PSS/SHA-256, `X-Installation-Signature`) that already exists for the end-to-end encryption (ADR 0028) — `sign_body`/`verify_body` already existed for the reverse direction (hub→installation, since P6-S9), this session merely completes the reverse direction already envisaged in the docstring since then. Full rationale: [ADR 0039](docs/adr/0039-federation-trust-hardening-request-signing-over-mtls.md).
- **Real weakness of the old `api_key` model fixed**: an `api_key` is a pure, copyable secret — whoever knows it (e.g. from a log) can fully impersonate the affected installation. A signature, by contrast, proves actual possession of the private key on every single request. The `api_key`/`api_key_hash` column was completely removed (via `ALTER TABLE ... DROP COLUMN IF EXISTS`, no transition period) — a deliberate, ADR-documented exception to the otherwise usual expand-only pattern, justifiable given the early development stage without productive federation participants outside this repo.
- **Self-consistency check on new registration**: `register_or_update_installation()` requires, for a new `id`, that the signature matches the `public_key_pem` submitted in the same request — prevents someone from depositing a foreign/fabricated public key under the name of a new installation without possessing its private counterpart.
- **Key rotation only via a dedicated, narrower path**: a regular re-registration deliberately no longer overwrites `public_key_pem` (a differing value in the payload is ignored) — only the new `POST /installations/{id}/rotate-key` (signed with the still-current key, a real proof of continuity) may change the cryptographic identity. Separates harmless metadata updates (version, callback URL) from security-relevant identity changes. `workflow-service` got a new, gated endpoint for this, `POST /federation/rotate-key` (same `admin.object_config` capability as `PUT /federation/config`).
- **Revocation deliberately NOT tied to the signature of the affected installation**: the purpose of revocation is precisely the case in which an installation can no longer sign trustworthily (compromised private key) — a self-revocation requirement would be useless in exactly this case. New `POST /installations/{id}/revoke`, gated via a separate, independent operator secret `DMS_HUB_OPERATOR_KEY` (`None` by default fully locks the endpoint) — same pattern as `DMS_FLEET_AGENT_API_KEY` from P13-S2/ADR 0037.
- **"Real installation identity" put into context**: the `installation_id` string itself deliberately remains self-chosen (7.4 does not require a globally unique, externally attested identifier, only uniqueness within the respective hub address book) — what changes is that the authenticity behind this identifier is now cryptographically anchored instead of hanging on a copyable string. An externally attested PKI identity would be a significantly larger, independent topic complex and not what 3a/7.4 actually require.
- **Real bug found in its own new code, proactively fixed**: converting several endpoints from automatic FastAPI body deserialization to manual `request.body()` + `Model.model_validate_json(body)` (necessary to keep the raw bytes unchanged for the signature check) would NOT automatically convert a manually raised `pydantic.ValidationError` into `422` (FastAPI only catches its own `RequestValidationError` from automatic parameter binding) — recognized before the first test run and fixed via a `_parse_body()` helper in `main.py`.
- **Tests**: `federation-hub-service` **35 tests** (previously 23, +12: self-consistency on new registration, update requires matching signature, re-registration ignores a changed key, deregistration/rotation/revocation each with signature check, revocation gate with/without/wrong operator key, a revoked target is rejected on `POST /handovers`), `workflow-service` **102 tests** (previously 100, +2: rotation without admin capability → `403`, a real round trip against the live running hub confirming that only its own installation changes its own key). Full backend regression (`scripts/run-tests.sh --build`, then a second run without `--build` to confirm idempotency, still 30 services) green twice, `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean.
- Concept changes: none — 7.4 was already accurate, this session merely followed up on the still-open hardening. Docs: [ADR 0039](docs/adr/0039-federation-trust-hardening-request-signing-over-mtls.md) (new), `docs/services/federation-hub-service.md`/`workflow-service.md` updated.
- **Phase 13 thereby fully completed** (P13-S0–S4). `graphify update` was run once for the entire phase directly following this session (standing rule since P13-S0: deferred until the end of the last Phase-13 session) — graph afterward at 8,129 nodes/14,842 edges in 676 communities, health check unremarkable.

### Comparison & Extension Preparation (Phase 14, P14-S0)

- **Phase kickoff, no implementation code** — Task: verify concept sections 3.3a (office integration), 2.5 (teamspace), 3.7a (full-text search query language), 7.1 (DMN decision tables + regional business calendars), 7.5 (delta/comparison function between installations), 8 (UI layer: reviewer/approval UI + migration console, bulk editing) and 12.2 (remaining extension candidates) against the actual code/service state, adjust the P14-S1–S12 session breakdown if needed.
- **Real finding regarding 7.1/DMN (P14-S4)**: `workflow-service` has used `SpiffWorkflow.camunda.parser.CamundaParser` since P6-S7 for the BPMN extension properties (signature task, federation, connector service tasks) — verified against the installed package (`CamundaParser.__mro__`) that this class itself is already a subclass of `SpiffWorkflow.dmn.parser.BpmnDmnParser`, and `businessRuleTask` is already a registered entry in `CamundaParser.OVERRIDE_PARSER_CLASSES`. The parsing/execution infrastructure required for 7.1's DMN requirement (`DMNEngine`, business rule task) is thereby **already present** via the already-made library choice, not something that still needs to be retrofitted with a new third-party component — lowers the expected implementation effort of P14-S4 essentially to: DMN XML storage alongside BPMN XML in process definition versioning (ADR 0027), wiring of `add_dmn_xml`/`DMNEngine` into `spiff_adapter.py`, as well as export/import via 7.3 (analogous to the already-existing `workflows` category pattern in `config-service`). No adjustment to the session breakdown needed, just an effort estimate.
- **Business calendars (P14-S5) deliberately NOT a SpiffWorkflow built-in**: `SpiffWorkflow.bpmn.specs.event_definitions` only offers pure duration/point-in-time timers (`DurationTimerEventDefinition`/`TimeDateEventDefinition`/`CycleTimerEventDefinition`, in use since P6-S2), no business-calendar/working-days concept whatsoever — confirms that 7.1's "freely configurable data structure" actually requires entirely new, own logic in `workflow-service` (its own calendar configuration table, working-day arithmetic wired in everywhere SLA deadlines are currently computed from pure duration), no library shortcut available.
- **3.7a verification against `search-service`**: confirms that Boolean combination/phrase search are already really implemented (`repository.py` uses Postgres' `websearch_to_tsquery` throughout, ADR 0012) — the only actually remaining open item is fuzzy/proximity search via `pg_trgm`, exactly what P14-S7 already envisages. No gap, no adjustment needed.
- **7.5 verification against `config-service`**: confirms that exactly **six real** export/import categories exist (`object_types`, `workflows`, `roles`, `approval_config`, `sensor_config`, `federation_config`, since P9-S0/P13-S3) — 7.5's example list additionally names "AD group mapping rules" and "UI customizations", which **do not exist** as a configuration category: the AD-group→role mapping from 4.4 was already deliberately not implemented at P2-S1/P2-S2 (still an open point, see "Authorization" above), UI customizations were never built as their own real category (`config-service`, per its own P9-S0 documentation, deliberately does not invent fictional empty categories for it). **Clarification for P14-S1**: the delta/comparison function compares the six actually existing categories, not 7.5's full example list — no concept change needed, 7.5 deliberately describes the comparison scope as "all comparable categories", not as a fixed enumeration.
- **8 verification (reviewer/approval UI + migration console, P14-S2)**: confirms that currently only `apps/admin-ui`, `apps/process-designer`, and `apps/user-ui` exist — both surfaces envisaged for P14-S2 are entirely new frontend applications, no existing UI scaffold to extend. Session breakdown unchanged.
- **12.2 unchanged**: the three remaining extension candidates (ERP/line-of-business connectors, native mobile clients, AI functions) remain fully open, no code touches them — P14-S3's task (prepare as plugin extension points, do not implement) remains as planned.
- **No contradiction found between the concept text and the code state** — all seven verified sections (3.3a/2.5/3.7a/7.1/7.5/8/12.2) match the actual service state, no concept change needed.
- Concept changes: none. `graphify update` deliberately not part of this session (pure planning session without code change).

### Comparison & Extension Preparation (Phase 14, P14-S1)

- **Task**: delta/comparison function between installations, ignore regex (7.5) — already pre-verified at P14-S0: the comparison operates on `config-service`'s six real export categories, not on 7.5's full example list (AD group mapping/UI customizations do not exist).
- **New `POST /config/compare`** in `config-service`: `compare` mandatory, `base` optional (if missing, the endpoint pulls in its own current live export — use case "what would change if this document were imported"). New `compare.py` module, pure functions without their own state (matches the existing "stateless orchestrator" character, ADR 0035): list categories (`object_types`/`workflows`/`roles` by `name`, `approval_config` by `action_type`) are matched to each other via their name field, `sensor_config`/`federation_config` compared directly as singletons. Result per category: `only_in_base`/`only_in_compare`/`differing` (only the actually differing top-level fields, with `{base, compare}` values)/`identical`.
- **Deliberately field-level rather than deep diff**: 7.5 requires "detailed display of which attributes concretely differ" — a statement about *which* top-level attributes differ, not a requirement for a recursive tree diff of nested structures (e.g. individual rows of an object-type layout). A deliberate, documented simplification for a reference implementation.
- **Ignore regex** (`{"<category>": "<pattern>"}`, `"*"` as global default) normalizes names before matching via `re.sub` (substring removal) — 7.5's own example (`100_testobjekt_typ_alpha` vs. `101__testobjekt_typ_alpha`) rebuilt verbatim as a test: with a matching regex, both are recognized as the same object, the content comparison of the remaining attributes still runs fully independently of that (the display name remains the raw base-instance value). Configurable globally and per category, exactly as 7.5 requires.
- **Deliberately NO automated cross-installation fetch** (neither directly nor via the Federation Hub) — 7.5 explicitly names this only as an option, not a prerequisite ("a hub is not a prerequisite for the function, however"). Such a fetch would have had either to make `GET /config/export` a public gateway route (a new, permanently open attack surface just for a convenience feature) or to make `config-service` its own federation participant with its own key pair/hub registration (a disproportionately large new trust relationship for a purely read-only diagnostic operation) — both exports must instead already be available to the calling side, each produced via its own regular installation authentication. Full rationale: [ADR 0040](docs/adr/0040-config-compare-field-level-diff-no-cross-installation-fetch.md).
- **Real by-product**: while designing the CLI command it turned out that `config-service`, despite existing since P12-S3 (7.3), had so far **not at all** been wired into `tools/cli` (neither export nor import) — without an export command, `dms config compare` would have had no input file, so this was included directly: new `dms config export [--category]... [--file]` and `dms config compare <compare.json> [--base] [--category]... [--ignore-regex]`. Table default shows a category summary with counts, `-o json` delivers the complete `CompareResult` structure (7.5: "structured, scriptable output ... for automated drift checks in a CI/CD pipeline").
- **No admin UI visualization in this session** — P14-S1 is not on `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s list of sessions with a browser-test obligation, deliberately deferred to a later UI session (same backend-first-then-UI pattern as in several earlier phases).
- **Installation-specific data structurally excluded** (7.5: license status/registry reachability) — `ConfigDocument` doesn't contain these fields at all, no own filter logic needed.
- **Tests**: `config-service` **33 tests** (previously 13, +14 pure unit tests in new `test_compare.py` without a running container, +6 API tests against the real running container: comparison against itself, without `base`, with real differences, with/without ignore regex, invalid regex → `422`, unknown category → `422`), `tools/cli` **77 tests** (previously 70, +7: `dms config export`/`dms config compare`, mocked gateway). Full backend regression (`scripts/run-tests.sh --build`) green, `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean.
- Concept changes: none — 7.5 was already accurate, this session followed up on the implementation. Docs: [ADR 0040](docs/adr/0040-config-compare-field-level-diff-no-cross-installation-fetch.md) (new), `docs/services/config-service.md` (new section "Delta/comparison function", API table, deliberate limits, tests), `docs/tools/cli.md` (scope table/command overview/open points/tests updated), READMEs.
- `graphify dms/ --update` deliberately deferred (not a phase completion, follows at the latest with the last Phase-14 session) — **user clarification during this session**: `graphify update` fundamentally runs only at the end of an entire phase, not after each individual session (generalizes the previously Phase-13-specific standing rule from P13-S0 into a general project convention).

### Comparison & Extension Preparation (Phase 14, P14-S2)

- **Task**: reviewer/approval UI + migration console (8) — already pre-verified at P14-S0: both surfaces are entirely new frontend apps, no existing UI scaffold to extend.
- **`apps/reviewer-ui`** (port 3005): two areas. "Tasks" consumes the new `GET /tasks` endpoint in `workflow-service` (see below) — edit form pre-filled with `completed_by` (the logged-in username), required field `signature_id` for signature tasks (3.10). "Approvals" consumes `permission-service`'s `GET /approval-requests` **unfiltered by action type** — the first generic consumer of this API in the entire system (previously three independent, tightly filtered individual consumers: `admin-ui`'s `ManipulationSection` for only three curated query-manipulation types, `admin-ui`'s `SuperuserBreakGlass` only for `auth.superuser.activate`, `user-ui`'s `ApprovalsPane` only for `document.delete`/`folder.delete` — all three remain unchanged, `reviewer-ui` is an additional complete overview, not a replacement).
- **New `GET /tasks`** in `workflow-service`: collects ready manual/signature tasks across **all** `running` instances (previously there was only the instance-scoped `GET /instances/{id}/tasks`) — iterates following the same pattern as the already-existing `_sla_poll_loop`. Filters out `federated`/`federated_return` tasks (7.4), since these are never directly completable by a human anyway (`409` on a direct attempt, unchanged). Real by-product of the live verification: the list immediately showed real tasks left over from dozens of earlier test runs — an additional, unplanned piece of evidence that the cross-instance aggregation actually works correctly.
- **`apps/migration-console`** (port 3004): "Paired installations" (7.2, direct pairing instead of hub mediation, ADR 0034) - create/list/remove, generated API key visible only once, directly after creation. "Transfers" - start form (source folder, target installation, dry run, optional deletion period), shows a note with the generated `approval_request_id` when four-eyes (`migration.transfer.start`) is active (the actual approval deliberately runs through `reviewer-ui`'s generic approvals inbox, no second approval UI here), detail view with a phase timeline, lightweight 5s polling (the transfer continues running as an asynchronous `workflow-service` instance in the background). Deliberately not represented: the `.../steps/*` endpoints (internal targets of automatic `connector_call` service tasks) and the entire `/inbound/*` API (called by the paired counterpart, never by local operating staff).
- **No new authorization layer in either app**: `workflow-service`'s task completion was already deliberately open to any authenticated principal (P6-S6, ADR 0024), `permission-service`'s approval endpoints already check server-side with sufficient granularity (optional `required_permission` per action type, initiator≠decider), `migration-service` gates only via its own license check. `RequireAuth` in both apps accordingly only checks whether a valid session exists at all — no `RequireCapability` redirect like in the admin UI. Full rationale: [ADR 0041](docs/adr/0041-reviewer-ui-migration-console-scope-and-cross-instance-tasks.md).
- **Two real, independent gaps found while wiring the Compose blocks**: (1) `GATEWAY_SERVICE_CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`'s built-in fallback in `docker-compose.yml` never contained `PROCESS_DESIGNER_PORT` (3002) since its introduction in P6-S8, although `.env.example`'s own default was correct — both gaps corrected while adding the two new origins. (2) the originally intended ports 3003/3004 collided with the already-occupied `GRAFANA_PORT` (10.1) — noticed before the first startup (Docker error message "port is already allocated"), corrected to 3004 (migration-console) / 3005 (reviewer-ui).
- **Third real bug, found only during the actual browser test**: the real, local `infra/.env` file (not just `.env.example`) contained an outdated, hard-coded `GATEWAY_SERVICE_CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` line that overrode `docker-compose.yml`'s updated default — login failed in the real browser with a CORS error, although `curl` checks and all mocked tests were already green. Found only via `docker compose up -d --force-recreate gateway-service` + checking the environment variable actually set in the container, then `infra/.env` corrected directly. **Confirms exactly the experience pattern already documented in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` from P4-S2/S3** ("pure curl-based verification is not sufficient") for a second time.
- **Real browser test instead of just curl/Vitest**: the development environment traditionally has no installed browser (since P4-S2, see "Open Points" above) — this session instead used a purely **ephemeral** `mcr.microsoft.com/playwright` Docker container (no `npm install`, no new project dependency, no persistent repo/environment state) for a real Chromium round trip: login, both areas in each app, complete create→one-time-key-display→delete cycle in `migration-console`, status filter switching in `reviewer-ui`, theme switch to dark - all without console errors (`console --errors` empty). This approach does not contradict the earlier user decision against a permanent Playwright installation, since nothing was changed/added in the project itself.
- **Tests**: `workflow-service` **104 tests** (previously 102, +2: `GET /tasks` collects across multiple running instances and correctly excludes an already-completed instance, `test_federation.py` additionally confirms the filtering out of a `federated` task), `reviewer-ui` **15 tests** (Vitest: `AuthProvider` 4, `TaskList` 5 including the signature required field and JSON validation, `ApprovalList` 6 including approve/reject/already-decided), `migration-console` **14 tests** (`AuthProvider` 4, `PairedInstallationList` 4, `TransferConsole` 6 including the four-eyes note and polling interval) - all `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` clean. Full backend regression (`scripts/run-tests.sh`, 30 services) green twice; a one-time `webdav-connector` timeout during the regression turned out to be the already-known root-folder legacy-artifact pattern from P12-S4 (120 accumulated test artifacts in the root folder), fixed via direct SQL soft-delete, unrelated to this session.
- Concept changes: none — 8 was already accurate. Docs: [ADR 0041](docs/adr/0041-reviewer-ui-migration-console-scope-and-cross-instance-tasks.md) (new), `docs/services/reviewer-ui.md`/`migration-console.md` (new), `docs/services/workflow-service.md` (new `GET /tasks` entry, test count), READMEs, `infra/docker-compose.yml`/`.env.example`/`.env` (two new service blocks, CORS correction).
- `graphify dms/ --update` still deliberately deferred (not a phase completion, follows with the last Phase-14 session).

### Comparison & Extension Preparation (Phase 14, P14-S3)

- **Task**: cleanly prepare the backlog candidates from 12.2 (ERP connectors, mobile, AI) as plugin extension points, explicitly **not implementing** them + a final repo-wide documentation/structure audit — two functionally separate sub-tasks in one session.
- **New `docs/extension-points.md`**: for each of the three remaining 12.2 candidates, it was demonstrated against the actual code (not merely conceptually) where exactly it would dock in:
  - **ERP/line-of-business connectors** (DATEV, SAP): follows exactly the already twice-realized `cmis-connector`/`webdav-connector` pattern — `libs/dms-connector-sdk`'s `DmsTreeClient`/`ConnectorDescriptor` for the DMS-side tree logic, `maybe_start_registration()` for registration (no gateway/registry change needed, the gateway is a fully generic `/api/{service_type}/...` proxy), a new entry in `registry-service`'s `licensable_components` dict for licensing.
  - **Native mobile clients**: structurally not a new backend service, but a new consumer of the same API as `apps/user-ui` — a real, concrete finding: the existing `dms-api` Keycloak client is a confidential client with resource-owner password grant (suitable for first-party web UIs/CLI); for a distributed native app client, a second, public client with authorization-code+PKCE would be needed per OAuth2 best practice — natively supported by Keycloak, without `auth-service`'s existing JWKS token validation needing to change. Push notifications would be a fourth channel type in `notification-service`'s already-existing `Channel` enum (`"email"|"in_app"|"webhook"`), structurally the same minimal intervention as `"webhook"` was at the time.
  - **AI functions** (document chat, summarization, process assistance): modeled on `signature-service`'s provider plugin pattern (ADR 0025) — an `AIProviderConnector` interface with an installation-wide configurable provider type, directly following the model of `SignatureProviderConfig.type: Literal["internal","qtsp"]`, where `"qtsp"` is already today a deliberately reserved, unimplemented placeholder (`ValueError` on selection) — an `AIProviderConfig.type: Literal["local","external_api"]` could be structured the same way from the outset. No own document content extraction needed (reuse of `document-service`/OCR text layer/search index). Already established precedent: concept 5.4 already excluded AI/ML anomaly detection with an identical rationale.
  - Common insight across all three: none of the candidates requires a change to `registry-service`'s registration protocol, `gateway-service`'s proxy logic, or an existing core service - 12.2's own claim ("addable without touching the foundation") was thereby concretely verified for each individual case instead of being left unsubstantiated.
  - Honestly co-documented limitation: the Plugin Orchestration Service (3.8) would already today offer a suitable manifest format for all three candidates, but remains a pure decision/audit engine without real container lifecycle access — in the real, existing Docker Compose environment (a single node, no Kubernetes/Swarm) this has no practical consequences until this already-documented gap is closed in a dedicated future session.
  - Konzept.md 12.2 got a brief pointer to the new document (no content change, 12.2's own statement was already correct).
- **Structure audit**: a background agent initially started for this was stopped by the user - instead performed directly via targeted greps/counts (faster, sufficient for the concrete question at hand). Result:
  - Service/app documentation coverage (every `services/*`/`apps/*` folder has a `docs/services/<name>.md` and vice versa) as well as `docker-compose.yml` completeness (every folder has a Compose block and vice versa) were already **completely error-free** — no correction needed.
  - `docs/adr/README.md` is deliberately not a per-ADR index (only a short template/process description, 21 lines) - an initial checking assumption ("every ADR must be listed there") was wrong and was discarded, not a real finding.
  - **Real finding 1**: `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s "total sessions" counter was frozen at **95**; a direct recount of the actual `| PX-Sy |` table rows yielded **107**. The many individual "+N" intermediate steps in the ongoing history could no longer be reconstructed without gaps at this point (carried by hand across very many sessions) - the counter was corrected directly to the recounted value, with a note that the rows themselves are the reliable source, not the accumulated sum.
  - **Real finding 2**: of the 64 rows not marked "✅" at session start, only 23 were actually still open (P14-S3 through P17-S3) - the remaining **41 rows (P0-S1 through P6-S8)** are long-since-completed, extensively documented early sessions in `PROGRESS.md` that never retroactively received the "✅" prefix, only introduced starting at P6-S9. Added via script (pure formatting, no content change to the rows themselves) - now makes the table evaluable throughout via a simple "✅ filter", without needing to keep the special rule "everything before P6-S9 still counts as done" in mind.
  - README spot checks, `CONTRIBUTING.md` reconciliation, and the up-to-dateness classification of the root documents (`docs/architecture.md` deliberately frozen at P6-S9, all others current) were not carried out to the full originally planned extent for time reasons, after the background agent for this was stopped by the user - no known open findings there, but also not as thorough a review as for the other points.
- Concept changes: a reference in 12.2 to `docs/extension-points.md` (no content change). Docs: `docs/extension-points.md` (new), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (session-count correction, 41 retroactively added ✅ prefixes, P14-S3 row).
- `graphify dms/ --update` still deliberately deferred (not a phase completion, follows with the last Phase-14 session, P14-S12).

### DMN 1.3 Decision Tables in the Process Designer (Phase 14, P14-S4)

- **Task**: business rule task, calling a named DMN decision table, export/import via the configuration export (7.3), based on SpiffWorkflow's own DMN support (already confirmed as present at P14-S0).
- **workflow-service** (`spiff_adapter.py`): `CamundaParser` is already a subclass of `SpiffWorkflow.dmn.parser.BpmnDmnParser`, `businessRuleTask` already mapped to `BusinessRuleTaskParser` via `OVERRIDE_PARSER_CLASSES` — no own override needed. `parser.add_dmn_str(xml)` loads a DMN file, keyed by its `<decision id="...">`; the DMN must be loaded before the referencing BPMN file (`get_spec()` builds the complete task spec tree including all referenced decisions).
  - **Real, empirically found SpiffWorkflow bug**: `add_dmn_xml()` always creates exactly ONE `dmn_parsers` entry per file (keyed by the ID of the FIRST `<decision>`) — `len(parser.dmn_parsers)` therefore CANNOT check the "exactly one decision per file" limit, it is ALWAYS 1. The actual SpiffWorkflow-own rejection (`ValidationException("Multiple decision tables are not current supported.")`) only happens LAZILY inside `DMNParser.parse()`, triggered by `BpmnDmnParser.get_decision()` when resolving a referencing `businessRuleTask` — a pure DMN upload without an accompanying BPMN reference would never have seen the error. `parse_dmn()` therefore explicitly calls `dmn_parser.parse()` itself. In the process, a second bug in the installed version was found: the `ValidationException` for the multiple-decision case passes a `list` instead of an lxml element as the `node` argument, `ValidationException.__init__` reads `node.tag` and thereby itself raises an `AttributeError` instead of coming through cleanly — `parse_dmn()`'s `except Exception` catches both.
  - New table `dmn_definition` (same versioning pattern as `process_definition`), `decision_id` as the actual resolution key (not `name`). `create_process_definition`/`start_instance` now load, before every BPMN parse, all latest DMN definitions of all families (a deliberate simplification instead of statically scanning the BPMN XML for actually referenced `decisionRef`s — unreferenced decisions are harmless). `DuplicateDecisionIdError`: two families with a colliding `decision_id` under their respective latest versions would no longer be distinguishable within the same parser. Deliberately NO "in use" check on `DELETE /dmn-definitions/{id}` (would require scanning all BPMN texts for `decisionRef`) — a documented limit.
- **config-service**: new 7th category `dmn_definitions` (export/import/compare, same pattern as `workflows`), deliberately applied BEFORE `workflows` on import, since a `businessRuleTask` is only resolvable if the referenced DMN family already exists at the target.
