- **process-designer, empirical verification before implementation**: a real browser test performed via an ephemeral Docker Playwright container (`mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.48.0-jammy`, `--network host`, no repo/environment entry) confirmed that the already-registered `CamundaPlatformPropertiesProviderModule` (in use since P6-S7/P6-S9) already renders a complete "Implementation" group (`Type: DMN`, `Decision reference`, `Binding`, `Tenant ID`, `Result variable`) for a selected business rule task — **no new properties-panel code needed**.
  - In the process, two pure test-fixture/environment issues were initially diagnosed (not real bugs): (1) a hand-written BPMN test fixture with `id="Start"` collided with SpiffWorkflow's own internal root task-spec name "Start" (`WorkflowException: No output task connected.`) — a pure test-script artifact, fixed by using a different ID; (2) the same fixture had no `bpmndi:BPMNDiagram` block (unlike every other project fixture) — bpmn-js' import crashed on this in the browser (`(0 , u.Cf)(...).get is not a function`), fixed by adding a complete DI section (irrelevant to SpiffWorkflow itself, purely visual).
  - **Real, DMN-independent bug found and fixed**: `apps/process-designer/src/app/designer/page.tsx`'s `loadedForId = useRef<string | null>(null)` was compared against `id = searchParams.get("id")` (also `null` without an `?id=` query parameter) — `null !== null` is `false`, so the new-creation branch (`setInitialXml(STARTER_BPMN_XML)`) NEVER ran on the very first call to `/designer/` WITHOUT `?id=`, the designer stayed permanently empty ("no diagram to display"). Made visible only by the first real browser navigation of this route in a long time (Vitest/jsdom never runs a real query-string-less navigation). Fixed via an `undefined` sentinel (`useRef<string | null | undefined>(undefined)`) instead of `null` — the same fix carried over from the outset into the new `dmn-designer/page.tsx`.
  - A third, purely environment-related problem (not code-caused, not caused by P14-S4): the Keycloak test account `config-admin` lacked `email`/`firstName`/`lastName` (present for `users-admin`) — triggered Keycloak's "Account is not fully set up" on the resource-owner password grant. Added afterward via the Keycloak admin API (same values as `auth_service/bootstrap.py`'s initial creation would have envisaged).
  - **`dmn-js` spike**: compatibility with the pinned `bpmn-js` 18.22.1 stack was unverified at session start (a P14-S0 flag). Isolated spike (temporary copy of `apps/process-designer`, `dmn-js@17.10.1` added, minimal test component): `npm install` clean, `next build`/static export clean (following the same `declare module "dmn-js/lib/Modeler"` workaround as for `bpmn-js-properties-panel`, since `dmn-js` likewise ships no own TS declarations), live rendering in the browser error-free (decision table including hit-policy dropdown, rule rows). `dmn-js` uses the same `diagram-js` major version (`^15.23.2`) as `bpmn-js` — no version conflict. Adopted directly as a real dependency, no fallback to a raw XML editor needed (see [ADR 0021](docs/adr/0021-bpmn-io-license-watermark.md) addendum).
  - New components: `DmnDesigner.tsx` (analogous to `BpmnDesigner.tsx`), `DmnDefinitionList.tsx` (analogous to `ProcessDefinitionList.tsx`, additional `decision_id` column), `/dmn-designer/` route. `app/page.tsx` got a simple tab switcher (`.tab-bar`) between process definitions and DMN decision tables instead of a second independent home-page route.
- **Real end-to-end browser verification after implementation** (same ephemeral Playwright container): login → tab switcher → "Create new" in the DMN designer → `dmn-js` canvas renders correctly → import of a real DMN 1.3 fixture (two rules, both visible) → assign a name, "Save" → success message including version number, redirect to new `id` → back to the list, new entry with correct `decision_id` visible. No console errors at any step. Test dataset subsequently deleted again.
- Tests: `workflow-service` 134 (previously 104, **+30**: DMN parsing/CRUD/versioning, end-to-end business rule task execution via repository and HTTP API), `config-service` (`dmn_definitions` cases added to existing export/import/compare tests, real import roundtrip against the running `workflow-service`), `process-designer` 39 (previously 28, **+11**: `DmnDesigner`, `DmnDefinitionList`, `HomePage` tab switcher), `ruff check`/`ruff format --check`/`npm run typecheck`/`npm run lint`/`npm run build` all green.
- Docs: `docs/services/workflow-service.md` (new DMN section, endpoint table, data model, tests, open points), `docs/services/process-designer.md` (new DMN section including the `loadedForId` finding, endpoint table, tests, open points), `docs/services/config-service.md` ("six" → "seven categories", new table row), [ADR 0021](docs/adr/0021-bpmn-io-license-watermark.md) addendum (dmn-js actually put into operation), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P14-S4 row).
- `graphify dms/ --update` still deliberately deferred (not a phase completion, follows with P14-S12).

### Business Calendars for SLA Deadline Calculation (Phase 14, P14-S5)

- **Task**: freely configurable, named calendars (non-working days), assignable to a process/an installation, deliberately as configuration data instead of hard-coded regional rules (7.1). P14-S0 had already established: SpiffWorkflow offers no built-in for this — entirely new logic needed.
- **Core design decision, empirically secured via a spike before the actual implementation**: `SpiffWorkflow.bpmn.specs.event_definitions.timer.DurationTimerEventDefinition.has_fired()` (installed version, read via `inspect`) evaluates the timer duration expression exactly ONCE — on the very first call, as soon as the associated timer task becomes `WAITING` — via `my_task.workflow.script_engine.evaluate(my_task, self.expression)`. This point in time is already exactly the right one for "N working days from now" (no need to pre-compute at instance start, which would simply be wrong for a boundary timer in the middle of a process run). `BpmnWorkflow(spec, script_engine=...)`/the public `wf.script_engine` property (both verified via `inspect`) allow their own `PythonScriptEngine` instance with `TaskDataEnvironment(environment_globals={"business_days": ...})` — SpiffWorkflow's own extension point intended for exactly this. Played out live against the installed version (a plain `python3 -c` REPL session, no test file): the duration expression `business_days(0)` in a real BPMN file correctly resolved via the injected engine and fired the timer.
- **`spiff_adapter.py`**: `business_days_duration(n, calendar_name=None, *, start=None)` (a pure, testable-via-`start`-parameter calculation function — weekends ALWAYS non-working, a calendar only adds the extra days) + a thin `business_days(n, calendar_name=None)` wrapper (the function actually exposed via `environment_globals`, without the test-only `start` parameter). Module-wide in-memory cache (`_business_calendars`/`_default_calendar_name`, `register_business_calendars()`) instead of DB access — SpiffWorkflow's engine is throughout synchronous, an `async` read would not be callable from within `has_fired()` (the same constraint as with the connector service task, P12-S2). Both `new_workflow()` AND `deserialize()` explicitly assign their own `_SCRIPT_ENGINE` instance (`wf.script_engine` is NOT part of the serialized state — `BpmnWorkflowConverter.from_dict()` always creates a new, generic `BpmnWorkflow` without a `script_engine` argument when restoring, verified by reading the source). Actually rebuilt as a test case: a fixture with a manual task BEFORE the timer ensures that `has_fired()` was guaranteed never called before serialization (no already-cached `event_value`), so resuming after deserialization forces a REAL re-evaluation of `business_days(...)` — confirms that the engine reassignment after `deserialize()` is actually necessary AND sufficient.
- **workflow-service**: new `BusinessCalendar` table (`name` unique, `non_working_dates` as a JSON list of ISO date strings, `is_default` flag — at most one row at a time, `repository.py` automatically resets all others when setting a new default). Deliberately NO versioning pattern like `ProcessDefinition`/`DmnDefinition` (ADR 0027) — a calendar is an ongoing-to-maintain reference data row (adding new holidays yearly), regular update-in-place (`PUT`) like `SensorConfig`/`ApprovalConfig`. `POST/GET/PUT/DELETE /business-calendars*`, gated like process/DMN definitions (`admin.object_config`). `repository.py` keeps the `spiff_adapter` cache current after every write access, `main.py`'s lifespan loads it once at startup (before the SLA poll loop, so no already-running timer hits an empty cache).
- **Assignment (concept 7.1: "assignable to a process/an installation")**: a calendar explicitly named in the BPMN file (`business_days(3, "de-national")`) already IS the process assignment — lives directly in the model, no additional assignment table needed. The `is_default` flag covers the installation assignment (only applies without an explicit argument). Full rationale: [ADR 0042](docs/adr/0042-business-calendar-script-engine-injection.md).
- **config-service**: new 8th category `business_calendars` — unlike `workflows`/`dmn_definitions`, an ordinary name-based upsert like `roles` (`apply_business_calendars`), since no versioning pattern exists.
- Tests: `workflow-service` 166 (previously 134, **+32**: `business_days_duration()` calculation including weekend/holiday/default-calendar/collision cases, two real end-to-end timer tests including a dedicated serialization round-trip test, calendar CRUD, cache-refresh tests), `config-service` 35 (business_calendars cases added to export/import/compare, real import roundtrip with upsert semantics against the running `workflow-service`), `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` both green.
- Docs: `docs/services/workflow-service.md` (new business-calendar section, endpoint table, data model, tests, open points), `docs/services/config-service.md` ("seven" → "eight categories", new table row), new [ADR 0042](docs/adr/0042-business-calendar-script-engine-injection.md), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P14-S5 row).
- Pure backend session, no browser test needed (not on `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s UI sessions list — `apps/process-designer` deliberately does not get a calendar management UI here, the BPMN model itself already references calendars via a text expression in the timer duration).
- `graphify dms/ --update` still deliberately deferred (not a phase completion, follows with P14-S12).

### Team Workspace Teamspace (Phase 14, P14-S6)

- **Task**: self-managed, permanent group workspace (2.5) — own folder/documents, shared appointments and contacts, "own access regime independent of the rest of RBAC". Deliberately distinguished from the circulation folder (2.3, `case-service`, sequential forwarding/completion process) — no defined end.
- **Core design decision**: an **own, local membership table** (`teamspace_member`) is the primary access check, NOT an extension of `permission-service`'s `Role`/`RoleAssignment` — most literally satisfies "independent of the RBAC model (4.1)", rather than actually using the model teamspace is supposed to differ from per the concept. **Additionally, complementarily**: when a member is invited/removed, `teamspace-service` creates or removes a resource-scoped `permission-service` role assignment (`teamspace-member`) on the teamspace root folder (get-or-create pattern like `migration-service`'s `apply_role_assignment`, P12-S2) — actually effective, since `search-service` already checks `document.read` at the folder level today (`POST /check/batch`, empirically verified); without this anchoring, documents in a teamspace folder would still show up in search results for non-members. Full rationale: [ADR 0043](docs/adr/0043-teamspace-service-membership-and-permission-integration.md).
- **New, standalone `teamspace-service`** (port 8032, schema `teamspace`, structure following the `favorite-service` template): four tables — `teamspace` (metadata + `root_folder_id` as an opaque `folder-service` reference, analogous to `case-service`'s opaque `document_id` references), `teamspace_member` (`principal_id` = Keycloak `sub` UUID, a single `can_manage_members` flag covers both member management and dissolving the entire teamspace — concept 2.5 itself does not distinguish these cases), `teamspace_appointment`, `teamspace_contact` (teamspace-local, deliberately NOT the future, installation-wide "Contacts" special area from 2.5/Phase 15). Creation automatically produces a real `folder-service` folder (`parent_id="root"`, without `object_type_id` — the field is fully optional at `folder-service`). Deletion removes only the teamspace metadata, the folder and its contents remain (a real folder deletion would be an independent, risky feature — retention periods, four-eyes deletion approval, 5.2).
- **Deliberately no group support**, although concept 2.5 names "members ... or groups": `permission-service`'s `RoleAssignment.principal_type` does know the value `"group"`, but `_collect_effective_roles` never reads it — no group-membership expansion exists anywhere in the project, and the gateway does not pass through a Keycloak group claim either. Tied to the AD/Keycloak group mapping (4.4) that has been open and permanently deferred since P2-S1/S2 — rebuilding a teamspace-own group membership on a nonexistent foundation would have been an entirely new, independent undertaking.
- **Invite-by-username gap found and closed**: `X-DMS-Principal`/`RoleAssignment.principal_id` is a Keycloak `sub` UUID that no user knows; the existing `GET /users` in `auth-service` is admin-gated (`admin.user_management`) and therefore unsuitable for a feature meant to be usable by everyone. New, deliberately narrow endpoint **`GET /users/lookup?username=`** (`auth-service`) — only `Depends(get_current_user)` (any valid token), exact name search (no enumeration), returns only `{id, username}`. Fixed autonomously as a clearly scoped, low-risk addition, without asking back, following the same pattern as the P12-S3 `PUT /roles/{id}` finding or the P14-S2 CORS gap.
- **`user-ui`**: new `TeamspacesPane.tsx` (master-detail, new IconRail entry 👥) — inviting first calls `lookupUserByUsername()`, only then calls `inviteTeamspaceMember()` with the resolved UUID; "Open folder" uses the same `openFolderPath` helper extracted from the favorites folder navigation as when opening a favorited folder (P7-S1d).
- Tests: `teamspace-service` **41** (17 repository + 24 API, the latter against the real running `folder-service`/`permission-service`, no mocking), `auth-service` **48** (previously 45, +3 for `/users/lookup`), `user-ui` **104** (previously 95, +9 `teamspaces-pane.test.tsx`), `audit-service` still **26 green** (new `teamspace.>` subject added, no regression). `ruff check`/`ruff format --check`/`npm run typecheck`/`npm run lint`/`npm run build` all green.
- Docs: `docs/services/teamspace-service.md` (new), `docs/services/auth-service.md` (new `/users/lookup` entry + open-points addition), `docs/services/user-ui.md` (new teamspaces section + test update), new [ADR 0043](docs/adr/0043-teamspace-service-membership-and-permission-integration.md), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P14-S6 row).
- **Real end-to-end browser verification** (ephemeral Playwright container, same pattern as P14-S2/P14-S4): in the process, a real network stumbling block was found — the browser process inside the container could reach neither `localhost:*` nor the host's real LAN address despite `--network host` (`net::ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED`, although `curl`/Node `http.get` from within the same container worked), but had no trouble addressing other Docker containers via the shared Compose network (`dms_dms-net`, container DNS names) — apparently an environment restriction specific to browser processes (not to ordinary processes), not specific to this session. Solved via a **separate, unpublished throwaway build** of `user-ui` with `NEXT_PUBLIC_GATEWAY_BASE_URL=http://gateway-service:8000` (Docker-internal name instead of `localhost:8009`), started in the same Compose network, plus `--disable-web-security` in the browser (origin mismatch, since the gateway's CORS only allows `localhost:3000/3001`) — does not touch the real `user-ui` production container. Played through: login as `config-admin` → create teamspace → detail view (member + "(management)" marker visible) → invite `users-admin` by username (correctly resolves via `GET /users/lookup`) → create an appointment → create a contact → "Open folder" correctly switches to the document view → login as `users-admin` confirms: teamspace appears in its own list, "Delete team workspace"/invite form are invisible as a non-management member (correct client-side mirroring of the server-side `can_manage_members` gate) → "Leave" works → deletion as a management member removes the teamspace from the list. No console/page errors at any step. Test teamspace subsequently removed via the API.
- **Two real findings made and fixed/documented in the process**: (1) **bug, immediately fixed**: `TeamspacesPane.tsx` called `t("common.delete")` for the appointment/contact delete buttons, a key that was never created in `de.json`'s `common` section — the buttons literally displayed "common.delete" instead of "Delete" (no crash, since `t()` returns the raw path on a missing key, but visibly wrong). Fix: `"delete": "Löschen"` added to `common` in `de.json`, `npm test`/`typecheck`/`lint`/`build` green again (still 104 tests), screenshot confirms correct display afterward. (2) **real, unfixed UX gap, documented instead of fixed in this session**: the member list shows the raw `principal_id` UUID, not a resolved username — `auth-service` only offers a forward resolution (`GET /users/lookup?username=`), no reverse resolution UUID→username for non-admins. Noted as "Open Points" in `docs/services/user-ui.md` instead of spontaneously building a new resolution API (scope expansion outside this session).
- `graphify dms/ --update` still deliberately deferred (not a phase completion, follows with P14-S12).

### Extended Full-Text Search Query Language (Phase 14, P14-S7)

- **Task**: 3.7a requires "matching common reference products" — Boolean combination with clear precedence rules, phrase search, wildcards, fuzzy search and proximity search, based on Postgres FTS + `pg_trgm`, no external search engine. P14-S0 had already confirmed: Boolean combination/phrase search are already really implemented via `websearch_to_tsquery` (ADR 0012) — the only remaining open item is fuzzy/proximity search.
- **Core design decision**: a small, own recursive-descent parser (`query_language.py`, hand-written tokenizer, no regex monstrosities) completely replaces `websearch_to_tsquery`, instead of trying to somehow smuggle wildcard/fuzzy syntax through the existing function (which grammatically knows neither). Syntax deliberately modeled on Lucene/Elasticsearch (`AND` implicit, `OR`, `-`/`NOT`, `"phrase"`, `(...)`, `*`, `~N`) — "market-standard" taken literally per the concept wording, a user who knows one of these reference searches immediately recognizes the syntax.
- **`query_compiler.py` translates the AST via two paths**, see [ADR 0044](docs/adr/0044-search-query-language-fuzzy-proximity.md) for the full rationale: (1) without a fuzzy leaf, the ENTIRE tree is assembled into ONE `to_tsquery('german', ...)` string (Postgres' own `&`/`|`/`!`/`<->`/`<N>`/`:*` algebra takes over precedence/parenthesization itself) — ranking remains exactly the previous single `ts_rank()`, no behavior change for the vastly predominant case of simple queries. (2) as soon as a fuzzy leaf appears anywhere (pg_trgm similarity is not part of the tsquery algebra), it is compiled leaf-by-leaf into SQL Boolean expressions — **mathematically exactly equivalent** to the combined-string variant (Postgres' `@@` operator provably distributes correctly over `&`/`|`/`!`), just structurally split rather than a single tsquery; ranking there deliberately simplified as a sum of independent per-leaf scores (consistent with ADR 0012's already-accepted "not BM25-level" limitation).
- **Proximity search via Postgres' `<N>` distance operator instead of a sloppy-phrase function**: `<N>` itself requires exact distance AND fixed order — "within N words, either order" (concept wording) is built as an OR chain over the distances 1..N in both directions. Deliberately limited to **exactly two words** (concept literally says "two terms"), `N` clamped to a maximum of 20 (prevents an inappropriately long OR chain for an arbitrarily large `~N`, the same kind of limit as `search_result_hard_limit`).
- **Fuzzy search via `pg_trgm`'s `word_similarity()`, not `similarity()`**: `similarity()` compares two strings as a whole (unsuitable for "does a long full text contain a similar word"), `word_similarity()` specifically finds the best substring match — exactly the use case needed here. Own, documented mapping of the concept's three "tolerance levels" onto `pg_trgm`'s 0..1 scale (`{1: 0.5, 2: 0.35, 3: 0.2}`, 1 = strict, 3 = tolerant) — not a Postgres convention, a deliberate own calibration, verified by test (a typo is found, a completely different word is not found even at the strict level).
- **Malformed queries return `400` instead of being silently interpreted differently** (missing parenthesis, invalid fuzzy level, proximity search with ≠2 words) — a deliberate difference from `websearch_to_tsquery`, which never throws a SQL error. A parser with an explicit grammar can and should tell the user when their query doesn't match the documented syntax.
- **`pg_trgm`**: standard contrib module, already present in the `postgres:16-alpine` image (as anticipated in ADR 0012) — `CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pg_trgm` + two new trigram GIN indexes (`title`/`full_text`) idempotently in the lifespan, the same ad-hoc schema migration pattern as everywhere else in this project (no Alembic).
- **`user-ui`**: new syntax hint text below the search field (`SearchPane.tsx`, `search.syntaxHint`) — a pure text change, no new interaction logic, since the entire new capability operates server-side within the existing `q` parameter.
- Tests: `search-service` **56** (previously 21, **+35**: 23 pure parser/tokenizer tests without DB — AND/OR/NOT precedence, parenthesization, phrases, proximity search including word-count/distance validation, fuzzy-level validation, the `E-Mail` hyphen edge case, syntax errors —, ten repository tests against the real running Postgres, two API tests including `400` over HTTP and a fuzzy hit via the full `TestClient` path), `user-ui` still **104** (existing search test extended with a hint-text assertion, no new test case needed). `ruff check`/`ruff format --check`/`npm run typecheck`/`npm run lint`/`npm run build` all green.
- Docs: `docs/services/search-service.md` (new query-language section, API/data-model/tests/open-points updates), `docs/adr/0012-search-postgres-fts.md` (consequences point marked as closed), new [ADR 0044](docs/adr/0044-search-query-language-fuzzy-proximity.md), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P14-S7 row).
- `graphify dms/ --update` still deliberately deferred (not a phase completion, follows with P14-S12).

### Office Add-in (Phase 14, P14-S8)

- **Task**: 3.3a requires a Microsoft Office add-in (Office.js, not the older VSTO/COM technology) for opening/saving a document directly from/to OG Doc, inline metadata editing, workflow start/continuation from within the document, and a central role-based template library — "the same already-existing connector/API path" instead of its own parallel document logic. P14-S9 (LibreOffice/OpenOffice, UNO `.oxt`) is a separate, later session.
- **Core design decision 1 — Word only, no Excel/PowerPoint**: the central mechanism for loading DMS content into the active Office document is `context.document.body.insertFileFromBase64(base64, InsertLocation.replace)` — a pure **Word** JS API (WordApi 1.5). Neither Excel nor PowerPoint offers a comparable "replace entire document" capability in the stable API. Deliberately limited to Word rather than a weaker workaround for the other apps — fully justified in [ADR 0045](docs/adr/0045-office-addin-word-only-reused-endpoints-settings-linking.md).
- **Core design decision 2 — not a single new backend endpoint**: opening/saving (`GET .../content`, `POST .../versions`), inline metadata (`PATCH /documents/{id}`, `GET /object-types/{id}`), workflow (`GET /instances?business_key=`, `GET .../tasks`, `POST .../complete`, `POST /process-definitions/{id}/instances`) run entirely via already-existing `document-service`/`workflow-service`/`object-type-service` endpoints — correlation between document and workflow instance via `business_key = document_id`, the same mechanism already established throughout the system. First frontend consumer of the explicit document lock (`POST`/`DELETE /documents/{id}/lock`) that was so far unused by any UI — makes more sense for a possibly long Word editing session than `user-ui`'s purely optimistic conflict check (ADR 0002).
- **Core design decision 3 — document linkage via `Office.context.document.settings`**: which DMS document belongs to an open Word file is stored as add-in-own state directly in the file itself (an own XML custom part) — automatically known again after close/reopen, no new server state/mapping table needed.
- **Core design decision 4 — template library as a naming convention rather than a new mechanism**: a template is an ordinary document in the configurable root folder "Templates" — role-basedness (3.3a wording) follows automatically from the already-existing folder read-permission check (`permission-service`), no new permission logic. Deliberately separated from the future structural "templates" from 2.5/P15-S6 (file-plan rough draft via the JSON structure export) — the same German term, two different concepts. `derived_from_document_id`/`derived_from_version_number` (document-service, already-existing schema from an earlier session) is actually set by a client for the first time when creating "new from template".
- **New, standalone app `apps/office-addin`** (Next.js static export like every other frontend app, port 3006, `manifest.xml` + `assets/` copied separately into the Docker image, no Next.js build output): `TaskPane.tsx` orchestrates four states (no linked document → `DocumentPicker`/`TemplatePicker`; template loaded, not yet saved → `MetadataForm` + "Save as new document"; linked document → `MetadataForm` + save/unlink + `WorkflowPanel`). `lib/office.ts` encapsulates every `Office`/`Word` touch point (settings get/set/save, `insertFileFromBase64`, `getFileAsync` slice assembly into base64) — the components themselves never call `Office`/`Word` directly, making them testable. Deliberately **no theme switcher/maintenance banner** (space constraints, ~300-450px narrow task pane) and a simplified `MetadataForm` (one text field per attribute instead of `user-ui`'s type-specific layout forms) — documented, justified simplifications, not oversights.
- **Manifest**: classic XML format (more broadly compatible than the newer JSON manifest), a single ribbon button on the Word Home tab that only opens the task pane (`ShowTaskpane`, no separate `FunctionFile` logic needed). Checked with the **official Microsoft tool** `office-addin-manifest validate` — "The manifest is valid.", per the manifest structure runnable on Word 2013+/Mac/Web/iPad.
- **No real Office host available in this development environment** (no Windows/Office/valid Microsoft 365 sideloading tenant, no headless/containerized way to actually run Word — unlike the ephemeral Playwright approach for browser UIs, there is no equivalent for Office). Instead verified threefold: (1) component tests against a hand-written `Office`/`Word` fake (`tests/office-mock.ts`, the identical principle as mocking `fetch` in the other apps), (2) the real manifest validation (see above), (3) **every reused backend endpoint played out live against the real running stack**: a real "Templates" folder + template document created, content downloaded (byte-for-byte comparison confirmed identical), a new document created from it with `derived_from_document_id`/`derived_from_version_number`, lock acquired, new version checked in (`is_conflict: false`), metadata patched, lock released, a workflow instance started with `business_key = document_id` and correctly found again via `GET /instances?business_key=`. All test artifacts subsequently removed. A human should actually sideload the add-in in Word before production use (`office-addin-debugging`, see README.md) — a documented open point.
- **HTTPS requirement**: Office only loads add-in web content over HTTPS — this stack runs consistently over HTTP in development like every other service here; `office-addin` would need its own TLS termination point for a real sideload test (not part of this session, documented in README.md under "Local sideload testing").
- Tests: **18** (Vitest) — `office-lib.test.ts` (8, `lib/office.ts` against the `Office`/`Word` fake), `auth-context.test.tsx` (4, the identical login/logout/session pattern as the other apps), `task-pane.test.tsx` (6, end-to-end interaction flows: empty state, open linked + load via `Word.run`, lock conflict shows a read-only notice, template→new document with correct provenance, save updates the linked version, unlinking releases the lock). `npm run typecheck`/`npm run lint`/`npm run build` all green. In the process, a real jsdom gap was found and fixed following the same pattern already established in `user-ui` (`Blob.prototype.text`, P5d-S2): `Blob.prototype.arrayBuffer` is likewise missing, retrofitted via a `FileReader` polyfill in `tests/setup.ts`.
- Docs: `docs/services/office-addin.md` (new), `apps/office-addin/README.md` (new, including "Local sideload testing"), new [ADR 0045](docs/adr/0045-office-addin-word-only-reused-endpoints-settings-linking.md), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P14-S8 row). `infra/docker-compose.yml`/`.env(.example)`: new `office-addin` service (port 3006), gateway CORS allowance added (`GATEWAY_SERVICE_CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`) — confirmed in the process that both `.env.example` and the real `.env` actually read by Docker Compose both must be manually kept in sync (no automatic reconciliation between the two files).
- `graphify dms/ --update` still deliberately deferred (not a phase completion, follows with P14-S12).

### LibreOffice Add-in (Phase 14, P14-S9)

- **Task**: 3.3a requires an equivalent LibreOffice/OpenOffice extension (UNO-API-based `.oxt` package) to the Microsoft Office add-in from P14-S8 — "same feature scope ... to the extent the respective platform allows", "shared backend interface with the MS Office add-in" (roadmap wording).
- **Core design decision 1 — Writer only**: the same Word-only restriction from ADR 0045 applies analogously to Writer — no comparably robust "load entire document" API for Calc/Impress. Consistent with P14-S8, no inconsistency between the two extensions.
- **Core design decision 2 — dialog hub instead of a sidebar panel**: UNO's sidebar deck API (`XSidebarPanel`) is practically only documented/common in Java/C++, a Python implementation would be fragile uncharted territory. Instead: a single menu entry ("Tools > Open OG Doc...") opens a programmatically assembled (no `.xdl`) "hub" dialog with context-dependent buttons that open further, focused dialogs — the realistic UNO implementation of "to the extent the platform allows", not a compromise of convenience.
- **Core design decision 3 — `loadComponentFromURL()` instead of in-place replacement**: Word JS' `insertFileFromBase64(..., replace)` has no 1:1 UNO equivalent with comparable robustness. Instead a real "open file": downloaded bytes → temporary file → `Desktop.loadComponentFromURL()` opens a NEW window — more idiomatic for a desktop program. For "New from template" additionally `AsTemplate=True` — LibreOffice's OWN "new from template" load option, a real advantage of this platform, even superior to the Word approach (no need to rebuild the template semantics). Subsequent actions (metadata/save/workflow) act on the newly opened window, not the original.
- **Core design decision 4 — no new backend endpoint**: `python/dms_client.py` (pure standard library, `urllib` — LibreOffice's bundled Python interpreter has no preinstalled third-party packages, no `pip install requests` as an installation hurdle for end users) mirrors exactly the same gateway calls as `apps/office-addin/src/lib/api.ts` (P14-S8) — open/save/lock, inline metadata, workflow correlation via `business_key = document_id`, template library as a naming convention (root folder "Templates", role-basedness follows automatically from existing folder read permissions) — identical to P14-S8, no second implementation of these concepts.
- **Core design decision 5 — document linkage via `UserDefinedProperties`**: `document.getDocumentProperties().UserDefinedProperties` is UNO's equivalent of Office.js' `document.settings` — state stored in the file itself (ODF `meta.xml`/OOXML core properties), survives close/reopen.
- **Real bug found before the live test**: a first version always saved as ODF (`writer8` filter) on "Save to OG Doc", regardless of the originally opened format (e.g. DOCX) — a document would have been silently converted. Fixed via a THIRD `UserDefinedProperties` property (`ogdoc_content_type`) that stores the HTTP `Content-Type` actually observed on open/template-load and reuses it on save.
- **Real stumbling block found during the live installation**: `OpenOffice.org-minimal-version` in `description.xml` does NOT check against the real product version, but against the internally constant-held `ReferenceOOoMajorMinor` value (`program/versionrc`, still `4.1` in LibreOffice 26.2) — an initially entered value `"7.0"` made `unopkg add` fail with "unsatisfied dependencies", although a real, newer instance was installed. Fixed via `value="4.1"` while keeping `d:name` readable.
- **Substantially more thorough verification than at P14-S8 was possible**, since this development environment actually contains a functioning LibreOffice 26.2 installation including a Python UNO binding (`/opt/libreoffice26.2`) — a real opportunity that does not exist for Word:
  - **30 `unittest` tests** (`python3 -m unittest discover`): pure business logic without UNO involvement (hub status text/buttons per state, attribute field filtering, file-extension detection per content type, `dms_client`'s multipart/error handling) AND real UNO property-container logic against a hand-written fake of `UserDefinedProperties`/`PropertySetInfo` (`tests/uno_mock.py`, the identical principle as `office-mock.ts` in P14-S8) — including a full import of `ogdoc_addin.py`, a real wiring/reference-error test.
  - **Real `.oxt` installation via `unopkg add`**: exit 0, `unopkg list --verbose` confirms "is registered: yes" for the package AND `Addons.xcu`, all four `python/*.py` files end up correctly in the directory expected by `ScriptProviderForPython` after installation (confirmed by filesystem inspection). Cleanly uninstalled afterward.
  - **Every single `dms_client.py` function played out live against the real running gateway stack** (not just curl like in the other sessions, but the ACTUAL Python client code of this extension): login → root folder listing finds "Templates" → create a template → move it into the folder → `list_documents_in_folder` finds it → `download_document_content` returns byte-for-byte identical content + correct content type → `create_document` with `derived_from_document_id` → `acquire_lock` → `checkin_version` (`is_conflict: false`) → `update_document_metadata` → `release_lock` → `start_instance` with `business_key = document_id` → `list_instances_for_document` finds it again via the correlation → `search_documents` finds the document. All test artifacts subsequently removed.
  - **No working headless UNO script-bridge access** (`soffice --accept=...`) — actually tried (TCP socket AND Unix pipe transport, both identical: clean, controlled process exit with code 81 directly after the internal IPC bootstrap, confirmed via `strace` as no crash/signal, no sandbox network restriction of this environment — a real TCP `bind`/`listen` on the same port was isolated and tested successfully). Likely an incomplete "base" package selection of this particular installation. As a result, NOT possible: actually calling `open_ogdoc()` via the script provider, real dialog interaction tests against `UnoControlDialog` objects — a documented open point, a human should actually sideload `OgDocAddin.oxt` in Writer before production use.
- Docs: `docs/services/libreoffice-addin.md` (new), `apps/libreoffice-addin/README.md` (new), new [ADR 0046](docs/adr/0046-libreoffice-addin-writer-only-dialog-hub-loadcomponent.md), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P14-S9 row), `.gitignore` (`apps/libreoffice-addin/*.oxt` — build artifact, not versioned, `build.py` generates it).
- `graphify dms/ --update` still deliberately deferred (not a phase completion, follows with P14-S12).
- **Phase-14 interim status after P14-S9**: 3.3a (office integration) thereby fully completed (both parts, P14-S8/S9) — remaining Phase-14 sessions: P14-S10 (public share link, 4.2a), P14-S11 (delegation/deputy, 4.4a), P14-S12 (bulk metadata editing, 8, including the `graphify update` phase completion planned since P14-S0/S12).

### Public Share Link (Phase 14, P14-S10)

- **Task**: 4.2a requires a time-limited, unauthenticated read-access link to exactly one document — mandatory expiry date, fully audited, revocable at any time, globally disableable installation-wide ("the feature is then not offered in the UI at all — no menu item, no reachable API route").
- **Architecture decision — directly in `document-service`, no new service**: same principle as locking (4.2)/retention/legal hold (5.2/5.2a), which already live there. New tables `share_link_config` (singleton row, `enabled`/`max_validity_days`, exactly the same pattern as `RetentionConfig`/`TrashConfig`/`UploadConfig`/`AuditTraceConfig`) and `share_link` (`token: str` as the **primary key**, `secrets.token_urlsafe(32)` — deliberately no separate, guessable ID field alongside it with the same access power).
- **Core decision 1 — token as a query parameter, not a path segment**: `gateway-service`'s `public_routes` exception list is an exact `"{service_type}:{path}"` string match without wildcard support. A per-link varying token as a path segment would have either required new matching logic at the gateway or would have been unmappable via the exact-match list at all. As a query parameter (`?token=...`), the two new `public_routes` entries (`document-service:public/share-links`, `document-service:public/share-links/content`) remain simple, static strings — only a config change, no logic change at the gateway itself.
- **Core decision 2 — deactivation returns `404` instead of `403`**: a running FastAPI instance cannot really "deregister" a route without a restart. `404` comes closer to the concept wording "no API route reachable" than `403` (which implicitly confirms that the route exists). Applied to all four affected endpoints (create, list, both public read endpoints). Deactivation deliberately also breaks ALREADY-issued links, not just new creation — `_resolve_active_share_link()` checks `config.enabled` BEFORE token validity.
- **Core decision 3 — first real `permission-service` read-permission check in `document-service`**: new `permission_client.py` (`PermissionServiceClient.check_read`, calls the already-existing `GET /check` single-resource endpoint). `document-service` previously only knew the `ApprovalClient` for the four-eyes principle, otherwise no own access control (a documented, project-wide gap, see `docs/architecture.md`). Rationale: issuing an anonymous, potentially publicly internet-reachable link to a document is qualitatively a bigger security step than yet another internal, already-authenticated API call — the same trade-off as in P14-S6's teamspace search-result filtering.
- **Public endpoints kept minimal**: `PublicShareLinkOut` returns only `title`/`content_type`/`size_bytes`/`expires_at` — no `attributes`/`folder_id`/`created_by`, per the concept wording "not beyond the minimum needed for display". Expired/revoked (but known) links return `410 Gone` with a distinguishable message instead of `404` — no information leak, since the requesting party already legitimately possesses the token.
- **Four new audit event types** (`document.share_link.created`/`.revoked`/`.viewed`/`.accessed`) use `audit-service`'s already-existing `document.>` consumption — unlike P14-S6 (new `teamspace.>`), no `audit-service` change was needed here.
- **`gateway-service`**: two new `public_routes` entries, one new test (`test_public_share_link_routes_bypass_gateway_auth_check`, same pattern as the existing fleet management route test).
- **`admin-ui`**: new `ShareLinkSettings.tsx` (checkbox `enabled` + number field `max_validity_days`, exact load/save pattern as `OcrSettings.tsx`) + new page `/share-link-settings/` (sidebar group "Security", next to superuser/forensics trace).
- **`user-ui`**: new `ShareLinkModal.tsx` (master-detail modal like `FolderRetentionModal.tsx` — list of existing links including a revoke button, creation form with expiry date) — the context-menu entry for this in `ExplorerPane.tsx` is visible ONLY when `GET /share-link-config` reports `enabled: true` (implementation of "no menu item" on deactivation). New, standalone route `/share/` (`src/app/share/page.tsx`) — **the only page in this app without `RequireAuth`** —, reads `?token=` from the URL, calls the two public gateway routes without a bearer token, shows title/expiry date/download link. Rendered client-side (no SSR), still satisfies concept 8's "no Python data access needed per request" for public exception pages, since the dynamic fetch runs via browser `fetch`, not server-side Python rendering.
- **Real bug found during test development**: `share/page.tsx`'s token state was initially initialized with `null` — exactly the value that `URLSearchParams.get("token")` also returns for a MISSING `?token=`. This made "URL not yet evaluated" (before the first effect) and "no token present" indistinguishable, the page remained permanently stuck in the loading state, regardless of the URL. Found via an own test case ("shows a hint text for a missing `?token=`") that hung on the first run instead of showing the expected text. Fixed via a third state `undefined` as a "not yet evaluated" sentinel, separate from `null` ("evaluated, no token").
- **Verification**: `document-service` 195 tests (previously 169, +26: 8 repository + 18 API including the two public endpoints, using for the first time a `_grant_document_read` test helper function against the real running `permission-service`, the same pattern as `search-service`'s `_grant_root_read`), `gateway-service` 17 (previously 16, +1), `admin-ui` 147 (previously 135, +3), `user-ui` 111 (previously 104, +7). `ruff check`/`ruff format` clean for all backend changes, `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` clean for both frontends. Full rationale (query-parameter design, 404-vs-403 interpretation, "deactivation also breaks existing links", real permission check, CSR-instead-of-SSR clarification): [ADR 0047](docs/adr/0047-public-share-link-query-param-token-and-disable-semantics.md).
- Docs: `docs/services/document-service.md` (new endpoint table rows, new section "Public share link", new "open points" row), `docs/services/gateway-service.md` (new `public_routes` entries documented), `docs/services/admin-ui.md` (new page + section + test count), `docs/services/user-ui.md` (new page + section + test count), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P14-S10 row).
- `graphify dms/ --update` still deliberately deferred (not a phase completion, follows with P14-S12).
- **Phase-14 interim status after P14-S10**: remaining Phase-14 sessions: P14-S11 (delegation/deputy, 4.4a), P14-S12 (bulk metadata editing, 8, including `graphify update` phase completion).

### Deputy Representation During Absence (Phase 14, P14-S11)

- **Task**: 4.4a requires that a person can designate one or more other people as a deputy, who can thereby take over the absent person's tasks/approvals (7.1) as a representative — deliberately NOT an identity switch: an action performed on someone's behalf is logged under the deputy's own account, with an additional note of on whose behalf it took place (5.3). Scope configurable (full or limited to object types/processes/folder areas), time-limited (start/end date), revocable early at any time by the represented person or an admin role.
- **Research before implementation yielded the decisive finding**: `workflow-service` today has NO concept whatsoever of "this task belongs to person X" — `lane` is pure, never-evaluated BPMN metadata, `completed_by` an unchecked free-text field, `GET /tasks` shows every authenticated principal, without exception, all open tasks system-wide (a documented, pre-existing state). Delegation "building on an existing access restriction" was therefore not an available option — no such restriction exists, and retrofitting one would be an independent, significantly larger undertaking (comparable to the P6-S6 retrofit for `admin.object_config`), not part of this concept task.
- **Architecture decision — `Delegation` lives in `permission-service`, not in `workflow-service`**: delegation is at its core an identity/permission question; `permission-service` already has related "who is allowed to do what" concepts (`RoleAssignment`, four-eyes `ApprovalRequest`). New table `delegation`: `delegator_principal_id`/`deputy_principal_id`, `starts_at`/`ends_at` (mandatory), `scope_object_type_ids`/`scope_process_definition_ids`/`scope_folder_resource_ids` (each a JSON list, all `None` = full takeover), never hard-deleted (`revoked_at`/`revoked_by`, same pattern as `ScopeLock`/document-service's `ShareLink` from P14-S10). Full rationale: [ADR 0048](docs/adr/0048-delegation-lives-in-permission-service-no-task-assignee-retrofit.md).
- **`GET /delegations/check` is the only real enforcement point** (same response format as the existing `GET /check`): `workflow-service`'s `POST .../tasks/{id}/complete` calls it when the request includes `on_behalf_of_principal_id`. The deputy identity checked in the process necessarily comes from the real `X-DMS-Principal` header injected by the gateway — NOT from `completed_by` (which remains an unchecked free-text field for regular, non-delegated completions) —, otherwise any caller could claim an arbitrary delegation simply by writing a different name into the free-text field.
- **Scope check only for `scope_process_definition_ids`**: of the three concept dimensions, only the process definition can be read directly from `ProcessInstance.process_definition_id` without a cross-service detour — `scope_object_type_ids`/`scope_folder_resource_ids` are stored (concept wording fully represented), but not evaluated at today's single check point (a documented limit, see ADR).
- **Audit trail — new, second envelope field `on_behalf_of`** (`dms-eventbus-client`'s `Event`, `audit-service`'s `AuditEvent`) alongside the existing `actor` (P7-S2) — `actor` always remains the deputy who actually acted, `on_behalf_of` the represented person, exactly the concept wording ("an independent, clearly attributable action instead of an identity switch ... with an additional note"). **Cutover versioning repeated**: same principle as the original `actor` cutover (P7-S2), but implemented technically differently — since the `audit_meta` row already exists (not, as with the very first cutover, newly created), `on_behalf_of_field_cutover_id` is added via a one-time ORM attribute update instead of an `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` insert.
- **`user-ui`**: new `components/DelegationsPane.tsx` (new IconRail entry 🧑‍🤝‍🧑) — two sections instead of master-detail: own designated delegations (create via username resolution like the teamspace invite, revoke button) on the left, a pure read-only list "who I represent" on the right (the actual acting happens in `reviewer-ui`).
- **`reviewer-ui`**: `TaskList.tsx` gets an "On behalf of" selector, visible ONLY with at least one active delegation for the logged-in person (`GET /delegations/active-for-deputy/{principal_id}` fetched on load) — default "For myself" corresponds to unchanged behavior.
- **`admin-ui`**: new `DelegationsAdmin` overview (`/delegations/`, sidebar group "Security") — a pure installation-wide table + admin revoke (`delegation_revoke_admin_role`, default `dms-admin`); creation deliberately remains self-service, not an admin UI feature.
- **Real cross-service pitfall found during test development itself**: `workflow-service`'s new delegation tests (real HTTP calls against the running `permission-service`, no mocking, same principle as `_grant_config_admin_permission` in its `conftest.py`) initially failed with `404 Not Found` on `/delegations` — the already-running `dms-permission-service-1` container still had the OLD image from before this session (`scripts/run-tests.sh <service>` only builds/starts the tested service itself, not its cross-service dependencies). Fixed via an explicit `docker compose build permission-service && docker compose up -d permission-service` before the `workflow-service` test run — not a code bug, but a real pattern relevant for future sessions: whoever tests service A against an already-running service B freshly changed in the SAME session must explicitly rebuild/restart B, `scripts/run-tests.sh A` does not do that automatically for B.
- **Tests**: `permission-service` 115 (previously 98, +17: 8 repository + 9 API), `dms-eventbus-client` 11 (previously 9, +2), `audit-service` 31 (previously 26, +5, including a more realistic cutover edge-case test than the original `actor` cutover — here rows already exist WITH `actor` but WITHOUT `on_behalf_of`), `workflow-service` 171 (previously 166, +5, including three real cross-service tests against the newly built `permission-service` container), `user-ui` 117 (previously 111, +6), `reviewer-ui` 18 (previously 15, +3), `admin-ui` 152 (previously 147, +5). `ruff check`/`ruff format` clean for all backend changes, `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` clean for all three frontends (including a pre-existing, session-unrelated `reviewer-ui` `node_modules` defect — missing native `@rollup/rollup-linux-x64-gnu` binary component, a known npm optional-dependency bug — fixed via a fresh `npm install`).
- Docs: `docs/services/permission-service.md` (new endpoint table rows, new section "Deputy representation during absence", new data-model/events/open-points entries), `docs/services/workflow-service.md` (new section, updated task-complete row/events table/open points), `docs/services/audit-service.md` (new section "on_behalf_of field", updated data model/filter API/tests/open points), `docs/services/user-ui.md`/`reviewer-ui.md`/`admin-ui.md` (each a new section + test count), `libs/dms-eventbus-client/README.md` (event field list expanded, had already been incomplete regarding `actor` before this session), new [ADR 0048](docs/adr/0048-delegation-lives-in-permission-service-no-task-assignee-retrofit.md), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P14-S11 row).
- `graphify dms/ --update` still deliberately deferred (not a phase completion, follows with P14-S12, the last Phase-14 session).
- **Serious, session-unrelated security finding during live verification, fixed immediately**: while testing `GET /delegations/check` via the gateway, it turned out that a self-supplied `X-DMS-Principal` header arrived at the downstream despite a valid bearer token — NOT the real value derived from the JWT. Root cause isolated and reproduced with a minimal Python snippet against Starlette's real `Headers` class: `gateway_service.upstream.filter_headers()` returns a `dict` whose keys take on the ASGI-normalized (lowercased) spelling of the incoming client header (`"x-dms-principal"`), while `proxy()`'s `identity_headers = {"X-DMS-Principal": ...}` is capitalized in the source code — `headers.update(identity_headers)` on a Python `dict` only overwrites keys spelled exactly the same, `"x-dms-principal"` and `"X-DMS-Principal"` are two different ones. Both headers thereby arrived at the downstream, which value "won" depended on the HTTP parser — empirically the spoofed client value won. **Scope**: every authenticated principal (the bearer-token requirement itself remained intact) could thereby impersonate any other person/with arbitrary roles toward any header-consuming backend service — affected every check built on this header since P5e-S2 (identifier admin role, P14-S6 teamspace membership, P14-S10 share-link creator/admin revoke, now P14-S11 delegation). **Fix**: `filter_headers()` now removes every incoming header whose lowercased name starts with `"x-dms-"`, regardless of exact spelling, before the real identity headers are set. Since `X-DMS-*` headers are exclusively intended for the internal gateway trust chain (no legitimate client ever sends them itself), a blanket removal without an exception list is safe and future-proof. **Re-verified live** (the same spoofing attempt after rebuilding/restarting the `gateway-service` container demonstrably returns the real identity), own tests added (3 pure `filter_headers()` unit tests + 1 real cross-service integration test against the running `permission-service` as an echo target — `POST /delegations` mirrors `X-DMS-Principal` 1:1 into `delegator_principal_id`). `gateway-service` 21 tests (previously 17, +4). Full rationale: [ADR 0049](docs/adr/0049-gateway-header-spoofing-fix-strip-client-x-dms-headers.md). `docs/services/gateway-service.md` updated (auth-validation section + open-points entry, also correcting a statement left standing since ADR 0005 that had by now become false, "currently not consumed by any backend service").
- **Phase-14 interim status after P14-S11**: remaining Phase-14 session: P14-S12 (bulk metadata editing, 8, including the `graphify update` phase completion — **last session of Phase 14**).

### Bulk Metadata Editing (Phase 14, P14-S12)

- **Task**: concept §8 requires that several selected documents/folders can be jointly assigned the same attribute values in one step — runs through the same rule engine/constraint checking as an individual change (4.5), i.e. it deliberately fails for individual, non-matching objects within the selection instead of silently putting the entire selection into an inconsistent state; the result (per-object success/failure) is clearly summarized and displayed after completion.
- **Architecture decision — deliberately frontend-only, no new backend endpoint**: `user-ui`'s new `components/BulkEditModal.tsx` runs, for each selected object individually, through the already-existing single-object PATCH endpoint (`PATCH /documents/{id}`/`PATCH /folders/{id}`), thereby validating through exactly the same code path as an individual change — satisfies concept §8 literally ("same rule engine/constraint checking as an individual change"), without reimplementing validation a second time in a new bulk endpoint. Full rationale: [ADR 0050](docs/adr/0050-bulk-metadata-edit-frontend-loop-over-existing-patch-endpoints.md).
- **Non-atomic result arises by itself**: a loop of independent single PATCH calls delivers the concept requirement "fails specifically for individual, non-matching objects instead of silently making the entire selection inconsistent" without any extra code — each call succeeds/fails independently of the others, the modal afterward shows a result list per object (success/failure, on failure the error message extracted from the `400` body).
- **Homogeneous selection as a deliberate boundary**: attribute schemas are bound to object types — a single form can only meaningfully render one schema. `BulkEditModal.tsx` therefore requires exclusively documents OR exclusively folders, all with the same, set `object_type_id` — otherwise a blocking hint text instead of a form. `title`/name is deliberately not part of the form (no sensible shared application of an identical name to multiple objects).
- **Empty field = "leave unchanged", not "clear"**: the server replaces `attributes` entirely (no server-side merge, existing behavior). Dedicated research before implementation identified the risk of a naive bulk call (only sending filled fields), which would otherwise have silently deleted, for every object, all other attributes not present in the bulk form. On submit, therefore, only actually filled fields are merged into a copy of each object's own existing `attributes` — the identical pattern to `MetadataPanel.tsx`'s single-object editing.
- **Real bug found before implementation via dedicated research, in `folder-service`**: `PATCH /folders/{id}` had so far only run the object-type constraint check on an actual move (`is_move`) — a pure attribute/name change without a move bypassed it entirely, unlike `document-service`'s `update_document` (there, validation runs on EVERY PATCH request as soon as an object type is set). Without this fix, bulk editing of folders would have factually not enforced the constraint check required by the concept for folders — fixed, now symmetric with `document-service`: validates on every change, `_validate_against_object_type()`'s placement parameters are only populated on an actual move, otherwise left empty (no behavior change for the move case itself).
- **Multi-selection in `ExplorerPane.tsx`**: new checkboxes per row, exclusively in the list view (tree-view multi-selection explicitly out of scope for this session). Selection as a `Set<"kind:id">` (same pattern as the existing favorites keys), automatically cleared on folder change or toggling the trash view, so it never points to objects that are no longer visible. As soon as at least one entry is selected, a second toolbar row appears ("N selected", "Bulk edit" button, "Clear selection" button).
- **Tests**: `folder-service` 79 (previously 75, +4: PATCH without a move is now validated — rejection and success cases, a PATCH with only `name` still correctly validates against the existing attributes, an untyped folder remains unaffected), `user-ui` 129 (previously 117, +12: 7 new, standalone `bulk-edit-modal.test.tsx` — hint texts for mixed object types/mixed document-folder selection/missing object type, loading object type+layout for a homogeneous selection, applying a filled field to all objects while preserving each object's other own attributes, a left-empty field leaves attributes unchanged, result list shows success AND failure side by side instead of all-or-nothing — plus 5 new integration tests in `document-workspace.test.tsx`: toolbar appears only from a selection onward and can be cleared, modal opens with a correctly filtered selection including a real submit against two documents, blocking hint for a mixed selection, selection is cleared when navigating into a subfolder and when toggling trash). `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean, `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` clean (only the pre-existing, session-unrelated `PreviewPane.tsx` `<img>` lint hint).
- Docs: `docs/services/folder-service.md` (new bugfix paragraph in the "object-type validation" section, updated test count), `docs/services/user-ui.md` (new section "Bulk metadata editing", updated test count), new [ADR 0050](docs/adr/0050-bulk-metadata-edit-frontend-loop-over-existing-patch-endpoints.md), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P14-S12 row, ✅).
- **Full backend regression** (`scripts/run-tests.sh --build`) across all 31 services green — including `folder-service` 79, `user-ui` typecheck/lint/test/build clean. Only deviation `webdav-connector` (9 failed) — a pre-existing, session-unrelated `ReadTimeout` flake (confirmed via `git status`/`git diff` as untouched by this session, the identical, already-at-P14-S10-documented pattern).
- **Live verification of the `folder-service` fix against the real running Compose stack** (curl, logged in as `config-admin`): a new object type with a required attribute `Projektnummer` (`applies_to: folder`) created, a test folder with a valid attribute value created — a pure attribute PATCH without a `parent_id` change that removes the required field is now correctly rejected with `400`/`"Required field 'Projektnummer' missing"` (before the fix this would have gone through unvalidated); a PATCH with a still-valid value returns `200`. Test artifacts (folder, object type) subsequently deleted again. **No browser available for visual/E2E testing in this development environment** (existing limitation documented since P4-S4) — `BulkEditModal.tsx`'s multi-selection/bulk editing itself is therefore secured exclusively via the 12 new Vitest component/integration tests, not visually confirmed in a real browser; a human should click through the UI before production use.
- **Phase 14 thereby fully completed** — `graphify dms/ --update` executed following this session (phase completion, see "Tooling & Testing"/graphify history).

### Area Structure: Special Areas — Phase Kickoff (Phase 15, P15-S0)

- **Task**: pure verification/planning session (no implementation code) — check concept §2.5 against the current code/service state, in particular the three dependencies that P15-S3/S5/S6 presuppose per `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (Phase 7/records disposal, Phase 12/config import-export, Phase 13/Federation Hub), adjust the session breakdown if needed.
- **All three dependency phases confirmed fully completed**: Phase 7 (`archival-service` with a complete `pending → locked → copied → verified → released → dehydrated` state machine since P7-S3/S3b, including XDOMEA for circulation folders), Phase 12 (`config-service` since P12-S3, five configuration categories plus `federation_config` since P13-S3), Phase 13 (Federation Hub including real end-to-end encryption and signature hardening since P6-S9/P13-S4). No blocking gap for any of the planned sessions.
- **Team workspace already done**: the concept table in §2.5 now also lists "Team workspace (Teamspace)" as a special area — this was already fully implemented at P14-S6, so it is no longer an open point of the remaining Phase-15 sessions (the table was subsequently amended with this entry when Teamspace was brought forward during a P12b-S1 completeness review).
- **Real finding 1 — P15-S6 (templates) CANNOT build on `config-service`'s existing 7.3 export, as the concept text suggests**: `config-service/src/config_service/schemas.py`'s `CATEGORIES` comprises exclusively schema-level configuration (`object_types`, `workflows`, `dmn_definitions`, `business_calendars`, `roles`, `approval_config`, `sensor_config`, `federation_config`) — no real folder-tree data instances. A "file-plan rough draft" (a concrete folder hierarchy with names/object-type assignments) is thereby a completely new capability, sensibly built directly in `folder-service` (the service that already owns the tree and brings `ROOT_FOLDER_ID` as a precedent for well-defined special nodes), not as a sixth `config-service` category. `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s P15-S6 row was corrected accordingly.
- **Real finding 2 — P15-S2 (quarantine) needs two new `virus-scan-service` endpoints that don't exist today**: currently only `POST /scan`/`GET /scans/{id}`/`GET /scans`, no release or delete endpoint. Important architecture fact found during research: an infected upload, per `virus_scan_service/main.py`'s own docstring, **never** creates a `Document` ("quarantine instead of automatic deletion ... no access via the regular document path, since no document is ever created for it") — a "release after false positive" (2.5 table) therefore does not mean lifting an existing lock, but actually creating a new document from the bytes already sitting in `quarantine/{scan_id}` via `document-service`, including specifying a target folder (not yet known at the time of the original scan). `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s P15-S2 row was extended with this finding.
- **Confirmation — the domain-separated admin-role pattern (4.6) is already proven three times over**: `kennzeichen_admin_role` (`document-service`, P5e-S3), `share_link_revoke_admin_role` (`document-service`, P14-S10), `delegation_revoke_admin_role` (`permission-service`, P14-S11) — all following the same pattern (installation-wide setting, default `"dms-admin"`, checked against `X-DMS-Roles`). P15-S1 can directly reuse this pattern for two new settings (`trash_hard_delete_admin_role`/`classified_trash_hard_delete_admin_role`), without designing a new mechanism.
- **Real, pre-existing gap confirmed (to be closed with P15-S1, not at P15-S0 itself)**: `document-service`'s `DELETE /documents/{document_id}` (the actual permanent deletion, to be distinguished from `POST /documents/{id}/trash`, the soft delete) is today **completely ungated** — no role check, any authenticated caller can permanently delete a document. Consistent with the general, already repeatedly documented authorization-gap status of this project (see e.g. the P14-S11 gateway-header finding), no new category of problem, but a concrete, now-known starting point for P15-S1.
- **`auth-service` gap for P15-S4 (contacts) confirmed**: `GET /users` is gated behind `admin.user_management` (intended only for admin UI user management), `GET /users/lookup?username=` is open to any authenticated user (since P14-S6, for teamspace invitations/delegation), but only exact single-name resolution, no searchable/browsable list. P15-S4 needs a new, ungated directory endpoint (e.g. `GET /users/directory`) that reuses `admin_users.list_users()` without the `admin.user_management` gate.
- **`ROOT_FOLDER_ID` pattern identified as a template for inbox/outbox**: `folder-service`'s fixed `root` folder row, automatically created at service startup (`folder_service/settings.py`), is the obvious, already-proven blueprint for two further fixed special nodes (inbox/outbox, P15-S3). For delineation: the remaining special areas are **not** new folder nodes, but pure filtered views onto already-existing data — trash variants (`deleted_at` filter, some additionally filtered by classification attribute/creator), quarantine (own `ScanResult` table), contacts (Keycloak user list), records disposal (`archival-service` transfers in the `released` state within the transition period).
- **Open decision identified for P15-S3 (a question at session start needed, same procedure as P12-S1's WebDAV-vs-CMIS question)**: `notification-service` has exclusively outbound SMTP (notification dispatch) — **no** inbound email connector (IMAP or similar) exists anywhere in the code. The concept names an email connector only as an example ("e.g."), not as a mandatory component. P15-S3 must therefore decide at session start whether a real connector is built (significantly larger scope, real protocol integration) or whether the core mechanics (receiving special folder + mail-room role + assignment UI/workflow) remain connector-agnostic for now (a generic receiving endpoint that a future connector can serve later, analogous to the already-established `libs/dms-connector-sdk` principle).
- **No session split needed**: despite both corrections, each of the six remaining sessions (P15-S1–S6) stays within the scope of a single session — both findings shift WHERE something is built (which service), not HOW MUCH.
- Docs: `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P15-S0 row ✅ with complete findings, P15-S1/S2/S3/S4/S6 rows extended with the respective findings, P15-S5 confirmed unchanged).
- `graphify dms/ --update` deliberately NOT run (pure planning session without code change, and the standing rule "only at the end of an entire phase" applies anyway — Phase 15 has only just started with P15-S0).

### Trash Family (Phase 15, P15-S1)

- **Task**: concept §2.5 requires three related trash special areas — regular trash (deletion marking by regular users, permanent deletion only by a dedicated deletion-administration role, 4.6), personal trash (each user sees only their own deletion markings), classified-documents trash (structurally separate, its own, narrower deletion-administration role, so that regular deletion-admin staff does not automatically gain insight into classified content).
- **Correction of the P15-S0 analysis during implementation**: the P15-S0 research had mistakenly identified `DELETE /documents/{document_id}` as the "actual, ungated hard-delete path". A closer check at P15-S1 found: `repository.delete_document` (used jointly by this endpoint AND `POST /documents/{id}/trash`) is a pure SOFT deletion (`deleted_at` set) — the endpoint itself is furthermore, per its own docstring, deliberately left ungated **because no frontend ever calls it** (an already-existing, deliberate decision, not a forgotten finding). The actual, permanent physical deletion (`repository.hard_delete_document`/`hard_delete_folder`) had so far had **no manual, on-demand endpoint whatsoever** — only the automatic `_retention_poll_loop`, which waits out `TrashConfig.restore_period_days`. That was the actually missing building block, not gating an already-dead endpoint.
- **`is_classified` as a new, schema-bound `ObjectType` field instead of an attribute-value convention**: concept §2.5 textually allows both ("marking via an object type/an attribute"), but an object-type property is more robust (no typo/empty-string risk) and can be resolved for the entire object-type set in a single call (`GET /object-types?applies_to=document&is_classified=true`, new filter) instead of looking it up per document individually. Only permitted for `applies_to="document"` (422 otherwise) — folders, per the concept text, know no classified-document marking.
- **New manual purge endpoint builds on the already-existing `retention_actions.purge_expired_trash_entry()` function** (so far only called by the poll loop after the retention period expires) — extended with optional `trigger`/`triggered_by` parameters (`"manual_purge"` instead of `"trash_expiry"`, `triggered_by` = the real principal from `X-DMS-Principal`), no second deletion implementation. `POST /documents/{id}/purge` checks the classification of the affected document (via `object-type-service`) and requires the respectively appropriate role; `POST /folders/{id}/purge` additionally requires the same non-empty check as the automatic forced deletion (`has_any_child_folder_row`/`document_client.count_active`, otherwise `409` — prevents an FK constraint violation).
- **Additive `scope` query parameter instead of retrofitting the existing endpoints**: `GET /documents/deleted`/`GET /folders/deleted` are currently called by numerous tests/callers completely without `X-DMS-*` headers — hard-enforcing auth checks on the existing, folder-related path would have broken that in a cascade. The new `scope` parameter (`personal`/`admin`/`admin_classified` for documents, `personal`/`admin` for folders) is purely additive: without it, the endpoint remains exactly as before (confirmed by an unchanged existing test), the new, actually enforced visibility rules apply only to callers who explicitly set it.
- **Real bug found during implementation**: `deleted_by` was accepted as a parameter in `document-service` (`repository.delete_document`, `cascade_trash_by_folder_ids`) AND `folder-service` (`repository.soft_delete_folder`) at several points, but NEVER actually written to the respective row — a prerequisite that personal trash first made truly visible. Fixed via a new `deleted_by` column on `Document`/`Folder` (expand-contract migration pattern) plus the corresponding assignments in all affected functions (including `cascade_restore_by_via_folder_id`/`restore_folder`, which reset the value again on restore).
- **Frontend: new, installation-wide `TrashPane` special area instead of extending the existing trash toggle**: the already-existing toggle in `ExplorerPane.tsx` deliberately shows only what is deleted IN THE CURRENTLY OPEN FOLDER (part of normal explorer navigation, remains unchanged) — that is not the same as an installation-wide special area (2.5). New `IconRail` entry 🗑️, three views via a `scope` switcher, client-side role-gated (`useAuth().user.realm_roles`, same pattern as `MetadataPanel.tsx`'s identifier-admin check). Deliberately no "open" button — `folder-service`'s regular `GET /folders/{id}` treats a folder already in the trash as nonexistent, an in-place preview would have been an independent feature.
- **Ripple fixes found and fixed at two further `ObjectType` consumers**: `is_classified` would otherwise have silently been lost or remained unusable at two points. `config-service`'s `ObjectTypeExport` schema is exhaustive (export/import roundtrip, 7.3) — without the new field, a configuration export would have silently dropped a document type's classification when distributing it to another installation (`export.py`/`imports.py`'s `_OBJECT_TYPE_MUTABLE_FIELDS` extended, new roundtrip test). `admin-ui`'s `ObjectTypeEditor.tsx`/`lib/api.ts` are the ONLY UI path to create/edit an object type at all — without this fix, no admin could ever have marked a document class as classified, the entire feature would have remained unoperable via the UI (new checkbox "Classified document", visible only for `appliesTo="document"`, same pattern as the existing identifier/signature field restriction).
- **Real schema drift found and fixed live**: live verification against the long-lived dev stack revealed that `document.document.object_type_id` still carried `VARCHAR(128)` there (a leftover from an early project phase — `create_all` never changes the type of already-existing columns), although the model has long declared `Integer`. Simple equality comparisons remained unaffected, but the new `object_type_id NOT IN (...)` filter for the classified-documents trash reproducibly failed with `operator does not exist: character varying <> integer` (confirmed both via API AND directly via `psql`). `folder.folder.object_type_id` was correct. Fixed via a protected migration check in `document-service`'s lifespan (`information_schema.columns` check before `ALTER COLUMN ... TYPE INTEGER`, a no-op on already-correct installations) instead of a purely manual one-off fix. Fully documented in [ADR 0051](docs/adr/0051-papierkorb-familie-classification-via-object-type-scoped-global-endpoints.md) "Live verification finding".
- **Tests**: `object-type-service` 85 (previously 81, +4: `is_classified` validation create/update, default value), `document-service` 211 (previously 195, +16: `deleted_by` persistence/reset/filtering at the repository level, `scope` visibility including 401/403/classification split at the API level, purge endpoint including 404/409/403/204 as well as deletion-register verification), `folder-service` 93 (previously 79, +14: analogous pattern, plus one test for the non-empty check on manual purge), `config-service` 36 (previously 35, +1: `is_classified` export/import roundtrip against the real running object-type-service), `user-ui` 132 (previously 129, +3: integration tests in `document-workspace.test.tsx`, no standalone component test — the same precedent pattern as `MetadataPanel.tsx`, which is likewise only tested via the full workspace harness, since both components call `useAuth()` directly), `admin-ui` 154 (previously 152, +2: classified-document checkbox visible + submitted only for document classes, loads the existing flag when editing). `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean, `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` clean for both frontends.
- **Live verification against the real running Compose stack** (curl, `config-admin` temporarily equipped with the real Keycloak `dms-admin` role, then revoked again afterward): a classified + a regular object type/document created, both trashed, `scope=personal`/`admin`/`admin_classified` each return exactly the expected, structurally separated subset (confirmed including the above-mentioned schema-drift fix), `deleted_by` is now actually persisted, `POST .../purge` returns `409` before trashing, correctly permanently removes documents (deletion register shows `trigger="manual_purge"` + correct `triggered_by`), folder purge additionally with a remaining subfolder row `409`. A spoofed `X-DMS-Roles` header was correctly replaced by the gateway with the real, JWT-derived roles in the process (ADR-0049 protection still effective) — confirmed, but this makes a clean "403 without role" live test impossible, already comprehensively covered by API tests for that (controlled headers in the in-process `TestClient`). All test artifacts subsequently cleaned up.
- **Session-unrelated, but found and fixed during the regression itself, infrastructure bug**: the full `scripts/run-tests.sh --build` run intermittently failed with `permission-service FAIL ... 14 errors` (`nats: JetStream.Error consumer is already bound to a subscription`) — the actual root cause of a NATS timing problem only ever crudely worked around since P4-S4, now fixed at the root in `libs/dms-eventbus-client` (`close()` now uses `drain()` instead of `nc.close()`). Full derivation/verification see "Tooling & Testing" below, section "Root cause of the ... NATS trap workaround from P4-S4 ... finally found and fixed".
- Docs: `docs/services/object-type-service.md` (new section "Classified-document marking"), `docs/services/document-service.md` (new section "Trash family" + schema-drift finding), `docs/services/folder-service.md` (new section "Trash family"), `docs/services/user-ui.md` (new section, updated test count), new [ADR 0051](docs/adr/0051-papierkorb-familie-classification-via-object-type-scoped-global-endpoints.md) (including live-verification-finding section), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P15-S1 row, ✅, including correction of the P15-S0 analysis).
- `graphify dms/ --update` deliberately deferred (not a phase completion, Phase 15 continues through P15-S6).

### Quarantine Area (Phase 15, P15-S2)

- **Task**: concept §2.5/10.3 requires a UI exposure of the already-existing virus-scan quarantine as its own, permission-restricted area with two audited actions — "delete permanently" and "release" (after clarifying a false positive), the latter explicitly "no silent restoration".
- **Central architecture conflict found during implementation**: `virus-scan-service`'s `POST /scan` had already been confirmed missing at P15-S0, but the obvious release implementation ("just call `document-service`'s existing `POST /documents` with the quarantined bytes") is structurally impossible — `POST /documents` unconditionally scans every attachment (10.3, ADR 0010), a repeat call would only reproduce the same (now classified as a false positive) finding, most obviously with the standard `eicar` test engine, which always recognizes an EICAR signature as infected, regardless of context. Release via the regular path would thus NEVER have been possible.
- **New internal creation path `POST /documents/from-quarantine-release`** (document-service) deliberately bypasses exactly this scan step. Refactoring instead of duplication: `_prepare_document_fields`/`_persist_new_document` consolidate the logic shared between `POST /documents` and the new endpoint (derivation check, folder/object-type validation, identifier assignment, retention defaults, storage upload, `repository.create_document`, event publication) — only the `virus_scan_client.scan()` call remains exclusive to the public path.
