- **Two independent role gates instead of one**: this project has no internal service-to-service auth mechanism anywhere (no shared secret) — every existing cross-service call relies on network trust within the Docker network. An endpoint that bypasses the mandatory virus scan is security-relevant enough not to depend on a single check: `virus-scan-service`'s new `quarantine_admin_role` setting (default `dms-admin`) checks first, `document-service`'s new, independent `quarantine_release_admin_role` setting checks a second time based on the passed-through `X-DMS-Principal`/`X-DMS-Roles` headers.
- **One role for viewing, release, AND permanent deletion** (unlike P15-S1's separate regular/classified roles) — concept §2.5 literally speaks of "one own, narrowly scoped role" that covers all three actions, no concept basis for an artificial split.
- **Additive `status` query parameter on `GET /scans`** (same additive pattern as P15-S1's `scope` parameter): only `status=infected` is role-gated (401/403), any other/no `status` remains exactly as before ungated — existing callers (e.g. a scan-status display in a document context) remain unaffected.
- **`ScanResult` gets two new, generic columns `resolved_by`/`resolved_at`** instead of four separate `released_*`/`purged_*` columns — both actions are mutually exclusive end states, `status` already disambiguates which applies. `quarantine_object_key` is never set to `NULL`, even after the bytes themselves have been removed — remains as a historical reference, same pattern as `DeletionRegisterEntry` at P15-S1.
- **`StorageClient` (virus-scan-service) extended with `download`/`delete`** (previously only had `upload`, since nothing had ever been read back from/removed from quarantine before this session) — identical scope to `document-service`'s client of the same name.
- **Frontend: new `QuarantinePane` special area (IconRail ☣️)** — unlike trash, per the concept there is NO "personal" view here: the icon-rail entry itself (not just the actions within it) is client-side role-gated in `IconRail.tsx` and invisible to all other users. "Release" opens a small inline form (title pre-filled with the file name, optional target folder as free text instead of a dedicated folder picker) instead of a single button, since `title` was never known at the time of the originally failed upload.
- **No new entry in `audit-service`'s `FORCE_DELETION_EVENT_TYPES` ledger (10.4)** for `virus_scan.purged` — the regular, already-subscribed-since-P5-S1 `virus_scan.>` audit trail already fully satisfies concept §2.5's "audited operation (5.3)"; the separate append-only ledger is, per an existing code comment, deliberately limited to forced physical deletions of active (not already-quarantined) objects.
- **Tests**: `document-service` 219 (previously 215, +4: role gate 401/403, EICAR content is deliberately accepted via the new path, dedicated event type including `source_scan_id` payload), `virus-scan-service` 32 (previously 17, +15: `status` filter at the repository and API level, `mark_resolved` for both end states, `/release`/`/purge` including role/404/409 cases, "not releasable/deletable a second time"), `user-ui` 138 (previously 132, +6: new standalone `quarantine-pane.test.tsx` — unlike `TrashPane`, `QuarantinePane` itself renders no `useAuth()` role check, an isolated component test suffices). `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean, `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` clean.
- **Live verification against the real running Compose stack** (curl via the gateway, real login via `POST /api/auth-service/login`; `document-service`/`virus-scan-service`/`user-ui` freshly rebuilt beforehand, since `docker compose up -d` does not automatically rebuild an already-existing image): a real EICAR upload via the normal `POST /documents` path was rejected with `422 virus_detected` and landed in quarantine; `GET /scans?status=infected` returned `403` without and (after a temporary, once again revoked afterward like at P15-S1, real Keycloak `dms-admin` role assignment to `config-admin`) `200` with the appropriate role; release created a real, confirmed-via-`GET /documents/{id}` document AND removed the quarantine bytes from the storage service (`404` on a repeat `GET`); a second release attempt returned `409`; a second, independent quarantine case was instead permanently deleted (`204`, bytes likewise `404` afterward, the row remains with `status="purged"` as evidence, a second deletion attempt `409`). `docker compose config` validated (no cycles due to the deliberately absent `depends_on: document-service` in `virus-scan-service`'s Compose block). **No browser available in this session environment** (no Playwright/Chromium/`xvfb` installed) — `QuarantinePane`'s actual DOM rendering/interaction is therefore secured ONLY via the Vitest component tests (jsdom) as well as the full backend API verification above through exactly the same gateway calls described above, not visually confirmed by a human — an open limitation, see `CONTRIBUTING.md`/PROGRESS.md convention "for UI sessions without an available browser, communicate explicitly".
- Docs: `docs/services/virus-scan-service.md` (new endpoints/events, updated tests/open-points sections), `docs/services/document-service.md` (new section "Quarantine area"), `docs/services/user-ui.md` (new section, updated test count), new [ADR 0052](docs/adr/0052-quarantaene-bereich-internal-creation-endpoint-bypasses-rescan.md), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P15-S2 row, ✅).
- `graphify dms/ --update` deliberately deferred (not a phase completion, Phase 15 continues through P15-S6).

### Inbox/Outbox (Phase 15, P15-S3)

- **Task**: concept §2.5/3.3/7.1 requires a technical receipt/dispatch of external correspondence, review/assignment by a dedicated mail-room role into a file plan or a (new/existing) circulation folder (2.3). Identified at P15-S0 as an open decision: **a question at session start needed** (real email connector vs. connector-agnostic core mechanics).
- **Question result (two rounds)**: the first question clarified real connector vs. bare-bones in principle — the user tied the answer directly to a second, more important question: whether assignment can be automated based on identifier/case number, and whether unassignable mail stays in the inbox (confirmed as expected behavior). Since that needed a broader decision basis, research was done before implementation: identifier is only unique per object type + year (no DB uniqueness, no cross-type lookup capability), circulation folders so far had no dedicated, searchable reference field at all. On this basis, two further questions: (1) match scope only document identifier or additionally a new case field — user chose **additionally**; (2) automation level on a unique hit fully automatic or suggestion-with-confirmation — user chose **suggestion with confirmation**.
- **Real connector, really tested**: `mailpit` (already present as a dev SMTP server) has, since v1.15, its own POP3 server activatable via `--pop3-auth-file` — verified via a real `poplib` round trip (SMTP delivery → POP3 pickup) before implementation began that this actually works. New `mail-connector` service fetches via this using the standard protocol (`backends/interface.py`'s `MailboxBackend`, same plugin principle as storage backends/virus-scan engines) — a self-loopback test without external infrastructure, the same principle as the Federation Hub self-loopback (P6-S9).
- **`Case.vorgangsnummer` as a new, first-class, installation-wide unique field** — deliberately NOT via the existing, document-specific identifier generator (`object-type-service`'s `applies_to` is strictly limited to `"document"`/`"folder"`, extending it to `"case"` would have risked several already-existing validation paths). Instead, its own, simpler single-counter generator directly in `case-service` (`case_number_config`/`case_sequence`, the same atomic `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` idiom as `object_type_service.ObjectTypeSequence`). New endpoints `GET`/`PUT /case-number-config`, `GET /cases/by-vorgangsnummer`.
- **Generic regex candidate extractor instead of a format-precise derivation**: `matching.py` searches for `X-Y`-like tokens (2-10 alphanumeric characters per side) in subject+body, checks each candidate against `GET /documents/by-kennzeichen` (new, document-service) AND `GET /cases/by-vorgangsnummer`. Exactly one hit across both reference types → `status="proposed_match"`; no hit or an ambiguous hit → `status="unassigned"`, all candidates remain visible for manual assignment.
- **The body text itself counts as the first (synthetic) attachment** — wrapped as `{Betreff}.txt` at the ingestion step and sent, just like every real file attachment, through the mandatory virus scan (10.3) (synergy with the P15-S2 quarantine: an infected attachment automatically lands there, no special handling needed here).
- **Two new, protected special folders `inbox`/`outbox`** (folder-service, same fixed-ID pattern as `root`) — unlike `root`, they are protected against renaming/moving/hard-deleting/trashing (409), since a special area, per the concept, "exists exactly once per installation". While building this protection it turned out that `root` itself never had this protection — deliberately not closed retroactively (see "Open Points" in `docs/services/folder-service.md`).
- **`sent_by` for outbox dispatch comes server-side from `X-DMS-Principal`**, not from the request body — originally designed as a body field, corrected during implementation itself (same principle as `deleted_by`/`confirmed_by`/`resolved_by` elsewhere in this project: no client-forged authorship).
- **Real bug found and fixed during live verification**: the document title supplied by the mail-room in the form (`ConfirmMatchRequest`/`AssignRequest.title`) was never actually passed on to `document-service` by `_create_documents_for_message` — every newly created document instead always got the raw attachment file name as its title (e.g. `"Rueckmeldung zu Az_ 2026-livetest99.txt"` instead of the form-entered `"Rueckmeldung zur Buergeranfrage"`). Only became visible during the real live test via the gateway, since the automated tests only checked the folder, not the title, of the created document — the test gap was fixed directly along with it (`test_confirm_match_creates_document_in_matched_folder` now additionally checks `created["title"]`). Fix: `title` applies to the first (deterministically body-text-derived) document, further attachments keep their own file name as the title, since a single form title cannot sensibly apply to multiple different attachments at once.
- **Tests**: `folder-service` 99 (previously 93, +6: `inbox`/`outbox` exist + sit under `root`, renaming/moving/hard-deleting/trashing `inbox` each `409`, a pure attribute change remains allowed), `document-service` 219 (previously 215, +4: identifier lookup hit/empty/excludes deleted/returns multiple hits on collision), `case-service` 45 (previously 39, +6: unique `vorgangsnummer` per new circulation folder, vorgangsnummer lookup hit/empty, `case-number-config` roundtrip including rejection of invalid formats), `mail-connector` 30 (new service: candidate extraction, unique/ambiguous/missing hit via fake clients, repository CRUD, complete API including a real case fixture via the real workflow-service/case-service, confirm/assign/reject including 400/409, outbound dispatch against real mailpit), `user-ui` 144 (previously 138, +6: new standalone `poststelle-pane.test.tsx`). `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean, `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` clean.
- **Test isolation gap found and fixed**: `case-service`'s `_clean_tables` fixture did not truncate `case_number_config`/`case_sequence`, causing a format string set via PUT in an earlier test run to leak into a later test run (`test_case_number_config_get_put_roundtrip` intermittently failed as a result, depending on which tests ran before it) — both tables added to the fixture, the same category of finding as originally with `case_archival_config`.
- **Live verification against the real running Compose stack** (curl via the gateway, real login; `mail-connector`/`audit-service`/`user-ui` as well as `folder-service`/`document-service`/`case-service` freshly rebuilt beforehand): a new Keycloak realm role `dms-poststelle` was created (permanent, a real prerequisite of this feature — unlike the purely test-only role **assignment** to `config-admin`, which was revoked again afterward like at P15-S1/S2) — a real SMTP→POP3 round trip via `mailpit` was successfully picked up, parsed (text+PDF attachment), scanned, and correctly classified with `status="unassigned"` or (after PATCHing a unique test identifier) `status="proposed_match"`; `confirm-match` created a real document in the reference document's folder (including the title bugfix described above), a second confirmation attempt returned `409`; manual assignment with `case_id` additionally created a real circulation-folder reference; rejection and outbox dispatch (`sent_by` correctly derived from the principal) likewise confirmed. A random identifier/case-number collision between test artifacts from earlier live tests confirmed live, in the process, the correct "ambiguous → unassigned" handling. `docker compose config` validated. **No browser available in this session environment** — `PoststellePane`'s DOM rendering/interaction is therefore secured only via Vitest/jsdom as well as the full backend API verification through exactly the same gateway calls, not visually confirmed (same open limitation as P15-S2).
- Docs: `docs/services/mail-connector.md` (new), `docs/services/folder-service.md`/`document-service.md`/`case-service.md`/`user-ui.md` (new sections/table rows), `docs/services/audit-service.md` (new `mail_connector.>` subject, noting a pre-existing, not-fixed-here backlog gap of the enumeration versus `Settings.subjects`), new [ADR 0053](docs/adr/0053-posteingang-postausgang-pop3-loopback-connector-and-cross-service-matching.md), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P15-S3 row, ✅).
- `graphify dms/ --update` deliberately deferred (not a phase completion, Phase 15 continues through P15-S6).

### Contacts (Phase 15, P15-S4)

- **Task**: concept §2.5/4.4/7.4 requires a directory for finding other staff members — "local, always available", optionally cross-installation as an extension of the Federation Hub. Confirmed at P15-S0: `GET /users` (auth-service) is admin-gated, `GET /users/lookup` only offers exact single resolution — both unsuitable for a general contact search. No question at session start marked as needed — all architecture decisions of this session were made autonomously and documented in [ADR 0054](docs/adr/0054-kontakte-directory-independent-second-federation-identity-per-installation.md).
- **New, ungated `GET /users/directory?q=` endpoint**: search via Keycloak's built-in `search` query parameter (username/first/last name/email), available to EVERY authenticated user (no `admin.user_management` gate like `GET /users`), reduced field set without the `enabled` field — cleanly separates "find a person" from "manage accounts".
- **Federated contact search via a SECOND registration, independent of `workflow-service`'s Federation Hub participation (P6-S9/P13-S4)**: a shared identity would have required either a forbidden cross-schema access to `workflow_service`'s own Postgres schema or a new delegation interface not belonging there ("sign this for me"). `auth-service` instead registers itself with the Federation Hub (own RSA-2048 key pair, own `installation_id`, display-name suffix `" (Contacts)"`) — thereby getting its **first own Postgres schema** (`auth`, `federation_identity` singleton); the service was previously fully stateless (a pure Keycloak proxy).
- **Direct installation-to-installation requests instead of relaying via the hub**: a deliberate departure from the existing workflow handover pattern (which mediates via the hub: A signs for the hub, the hub signs anew for B). Here the hub only provides the address book (already readable, ungated, via `GET /installations`) + key distribution, but is not necessarily the transport path for every future federated capability — a relay would have either misappropriated the workflow-specific handover schema or required a new, generic relay capability in federation-hub-service, both disproportionate for a synchronous request/response without a store-and-forward need. Signed with `federation_crypto.py` (RSA-PSS/SHA-256, identical scheme to ADR 0039) — the third independent copy of this primitive in the project (workflow-service, federation-hub-service, now auth-service), deliberately not extracted into a `libs/` package (an established pattern: small duplication instead of premature abstraction). Deliberately WITHOUT the encryption half of the handover schema (the hub is never in the request path for direct calls, the "hub must never see plaintext" threat model does not apply here).
- **Capability marking via the already-existing `Installation.supported_process_types` field** (`"dms.contact-directory.v1"` as a sentinel) instead of a new field in federation-hub-service — no code change needed to an already-stable service used by `workflow-service`.
- **`POST /users/directory/federated-search-inbound`**: a public gateway route (no `X-DMS-Principal`), authenticated via `X-Installation-Signature`/`X-Installation-Id`, checked live against `GET /installations` at the hub (no local peer key store — deliberate, given the low expected volume).
- **Frontend**: new `KontaktePane` special area (IconRail 📇) — unlike all other P15 special areas deliberately **with no role gate at all**, a literal implementation of "local, always available".
- **Tests**: `auth-service` 59 (previously 48, +11, `test_directory.py`): local directory search, federation-status default, `403` on federated endpoints without activation, real self-registration against the running `federation-hub-service` including the real signature-check path (valid/invalid signature, unknown installation, self-exclusion) — runs against real Postgres/Keycloak/federation-hub-service, no mocks. `user-ui` 150 (previously 144, +6: `kontakte-pane.test.tsx`). `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean, `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` clean.
- **Real bug found and fixed during the live self-loopback test**: `FederationHubClient.register()` originally did not transmit `supported_process_types` to the hub at all — its own capability marking (`dms.contact-directory.v1`) would thereby never have actually been visible in the address book, every incoming federated request (even a legitimate one) would have been rejected with `401`. Only became visible through the real live test (`test_federated_search_inbound_accepts_valid_self_signed_request` expected `200`, got `401`), not through pure mocking — client method + both call sites in `_ensure_federation_identity` corrected.
- **Second finding during live verification against the running gateway**: Keycloak's `search` parameter is, contrary to the original assumption ("substring"), actually a **prefix match per field** — `q=admin` does not find the username `config-admin`, whereas `q=config`/`q=conf` do; likewise for first/last name (`q=Workflow` matches `Workflow-Konfiguration`, `q=Konfiguration` mid-field does not). Deliberately not compensated by an own post-processing filter (would require a full `GET /users` dump + client-side filtering, disproportionate for a reference implementation) — docstring in `admin_users.py`, ADR 0054, and `docs/services/auth-service.md` corrected accordingly.
- **Third observation**: the repeated, real (not mocked) `federation_enabled` test runs of this session left, at every single test run, a new, real registration in the shared `federation-hub-service` address book (`_clean_tables` resets the local identity before every test, `_ensure_federation_identity()` therefore re-registers with every fresh `TestClient`) — became visible live as a two-digit number of `"... (Contacts)"` entries. Not a bug: `revoke_installation` is deliberately fully locked without a configured `hub_operator_key`, and none is set in this development environment — a cleanup is therefore not readily possible. Documented as "Open Points" in ADR 0054, the same category as other accepted test-residue data in shared dev environments elsewhere in this project.
- **Live verification against the real running Compose stack** (curl via the gateway, real login as `config-admin`, `auth-service`/`gateway-service`/`user-ui` freshly rebuilt beforehand): `GET /users/directory?q=` via the gateway confirmed (prefix-match behavior as above reproduced live), `GET /users/directory/federation-status` returns `{"enabled": true, "peer_installation_count": N}` (N fluctuates with the number of test-run registrations, see above — technically correct, but only explicable via test artifacts, no real second peer available). The actual signed federation round trip (signature generation requires the container's private key, not accessible from outside) remains, as with the other Federation Hub self-loopback cases in this project (federation-hub-service P6-S9, mail-connector P15-S3), limited to the automated test (`test_federated_search_inbound_accepts_valid_self_signed_request`), not additionally reproduced by hand via curl — a real, independent second installation stack is not sensibly set up in this sandbox. **No browser available in this session environment** — `KontaktePane`'s DOM rendering is therefore secured only via Vitest/jsdom as well as the full backend API verification through the same gateway calls, not visually confirmed (same open limitation as P15-S2/S3). No test-only Keycloak role assignments needed (unlike P15-S1/S2/S3) — both directory endpoints only require a valid token, no additional role gate.
- Docs: `docs/services/auth-service.md` (new "Contacts" section + first "Tests" section of this service's docs), `docs/services/user-ui.md` (new section, test count 144→150), `docs/services/federation-hub-service.md` ("Usage" extended with a second, independent caller), new [ADR 0054](docs/adr/0054-kontakte-directory-independent-second-federation-identity-per-installation.md) (including "Open Points"), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P15-S4 row, ✅).
- `graphify dms/ --update` deliberately deferred (not a phase completion, Phase 15 continues through P15-S6).

### Records Disposal Access Area (Phase 15, P15-S5)

- **Task**: concept §2.5/5.6/7.2 requires that already-disposed-of, but still within-transition-period, files/circulation folders remain directly searchable/viewable, instead of being findable only indirectly via audit-trail references. Per `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`, the session should build on `archival-service`'s already-complete `released` state machine (P7-S3/S3b) — "a purely filtered view, no new data store". No question at session start marked as needed — all architecture decisions were made autonomously and documented in [ADR 0055](docs/adr/0055-aussonderungs-zugriffsbereich-hydrated-read-only-view.md).
- **Preliminary research confirmed the task literally**: `docs/services/archival-service.md`'s "Open Points" had already explicitly named this gap ("No searchable records-disposal special area ... the admin UI table is a pure management/status view, not an end-user search interface") — not a newly discovered, but an already-documented, now-closed point.
- **New, hydrated `GET /released-items?q=` endpoint** (archival-service, new module `browse.py`): combines `repository.list_transfers(status="released")` (documents) and `list_case_transfers(status="released")` (circulation folders) — both already existing since P7-S3/S3b — with live metadata from `document_client.get_document`/`case_client.get_case` (title/`attributes["Kennzeichen"]` or `name`/`vorgangsnummer`). No new database field, no new table — pure aggregation at display time. A single no-longer-resolvable reference is skipped (logged) instead of aborting the entire request, the same error-tolerance principle as `directory_federation.search_all_peers` (ADR 0054).
- **Role gate reused instead of a new setting**: `settings.archive_retrieval_role` (default `dms-admin`) — the same role that already protects `POST /archival-transfers/{id}/retrieve` and `GET /case-archival-transfers/{id}/package`. Concept 2.5 literally names "a dedicated archive/registry role" for this area — congruent with the already-existing retrieval role, no second setting needed.
- **Search as server-side substring filtering after hydration**, no own search index (no `pg_trgm` like `query-service`, 5.4) — the expected target set (only items within the transition-period window) is structurally small enough that an in-memory filter after the already-necessary live fetch suffices.
- **`purge_at` computed only for documents** (`released_at + dehydration_delay_days`) — deliberately `null` for circulation folders, since `CaseArchivalTransfer` (unlike `ArchivalTransfer`) knows no `dehydrated` status and a circulation folder has no own live storage content that could be removed (P7-S3b design). **Deliberate open point, only made visible by this session**: concept 5.6 literally says "After the transition period expires, an item disappears from this area" — that technically applies only to documents, a circulation folder remains visible indefinitely in the records-disposal access area. A real case-purge concept is not part of this session, see ADR 0055 "Consequences".
- **Frontend uses exclusively already-existing actions, no new mutations**: new `AussonderungPane` special area (IconRail 🗄️, role-gated like quarantine — unlike the deliberately ungated contacts from P15-S4) offers "Retrieval" (documents, calls the already-existing `POST /archival-transfers/{id}/retrieve`) and "Download package" (circulation folders, calls the already-existing `GET /case-archival-transfers/{id}/package`, blob download analogous to `PreviewPane`'s pattern). Both endpoints already existed before this session, but were only reachable via the pure admin UI management table — this session makes them visible for the first time to regular authorized end users in the user UI.
- **Tests**: `archival-service` 59 (previously 50, +9: `test_browse.py` new — hydration of document+circulation folder, skipping non-resolvable references, substring filtering against title/identifier, sorting by `released_at`; `test_api.py` extension for `/released-items` — role gate, empty, exclusion of non-`released` transfers, hydrated mixed results, search filter). `user-ui` 156 (previously 150, +6: new standalone `aussonderung-pane.test.tsx`). `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean, `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` clean.
- **Live verification against the real running Compose stack** (curl via the gateway, real login as `config-admin`, `archival-service`/`user-ui` freshly rebuilt beforehand): initially, `GET /released-items` already showed two real circulation-folder transfers left over from an earlier P7-S3b smoke test — a genuine live-data example, not manually fabricated. To also genuinely demonstrate the document branch (including `purge_at` computation and retrieval), a real live document was marked for immediate records disposal via `POST /documents/{id}/archive-request`, and `archival-service` was restarted for testing with `DMS_ARCHIVAL_POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS` shortened to 5s (reset to the default of 3600 afterward) — the transfer live-ran through `pending → locked → copied → verified → released`. Confirmed: mixed document/circulation-folder results with correct `purge_at` (document) resp. `null` (circulation folders), search filter by title match and by identifier match (`q=Manuell` hit only the document, `q=Umlaufmappe` only the two cases), real retrieval (`POST .../retrieve` returned `200`, `dehydrated_at=null`, fresh `released_at`), real ZIP package download for a circulation folder (`content-type: application/zip`, valid ZIP signature). Test-only Keycloak role assignment `dms-admin` to `config-admin` revoked again afterward (same discipline as P15-S1/S2/S3). **No browser available in this session environment** — `AussonderungPane`'s DOM rendering is therefore secured only via Vitest/jsdom as well as the full backend API verification through the same gateway calls, not visually confirmed (same open limitation as P15-S2/S3/S4).
- Docs: `docs/services/archival-service.md` (new section "Records disposal access area", API table, test count 50→59, "Open Points" updated — the formerly open point about the missing special area marked as closed, a new open point added about the unbounded case retention), `docs/services/user-ui.md` (new section, test count 150→156), new [ADR 0055](docs/adr/0055-aussonderungs-zugriffsbereich-hydrated-read-only-view.md), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P15-S5 row, ✅).
- `graphify dms/ --update` deliberately deferred (not a phase completion, Phase 15 continues through P15-S6).

### Structure Templates (Phase 15, P15-S6)

- **Task**: concept §2.5/7.3 requires structure templates (e.g. a file-plan rough draft) — last session of Phase 15. Already corrected at P15-S0: NOT based on `config-service`'s existing 7.3 export (which exports only schema/configuration categories, no real folder tree), but a new capability built directly in `folder-service`. No question at session start marked as needed — all architecture decisions were made autonomously and documented in [ADR 0056](docs/adr/0056-struktur-vorlagen-folder-service-json-tree-no-attribute-values.md).
- **New `folder_template` table with a nested JSON tree** (`{"name", "object_type_id", "children"}`) instead of standalone "dead" `Folder` rows — `repository.build_template_structure` recursively walks the active subtree via the already-existing `list_children` (automatically excludes soft-deleted subfolders, no new logic needed for that). No FK to `Folder` — a template remains valid even if the source folder is later moved/renamed/deleted.
- **Deliberately structure ONLY, no attribute values**: the concept literally names "file-plan **rough draft**" — attribute values are instance data, not structure, only set later when filling in via `PATCH /folders/{id}`.
- **Applying deliberately skips object-type validation**: `repository.apply_template`/`_apply_structure_node` call `repository.create_folder` directly, not the validating `POST /folders` endpoint code path — a folder freshly created from a template has `attributes={}`, a required-attribute check would either completely prevent applying with required-attribute object types or abort mid-subtree (an inconsistent half-state). Since `object-type-service` offers no "structure only" validation mode, validation was deliberately skipped entirely on apply (including parent-child object-type nesting rules, 2.2b) — a documented limitation instead of a new partial validation in object-type-service that would be disproportionate for this session.
- **`folder.resource.created` is published individually for every newly created folder** (main.py, after the commit, identical event/payload as for a regular `POST /folders`) — necessary to keep `permission-service`'s `ResourceNode` tree in sync; without this event, applied template folders would have remained invisible to permission checks. Verified live (see below).
- **Ungated** (no role required, like the regular `POST /folders`) — the concept requires no restriction here, unlike records disposal (P15-S5, a dedicated archive/registry role) or quarantine (P15-S2).
- **`created_by` remains client-side in the body** instead of coming from `X-DMS-Principal` — deliberately follows the pattern already consistently used throughout `folder-service` (`FolderCreate.created_by`, `TrashRequest.deleted_by`), not the convention already hardened elsewhere in the project (mail-connector, P15-S3) — a new, deviating convention just for the template endpoints would have increased the already-existing inconsistency within the same service instead of reducing it; noted as "Open Points" in `docs/services/folder-service.md`, not fixed in this session.
- **Frontend uses raw folder-ID text inputs** for source/target folders instead of a purpose-built tree picker — the identical, already-established pattern as `QuarantinePane`'s target-folder field (P15-S2) and `PoststellePane`'s target-folder override (P15-S3), no new UI building block for this session.
- **Tests**: `folder-service` 115 (previously 99, +16: `test_folder_templates.py` new — structure capture including names/object types per node, exclusion of soft-deleted subfolders, unknown source folder, capture/list/read/delete roundtrip, applying produces the complete subtree under the target without changing the source tree, unknown target; `test_api.py` extension for the four new `/folder-templates` endpoints including `404` cases — object-type-`7` cases deliberately tested only at the repository level, since the real API tests would otherwise run against the real `object-type-service` validation and get `404` there without an actually registered object type). `user-ui` 163 (previously 156, +7: new standalone `vorlagen-pane.test.tsx`). `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean, `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` clean.
- **Live verification against the real running Compose stack** (curl via the gateway, real login as `config-admin`, `folder-service`/`user-ui` freshly rebuilt beforehand): a real subtree (`Projekte-LiveTest` → `Vertraege-LiveTest`) was created, captured as a template (`GET .../{id}` confirmed the correctly captured structure including nested `children`), applied under a new target folder (`created_count: 2`, root + one child, `POST /folders/{id}/children` confirmed the 1:1 rebuilt structure, `attributes={}` as expected). Additionally, beyond pure backend behavior: `permission-service`'s `GET /resources/{id}` was queried for both newly created folders and confirmed correct `parent_id`/`resource_type: "folder"` entries — the per-node event publication was thereby actually verified live, not merely assumed in code (an easy-to-overlook error would have been publishing the event only once for the root instead of for every node). All test artifacts (both folder trees, the template) subsequently deleted again. No test-only Keycloak role assignments needed (unlike P15-S4) — all template endpoints are ungated. **No browser available in this session environment** — `VorlagenPane`'s DOM rendering is therefore secured only via Vitest/jsdom as well as the full backend API verification through the same gateway calls, not visually confirmed (same open limitation as P15-S2/S3/S4/S5).
- Docs: `docs/services/folder-service.md` (new section "Structure templates", API table, test count 99→115, two new "Open Points" entries), `docs/services/user-ui.md` (new section, test count 156→163), new [ADR 0056](docs/adr/0056-struktur-vorlagen-folder-service-json-tree-no-attribute-values.md), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P15-S6 row, ✅).
- **Phase 15 thereby fully completed** (seven sessions: P15-S0 phase kickoff, P15-S1 trash family, P15-S2 quarantine, P15-S3 inbox/outbox, P15-S4 contacts, P15-S5 records-disposal access, P15-S6 templates) — `graphify dms/ --update` executed following this session (phase completion, see "Tooling & Testing"/graphify history).

### Phase Kickoff Planning (Phase 16, P16-S0)

- **Task**: pure verification/planning session (no implementation code), first session of the new Phase 16 "Flexible User-UI Workspace" — verify concept section 8 (flexible, dockable workspace) against the current code/service state, evaluate a React docking-layout library, adjust the session breakdown if needed. Same pattern as all previous `PN-S0` kickoff sessions since Phase 7.
- **Concept section 8 verified, scope confirmed unchanged**: the literal task ("panels (explorer, metadata, preview, further document tabs) can be arranged side by side or stacked, split off into their own areas, or reunited, comparable to the dockable editor/panel layout of Visual Studio Code ... several documents visible simultaneously ... remembered locally per device ... reset to default layout") matches exactly the P16-S1 description already formulated in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` — no correction needed, unlike several previous `PN-S0` sessions (e.g. P15-S0's correction regarding templates/quarantine). Confirmed: the IconRail special areas (contacts, templates, records disposal, quarantine, inbox/outbox, trash) live, per the concept, "deliberately outside the three-part main workspace" — the dockable area concerns exclusively explorer/metadata/preview/document tabs, none of the special-area panes.
- **Current starting state checked**: `DocumentWorkspace.tsx` already has a `tabs: DocumentSummary[]` list with a single `activeTabId` (classic tab switching, no simultaneous visibility of multiple documents) as well as a hand-built `Splitter` for the fixed three-column size distribution, whose sizes are already remembered per device via `usePersistedSize`/`localStorage` (the "local per device" pattern therefore already exists for sizes, but not for the arrangement itself) — P16-S1 builds on this, but does not simply replace the fixed arrangement without further thought, see docking-library decision.
- **Docking-layout library actually researched** (license/maintenance status of five serious candidates each directly verified via GitHub/npm APIs, not taken from README badges — the same research depth as the earlier bpmn-js license check, ADR 0021): `dockview`, `rc-dock`, `FlexLayout`, `golden-layout`, `react-mosaic`.
  - **`dockview` selected**: MIT license for the entire required feature scope including React bindings (only `dockview-enterprise` is proprietary, not needed — dockable grid, floating groups, popout windows, full drag&drop, layout save/restore are all included in the free tier), zero runtime dependencies, most actively maintained of all candidates (release one day before the research), React 19 support, built-in `toJSON()`/`fromJSON()` serialization — fits directly onto "save layout locally per device" + "reset to default layout" (default layout simply as shipped JSON, `localStorage` for the user's deviation).
  - **`golden-layout` deliberately discarded** — although the concept text itself says "comparable to the dockable editor/panel layout of Visual Studio Code" and golden-layout is historically the best-known VS-Code-like docking layout for the browser: no feature version since September 2022 (only isolated docs-typo commits), no official or maintained React binding (its own React integration would have to be built) — disproportionate extra effort for a reference implementation. License history checked for completeness (as with bpmn-js/golden-layout there used to be a GPL/commercial dual licensing, but per the current `LICENSE` file and npm package clearly MIT today) — not the reason for rejection, only the missing React support and the maintenance state.
  - **`rc-dock`** (Apache-2.0, correctly licensed) deferred: the `latest` npm tag currently points to an alpha version (`4.0.0-alpha.2`), the last real stable version is already over a year old — not a solid basis for a new reference implementation.
  - **`FlexLayout`** (MIT, zero dependencies, also very actively maintained, the same `Model.toJson()/fromJson()` pattern) documented as an equally valid alternative, should `dockview`'s enterprise side product (although unused) be judged undesirable in a future session — not a decision against it, just second choice.
  - **`react-mosaic`** (Apache-2.0, actively maintained, v7 released just shortly before the research) deferred: significantly heavier dependency footprint (full `react-dnd` stack), tab support itself still new/little proven (only since v7) — for a foundation with lower risk, `dockview` fits better.
  - **Static Next.js export/SSR risk** (`output: "export"`) affects all five candidates equally (pure browser/DOM libraries, no Next.js-own SSR-safe build) — the standard remedy (`"use client"` + delayed mounting after first render) suffices for all of them, not a distinguishing factor.
- **No session split needed**: P16-S1 remains, as originally planned, a single session — the research found no reason to enlarge or split the scope.
- Docs: `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P16-S0 row ✅ with findings, P16-S1 row extended with the concrete library choice).
- `graphify dms/ --update` deliberately NOT run (pure planning session without code change, Phase 16 has only just started with P16-S0, and the standing rule "only at the end of an entire phase" applies anyway).

### Dockable Workspace Foundation (Phase 16, P16-S1)

- **Task**: last session of Phase 16 — replace the fixed three-column layout (explorer/metadata/preview, P4-S4) with a real, VS-Code-like dockable layout (`dockview-react`, chosen at P16-S0): panels freely dockable/movable/stackable, several documents simultaneously visible and arrangeable, document tabs in the future above rather than beside the preview, layout preference remembered locally per device with "reset to default layout". Deliberately not part of this session: free CSS/theme customization by end users.
- **Architecture** (full rationale in [ADR 0057](docs/adr/0057-dockbarer-arbeitsbereich-dockview-context-not-params.md)): a new component `DockableDocumentArea` hosts three dockview panel types (`explorer`, `documentPreview` per open document, `metadata`), fully replaces `DocumentWorkspace`'s previous `Splitter` three-column layout. Frequently changing data (folder contents, open documents, active document, handlers) flow via a `WorkspaceContext` that `DockableDocumentArea` provides — dockview's `params` mechanism is deliberately used only for the fixed-at-creation-time `documentId` of a preview panel, not for constantly changing app knowledge. `DockableDocumentArea` remains **always mounted** on switching to an IconRail special area, only hidden via CSS (unlike the other special-area panes) — a conditional unmount would discard both the user's arrangement and all open document panels. A single, shared "open document" entry point (`DocumentWorkspace.openDocumentTab`, via a `useImperativeHandle` ref) is now called equally by explorer, search, favorites, and teamspaces, and always switches to the document view in the process — incidentally fixes a pre-existing UX flaw (documents opened from search remained unnoticed open in the background). The dockview tab title deliberately shows the raw document title, not the identifier-formatted display (`formatDocumentTitle`) — the same simplification already accepted for `PreviewPane`.
- **Three real bugs found and fixed while testing** (not just code written that was confirmed by tests — the same pattern as every previous frontend session of this project):
  1. **Stale placeholder panel after `resetLayout()`**: `buildDefaultLayout` originally created an empty placeholder and then patched it in a second step — `api.addPanel()` followed by an immediate `api.getPanel()`/`api.removePanel()` on the same panel ID within the same synchronous tick proved unreliable. Fixed via a single, internally consistent build pass (no more intermediate-step placeholders), verified via direct `api.panels` inspection.
  2. **`activeDocumentId` was incorrectly cleared on clicking into metadata/explorer**: `onDidActivePanelChange` originally reset the state for EVERY panel activation, including a click into the metadata panel itself — would have immediately cleared the field currently being edited. Fixed: only `doc:`-prefixed panels update `activeDocumentId`, `onDidRemovePanel` clears it only if the removed panel was actually the active one.
  3. **dockview focus behavior**: interactive elements in a panel group are, via `@testing-library/user-event`, only reliably focusable after the group has been "activated" by a click — this fix only became reliably testable through bugfix 2 (previously, the activation click itself would have cleared the metadata content).
- **Tests**: 167 frontend tests (previously 163, 4 new for the dockable area: a second document as a further tab in the same preview group, closing a tab removes the document from state, resetting via the toolbar button without losing open documents, layout persistence in `localStorage`), plus ~15 adjusted existing assertions (dockview's own panel groups duplicate `aria-label`s via an additional `role="region"` wrapper — tests now distinguish by tag instead of just by label; `PreviewPane`'s `aria-label` is now unique per document, `"Preview: {title}"` instead of just `"Preview"`, a real, deliberate accessibility improvement, not a pure test crutch, since multiple `PreviewPane` instances can now stand in the DOM simultaneously). `tsc`/`eslint`/`next build` all clean, no new npm vulnerabilities from `dockview-react` (checked via `npm audit`).
- **Open point, honestly documented instead of concealed**: a jsdom-specific effect on reusing a panel ID within the same tick as a preceding reset — the dockview model is demonstrably correct (`api.panels`/`localStorage` layout match), but the React portal content of the reused panel does not reliably re-render within jsdom. Very likely a jsdom peculiarity (missing real layout/animation-frame cycle), not a real model bug — **no browser available in this environment** (identical limitation as at P15-S2/S3/S4/S5/S6), therefore not conclusively verifiable in a real browser. The test additionally checks the `localStorage` layout for this case instead of only the DOM content.
- **Bundle size**: the home page grew from ~19.6 kB to ~106 kB initial load size (dockview + grid/splitting logic) — accepted as an appropriate price for a real docking library. `Splitter.tsx` remains unused in the repo (no compelling reason to delete, no own component test present, was not present before either).
- Docs: new [ADR 0057](docs/adr/0057-dockbarer-arbeitsbereich-dockview-context-not-params.md), `docs/services/user-ui.md` (layout section fully rewritten to the new architecture, including the corrected search-hit-open behavior description), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P16-S1 row ✅ with findings).
- **`graphify dms/ --update` executed following this session (phase completion, mandatory)** — Phase 16 consisted only of P16-S0 (planning) + P16-S1 (implementation). → **Phase 16 thereby fully completed**. Next step: **P17-S0** (phase kickoff planning for Phase 17 "Preconfigured configuration packages", concept section 14).

### Phase Kickoff Planning (Phase 17, P17-S0)

- **Task**: pure verification/planning session (no implementation code), first session of the new Phase 17 "Preconfigured configuration packages" — verify concept section 14 against the current code/service state (in particular object types/identifier generator/retention/classified-document classification/four-eyes/business calendars/special areas/admin roles from Phases 5e/6/14/15), adjust the session breakdown if needed. Same pattern as all previous `PN-S0` kickoff sessions since Phase 7.
- **Eleven concept assumptions actually checked against the code** (`config-service`, `object-type-service`, `permission-service`, `workflow-service`, `auth-service` concretely read, not assumed from the docs) — **four real deviations found, seven confirmed**:
  1. **`ConfigDocument` (config-service, 7.3) has no manifest fields**: `schema_version` (a fixed constant "1.0", no compatibility *range*) plus eight category lists (`object_types`, `workflows`, `dmn_definitions`, `business_calendars`, `roles`, `approval_config`, `sensor_config`, `federation_config`) — no `name`/`version`/`compatibility_range`/`description`/`origin`/`license`. Confirmed: P17-S1 is a real, non-trivial extension, not a pure wrapping of the existing format. Per-category upsert already exists (`object_types`/`business_calendars`/`roles`/`approval_config`/`sensor_config`/`federation_config` by name key, `workflows`/`dmn_definitions` deliberately as an exception always version-incrementing rather than updating). `POST /config/compare` (preview/diff before application) already exists fully — covers the "preview before application" requirement demanded in 14.1, no new endpoint needed.
  2. **The identifier generator only knows date/counter placeholders** (`{YYYY}`/`{YY}`/`{MM}`/`{DD}`/`{Laufende_Nummer}`, `object_type_service/repository.py:_render_kennzeichen`) — an attribute-based placeholder like the `{Abteilung}` named in 14.2 does not exist and would today fail with `KeyError`. Real new functionality for P17-S2, not just package-data population.
  3. **`is_classified` (object-type-service) is a plain bool**, no multi-level classified-document rating (VS-NfD/VS-VERTRAULICH/GEHEIM/STRENG GEHEIM) — real new attribute work for P17-S2. Additionally a structural clarification (no concept correction needed): the "classified-documents trash" is not its own trash type, but the same deletion mechanism as regular trash, only with a different role check depending on the flag — the separate role (`domain-admin-deletion-vs`, Phase 15) already exists correctly and fulfills the actual intent (separation of duties on deletion).
  4. **No generic import capability for new Keycloak realm roles**: the role checked by the frontend, `dms-poststelle` (`IconRail.tsx`), is never created by `auth-service/bootstrap.py` (only `dms-admin` is bootstrapped there) — a configuration package cannot today bring along a new realm role. `permission-service`'s DB-based `Role`s, by contrast, are already importable via the existing `roles` category (already fully covers the "extended admin roles" such as registry/records administration/office management named in 14.2). **Decision** (not a question-needed case — a small, already-precedented extension of the mechanism already planned for P17-S1, no new concept needed): P17-S1 gets an optional `realm_roles` field in the manifest, applied via the already-existing `create_realm_role(..., skip_exists=True)` primitive — the identical, already-proven pattern as the existing `dms-admin` bootstrap (P5e-S2). Solves the mail-room gap without a second, parallel mechanism.
  - **Seven assumptions confirmed, no correction needed**: object-type hierarchy (`allowed_parent_types`) and required attributes (`required` field per attribute) exactly match 2.2a; retention-period suggestions already exist as `default_retention_days` per object type (5.2, pure package data for P17-S2, no new mechanism); four-eyes configuration is already fully upsertable via `approval_config` (4.3, pure package data for P17-S3); `BusinessCalendar` (workflow-service) is already an importable `config-service` category, but has no region/state field — the package must supply the concrete holiday lists itself (7.1, P17-S3); BPMN process templates are already a pure file upload via the `workflows` category today, no separate template-library concept needed (2.3/7.1, P17-S3 can use the existing mechanism directly).
- **No session split needed**: P17-S1–S3 remain, as originally planned, one session each — the four deviations found shift work between the already-planned sessions (P17-S1 gets the `realm_roles` field, P17-S2 gets the new identifier placeholder type + the multi-level classification attribute), but do not fundamentally enlarge the overall scope.
- Docs: `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P17-S0 row ✅ with all four findings, P17-S1/S2/S3 rows extended with the respective affected clarifications).
- `graphify dms/ --update` deliberately NOT run (pure planning session without code change, Phase 17 has only just started with P17-S0, and the standing rule "only at the end of an entire phase" applies anyway). Next step: **P17-S1** (package format + application mechanism).

### Package Format + Application Mechanism (Phase 17, P17-S1)

- **Task**: implement concept 14.1 — a configuration package is a curated, versioned configuration document (existing 7.3 format) plus a slim manifest, applied via the already-existing, secured configuration import (additive/upsert, repeatedly applicable), package management in the admin UI (import/preview before application). Four deviations found at P17-S0 flow in: new manifest fields, `realm_roles` category/field for `dms-poststelle` and similar.
- **`ConfigDocument` extended** (config-service, `schemas.py`): new optional `manifest: PackageManifest` (`name`, `version`, `compatibility_range`, `description`, `origin`, `license` — purely descriptive, `compatibility_range` is not automatically enforced) as well as a new, **regular** category `realm_roles: list[str]`. **Deliberate deviation from the P17-S0 sketch**: envisaged there as a manifest field, generalized during implementation to a standalone `ConfigDocument` category — a data field (realm roles actually to be applied) does not belong in a purely descriptive metadata object, and only as its own category is `realm_roles` individually selectable, just like `roles`/`business_calendars`, and appears in the `POST /config/compare` diff. `compare.py` gets a third diff mode for this (`diff_string_list_category`/`STRING_LIST_CATEGORIES`) alongside the existing list/singleton categories, since `realm_roles` is a pure `list[str]`, not a `list[dict]` with a name field.
- **New `auth-service` endpoints** `GET`/`POST /realm-roles`: `GET` (ungated, like `permission-service`'s `GET /roles`) lists current Keycloak realm roles, filtered to remove Keycloak built-ins (`offline_access`, `uma_authorization`, `default-roles-*`). `POST` (gated behind `admin.user_management`, service-to-service via `X-DMS-Principal`) idempotently creates the submitted names (`create_realm_role(..., skip_exists=True)`) — generalizes exactly the existing `bootstrap._ensure_dms_admin_role` primitive (P5e-S2) to arbitrary names, instead of building a second mechanism. `config-service` accordingly self-bootstraps `domain-admin-users` in addition (now three self-assignments: `domain-admin-config`, `domain-admin-monitoring`, `domain-admin-users`).
- **Real bug #1 found and fixed — gateway route separation**: `config-service:config/import` had been in `gateway-service.settings.public_routes` since P13-S2 (ADR 0037), so that `fleet-management-service` (no Keycloak principal) could present its fleet agent key. For public paths, the gateway **fundamentally validates no bearer token and never sets `X-DMS-Principal`** — regardless of whether a valid token is sent along. `config-service`'s import gate's RBAC branch was thereby factually unreachable for ANY call via the gateway, even for a real, logged-in `config-admin`. This went unnoticed at P13-S2, since `config-service` had no frontend connection at all up to this session — only building the first admin UI page exposed the gap. **Fix**: new, dedicated path `POST /config/fleet-import` (remains public, exclusively the fleet-agent key, no RBAC branch anymore), `POST /config/import` is henceforth a regular, token-required RBAC path without the fleet bypass. `gateway-service.settings.public_routes` adjusted accordingly, `fleet-management-service.agent_client.py`'s `provision_config()` calls the new path. Test `test_config_import_route_now_requires_gateway_auth_check` (gateway-service) explicitly confirms the new bearer-token requirement. Details see [ADR 0058](docs/adr/0058-konfigurationspakete-manifest-realm-roles-and-gateway-import-route-split.md).
- **Real bug #2 found and fixed — preview noise**: the first version of the new admin UI page called `POST /config/compare` without a `categories` restriction — every category untouched by the uploaded package (empty in the document) was thereby fully compared against the live export and listed as "present only in the current system". For a test package with only one category (`approval_config`), this caused **all** eight other categories to appear with the complete current inventory (hundreds of test entries from earlier sessions) — found during live verification in the real browser, not via component tests (which already correctly mocked `compareConfig`). **Fix**: `ConfigPackages.tsx` now passes `presentCategories(configDoc)` to `compareConfig()` — the preview now only compares what the package actually brings.
- **New admin UI page `/config-packages/`** (`ConfigPackages` component) — **the first frontend connection of `config-service` at all** (previously only CLI/raw API/fleet agent): load a package file (`<input type="file">`, `FileReader` instead of `File.text()` — the latter is web standard, but not implemented in the jsdom test environment used here, verified via a debug script), display manifest/categories, preview (`POST /config/compare` without `base` — automatically pulls in its own live export, 7.5/P14-S1), apply (`POST /config/import`, result table new/updated/skipped/error per category), plus "export current configuration" as a download (an obvious addition, no extra effort). Sidebar visibility via `requiresCapability: "admin.object_config"`, deliberately without an additional `RequireCapability` wrapper around the route (follows the more common, already-established pattern in this project, e.g. `/query-console/`).
- **Tests**: `auth-service` 64 tests (previously 59, 5 new: `test_realm_roles.py`), `config-service` 42 tests (fleet-import split, `realm_roles` roundtrip, `diff_string_list_category` unit tests), `gateway-service`/`fleet-management-service` adjusted (path renaming, new test for the now token-required `config/import` route) — all four services green, `ruff check`/`ruff format` clean. Admin UI: 161 tests (previously 152, 7 new: `config-packages.test.tsx`), `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint .`/`next build` clean.
- **Fully verified live against the built stack**: `curl` confirms `config/import` without a token → `401` (previously impossible to test, since the path was public), `config/fleet-import` with the correct key works. **Real browser test** (Playwright in a temporary Docker container, no `chromium-cli`/native browser available in this session environment): login as `config-admin`, navigation to "Configuration packages", export of the current configuration as a download (verified), upload of a test package (manifest correctly displayed), preview (correctly limited to one category after the noise fix), apply (result table correctly shows `1 updated`) — no console errors during the entire run. Both bugs described above were found exactly in this live run, not by mere code reading.
- Docs: new [ADR 0058](docs/adr/0058-konfigurationspakete-manifest-realm-roles-and-gateway-import-route-split.md), `docs/services/config-service.md` (new category, configuration-packages section, gateway route separation, gating), `docs/services/auth-service.md` (new endpoints, realm-role management), `docs/services/gateway-service.md` (correction section), `docs/services/fleet-management-service.md` (path change explained), `docs/services/admin-ui.md` (new page, backend calls table, authorization section), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P17-S1 row ✅ with findings).
- `graphify dms/ --update` deliberately NOT run (Phase 17 still running, P17-S2/S3 follow — standing rule "only at the end of an entire phase"). Next step: **P17-S2** (eGov configuration package part 1: file-plan object-type hierarchy, new `{Abteilung}` identifier placeholder type, retention-period suggestions, new multi-level classified-document classification attribute).

### eGov Configuration Package Part 1 (Phase 17, P17-S2)

- **Task**: implement concept 14.2 part 1 — file-plan object-type hierarchy with enforced hierarchy and sensible required attributes, file-reference-number format following common administrative convention, retention-period suggestion per file type, classified-document classification tied to the already-existing separate deletion role. Three gaps found at P17-S0 had to be closed first (attribute-based identifier placeholder, multi-level classified-document rating), before the actual package could be filled with them.
- **Attribute-based identifier placeholders** (object-type-service): `_validate_kennzeichen_format` now accepts any placeholder that is either a date/counter placeholder OR an attribute defined on the object type (previously: `422` for anything except the five hard-wired ones). `_render_kennzeichen` pulls the missing values from a new `attribute_values` parameter; `POST /object-types/{id}/next-kennzeichen` accepts a new optional body `{attributes: {...}}` for this. `document-service` passes through, for this purpose, the attributes already parsed at creation time (`object_type_client.next_kennzeichen(object_type_id, parsed_attributes)`) — if a referenced value is still missing anyway (attribute not marked as required, in which case `validate()` would not already have caught it), the new `MissingKennzeichenAttributeError` path returns `422` instead of a raw `KeyError`/`500`. **Incidentally fixed limitation**: the old placeholder regex (`[A-Za-z_]+`) would not have recognized umlauts, as in "Federführung", as a valid placeholder name at all — widened to `\w+` (Python identifiers/`str.format()` support Unicode natively anyway, PEP 3131).
- **`is_classified: bool` → `classification_level: str | None`** (the four common German classified-document ratings `VS-NfD`/`VS-VERTRAULICH`/`GEHEIM`/`STRENG GEHEIM`, taken literally from 14.2): replaced, not added as a second parallel field — every set value still triggers exactly the same gate as the previous plain bool (classified-documents trash/`domain-admin-deletion-vs`, Phase 15), the level itself is pure additional information. Threaded through `object-type-service` (schema/model/repository/ad-hoc migration), `document-service` (gate check), `config-service` (`ObjectTypeExport`), `admin-ui` (`ObjectTypeEditor`: checkbox → `<select>` with the four levels + "Not classified"), `user-ui` (type declaration, unused). `GET /object-types`'s filter parameter deliberately remained named `is_classified: bool` (translated internally to `classification_level IS (NOT) NULL`) — the only caller (`document-service`'s classified-documents-trash resolution) only asks anyway "any classification or none".
- **First concrete eGov configuration package** (`packages/egov/config.json`, new top-level directory `packages/` analogous to `services/`/`apps/`/`libs/`, `manifest.version` `0.1.0`): the hierarchy **Department (folder) → File plan (folder) → File (document)** already literally fixed in concept 2.5, enforced via `allowed_parent_types`. `Akte` (file) gets `kennzeichen_format: "{Federführung}-{YYYY}-{Laufende_Nummer}"`, required attributes `Aktentitel`/`Federführung`, `default_retention_days: 3653` (10 years, a changeable default). A second, otherwise identical object type `Verschlusssache-Akte` (classified file) additionally demonstrates `classification_level: "VS-NfD"`. **Deliberate interpretation decision, documented rather than silently made**: concept 14.2's example placeholder `{Abteilung}` is implemented concretely in the package as `{Federführung}` — the responsible office is already one of the three required attributes demanded by 14.2 and avoids a second, redundant "Abteilung" attribute that could have diverged from the already structurally present department folder. "Aktenzeichen" (file reference number) from 14.2's required-attribute enumeration is read as "the generation is configured", not as an additional manual field (the reserved key `Kennzeichen` has always been server-side overwritten since P5e-S2 anyway). Rationale for all four decisions in [ADR 0059](docs/adr/0059-egov-paket-aktenplan-hierarchie-und-mehrstufige-vs-einstufung.md).
- **Tests**: object-type-service 90 tests (previously 85 — 4 `is_classified` tests replaced by 5 `classification_level` tests, 4 new identifier-placeholder tests), document-service 221 tests (2 new: identifier rendering with a real attribute value, `422` for a missing value), config-service 42 tests (`classification_level` roundtrip test renamed/adjusted) — all three green, `ruff check`/`ruff format` clean. Admin UI 161 tests (2 existing tests converted to the new `<select>`), user UI 167 tests (pure type fix, no logic change) — `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint .`/`next build` clean for both.
- **Fully verified live against the built stack**: the eGov package uploaded and applied via the `/config-packages/` page (P17-S1) — result table shows `object_types: 4 newly created, 0 errors` (also confirms that the object-type order in the package file correctly satisfies the `allowed_parent_types` reference check: department before file plan before file). Confirmed in the object-type editor: `Verschlusssache-Akte` correctly shows `VS-NfD` preselected. Via `curl` against the real stack: an attempt to place `Akte` directly under `$ROOT` was correctly rejected with `400` ("allowed parent classes: ['Aktenplan']") — hierarchy enforcement actually takes effect; after creating a matching department/file-plan folder chain, a real file document with `Federführung="IT"` was created, `attributes.Kennzeichen` correctly contained `IT-2026-001`. All test artifacts (2 test documents, 2 test folders) subsequently removed via the regular delete/purge endpoints; the four eGov object types themselves deliberately remain (they are the actual result of the package application, not a test artifact).
- Docs: new [ADR 0059](docs/adr/0059-egov-paket-aktenplan-hierarchie-und-mehrstufige-vs-einstufung.md), `packages/egov/README.md` (new), `docs/services/object-type-service.md` (identifier-generator section, classified-document classification renamed/extended), `docs/services/document-service.md` (identifier-generator/trash-family sections), `docs/services/admin-ui.md` (object-type editor section), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P17-S2 row ✅ with findings).
- `graphify dms/ --update` deliberately NOT run (Phase 17 not yet completed, P17-S3 follows — standing rule "only at the end of an entire phase"). Next step: **P17-S3** (eGov configuration package part 2: mail-room role template, process templates, four-eyes pre-configuration, business calendar, extended admin roles — completes Phase 17).

### eGov Configuration Package Part 2 (Phase 17, P17-S3)

- **Task**: Complete Concept 14.2 Part 2 — mail room role template, BPMN process templates for circulation folders (release/acknowledgment/task), four-eyes principle pre-configuration for sensitive action types, business calendar with nationwide/state-specific public holidays, extended domain-separated admin roles — supplements the same `packages/egov/config.json` (Part 1, P17-S2), and concludes Phase 17 and thereby the **entire 107-session roadmap**.
- **Real finding that corrected the P17-S0 confirmation**: 14.2 names "final deletion, permission change, configuration import" as the three action types relevant to the four-eyes principle pre-configuration. A grep check across all `action_type="..."` locations showed: only "final deletion" (`document.force_delete`/`folder.force_delete`/`document.force_unlock`) had actual enforcement — for "permission change" and "configuration import" NOT A SINGLE location existed anywhere in the code. Delivering an `approval_config` entry for these would have been a pure sham (`requires_approval: true` importable without changing behavior in any way) — both gaps were therefore genuinely closed in this session instead of being simulated.
- **`permission-service`**: `POST /role-assignments` now checks `permission.role_assignment.create` via the same generic `_request_approval()` mechanism as scope locks (4.3/4.7) — new `RoleAssignmentActionResult` (`status`/`role_assignment`/`approval_request_id`) instead of the previous flat `RoleAssignmentOut`. **Breaking response change**: all callers checked (grep across all services) — only two test helper functions (`config-service`/`auth-service`) and two permission-service-internal tests parsed the body as a flat object and were adjusted; all bootstrap callers (seven other services) only check `raise_for_status()`, unaffected. `admin-ui`'s `UserManagement.tsx`/`api.ts` adjusted accordingly (four-eyes principle notice instead of an immediate reload on `pending_approval`). Approved assignments are applied through the existing self-consumption branch of `approval_consumer.py`.
- **`config-service`**: `POST /config/import` now checks `config.import` via a new `ApprovalClient` (a copy of the `document-service` pattern) — response `ImportActionResult`. Since the service previously had no event bus connection at all ("stateless orchestrator", P17-S1), it received its **first NATS consumer ever** (`consumer.py`, pure, `ensure_stream=False` — no stream of its own, only reacts to permission-service's `permission.approval.approved`), which on approval calls the same `_apply_config_document()` logic as the immediate path. `POST /config/fleet-import` deliberately remains ungated (no human-in-the-loop in the fleet agent provisioning path, ADR 0037). `admin-ui`'s `ConfigPackages.tsx`/`api.ts` adjusted accordingly.
- **Three BPMN process templates** (`packages/egov/workflows/egov_{freigabe,kenntnisnahme,aufgabe}.bpmn`, embedded as `workflows` category): `Freigabe` (release) = one `manualTask` + `exclusiveGateway` (branching on process variable `entscheidung`, default "Rejected"); `Kenntnisnahme` (acknowledgment)/`Aufgabe` (task) = each a linear `manualTask`. All three uploaded live against the running `workflow-service` before being incorporated, instantiated, and run through to completion (including both `Freigabe` gateway branches).
- **Business calendar**: `DE-Bund` (default, nine nationwide public holidays) plus 16 complete state calendars `DE-BW` … `DE-TH`, real dates for 2026/2027 (Gaussian Easter algorithm for all movable holidays, Buß- und Bettag [Day of Repentance and Prayer] as "Wednesday before November 23"), computed programmatically instead of researched by hand.
- **Two extended, domain-separated admin roles** (permission-service's DB `Role`, not the technical `domain-admin-*` system roles from 4.6): `Registratur/Aktenverwaltung` (registry/file management) (`read`, `write`), `Amtsleitung` (office management) (`read`, `write`, `scope_lock.bypass`) — 14.2 names both names only as an example; the concrete permission lists are an interpretation made and documented in this session.
- **`realm_roles: ["dms-poststelle"]`** — the role already exists for real (manually created since P15-S3, enforced by `mail-connector`'s `_require_poststelle()`), but was not contained in any package; it is now automatically part of the package application.
- **Two real bugs found and fixed during regression itself**: (1) `infra/docker-compose.yml`'s `config-service` block did not set `DMS_NATS_URL` — inside the container it fell back to the unreachable `localhost:4222`, and the new consumer ran in an endless loop of connection errors without visibly blocking startup; fixed (`nats://nats:4222` + `depends_on: nats`). (2) `scripts/run-tests.sh`'s `CONSUMER_SERVICES` was initially mistakenly extended to include `config-service` — but its tests (unlike the other entries) deliberately run against the real running container instead of the in-process `TestClient`, so stopping it broke every `config-service` test with `Connection refused`; removed again, with the comment expanded to explain the distinction.
- **Tests**: permission-service 118 (previously 115, +3: role assignment with approval requirement enabled returns `pending_approval` without immediate creation, approved assignment gets created + published by the consumer, malformed payload gets logged instead of crashing), config-service 48 (previously 42, +6: `status="applied"` by default, `config.import` four-eyes gate returns `pending_approval`, new `test_consumer.py` with 4 pure unit tests of the consumer callback without DB/downstream calls), auth-service 64 (test helper function adjusted to the new response envelope) — all three as well as the full `scripts/run-tests.sh --build` regression across all 32 backend services green (`webdav-connector`'s already repeatedly documented, session-unrelated root test data `ReadTimeout` flake observed again, isolated and confirmed via retry, not fixed — see P12-S4/P13-S1/P14-S10), `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean. Admin-UI 164 tests (previously 161, +3: two new in `user-management.test.tsx`, one new in `config-packages.test.tsx` for the `pending_approval` states) — `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint .`/`next build` clean.
- **Fully verified live against the built stack**: the complete `packages/egov/config.json` applied via the real `/config-packages/` page (gateway, real `config-admin` login) — 4 object types updated, 3 workflows created, 17 business calendars created, 2 roles created, 5 four-eyes principle configurations activated, `dms-poststelle` confirmed. Both newly gated action types played through end-to-end: `config.import` (tiny test document → `pending_approval` → `POST .../approve` as a second principal → consumer applies it, confirmed via realm role query) and `permission.role_assignment.create` (assignment → `pending_approval` → approval → consumer creates it, confirmed via `GET /role-assignments`). All test artifacts created for the verification (test realm role removed directly via the Keycloak admin API, since `auth-service` offers no `DELETE` endpoint for realm roles — a known gap, not fixed here; test role assignment removed via the regular `DELETE /role-assignments/{id}` endpoint) removed again; the actual package contents (roles/calendars/workflows/`dms-poststelle`/activated four-eyes principle configuration) deliberately remain — they are the actual result of the package application on this installation, not a test artifact.
- Docs: new [ADR 0060](docs/adr/0060-egov-paket-teil-2-vier-augen-luecken-und-umlaufmappen-prozessvorlagen.md), `packages/egov/README.md` (Part 2 section), `docs/services/permission-service.md` (`POST /role-assignments` gating), `docs/services/config-service.md` (`config.import` gating + new consumer), `docs/services/admin-ui.md` (test count update), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P17-S3 line marked done with finding, **roadmap complete**).
- `graphify dms/ --update` executed afterward (phase completion — the last phase of the entire roadmap).

### Post-Roadmap: graphify full rebuild + user-UI bug fixes (2026-08-11, after P17-S3)

The session roadmap is fully complete with P17-S3 (107/107) - the following items are
no longer a numbered plan entry, but direct user feedback after completion.

- **Full `graphify dms/` rebuild instead of `--update`** (user request: the incremental updates were
  assessed as possibly not having run cleanly): the complete rebuild yielded **10173 nodes,
  19815 edges, 670 communities** — a net -410 nodes compared to the previous (incrementally
  grown) graph, the shrink guard deliberately overridden with `force=True` (exactly the case
  described by the guard itself as legitimate: "Run a full rebuild to be safe"). **Two real coverage
  gaps found and closed** (user hint "I currently only see docs"/"packages, scripts etc."):
  `packages/egov/config.json` was indeed recognized as `code`, but was extracted with 0 nodes by
  the structure parser due to lacking JSON grammar; all 21 `.bpmn`/`.dmn` files in the project (eGov
  process templates, migration-service resources, workflow-service test fixtures) were captured by
  `detect()` in **no** category (neither `code` nor `document`). Both manually given to a subagent as
  a third semantic chunk (BPMN/DMN/JSON treated as document content, since graphify's structure
  parser does not know these formats) — 100 additional nodes/99 edges/3 hyperedges, among them the
  three eGov circulation-folder templates as a cohesive `umlaufmappen_muster` hyperedge, as well as
  `packages/egov/config.json`'s manifest/object types/roles/calendars/four-eyes principle
  configuration modeled individually. 670 communities were, for lack of a practical individual
  hand-naming approach, named programmatically from the dominant source path per
  community (e.g. "services → permission-service → core") instead of being individually read and
  named — a deliberate deviation from the usual labeling step given this corpus size. Health check
  showed 3690 "dangling" edges, checked and confirmed harmless (almost exclusively `imports` edges
  to external libraries such as `httpx`/`pytest`/`sqlalchemy`/`react`, for which no dedicated nodes
  are ever intended — no corruption).
- **Three user-reported user-UI bugs found and fixed** (all three traced back to the same P16-S1
  regression: `ExplorerPane`/`MetadataPanel`/`PreviewPane`/`SearchPane` were never given their own
  height when switching over to `dockview-react` panels):
  1. **Explorer list does not scroll**: `.explorer-pane` had `overflow-y: auto` but no `height`
     - since dockview's panel host chain (`.dv-content-container` → `div.dv-react-part`) is not
     Flex/Grid, `height: auto` (sizing-to-content) remained in effect, so the box was always as large
     as its content and `overflow-y` never took effect; the excess was instead invisibly cut off by
     dockview's own `.dv-groupview { overflow: hidden }`.
  2. **Popups/modals appear behind system areas**: `.modal-backdrop` had no `z-index` at all
     (thereby losing against dockview's own chrome, which uses values up to `9999`), `.context-menu`
     (`100`) and `.icon-rail-popover` (`10`) were likewise below it.
  3. **PDF preview does not resize in height**: `.preview-pdf-frame`'s `<embed>` had `height: 100%`,
     but the parent box `.preview-pane` (like `.explorer-pane`) had no defined height — the
     percentage chain ran into nothing, and the embed fell back to `min-height: 480px`, independent
     of the actual panel size.
  - **Fix**: `.explorer-pane, .metadata-panel, .preview-pane, .search-pane { height: 100%; }` added
    (fixes 1 and 3 at once, since both stem from the same missing defined height);
    `.icon-rail-popover`/`.context-menu`/`.modal-backdrop` raised to `z-index: 10000`/`10001`/`10002`
    (safely above dockview's maximum value `9999`).
  - **Verified live via Playwright-in-Docker against the newly built `user-ui` container** (no
    browser natively available in this environment, same pattern as earlier sessions): scroll test
    showed `scrollHeight` 20774px vs. `clientHeight` 752px and actual movement after
    mouse-wheel scrolling (`scrollTop` 0→2000, visibly different list in the screenshot); context menu
    and retention modal screenshots both showed correctly positioned above the entire UI (modal
    backdrop demonstrably blocked clicks on elements behind it); PDF preview at 900px and
    1300px viewport height showed exactly the expected embed height (482px and 544px respectively,
    both values matching the `min-height: 480px` floor and the actually available panel height as
    calculated). Test artifact (uploaded PDF) subsequently removed via the regular
    `DELETE /documents/{id}` endpoint. Full `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`test`/`build` for `user-ui` clean
    (167 tests, no regression).
- `graphify update .` executed again after the CSS fixes (AST-only, no API cost, no
  phase completion needed anymore — the "only at phase end" rule applied to the now-completed
  roadmap, not to future ad-hoc changes).

### Post-Roadmap: Office direct editing + SSO/Kerberos login (2026-08-13, after the user-UI bug fixing)

Direct continuation of the previous post-roadmap item: four further open points reported by the user,
three of them pure status clarification, two real new features (details/architecture rationale in
[ADR 0061](docs/adr/0061-office-direktbearbeitung-webdav-edit-token-in-start-url.md) and
[ADR 0062](docs/adr/0062-sso-automatischer-login-oidc-redirect-und-optionales-kerberos.md)).

- **Theme switch in the workspace stayed in dark mode** — real bug, fixed:
  `DockableDocumentArea.tsx`'s `<DockviewReact>` never received a `theme` prop, falling back to
