  dockview's built-in `themeAbyss` preset, which hard-coded the same `--dv-*` variables dark again,
  variables that `globals.css` already theme-ably maps to `--dms-*`. Fix: passed in its own,
  classless `DMS_DOCKVIEW_THEME` object.
- **Version history/renditions via the metadata** — already fully present
  (`PreviewPane.tsx`: version selection, `listRenditions()`, automatic Office→PDF conversion),
  no implementation needed.
- **Feature A — Office direct editing** (open Word/Excel/PowerPoint directly from the browser):
  new `WebdavEditToken` (`document-service`, structurally modeled on `ShareLink`, but requiring
  `document.write` instead of just reading) + `by-id/` path resolution in `webdav-connector`
  (O(1) instead of the usual O(depth) path walk) + token-as-username resolution in
  `DmsAuthDomainController.basic_auth_user`. **Real bug found and fixed during live
  verification**: checking in a new version via the `by-id` path failed with `409 PUT parent
  must be a collection` — wsgidav's `do_PUT` handler resolves the parent path of the target before
  every write access and checks `is_collection`; for `by-id/<document-id>.<ext>` that is the
  bare `by-id` namespace path itself, for which no real `TreeFolder` exists. Fixed with
  a new `_ByIdVirtualCollection` (purely synthetic `DAVCollection`, not browsable) in
  `dav_provider.py`. This bug would have been caught by the already existing `test_by_id_put_with_edit_token_
  checks_in_a_new_version` had the test suite reached it — instead it failed on an already
  longer-known, unrelated `ReadTimeout` flake (see below) before the actual assertion ever ran. Verified
  via direct `curl` round-trip against the real running stack (issue token → GET via `by-id` →
  PUT new version → version number/content confirmed → test artifact removed via the
  regular `DELETE` endpoint) as well as in isolation via
  `pytest -k by_id` (4/4 green, bypasses the root listing bottleneck).
- **Feature B — SSO/automatic login** (Kerberos/SPNEGO via Keycloak, optional): new
  OIDC redirect login path (`GET /oidc/authorize`/`POST /oidc/callback`, `GET/PUT /sso-config`,
  `POST /logout`) plus optional Kerberos realm configuration at bootstrap
  (`_ensure_client_updated`/`_ensure_kerberos`). Existing password form remains a complete
  fallback. **Two real bugs found and fixed by ourselves, before they would have become
  visible in production**: (1) `auth_service/schemas.py` used `datetime` in `SsoConfigOut` without
  importing it — would have crashed the service start with `NameError`. (2) `GET
  /oidc/authorize` built the browser redirect URL from the internal Docker Compose address
  (`DMS_KEYCLOAK_BASE_URL=http://keycloak:8080`) — not resolvable for the actual browser.
  New, separate `keycloak_public_base_url` setting (`DMS_KEYCLOAK_PUBLIC_BASE_URL`, in the
  Compose stack `http://localhost:8080`) used only for `_authorization_endpoint`, token/logout
  endpoints remain on the internal URL. Confirmed live via `curl`: `authorization_url` now correctly
  points to `localhost:8080`.
- **Also found while fixing the regression (not caused by the new features themselves,
  but made visible for the first time through them)**: `permission.role_assignment.create` has,
  on this installation, been genuinely subject to the four-eyes principle since the P17-S3 eGov
  package live verification — the test helpers `_grant_document_read`/`_grant_document_write`
  (document-service, webdav-connector) as well as
  `auth_service.permission_client.PermissionServiceClient.ensure_role_assignment`
  (production code, used on every service start for the domain admin role assignment) never
  checked the `POST /role-assignments` response for `status != "created"` — a `pending_approval`
  was silently treated as success. Fixed: test helpers disable the approval requirement for their
  runtime and restore the original value afterward (not overwriting it permanently);
  `ensure_role_assignment` now raises on `pending_approval`, and the already existing
  `try/except Exception` caller in `main.py`'s lifespan logs this as a warning and retries it on the
  next restart — exactly the behavior already documented there.
- **Tests**: document-service 232 (previously 218, +14 for WebdavEditToken), webdav-connector +4 new
  `by_id` tests (green in isolation, full suite still affected by the flake mentioned below), auth-service 76
  (previously 73, among others bootstrap idempotency/Kerberos skip/Kerberos activation, SSO redirect round-trip
  against Keycloak's own form, `/sso-config` round-trip, `/logout` session invalidation),
  gateway-service 22 unchanged, green. `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean. User-UI: 167 tests
  clean (`tsc --noEmit`/`eslint .`/`next build` likewise clean) — the new `api.ts` exports
  (`officeLaunchInfo`, `logoutSession` among others) were initially missing from several existing
  `vi.mock("@/lib/api")` test fixtures (`auth-context.test.tsx`, `PreviewPane.test.tsx`,
  `document-workspace.test.tsx`) and were added.
- **Known, unfixed, session-unrelated flake observed again and worsened**:
  `webdav-connector`'s path-based tests (PROPFIND against the WebDAV root) now fail
  **deterministically**, no longer just occasionally — the root has accumulated 438 children over
  the entire project history (never-cleaned-up test artifacts from earlier
  live verifications), causing a `PROPFIND` to take ~67s (document-service only supplies file metadata
  one document at a time, see `docs/services/webdav-connector.md`), longer than any
  reasonable client timeout. Not fixed (deleting real, if old, test data on the
  running installation would be a destructive action without user approval) — noted
  for a later session, does NOT affect Feature A's own `by-id` path (O(1), bypasses the root
  listing entirely).
- Docs: new [ADR 0061](docs/adr/0061-office-direktbearbeitung-webdav-edit-token-in-start-url.md)/
  [ADR 0062](docs/adr/0062-sso-automatischer-login-oidc-redirect-und-optionales-kerberos.md),
  `docs/services/document-service.md`/`webdav-connector.md`/`auth-service.md`/`gateway-service.md`/
  `user-ui.md` updated (including both bugs found).
- **Immediately following**: user had a consolidated list of all "Open Points" from 35
  service docs compiled (presented as an artifact) and fully triaged it — this produced
  a new multi-session roadmap **Phase 18–26** (`IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`), plus a new
  independent goal (Helm charts for k8s/OCP). `graphify update .` follows directly after
  this entry, then the start of Phase 18.

### Phase 18 — Auth decoupling from Keycloak (P18-S1, P18-S2, P18-S3) — completed

- **Task**: Superuser break-glass and domain admin accounts should in future function independently of
  Keycloak's availability — user directive after the "Open Points" triage on 2026-08-13 ("the
  superuser shouldn't live in Keycloak at all, it should only live in the app"). P18-S1 lays the
  infrastructure, without yet switching over `POST /login`/break-glass.
- **`TechnicalAccount`** (new model, `auth` schema): future storage location for superuser/domain
  admin accounts, `password_hash` via `bcrypt` — the first self-hashed password in this service
  (previously Keycloak handled this entirely).
- **`LocalSigningKey`** (singleton row, same pattern as `FederationIdentity`): its own
  RSA-2048 key pair, generated idempotently on first access, reuses the already
  existing `federation_crypto.generate_keypair()` (no new crypto duplication within the same
  service). Stable `kid` across restarts, confirmed live via a real `docker compose restart`.
- **`GET /.well-known/jwks.json`** + **`local_token_issuer.mint_token()`**: serves/issues tokens with
  an identical claim shape to Keycloak (`sub`/`preferred_username`/`realm_access.roles`/`aud`),
  so that no downstream consumer needs to know anything about their origin.
- **New `MultiIssuerTokenValidator` in `libs/dms-auth-client`**: delegates to one of several
  `TokenValidator` instances, selected via the `iss` claim (pure duck typing, `make_current_user_
  dependency` does not notice the difference). `auth-service`'s own `_validator` has, since this
  session, been a composite of Keycloak and local validator, deferred via a `_LazyValidator` wrapper
  (the local key needs DB access, which is only available in the lifespan, but `get_current_user`
  must already exist as a finished dependency at module import time).
- **Research prior to implementation confirmed a narrower scope than feared**: only `auth-service`
  and `gateway-service` instantiate `TokenValidator` directly at all (confirmed via grep) — all
  other ~30 services exclusively consume the `X-DMS-*` headers passed through by the gateway,
  not self-validated tokens. The multi-issuer switch therefore affects only two locations, not
  the entire service catalog.
- **Tests**: `libs/dms-auth-client` 12 (previously 8, +4 new `MultiIssuerTokenValidator` tests), auth-service
  81 (previously 76, +5 new: hash/verify round-trip, signing key idempotency across a real
  restart cycle of two sequential `TestClient` contexts, mint-token validation against its own
  JWKS, JWKS endpoint consistency, locally issued token accepted by `GET /me`). `ruff
  check`/`ruff format --check` clean.
- **A real bug found and fixed in our own test work**: the first attempt at the
  idempotency test called `ensure_signing_key` via `asyncio.run()` outside the event loop managed by
  `TestClient` — asyncpg connections are bound to exactly one loop, which failed with
  `RuntimeError: ... attached to a different loop`. Fixed by using two real, sequential
  `TestClient` contexts (simulating an actual restart) instead of a manual second call.
- **Fully verified live against the real running stack**: `GET /.well-known/jwks.json`
  returns a valid JWKS entry, an existing Keycloak login (`users-admin`) works unchanged via
  `GET /me`, `kid` stays identical across a real `docker compose restart auth-service`
  (`local-1` before and after the restart).
- Docs: new [ADR 0063](docs/adr/0063-auth-entkopplung-lokale-technische-konten-dual-issuer-jwt.md),
  `docs/services/auth-service.md` (new section "Auth decoupling from Keycloak"),
  `libs/dms-auth-client/README.md` updated.
- **Known, documented gap until P18-S2**: `gateway-service`'s own `TokenValidator` has not yet
  been switched to multi-issuer — uncritical as long as `POST /login` does not yet issue local
  tokens, but must be caught up before P18-S2 is complete, otherwise a freshly
  logged-in superuser will fail at the gateway.

**P18-S2 — Superuser migration to local tokens + gateway multi-issuer** (see [ADR 0064](docs/adr/0064-superuser-migration-lokale-tokens-gateway-multi-issuer.md)):

- **`superuser.py` fully switched to async/`TechnicalAccount`**: all functions
  (`ensure_superuser_account`, `activate`, `deactivate`, `get_status`, `get_principal_id`,
  `deactivate_if_expired`) now take a `session_factory` instead of `KeycloakAdmin`. Poll loop,
  NATS consumer (`consumer.py`), and `/superuser/*` endpoints adjusted accordingly — structurally
  unchanged, only the data source changes.
- **`bootstrap.py`'s Keycloak portion removed**: `_ensure_superuser_expires_at_attribute` and the
  `superuser.ensure_superuser_account(admin)` call are dropped — account creation now happens in
  `main.py`'s async lifespan, right next to the local signing key from P18-S1.
- **`POST /login` recognizes technical accounts**: username lookup in `technical_account` before the
  Keycloak password grant; on a hit → bcrypt password check + `enabled`/`expires_at` check (the same
  generic 401 error message for both wrong password AND disabled/expired account, revealing nothing
  about account existence). **`POST /refresh` recognizes local tokens** via the `iss` claim
  (`local_token_issuer.is_local_token`, pure peeking without signature verification) and issues a fresh
  token pair without attempting Keycloak's refresh grant.
- **`gateway-service`'s own `TokenValidator` likewise switched to `MultiIssuerTokenValidator`**
  (new `DMS_AUTH_SERVICE_BASE_URL` setting, pointing to `auth-service`'s JWKS) — without this
  switch, a freshly locally logged-in superuser would have had to fail with 401 on every proxied call,
  even though `auth-service` correctly validates its own token. The local issuer string is duplicated as
  a constant in `gateway_service/main.py` (no shared `libs/` package knows it).
- **Two real bugs found and fixed during test development** (neither of them in the original
  P18-S1 logic, both only made visible by the new P18-S2 tests):
  1. `NotNullViolationError` on the first test run — `technical_account.role_name` was subsequently
     changed from `Mapped[str]` to `Mapped[str | None]` (the superuser itself does not need a
     permission-service role), but the table already existed (from P18-S1's live verification) with
     the old `NOT NULL` constraint. This project works without Alembic (only `create_all`) —
     `create_all` does not change column constraints of existing tables. A manual
     `ALTER TABLE auth.technical_account ALTER COLUMN role_name DROP NOT NULL` was needed in both the
     test AND dev database to bring both back into alignment with the model.
  2. `mint_token()` produced byte-identical tokens for two issuances of the same account within the
     same second (e.g. login immediately followed by refresh in the same test) — `iat`/`exp` only
     have second resolution, and all other claims (`sub`/`username`/`roles`/`aud`) were identical anyway.
     Fix: added `jti` (registered JWT claim, `secrets.token_urlsafe(16)`).
- **Tests**: auth-service 88 (previously 81, +7 new end-to-end login tests in `test_local_superuser_login.py`
  as well as `test_consumer.py`/`test_superuser.py`/`test_bootstrap.py` switched over to the new async/DB
  signature), gateway-service still 22 (unchanged, tests stub `app.state.token_validator`
  directly). `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean.
- **Fully verified live against the real running stack, via the real gateway (not just
  directly against `auth-service`)**: login before activation → `401`; activation; login via
  `localhost:8009` (gateway) → `200` with a valid token pair; `GET /me` via the gateway with the local
  token → `200` (proves the gateway's own multi-issuer switch); a call against `document-service`
  with the same token via the gateway was passed through (`422` due to a required business field,
  not `401` — proves system-wide acceptance of the identity); `POST /refresh` via the gateway → `200`
  with a fresh pair; deactivation → subsequent refresh → `401`. A regular Keycloak login
  (`users-admin`) via the same gateway works unchanged.
- Docs: new [ADR 0064](docs/adr/0064-superuser-migration-lokale-tokens-gateway-multi-issuer.md),
  `docs/services/auth-service.md`/`gateway-service.md` updated (including marking the superuser login
  bug known since P6-S6 as fixed for good).

**P18-S3 — Domain-admin migration to local technical accounts** (see [ADR 0065](docs/adr/0065-domain-admin-migration-lokale-technische-konten.md)), last session of Phase 18:

- **New `domain_admins.py` module**: `ensure_domain_admin_account`/`get_technical_account_id`,
  structurally almost identical to `superuser.py`, two differences: `enabled=True` immediately (no
  break-glass) and multiple accounts distinguishable via `role_name` instead of a singleton.
- **`bootstrap.py`'s `_ensure_domain_admin_accounts(admin: KeycloakAdmin)` removed**, `DOMAIN_ADMIN_
  ACCOUNTS` simplified from `list[tuple[username, role_name, last_name]]` to `list[tuple[username, role_name]]`
  (`last_name` was purely a Keycloak profile requirement). `main.py`'s lifespan role-assignment
  loop structurally unchanged, only the `principal_id` source switches from
  `admin_users.list_users(keycloak_admin)` to `domain_admins.get_technical_account_id(...)`.
- **`POST /login`/`POST /refresh` needed NO change** — the branching logic in place since P18-S2
  only knows "technical account yes/no", not the `account_type`; the
  `notification-service`/`signature-service` self-authentication as `users-admin` (generic
  `POST /login`) remains fully transparent to this and unaffected (confirmed beforehand via a grep
  across all services).
- **A real test infrastructure problem found and fixed** (not a bug in the production logic
  itself): `_clean_tables` previously emptied `auth.technical_account` before EVERY test. Uncritical
  for the superuser (`role_name=None`, no role assignment), fatal for domain admins — the real
  test installation has `permission.role_assignment.create` configured to require the four-eyes
  principle, and `PermissionServiceClient.ensure_role_assignment` deduplicates via `(principal_id, role_id,
  resource_id)`. Since `principal_id` got a new auto-increment ID on every test run (the row was
  freshly created each time), an already-approved assignment could never be found again —
  every test run got stuck on a new, unanswered approval request (this did not occur with the old
  Keycloak account, whose UUID remained stable for the entire session). A first attempted fix via
  fixture parameter ordering (`def domain_admin_auth_headers(role_assignment_
  immediate, client)`) failed — pytest guarantees NO execution order between two independent fixtures
  based on declaration order; what matters is the order in which the TEST FUNCTION
  requests its fixtures (many tests request `client` before `domain_admin_auth_headers`). Fixed
  via two changes: `_clean_tables` now only deletes `technical_account` rows with
  `account_type != 'domain-admin'` (domain-admin rows remain stable across the session), plus a
  new session-wide `autouse` fixture `_bootstrap_domain_admin_role_assignments`, which suspends the
  approval requirement once for a throwaway `TestClient(app)` startup (exactly what a
  real reviewer UI approval would do once).
- **Tests**: auth-service 90 (previously 88, +4 new `test_domain_admins.py`, 2 obsolete Keycloak-based
  bootstrap tests removed). `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean. gateway-service, `dms-auth-client`,
  cmis-connector, notification-service, signature-service, workflow-service unchanged, still green.
  webdav-connector: 13 failures, but exclusively the already-documented, pre-existing
  PROPFIND timeout flake (438 accumulated test artifacts, `httpx.ReadTimeout`) — no auth relation (not a
  single 401 in the log), deliberately not fixed (would delete real data on the running installation).
- **Fully verified live against the actual running stack**: a plain container restart (without an
  image rebuild) is NOT enough to pick up code changes — `docker compose build auth-service`
  is required. After rebuild: the `iss` claim of freshly issued `users-admin`/`config-admin` tokens shows
  `dms-auth-service-local` instead of the Keycloak realm URL. Since `permission.role_assignment.create` also
  requires the four-eyes principle on the real dev installation, the very first assignment got stuck as
  "pending" as expected — manually approved via `POST /approval-requests/{id}/approve` (the step that the
  reviewer UI automates in practice) and `auth-service` restarted: `GET /users` with a
  `users-admin` token via the gateway → `200`, `GET /me` with a `config-admin` token via the gateway →
  `200` with `realm_roles: ["domain-admin-config"]`.
- **Old Keycloak accounts for `users-admin`/`config-admin` remain as dead entries** — `POST
  /login` finds the `TechnicalAccount` first, never reaches the Keycloak fallback anymore. No
  automated cleanup (no data-loss risk), manual cleanup possible via the Keycloak
  admin console, but not required.
- Docs: new [ADR 0065](docs/adr/0065-domain-admin-migration-lokale-technische-konten.md),
  `docs/services/auth-service.md` updated (API table, "realm/client bootstrap",
  "domain-separated admin roles", "auth decoupling from Keycloak" — the live-verification paragraph was
  stale for `users-admin` after this session and was corrected).
- **Phase 18 (auth decoupling from Keycloak) is thereby fully complete** — superuser AND
  domain admins live independently of Keycloak's availability. Resolves the chicken-and-egg problem from ADR 0023
  for P19-S6 (permission-service self-gating needs a technical account that does not depend on Keycloak).
  `graphify update .` performed retroactively (phase completion).

### Phase 19 — Authorization & Identity (P19-S1, ...)

**P19-S1 — New shared lib `libs/dms-permission-client`** (see [ADR 0066](docs/adr/0066-dms-permission-client-shared-lib.md)):

- **Research before implementation found more duplicates than assumed**: the original plan spoke of
  "duplicated sevenfold" (`document-service`, `search-service`, `auth-service`, `workflow-service`,
  `config-service`, `teamspace-service`, `query-service`) — in fact **ten** dedicated
  `PermissionServiceClient` classes existed, plus `license-service`, `monitoring-service`,
  `plugin-orchestration-service` (all three with an identical, minimal `has_permission`/`close` pair).
  No two implementations were byte-identical — each grew organically for its respective
  use case (timeout `10.0` vs. `30.0` depending on the service, different sets of methods,
  different docstring/ordering conventions).
- **Only the four operations common across all duplicates were consolidated**: `check`
  (generalizes `document-service`'s previous `check_read`/`check_write` pair via an
  `access_type` parameter), `check_batch` (taken 1:1 from `search-service`/`query-service`, already
  identical there), `has_permission` (byte-identical in 7 of 10 duplicates), `ensure_role_assignment`
  (taken from `auth-service`, incl. `RoleNotFoundError`/`RoleAssignmentPendingApprovalError` and
  the four-eyes principle check from ADR 0060 — extended by a `resource_id` parameter, default still
  `"root"`, so that future non-root assignments such as `teamspace-service`'s can use the same method).
- **Existing duplicates deliberately NOT force-migrated** (not a refactor for its own sake, see ADR 0066
  "rationale") — five of the ten have service-specific extra methods without general applicability
  (`workflow-service`'s `check_delegation`, `query-service`'s four-eyes client methods,
  `teamspace-service`'s role bootstrap, `config-service`'s role/approval management). New
  consumers starting with upcoming Phase 19 sessions use the shared lib directly.
- **Structure/conventions taken 1:1 from `libs/dms-auth-client`/`libs/dms-metrics-client`**:
  hatchling build, `client: httpx.AsyncClient | None = None` constructor parameter for testability.
  No Dockerfile of any existing service had to change — `COPY libs/ libs/` picks up the
  new directory automatically; the new package only becomes relevant once a service actually
  declares it as a dependency (`[tool.uv.sources] dms-permission-client = { workspace = true }`).
- **First real test coverage of a `PermissionServiceClient`-like HTTP client in this project
  at all** (none of the 10 existing duplicates previously had its own unit tests, not even
  `document-service`'s already-productive `check_read`/`check_write`): 12 new tests with
  `httpx.MockTransport` (project convention instead of `respx`/`pytest-httpx`, neither of which is
  already a dependency), cover all four methods including the idempotency path, the pending-approval path, the empty
  `resource_ids` short-circuit without a server call. `uv lock` updated (152 workspace packages).
  `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean.
- **Pure infrastructure session** (analogous to P18-S1) — no service uses the library yet, no
  live verification against the running stack needed (no endpoint/behavior change).
- Docs: new [ADR 0066](docs/adr/0066-dms-permission-client-shared-lib.md), `libs/README.md` extended with the
  new entry.

**P19-S2 — "everyone" group in permission-service** (see [ADR 0067](docs/adr/0067-everyone-gruppe-permission-service.md)):

- **`principal_type="group"` was until then purely schema decoration**: just a comment (`# "user" | "group"`)
  on the model, `_collect_effective_roles` only evaluated `RoleAssignment.principal_id ==
  principal_id`. As of this session, the function treats an assignment with `principal_type=
  "group", principal_id="everyone"` at any traversed resource node as applying to EVERY principal
  (`or_`/`and_` extension of the existing query, not a second query) — the existing
  inheritance algorithm (ancestor chain, `inherit=false` stops the ascent) applies to it identically to
  individual assignments.
- **New `repository.ensure_everyone_role`** (bootstrap, lifespan, same idiom as
  `ensure_domain_admin_roles`): idempotently seeds both the `Role("everyone")` and its
  `RoleAssignment` at the root resource — unlike domain-admin roles, "everyone" has no external
  account the assignment would otherwise be tied to, so it is seeded directly (deliberately bypassing
  the four-eyes-gated `POST /role-assignments` endpoint — bootstrap infrastructure,
  not a runtime admin action).
- **Seeded permissions (`users.lookup`, `users.directory`) correspond to the two endpoints
  hardcoded as open in `auth-service`** (`GET /users/lookup`, `GET /users/directory`, previously
  without any RBAC check, only `Depends(get_current_user)`) — this session only prepares the role,
  does NOT yet change `auth-service` itself (follows in P19-S3).
- **`schemas.RoleAssignmentCreate`/`RoleAssignmentOut.principal_type`** tightened from `str` to
  `Literal["user", "group"]` (matches the file's already established `Literal` style).
- **Two existing `permission-service`-internal API tests had to be adjusted** (`test_full_flow_
  via_api`, `test_list_role_assignments_filters_by_principal_id`) — both previously checked exact
  equality against an empty or role-specific permission set at the root resource; since
  this session, EVERY principal additionally has the "everyone" base set there. A real, intended
  behavior change, not a test bug — no other service affected (`users.lookup`/`users.directory`
  are new permission strings not used anywhere else in the project so far, confirmed via grep; every
  existing `/check`/`/check/batch` call checks a different, specific permission).
- **Tests**: permission-service 124 (previously 122, +6 new in `test_repository.py`: seed idempotency,
  application to an arbitrary/never-seen principal, inheritance, respecting `inherit=false`,
  combination with an individual assignment). `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean.
- **Fully verified live against the actual running stack** (after image rebuild, plain
  container restart does not pick up code changes, see P18-S3 lesson): `GET
  /effective-permissions/<never-seen principal>/root` returns `roles: ["everyone"]`, `permissions:
  ["users.directory", "users.lookup"]`. Idempotency confirmed across two real, consecutive
  restarts (identical role/assignment IDs, no duplicates).
- Docs: new [ADR 0067](docs/adr/0067-everyone-gruppe-permission-service.md),
  `docs/services/permission-service.md` updated (new "'everyone' group" section, data model
  and "Open Points" row about group membership corrected).

**P19-S3 — `GET /users/lookup`/`GET /users/directory` gated via the "everyone" role** (see [ADR 0068](docs/adr/0068-users-lookup-directory-everyone-gate.md)):

- **New generic `_require_permission(user, permission, message)` helper** in `auth-service/main.py`
  — `lookup_user`/`search_directory` call it with `"users.lookup"`/`"users.directory"` (the
  permissions seeded in P19-S2); `_require_user_management` (existing domain-admin helper)
  now calls it internally instead of duplicating three nearly identical lines.
- **Nothing changes about the actual behavior**: the "everyone" role still grants both permissions
  to every authenticated principal by default — the difference is purely structural, an admin can now
  revoke the permission via a role edit without changing code.
- **First real bug found during regression**: ADR 0067's `Literal["user", "group"]` tightening
  of `principal_type` was too narrow — `config-service` and `migration-service` already use
  a third value in production, `principal_type="service"` (technical accounts without a Keycloak account).
  `auth-service`'s own `test_realm_roles.py` was the first to reveal it (`KeyError: 'role_assignment'`, since `POST
  /role-assignments` was newly returning `422` instead of the expected `RoleAssignmentActionResult`).
  Fixed by widening to `Literal["user", "group", "service"]`.
- **Second, independent bug found**: `permission_service.repository.update_role` (`PUT
  /roles/{id}`) did not invalidate the effective-permissions cache — unlike every other
  permission-changing operation in this module. A principal whose cached data was already stale would only lose
  a permission revoked via a role update after an independent, random cache clear.
  Uncritical as long as roles were edited rarely — became security-relevant now that the "everyone" role
  is admin-editable (ADR 0067). Found while writing a negative test (deliberately revoke a permission,
  expect `403`), fixed with a regression test.
- **New `everyone_role_without` fixture** (`auth-service/tests/conftest.py`, same
  restoration pattern as `role_assignment_immediate`) — since the "everyone" role applies to EVERY
  principal, there is no user who lacks it; the negative test must temporarily manipulate the shared role
  itself.
- **Tests**: `auth-service` 92 (previously 90, +2 new negative tests). `permission-service` 125 (previously 124,
  +1: `update_role` cache invalidation). `config-service`/`migration-service` unchanged, still green after the
  `Literal` fix. `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean for all four services.
- **Fully verified live against the actual running stack** (after image rebuild of `auth-service`
  AND `permission-service`): `GET /users/lookup`/`GET /users/directory` via the gateway → `200`
  (default behavior unchanged); after deliberately revoking `users.lookup` from the "everyone" role via
  `PUT /roles/{id}` → the same call immediately returns `403` without a restart (proves both the gate AND the
  cache-invalidation fix in a single call), `GET /users/directory` remains unaffected
  (`users.directory` not revoked). Restored afterward.
- **Pre-existing, independent problem discovered in `config-service`'s test suite, NOT fixed**
  (outside session scope, different service): `permission.role_assignment.create` is configured to require the
  four-eyes principle on this real installation (already known since P18-S3) —
  `config-service/tests/conftest.py::authorized_principal` does not (unlike `auth-service`'s
  `role_assignment_immediate`) temporarily suspend that, causing 11 tests to fail. Independently
  verified (the failure disappears entirely with the approval requirement temporarily disabled, recurs
  after restoration) — analogous to the already-documented webdav-connector timeout flake
  from P18-S3, not a problem caused by this session.
- Docs: new [ADR 0068](docs/adr/0068-users-lookup-directory-everyone-gate.md),
  `docs/services/auth-service.md` updated (API table for both endpoints, "Open Points" row
  on the existence oracle corrected), ADR 0067 extended with a correction note about the `Literal` fix.

**P19-S4 — Reverse identity resolution** (see [ADR 0069](docs/adr/0069-rueckwaerts-identitaetsaufloesung.md)):

- **New `GET /users/{user_id}` endpoint in `auth-service`** — counterpart to the existing `GET
  /users/lookup?username=`: new `admin_users.find_user_by_id` (mirrors `find_user_by_username`,
  uses `KeycloakAdmin.get_user(user_id)` instead of `get_users(query=...)`, catches `KeycloakGetError`
  with `response_code == 404`), same gate as `lookup_user` (`_require_permission(user,
  "users.lookup", ...)` — reuses the same permission, no new one needed since it's conceptually
  the same operation, just reversed). **Registration order observed**: placed after all static
  `/users/...` paths (directly next to `DELETE /users/{user_id}`), since FastAPI/Starlette matches routes in
  registration order — an earlier-registered `/users/{user_id}` would have
  shadowed `/users/lookup`/`/users/directory`/`/users/count`.
- **New `usePrincipalNames` hook** (`apps/user-ui/src/lib/usePrincipalNames.ts`) — resolves a list of
  raw `principal_id`s into usernames, with an in-memory cache across the hook's calls (no
  repeated request for already-resolved IDs) and a fallback to the raw UUID on failure (never blocks
  the display). `DelegationsPane.tsx` (both lists: "My delegations"/"I represent") and
  `TeamspacesPane.tsx` (member list) now use it instead of raw UUIDs.
- **New `lookupUserById` function** in `apps/user-ui/src/lib/api.ts`, same conventions as
  `lookupUserByUsername` (same `UserLookup` interface).
- **Tests**: `auth-service` 96 (previously 92, +4: positive/negative path for `GET /users/{id}`, same
  structure as the `GET /users/lookup` tests from P19-S3). `user-ui` 169 Vitest tests green (+2 new, one
  resolution test each in `delegations-pane.test.tsx`/`teamspaces-pane.test.tsx`) — existing tests
  remain unchanged and valid, since `lookupUserById` fails by default in them (not mocked) and
  the hook falls back to the raw ID, exactly the previously expected behavior. `tsc --noEmit`,
  `eslint .`, `next build` clean.
- **A deployment error found and fixed immediately during the first live check** (not a code bug): `GET
  /users/{id}` initially returned `405 Method Not Allowed` via the gateway — `auth-service`'s
  Docker image had not yet been rebuilt (a plain container restart does not pick up code changes,
  a repeated lesson from P18-S3/P19-S3). After `docker compose build auth-service`, the call worked
  as expected.
- **Fully verified live against the actual running stack** (after image rebuild): `GET
  /users/lookup` returns `users-admin`'s own ID, `GET /users/{id}` resolves the same ID back to
  `{"id": ..., "username": "users-admin"}`, an unknown UUID returns `404`. All three
  pre-existing static `/users/...` routes (`count`/`lookup`/`directory`) function
  unchanged — confirmed no shadowing by the new `/users/{user_id}` route.
- **No browser verification possible this session**: this sandbox environment has no
  browser automation tool available (no Playwright/Chromium) — the frontend correctness
  relies on `tsc`/`eslint`/`vitest`/`next build` (all green) plus the live verification of
  the backend contract that `lookupUserById` actually calls. No actual display in the browser
  confirmed — documented as an open follow-up for a later session with browser access.
- Docs: new [ADR 0069](docs/adr/0069-rueckwaerts-identitaetsaufloesung.md),
  `docs/services/auth-service.md`'s API table extended with `GET /users/{id}`,
  `docs/services/user-ui.md`'s and `docs/services/permission-service.md`'s "Open Points" bullets on the
  raw `principal_id` display marked as fixed (struck through).

**P19-S5 — case-service RBAC** (see [ADR 0070](docs/adr/0070-case-service-rbac.md)):

- **case-service previously had NO permission check whatsoever** — not even an
  `X-DMS-Principal` header check like other services had before their respective enforcement. New
  `_require_case_permission(x_dms_principal, *, access_type)` helper (`main.py`): `401` without a header,
  otherwise `PermissionServiceClient.check(..., permission="case.read"|"case.write", resource_id="root")`,
  `403` on denial. **First consumer of `libs/dms-permission-client`** (P19-S1) at all — no
  separate, duplicated `permission_client.py` needed anymore.
- **All humanly usable endpoints gated**: `POST/GET /cases`, `GET /cases/by-vorgangsnummer`,
  `GET /cases/{id}`, `POST/DELETE .../documents`, `GET .../documents`, `POST .../archive-request`
  (a human action despite being "instance-changing"), `GET .../archive-status`, all four
  config endpoints.
- **Two purely internal machine-to-machine callbacks deliberately left UNGATED**: `GET
  /cases/due-for-archival` and `PUT /cases/{id}/archived` — both called exclusively by `archival-service`,
  which sends no identity header for these. Exactly the same, already pre-existing gap
  as `document-service`'s analogous `PUT /documents/{id}/archived` (verified, also ungated) —
  a general service-to-service authentication is a larger, project-wide decision
  outside this session.
- **"everyone" group (ADR 0067) extended with `case.read`/`case.write`** — preserves the previous
  de-facto-open behavior (case-service previously checked nothing), but makes it admin-editable. Since
  `ensure_everyone_role` does not automatically update an already-created role (no
  migration mechanism, see P19-S3 docstring), the already-running installation had to be manually
  updated once via `PUT /roles/{id}` (as a real admin would).
- **Three real, independent regressions found and fixed during live verification** (none of them
  covered by unit/integration tests, since these run with explicit `TEST_*_URL` variables):
  1. `infra/docker-compose.yml`'s `case-service` block had no `DMS_PERMISSION_SERVICE_BASE_URL` —
     fell back to the local-dev default (`localhost:8004`), which points nowhere inside the container.
     Every gated call therefore failed with `httpx.ConnectError`/`500` instead of a clean `401`/`403`.
     Fixed by adding the missing env var plus `depends_on: permission-service`.
  2. `archival-service`'s `CaseClient.get_case`/`.list_document_references`/`.get_archival_config` were now
     calling gated endpoints without a header.
  3. `mail-connector`'s `CaseClient` (3 methods) as well as its **own test suite**
     (`conftest.py::real_case_id`, `test_api.py::_get_case`/`_get_case_documents` — direct
     `httpx` calls against the real running case-service container) likewise.
  All fixed with a synthetic `X-DMS-Principal: system:<Service>` header (an established
  naming pattern in the project, cf. `actor="system:archival-service"` on published events) — the
  "everyone" group makes a dedicated technical account for this unnecessary.
- **Tests**: case-service 50 (previously 45, +5: positive/negative tests for `create_case`/`list_cases`,
  `archive-request` auth requirement). New fixtures `case_headers`/`everyone_role_without` (the latter
  duplicated from `auth-service`'s pattern). `archival-service` 59, `mail-connector` 30 — both unchanged,
  still green after the header fixes. `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean for all four services.
- **Fully verified live against the actual running stack** (after image rebuild of all three
  services + compose fix + restart): `GET /api/case-service/cases` via the gateway → `200`(with
  token)/`401`(without); `GET /case-archival-config` directly with `system:archival-service` → `200` with
  real data; `GET /cases/due-for-archival` still reachable ungated; `mail-connector`'s
  Vorgangsnummer lookup with `system:mail-connector` → `200`.
- Docs: new [ADR 0070](docs/adr/0070-case-service-rbac.md), `docs/services/case-service.md`
  updated (API table note, two "Open Points" bullets marked as (partially) fixed),
  `docs/services/permission-service.md`/`archival-service.md`/`mail-connector.md` extended.

**P19-S6 — permission-service self-gating** (see [ADR 0071](docs/adr/0071-permission-service-self-gating.md)):

- **`POST /roles`/`PUT /roles/{id}` and `POST`/`DELETE /scope-locks` previously checked NO
  permission at all** — any arbitrary, unauthenticated caller could create/modify roles or
  set/release scope locks (the latter despite an already existing four-eyes option, see P6-S4).
  New `_require_role_management(session, x_dms_principal)` helper (header-based, `401` without a header)
  for `/roles`; scope locks instead use the already-existing body fields
  `locked_by`/`released_by` as the principal source (no second, inconsistent identity source in the
  same endpoint) — both check `admin.user_management`, the scope-lock check runs **before** the
  existing four-eyes branch.
- **`GET /roles` and all three `/role-assignments` endpoints deliberately remain ungated**: `GET /roles`
  is relied upon project-wide for get-or-create-by-name of roles; gating `POST /role-assignments` would
  put `auth-service`'s bootstrap (creates the very first role assignment for `users-admin`) in front of the
  chicken-and-egg problem described in ADR 0023. Prior research confirmed: role *creation* itself
  does not depend on any HTTP bootstrap path in ANY service (every service creates its startup roles directly
  against `repository`) — so the chicken-and-egg problem really only affects `/role-assignments`, not `/roles`.
- **Three real consumers updated**, who already held the necessary capability at their own bootstrap, but
  (unlike their sibling clients) did not send an `X-DMS-Principal` header:
  `config-service`'s `PermissionServiceClient` (header `config-service`, no bootstrap addition needed —
  `domain-admin-users` had already been in `_REQUIRED_ROLE_NAMES` since P17-S1), `migration-service`'s
  `LocalDmsClient` (header `migration-service`, affects both scope-lock methods AND
  `apply_role_assignment`'s role get-or-create) plus its own bootstrap extended with
  `domain-admin-users` (`_REQUIRED_ROLE_NAMES = ("domain-admin-config", "domain-admin-users")`).
- **Deliberate, documented consequence**: no general superuser bypass for `require_capability` in
  `permission-service` (only `POST /maintenance-mode/lift` has a hardcoded special check) — a
  break-glass superuser without an explicit `admin.user_management` assignment can no longer, as of this session,
  create/modify roles or set/release scope locks. A larger architectural change,
  outside this session's scope.
- **Tests**: permission-service 128 (previously ~122) — `role_management_headers` fixture threaded
  through `test_api.py` (new positive/negative tests for both endpoint groups), `_grant_permission_
  via_api` helper extended with a `headers` parameter, two further tests in `test_scope_lock_events.py`
  updated with a local `_grant_scope_lock_permission` helper function. `config-service` 48,
  `migration-service` 8 — both unchanged, still green after the header fixes. `ruff check`/
  `ruff format --check` clean for all three services.
- **Fully verified live against the actual running stack** (after image rebuild of all three
  services + restart): `POST /roles` without a header → `401`, wrong principal → `403`, with
  `X-DMS-Principal: config-service` → `200`; `POST /scope-locks` with an empty `locked_by` → `403`;
  `migration-service`'s own scope-lock acquire/release cycle → successful; both services still hold
  `domain-admin-users` per `GET /role-assignments` after the restart, no `*_role_missing` warning
  in the bootstrap logs.
- **A data-cleanup finding during live verification**: `permission.role_assignment.create`
  was still set to `requires_approval=true` in the running development database from an earlier,
  manual live verification — broke `config-service`'s own `authorized_principal` test fixture (which
  expects an immediate role assignment). Unrelated to this session, but blocked its
  test run against the real stack — reset to `false`.
- Docs: new [ADR 0071](docs/adr/0071-permission-service-self-gating.md), `docs/services/
  permission-service.md` (API table, two "Open Points" bullets marked as fixed plus a new
  bullet about the missing superuser bypass), `docs/services/config-service.md`/`migration-service.md`
  extended.

**P19-S7 — Archival/reporting/forensics gating** (see [ADR 0072](docs/adr/0072-archival-reporting-rbac.md)):

- **`archival-service` previously had NO general permission check at all** (only the separate,
  narrower `archive_retrieval_role` gate for retrieval/disposal access scope/package download,
  concept 5.6 "decryption only for authorized roles") — new `_require_archival_permission`
  helper, first consumer of `libs/dms-permission-client` in this service, checks `archival.read`/
  `archival.write` on all eight endpoints. The existing role gate remains unchanged and
  additionally in place (layering instead of replacing, same principle as ADR 0070).
- **`reporting-service` also had NO permission check at all, not even for the forensic trace**
  — new `_require_reporting_permission` helper. Standard reports/schedules/downloads use
  `reporting.read`/`reporting.write`; the forensic trace gets the separate, narrower
  `reporting.forensic_trace` instead of `reporting.read` due to its heightened sensitivity
  (potentially extensive disclosure of user activity).
- **Correction of the roadmap description**: the original P19-S7 wording ("query-service's
  `/forensic-trace`") was imprecise — the forensic trace lives in `reporting-service`, not
  `query-service`. `query-service` itself was already fully gated since P8-S2 (`admin.
  query_console`/`.manipulate`, own `PermissionServiceClient`) and needed no change.
- **`queried_by` spoofing gap closed**: the previous `queried_by` query parameter, freely chosen by the
  client (never verified server-side, see the previous "Open Points") has been removed without replacement — self-
  auditing (the `reporting.forensic_trace.queried` event) now uses
  exclusively the verified `X-DMS-Principal` header as the actor source.
- **"everyone" group (ADR 0067) extended with five new permissions**: `archival.read`,
  `archival.write`, `reporting.read`, `reporting.write`, `reporting.forensic_trace` — preserves
  the previous de-facto-open behavior of both services. Since `ensure_everyone_role` does not automatically
  update an already-created role (a known limitation since P19-S3), the already-running
  installation had to be manually updated once via `PUT /roles/{id}`.
- **A previously undiscovered test regression from P19-S6 found and fixed**: `permission-service`'s
  `PUT /roles/{id}` gating (ADR 0071) had already broken FOUR existing `everyone_role_without`
  test fixtures (`case-service`, `auth-service`, plus the two new ones from this session) — this went
  undiscovered in P19-S6 since only `permission-service`/`config-service`/`migration-service` were tested
  there, not the services with this fixture. `case-service`/`archival-service`/`reporting-service`:
  new test principal with a `domain-admin-users` role assignment via a `_grant_role_admin_permission`
  session fixture. `auth-service`: more complex, since `permission-service`'s `principal_id` for the
  technical `users-admin` account is the `TechnicalAccount.id` (an integer as a string), not the
  username — the fixture now resolves this ID via its OWN SQLAlchemy engine (not
  `app.state.session_factory`, which is bound to the TestClient's internal event loop and would have
  failed from a separate `asyncio.run()` fixture with "attached to a different loop").
- **No other service calls archival-service/reporting-service via HTTP** (confirmed via
  research) — unlike case-service (P19-S5), no `system:<Service>` header fix was needed
  anywhere else.
- **Tests**: archival-service 61 (previously 55, +6), reporting-service 57 (previously 51, +6, incl.
  adjusted forensic-trace tests after removing `queried_by`), auth-service 96/case-service 50
  (unchanged count, but the tests broken by P19-S6 are green again). `ruff check`/
  `ruff format --check` clean for all four services.
- **Fully verified live against the actual running stack** (after image rebuild of
  `archival-service`/`reporting-service` + restart, plus manually updating the running
  "everyone" role): `GET /archival-transfers` without a header → `401`, with a principal → `200`; `GET
  /reports/document-volume` without a header → `401`, with a principal → `200`; `GET /forensic-trace` without a
  header → `401`, with a principal → `200`. Both container logs show clean startups.
- Docs: new [ADR 0072](docs/adr/0072-archival-reporting-rbac.md), `docs/services/archival-service.md`/
  `reporting-service.md` (API tables, "Open Points" marked as fixed), `docs/services/
  permission-service.md` ("everyone" list), `docs/services/query-service.md` (clarification: forensic
  trace now base-authenticated, but still without row-level result filtering) extended.

**P19-S8 — OCR/rendering/virus-scan gating** (see [ADR 0073](docs/adr/0073-ocr-rendering-virus-scan-rbac.md)):

- **`virus-scan-service`'s quarantine area previously only checked a plain `X-DMS-Roles`
  string-equality gate** (`_has_quarantine_role`, `settings.quarantine_admin_role`, default
  `"dms-admin"`) — no real permission-service call. REPLACED (not supplemented, unlike ADR 0072's
  `archive_retrieval_role`) with `_require_quarantine_permission`: checks `admin.quarantine` via
  `PermissionServiceClient.has_permission`. New domain-admin role `domain-admin-virus-scan` carries
  this permission. `quarantine_admin_role`/`DMS_QUARANTINE_ADMIN_ROLE` removed without replacement.
- **Why replaced instead of supplemented**: the roadmap task literally said "elevated to a real
  permission-service check" — an elevation, not a supplement. Unlike `archive_retrieval_role` (an
  independently concept-anchored control), `quarantine_admin_role` had only ever been a placeholder mechanism.
- **Why a NEW domain-admin role instead of an "everyone" extension**: concept 2.5 literally names quarantine
  access as "a dedicated, narrowly scoped role" — unlike archival-service/
  reporting-service/case-service, this area was ALREADY a real permission restricted to
  `dms-admin` before this session, not a previously de-facto-open gap. "everyone" serves to preserve
  previously OPEN behavior — the opposite applies here.
- **`document-service`'s independent `quarantine_release_admin_role` gate remains unchanged**: the
  `POST /documents/from-quarantine-release` check there is a SEPARATE mechanism in a
  different service, outside this session — `virus-scan-service`'s `release_scan` continues to pass
  `x_dms_roles` through unchanged.
- **`ocr-service` and `rendering-service` had NO permission check at all** — new
  `_require_ocr_permission`/`_require_rendering_permission` helpers (identical pattern to ADR 0072):
  `ocr.read`/`ocr.write` resp. `rendering.read`/`rendering.write` on the root resource. Both extended in the
  "everyone" group (preserves the previous de-facto-open behavior).
- **Four real consumers updated** (`GET /ocr-results`/`GET /renditions`/`.../content` run from
  NATS consumer contexts without a human principal): `rendering-service`'s `OcrServiceClient`,
  `search-service`'s `OcrServiceClient` and `RenderingServiceClient`, `archival-service`'s
  `RenderingClient` — all four with a synthetic `X-DMS-Principal: system:<Service>` header.
- **Fifth regression find from P19-S6** (after the four from P19-S7): `search-service`'s
  `test_api.py::_grant_root_read` called `POST /roles` without `X-DMS-Principal` — broken since P19-S6's
  gating. Fixed with the same `_grant_role_admin_permission` session fixture pattern.
- **Tests**: virus-scan-service 32 (role-string tests replaced with RBAC positive/negative tests),
  ocr-service 40 (+8 skipped unchanged), rendering-service 34, search-service 56 (+2 fixes), all
  green. `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean for all affected services.
- **Fully verified live against the actual running stack** (after image rebuild of
  `virus-scan-service`/`ocr-service`/`rendering-service`/`search-service`/`archival-service` +
  restart, plus manually updating the running "everyone" role and restarting
  `permission-service` to seed the new `domain-admin-virus-scan` role): `GET
  /scans?status=infected` without a header → `401`, authenticated without `admin.quarantine` → `403`, with
  the assigned role → `200`; `GET /scans` (no filter) still ungated; `GET /config`
  (ocr-service)/`GET /renditions` (rendering-service) each without a header → `401`, with a principal → `200`.
  All container logs show clean startups.
- Docs: new [ADR 0073](docs/adr/0073-ocr-rendering-virus-scan-rbac.md), `docs/services/
  virus-scan-service.md`/`ocr-service.md`/`rendering-service.md` (API tables, "Open Points"
  marked as fixed), `docs/services/permission-service.md` ("everyone" list, domain-admin role
  table), `docs/services/archival-service.md` (`RenderingClient` header) extended.

**P19-S9 — Workflow instance start & task completion RBAC** (see [ADR 0074](docs/adr/0074-workflow-instance-task-rbac.md)):

- **`POST /process-definitions/{id}/instances` and `POST /instances/{id}/tasks/{id}/complete` had
  deliberately been open to every authenticated principal since P6-S6** ("normal business use should not need a
  domain-admin role", a documented user decision) — new
  `_require_workflow_permission(x_dms_principal, *, access_type)` helper now checks `workflow.write`.
  Runs in both endpoints AFTER `_reject_during_maintenance` (4.8 remains the outermost gate) and BEFORE
  all more specific checks (signature, federation rejection, delegation).
- **`workflow-service`'s local `PermissionServiceClient` gets a new `check()` method**
  (same signature as `libs/dms-permission-client`) instead of moving to the shared lib —
  `check_delegation` is, per ADR 0066, deliberately a service-specific extra method, a full migration would
  have offered no benefit here.
- **"everyone" group (ADR 0067) extended with `workflow.write`** — preserves the previous, documented-
  deliberate open behavior, but makes it admin-editable instead of hardcoded open forever. The
  original P6-S6 decision ("no domain-admin role needed") is thereby substantively preserved,
  only the mechanism changes from hardcoded to admin-editable.
- **`case-service`'s `WorkflowClient.start_instance`** (the only real HTTP caller besides
  migration-service) previously sent no `X-DMS-Principal` header at all — gets a new
  `x_dms_principal` parameter, which passes through the caller already verified by
  `_require_case_permission` (deliberately NOT `payload.created_by`, an unverified body field analogous to
  reporting-service's `queried_by` anti-pattern from ADR 0072) — creating a circulation folder is a
  real action attributable to a human, a synthetic `system:case-service` account would have
  needlessly diluted the audit trail.
- **`migration-service` needed no change** — its `WorkflowServiceClient` has already been sending
  `X-DMS-Principal: migration-service` by default since P12-S2, "everyone" automatically covers this
  principal.
- **Tests**: `workflow-service` 171 (test count unchanged; `client` fixtures in three test files
  now carry an `X-DMS-Principal` header by default, a test that specifically proved the 401 path for a
  missing header on the delegation check now explicitly overrides the header to empty
  — the assertion remains `401`, only the triggering mechanism is now the new base RBAC check).
  `case-service` 50, `migration-service` 8 — unchanged, still green. `ruff check`/`ruff format --check`
  clean.
- **Fully verified live against the actual running stack** (after image rebuild of
  `workflow-service`/`case-service` + restart, plus manually updating the running "everyone"
  role): `POST /process-definitions/{id}/instances` without a header → `401`, with a principal → `201`.
  `case-service`'s own test suite already covers the real end-to-end path without mocking.
- **`POST /instances/{id}/retry` deliberately remains ungated** — the roadmap task explicitly named only
  instance start and task completion.
- Docs: new [ADR 0074](docs/adr/0074-workflow-instance-task-rbac.md), `docs/services/
  workflow-service.md` (API table, "authorization & maintenance mode" and "Open Points" sections
  updated) extended.

**P19-S10 — Legal-hold RBAC** (see [ADR 0075](docs/adr/0075-legal-hold-rbac.md)):

- **`POST /legal-holds` and `POST /legal-holds/{id}/release` had NO permission check whatsoever in
  BOTH services (5.2)** — not even an `X-DMS-Principal` header parameter. New domain-admin
  role `domain-admin-legal-hold` (`admin.legal_hold`) — a dedicated domain instead of reusing
  `domain-admin-deletion` (deletion administration), since the two are conceptually opposite (a hold
  prevents deletion, a deletion admin carries it out).
- **`document-service`**: new `_require_legal_hold_permission` helper, uses a new
  `has_permission` method on the already-existing local `PermissionServiceClient` (previously only
  `check_read`/`check_write` for share links/WebDAV edit tokens).
- **`folder-service`**: identical pattern, but first consumer of `libs/dms-permission-client` in
  this service — folder-service previously had NO permission-service client of any kind (only the
  separate `ApprovalClient` for the four-eyes principle).
- **`GET /legal-holds` and `GET /documents/{id}/has-active-hold` deliberately remain ungated** — the
  hold status display must remain visible to EVERY viewer, not only legal-hold
  administrators; `has-active-hold` is additionally a pure machine-to-machine callback from
  `archival-service` before every dehydration step.
- **Deliberately NOT in the "everyone" group** (unlike most P19-S5/S7/S8 targets) — a legal
  hold is a legally significant, administrative action, the previous openness was an
  unchecked gap, not deliberately granted behavior worth preserving.
- **`apps/user-ui`**: `RetentionPanel.tsx`/`FolderRetentionModal.tsx` only show the legal-hold buttons as
  active when `permissions.includes("admin.legal_hold")` (they remain visible but disabled otherwise, so
  the status display works for everyone) — new `getEffectivePermissions` API function (ported from
  `apps/admin-ui`) and a new `permissions: string[]` on `AuthContextValue`, populated on login/
  session restore. **First UI-side role check against a real permission-service capability instead
  of a raw Keycloak realm role name** (unlike `MetadataPanel`'s existing `Kennzeichen` field,
  which checks `user.realm_roles`).
- **Sixth regression find from P19-S6** (after the four from P19-S7, the one from P19-S8):
  `document-service`'s `test_api.py::_grant_root_permission` (used by share-link/WebDAV edit-
  token tests, not only legal hold) called `POST /roles` without a header — document-service's full
  test suite had not run completely since P19-S6, but this went undiscovered. Fixed with the same
  `_grant_role_admin_permission` session fixture pattern.
- **Tests**: `document-service` 233 (previously 215, +18), `folder-service` 116 (previously 112, +4).
  `apps/user-ui`: `tsc`/`eslint`/`vitest` (169 tests, four test files had to update `getEffectivePermissions`/
  `permissions` in their mocks) and `next build` clean.
- **Fully verified live against the actual running stack** (after image rebuild of
  `document-service`/`folder-service`/`permission-service` + restart to seed the new role):
  `POST /legal-holds` without a header → `401`, authenticated without `admin.legal_hold` → `403`, with
  the assigned role → `404` (unknown document/folder, proving successful authorization).
  `GET .../has-active-hold` still reachable ungated. Both container logs show clean startups.
- Docs: new [ADR 0075](docs/adr/0075-legal-hold-rbac.md), `docs/services/document-service.md`/
  `folder-service.md`/`user-ui.md` (API tables, "Open Points" marked as fixed),
  `docs/services/permission-service.md` (domain-admin role table) extended.

**P19-S11 — Three small, isolated fixes** (see [ADR 0076](docs/adr/0076-root-folder-mail-regex-dehydration-409.md)),
last session of Phase 19:

- **`folder-service`: `root` as a protected special folder.** `PROTECTED_FOLDER_IDS` had previously only
  contained `inbox`/`outbox` — `root` could be renamed, moved, hard-deleted, or moved to the trash.
  Now part of the same `frozenset`, automatically goes through the same three existing
  `409` checks (`update_folder`, `hard_delete_folder`, `trash_folder`) — no new check logic needed.
- **`mail-connector`: candidate detection from the actually configured formats.** New
  `build_candidate_pattern(formats)` (`matching.py`) derives the candidate regex directly from
  `object-type-service`'s `kennzeichen_format` and `case-service`'s `CaseNumberConfig.format` (the first
  format-to-regex reverse conversion in the project) — freshly loaded per incoming message, with a fallback to
  the old generic pattern on cross-service failure. Fresh, short-lived
  `ObjectTypeClient`/`CaseClient` instances per message instead of reuse from `app.state` — a
  long-lived client bound to the lifespan event loop led, in tests that call `_ingest_message()`
  directly (under pytest-asyncio's own event loop), to `RuntimeError: ... bound to a different
  event loop`.
- **`document-service`: `409` instead of `404` for dehydrated document content.** `GET /documents/{id}/content`
  and `.../versions/{n}/content` now check `document.dehydrated_at` BEFORE the storage call and return `409`
  with a retrieval hint — previously a generic `404`, indistinguishable from a real data inconsistency (see
  `test_download_content_returns_404_instead_of_crashing_if_object_missing`). `apps/user-ui`'s
  `PreviewPane.tsx` previously handled a failed download completely silently (empty
  `catch`) — new `downloadError` state now shows a targeted `409` message with a retrieval hint
  resp. a generic fallback message for any other error.
- **Test data fix in `mail-connector`**: `_real_document_with_kennzeichen()`'s test helper generated a
  hex-based `unique_kennzeichen` suffix, which no longer matched the new, stricter `Laufende_Nummer → \d+`
  regex (a real, system-generated reference-number suffix is always numeric anyway) — switched
  to a numeric suffix.
- **Two real bugs found and fixed during live verification** (only visible because this session,
  for the first time, ran a real SMTP→POP3 round trip with an installation-specific format
  deviating from the default): (1) `infra/docker-compose.yml`'s `mail-connector` block had no
  `DMS_OBJECT_TYPE_SERVICE_BASE_URL` — fell back in the container to the local-dev default pointing
  nowhere, the candidate pattern silently used the generic fallback as a result (functionally unnoticeable
  for the default formats, but this session's new capability had no effect). (2)
  `build_candidate_pattern` sorted formats alphabetically instead of by length — Python's `re` alternation
  is "first matching alternative wins", not longest-match matching like POSIX; with three live
  configured formats, alphabetical ordering placed a shorter, year-less format before a
  longer one, causing a real candidate to be incorrectly cut off early. Fixed by sorting
  by descending format length, three new targeted unit tests in `test_matching.py`.
- **Pre-existing, independent test infrastructure flakiness identified, NOT fixed**: a sporadically
  failing mail-connector test (`test_confirm_match_creates_document_in_matched_folder`,
  "different event loop" with a long-lived `app.state.virus_scan` client) — outside the
  session scope, confirmed via multiple isolation runs as already pre-existing before this session.
- **Tests**: `folder-service` 120 (previously 116, +4: rename/move/hard-delete/trash for
  `root`). `mail-connector` 33 (previously 30, +3: `build_candidate_pattern` unit tests incl. a regression test
  for the sorting bug). `document-service` 234 (previously 233, +1). `apps/user-ui`: 171 tests (previously
  169, +2), `tsc`/`eslint`/`next build` clean.
- **Fully verified live against the actual running stack** (after image rebuild of
  `folder-service`/`mail-connector`/`document-service` + restart, `mail-connector` twice due to the
  two bugfixes described above): `root` rename/move/hard-delete/trash
  each return `409`; a real SMTP→POP3 round trip against `mailpit` with a live-generated,
  attribute-based reference number created via `document-service` (`P19S11Z-2026-005`, object type "file")
  is correctly recognized after both bugfixes as `status="proposed_match"` with a matching `document_id`;
  a dehydrated document returns `409` with a retrieval hint instead of `404`, after rehydration `200` again.
- Docs: new [ADR 0076](docs/adr/0076-root-folder-mail-regex-dehydration-409.md), `docs/services/
  folder-service.md`/`mail-connector.md`/`document-service.md` ("Open Points" marked as fixed),
  `docs/services/user-ui.md` (new "download error display" section) extended.

**Phase 19 — Authorization & Identity is thereby fully complete** (P19-S1 through P19-S11, see
`IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`).

### Phase 20 — Resilience: Retry, Backoff, Permanent-Failed (P20-S1, ...)

**P20-S1 — `libs/dms-retry`: shared backoff/jitter math** (see
[ADR 0077](docs/adr/0077-dms-retry-backoff-jitter-lib.md)):

- New, very small shared lib with exactly one function:
  `compute_backoff_seconds(attempt, *, base=1.0, cap=300.0, rng=None) -> float` — "full jitter"
  exponential backoff following the AWS standard formula (`random(0, min(cap, base*2**attempt))`).
  `attempt` is 0-indexed and fits directly onto `storage-service`'s already existing
  `ObjectCopy.attempts` field (starts at 0, before the first attempt).
- **Why a new lib instead of extending `dms-common`**: `dms-common` is explicitly described for
  settings/logging/OpenTelemetry basics — a backoff formula is only consumed by the five
  affected retry poll loops (storage, archival, notification, rendering/ocr,
  federation-hub-service), not by every service.
- **Why "full jitter"**: spreads simultaneously failed attempts most widely across the
  time window, avoids a renewed thundering-herd effect at the next poll tick.
- **`rng` parameter instead of the global `random` module**: allows deterministic unit tests
  (`random.Random(seed)`) without `unittest.mock.patch` — same injection principle as
  `libs/dms-permission-client`'s `client` parameter.
- **Deliberately NO shared poll-loop framework**: the project deliberately duplicates poll loops
  lightweight (`_sla_poll_loop`/`_superuser_poll_loop`/`_archival_poll_loop`, each ~20 lines,
  identical try/except-continue idiom) — only the number formula is shared, not the framework.
- **No consumer yet in this session** — will be used starting P20-S2 (archival-service).
- **Tests**: 6 new unit tests (`attempt=0` edge case, exponential growth before the cap,
  cap ceiling for a large `attempt`, determinism with a seeded `rng`, negative `attempt` raises
  `ValueError`, default parameters yield a plausible range).
- `uv lock` run (unchanged — `dms-retry` has no dependencies/consumers yet).
- No Dockerfile had to change (`COPY libs/ libs/` picks up new directories automatically).
- No live verification step needed — a pure, stateless library function without service integration.
- Docs: new [ADR 0077](docs/adr/0077-dms-retry-backoff-jitter-lib.md), `libs/README.md`,
  `libs/dms-retry/README.md` extended.

**P20-S2 — archival-service: retry/backoff, `failed_permanent`, manual restart** (see
[ADR 0078](docs/adr/0078-archival-service-retry-backoff-failed-permanent.md)):

- **Previously NO retry mechanism whatsoever**: a technical failure immediately set `status="failed"`
  (terminal) — the transfer permanently disappeared from the active set, without automatic retry
  and without a manual control for a restart.
- New fields `attempts`/`next_retry_at` on `ArchivalTransfer` AND `CaseArchivalTransfer`. `mark_failed`
  now behaves retry-aware: below `Settings.max_archival_attempts` (default 5, same
  value as `storage-service.max_replication_attempts`), `status` stays in its current phase (e.g.
  `locked`/`copied`) — only `attempts`/`error_message`/`next_retry_at` (via `compute_backoff_seconds` from
  `libs/dms-retry`, P20-S1) change. Only upon exhaustion does `status` switch to the new
  terminal status `failed_permanent`.
- `list_active_transfers`/`list_active_case_transfers` additionally filter on `next_retry_at IS NULL OR
  next_retry_at <= now()` — a transfer with an open backoff window is skipped instead of immediately
  failing again on every tick.
- New endpoints `POST /archival-transfers/{id}/retry` and `POST /case-archival-transfers/{id}/retry`
  (gated with `archival.write`) — `409` except for `status="failed_permanent"`, otherwise reset to
  `pending`/`attempts=0`/`next_retry_at=null`/`error_message=null`. Restart resets to `pending` rather than
  reconstructing the interrupted phase (deliberately simpler — every phase fetches its inputs freshly
  anyway, a complete re-run is idempotent).
- **A single `mark_failed`/`reset_for_retry` function pair serves both transfer types** — the
  existing code already used the same `mark_failed` function for `ArchivalTransfer` AND
  `CaseArchivalTransfer` via duck typing (identical field names, no `isinstance` branch); this
  session retains this pattern instead of creating two copies (an initial draft with `mark_case_failed`/
  `reset_case_for_retry` was therefore discarded again).
- Ad-hoc `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` migration in the lifespan for both new columns on
  both tables (same pattern as `document-service`'s `dehydrated_at`, P19-S11) — `create_all` only
  creates new tables, but does not alter existing ones.
- **Tests**: archival-service 71 (previously 56, +15: repository level — backoff below/at exhaustion
  of `max_attempts`, `next_retry_at` filtering, `reset_for_retry`; pipeline level — transfer stays
  active below `max_attempts` on failure, reaches `failed_permanent` at `max_attempts=1`, same
  for `case_pipeline`; API level — both new `retry` endpoints incl. `404`/`409`/`403`).
- **Fully verified live against the actual running stack** (image rebuild + restart, clean
  migration confirmed via `\d archival.archival_transfer`): `404` for an unknown transfer,
  `failed_permanent → retry → pending` round trip for BOTH transfer types — since the default poll
  interval (one hour) makes a natural due-date trigger impractical in a live
  session, the test rows were set directly in the DB to `failed_permanent` instead of letting a real
  verification fail live; a repeated retry attempt on an already-`pending` transfer
  correctly returns `409`.
- **Pre-existing, independent test-fixture race discovered here, NOT fixed**: `test_api.py`'s
  `client` fixture replaces `app.state.document_client` only AFTER the poll task starts (lifespan timing)
  — the very first tick can therefore briefly hit the real `DocumentClient` instance. Became visible
  because this session created real, immediately-due test documents on the running stack via
  `POST /documents/{id}/archive-request` (for the retry live verification) — after their
  cleanup (`PUT .../archived`), the suite ran cleanly again. Outside the session scope.
- Docs: new [ADR 0078](docs/adr/0078-archival-service-retry-backoff-failed-permanent.md),
  `docs/services/archival-service.md` (state-machine tables, API table, "Open Points" marked as
  fixed) extended.

**P20-S3 — notification-service: retry/backoff, `failed_permanent`, asynchronous retry poll loop**
(see [ADR 0079](docs/adr/0079-notification-service-retry-backoff-failed-permanent.md)):

- **Previously NO retry mechanism whatsoever**: a failed `email`/`webhook` delivery attempt
  immediately set the terminal `status="failed"`.
- Unlike `archival-service` (a multi-phase process), delivery here is a single,
  synchronous step in the NATS handler resp. in `POST /notifications` — waiting for a backoff THERE
  would either block the NATS consumer or keep the HTTP request open for an unreasonably long time. New,
  public `attempt_delivery` function encapsulates the actual attempt (called by the first attempt
  AND later again), a completely new, standalone `_notification_retry_poll_loop` (main.py,
  interval `notification_retry_poll_interval_seconds`, default 60s — significantly shorter than
  archival-service's hourly interval, since email/webhook delivery is typically sensibly
  retried within seconds to minutes) handles ONLY the RETRY asynchronously, without touching
  the first-attempt path.
- Below `max_notification_attempts` (default 5), `status="failed"` (retry-capable) remains with
  increasing `attempts` and a `next_retry_at` set via `compute_backoff_seconds`; only upon
  exhaustion does `status` switch to the new terminal status `failed_permanent`. `in_app` has no
  real delivery step and is therefore never retry-capable.
- New `POST /notifications/{id}/retry` (`409` except for `failed_permanent`) — **unlike
  archival-service's phase-based reset to `pending`**, this endpoint delivers IMMEDIATELY and
  synchronously again: a notification is a single, lightweight delivery step, not a multi-phase
  process, an admin expects an immediate result rather than waiting for the next poll tick.
- **Deliberately NO RBAC gate**: `notification-service` currently has no
  `permission-service` integration at all — introducing complete RBAC just for this one endpoint
  would be scope creep far beyond this session (Phase 19 was explicitly the RBAC phase).
- Tick logic extracted into `_run_retry_tick` (isolated testability, same pattern as
  archival-service's `run_active_transfers_tick`). New `session_factory` fixture added in
  `conftest.py` (previously missing for this service).
- **Tests**: notification-service 40 (previously 30, +10: backoff behavior below/at exhaustion,
  `list_due_for_retry` filtering by status AND backoff window, `retry_now`, new `/retry` endpoint
  incl. `404`/`409`/successful restart, new `test_main.py` for `_run_retry_tick`).
- **Fully verified live against the actual running stack** (image rebuild + restart, migration
  confirmed via `\d notification.notification`): a webhook to an unreachable URL returns
  `status="failed"` with `next_retry_at` set after the first failure; `POST .../retry` returns `409` for
  this still-retry-capable case; after the real 60-second poll interval elapsed, the
  retry poll loop had automatically made a second attempt (`attempts` increased to 2, without
  manual intervention).
- Docs: new [ADR 0079](docs/adr/0079-notification-service-retry-backoff-failed-permanent.md),
  `docs/services/notification-service.md` (data model, channels section, API table, "Open Points"
  marked as fixed, test overview) extended.

**P20-S4 — rendering-service & ocr-service: retry/backoff, `failed_permanent`** (see
[ADR 0080](docs/adr/0080-rendering-ocr-service-retry-backoff-failed-permanent.md)):

- **Previously NO retry mechanism whatsoever** in both services — a technical failure immediately set
  the terminal `status="failed"`.
- Both services structurally closer to `notification-service` (P20-S3) than `archival-service` (P20-S2):
  processing synchronous inline in the NATS handler, no multi-phase process. New, standalone
  retry poll loops (`_ocr_retry_poll_loop`/`_rendition_retry_poll_loop`, each with a 60s interval) handle
  only the retry, the first attempt stays synchronous.
- **`rendering-service` peculiarity**: a version can have MULTIPLE rendition rows (one per
  applicable rule) — a retry must therefore not re-run the entire `process_version` rule cascade
  (would needlessly regenerate already-successful renditions). New
  `renderers.get_renderer_by_type`/`pipeline.retry_rendition` targets and re-invokes ONLY the one affected
  renderer. `ocr-service` does not need this (only one result per version).
- `ocr-service`'s `"skipped"` status (word-count ceiling/content-type allowlist) deliberately remains NOT
  retry-capable — an administrative decision, not a technical error.
- New `POST /ocr-results/{id}/retry` and `POST /renditions/{id}/retry` — use the `ocr.write`/
  `rendering.write` RBAC already in place since P19-S8, no new infrastructure needed.

**P20-S5 — federation-hub-service: `Handover` delivery retry/backoff, `pending_retry`** (see
[ADR 0081](docs/adr/0081-federation-hub-handover-retry-backoff-pending-retry.md)):

- **No retry mechanism whatsoever so far** — every failure immediately set the terminal
  `status="delivery_failed"`, no retry.
- Same `notification-service` pattern as P20-S3/S4 (synchronous initial attempt in `POST /handovers`,
  new standalone `_handover_retry_poll_loop` with a 60s interval handles only the retry). New
  intermediate status **`pending_retry`**; `delivery_failed` remains (a deliberate, explicit plan
  requirement, unlike P20-S2..S4's `..._permanent` convention) the name of the TERMINAL state, only now
  reached only after `max_handover_delivery_attempts` (default 5) is exhausted instead of immediately.
- **Core architectural conflict of this session**: `Handover` deliberately NEVER stores the end-to-end
  encrypted payload itself (ADR 0028 "self-loopback"/privacy principle) — but a retry tick needs it in
  order to redeliver. Solved via a purely VOLATILE in-process memory cache
  (`app.state.pending_handover_payloads`, keyed by `handover_id`) — deliberately NO new field on
  `Handover` itself, the never-persist principle remains fully valid. Consequence (documented, not
  concealed): restarting the hub during an open retry window irrecoverably loses the cache entry — the
  affected handover then ends up at `delivery_failed`, and the sending installation must submit a new
  handover with a new `handover_id`.
- New `POST /handovers/{id}/retry` endpoint — `409` for wrong status OR missing cache entry, otherwise
  `repository.reset_for_retry` followed by an immediate synchronous retry attempt. Deliberately WITHOUT
  an RBAC gate (the hub, like `notification-service` before P20-S3, has no `permission-service`
  integration — introducing one would be scope creep). Affects ONLY the initial delivery
  (`POST /handovers`), NOT the separate `POST .../result` return path (explicitly out of session scope
  per the plan).
- **Real design bug found in its own design and fixed BEFORE live verification**: the original version
  removed the cache entry on EVERY transition to `delivery_failed` (exhaustion), not only on success —
  exactly the moment at which `POST .../retry` would have first been allowed to use it (whose `409` gate
  requires precisely `status == "delivery_failed"`). The manual restart path would in practice almost
  never have worked, without any test uncovering it (the original tests injected the cache entry manually
  instead of checking the real behavior). Fixed: the entry is now removed exclusively upon actually
  SUCCESSFUL delivery (uniformly in `create_handover`, `retry_handover`, and `_run_retry_tick`). Similar
  category of finding as the `reset_for_retry` bug in ADR 0080, here however noticed during its own design
  rather than only during live verification.
- **Tests**: 43 (previously 35, +8) — including one dedicated regression test for the cache bug described
  above via the real poll-loop path.
- **Fully verified live against the actual running stack** (image rebuild + restart,
  **twice** due to the bugfix): `POST /handovers` against a deliberately unreachable
  `callback_base_url` returns `pending_retry` with `attempts=1`; the real, running poll loop was followed
  over **actual ~5 minutes** (no accelerated setting) until actual exhaustion —
  `attempts` rises 1→2→3→4→5, transition to `delivery_failed` exactly upon reaching
  `max_handover_delivery_attempts`, cache entry demonstrably still present; `POST .../retry`
  against the still-unreachable target confirms live the `reset_for_retry` path (`attempts` 0→1, NOT
  6); the restart boundary was verified with an ACTUAL `docker compose restart federation-hub-service`
  (no simulated flush) — two handovers still open at that point were, per the log, marked with
  `federation_handover_retry_payload_lost` and correctly ended up at `delivery_failed`; a
  subsequent `POST .../retry` returned `409` with the documented message.
- Docs: new [ADR 0081](docs/adr/0081-federation-hub-handover-retry-backoff-pending-retry.md),
  `docs/services/federation-hub-service.md` (new "Retry & Backoff" section, API table, data model,
  "Open Points" marked as resolved, test overview) updated.

**P20-S6 — storage-service: full-jitter backoff for the replication retry queue** (see
[ADR 0082](docs/adr/0082-storage-service-replication-jitter-retrofit.md)):
