- **The only one of the five resilience services in this phase already having the COMPLETE base
  pattern**: `ObjectCopy.attempts` + `max_replication_attempts` (default 5) + terminal status
  `failed_permanent` had already existed since [ADR 0004](docs/adr/0004-storage-redundancy-scope.md) —
  only full-jitter backoff itself was missing. `POST /replication/process-pending` previously picked up
  every `status IN ("pending","failed")` row again immediately on EVERY call, with no waiting period at
  all.
- New field `next_retry_at` on `ObjectCopy` (no new status needed). `repository.list_pending_copies`
  now additionally filters on `next_retry_at IS NULL OR next_retry_at <= now()`. `repository.record_copy`
  gets a new `next_retry_at` parameter that — unlike `retention_until`'s "only take over on an explicit
  value" — is set UNCONDITIONALLY, like `last_error`: a successful/fresh attempt needs no remaining
  backoff time.
- `replication.process_pending` computes the new `next_retry_at` on a `"failed"` result via a new
  `_next_retry_at(attempts)` helper function (`libs/dms-retry`, same formula as at the four other
  resilience points of this phase); on `"failed_permanent"` explicitly `None`.
- **No CronJob in this session** — the actual periodic execution
  (`/replication/process-pending`, `/object-verify/{key}/all`) remains, as planned, reserved for
  **P26-S4** (Helm chart CronJob template), since Phase 26 does not yet exist. ADR 0004's decision
  "explicit endpoint instead of in-process background task" remains unchanged and valid.
- **Existing test had to be adapted**: `test_process_pending_marks_permanently_failed_after_max_attempts`
  called `process_pending` twice in a tight loop and expected immediate reacquisition —
  no longer deterministically guaranteed with real jitter. The test now explicitly resets `next_retry_at`
  back into the past between calls (same pattern as the tick tests of the other services
  in this phase).
- **Tests**: 113 (previously 109, +4) — `next_retry_at` is set after a failure and prevents immediate
  reacquisition, after fast-forwarding the timestamp a run picks it up again, `list_pending_copies`
  filters out a row that is not yet due.
- **Fully verified live against the actual running stack** (image rebuild + restart, migration
  confirmed): a real object uploaded, a second `object_copy` row created via a direct SQL insert with a
  `backend_id` not configured in the container (produces a genuine `KeyError` on the backend dict access,
  no mocking) — the first `POST /replication/process-pending` call returns
  `attempts=1` and a set `next_retry_at`; an IMMEDIATELY following second call returns
  `processed=0` (not yet due); after manually backdating `next_retry_at`, a third
  call picks up the row again (`attempts=2`) — confirms the full jitter cycle 1:1 against the actual
  container.
- Docs: new [ADR 0082](docs/adr/0082-storage-service-replication-jitter-retrofit.md),
  `docs/services/storage-service.md` (data model, replication section, "Open Points" refined,
  test overview) updated.

**P20-S7 — Admin UI: "permanently failed" visibility + manual restart** (last session of the
resilience phase, see [ADR 0083](docs/adr/0083-admin-ui-processing-failures-visibility.md)):

- **`ArchivalTransfersView`** (already existing) gets `failed_permanent` as a new filter option in
  both sections (document and circulation folder disposal) as well as a "Retry" button that
  only appears at this status (`POST .../retry`, already present server-side since ADR 0078, never before
  connected to the frontend). `ArchivalTransfer`/`CaseArchivalTransfer` frontend types get the
  previously missing fields `attempts`/`next_retry_at`. `statusLabel`/`caseStatusLabel` were
  switched to PascalCase per word part (`"failed_permanent"` → `statusFailedPermanent` key instead of an
  unlocatable `statusFailed_permanent`).
- **New, shared page `/processing-failures/`** (`ProcessingFailuresView`, three independent
  sections: notifications/renditions/OCR results) instead of three separate pages — each
  section loads exclusively `status=failed_permanent` records and offers a restart button per
  row. Deliberately THREE independent sections without a shared generic hook (same
  "lightweight duplication instead of abstraction" principle as with the poll loops of the associated
  backend services).
- **`rendering-service`/`ocr-service`: `GET /renditions`/`GET /ocr-results` get `document_id`
  optional** (previously a required parameter) plus a new `status` query parameter — without this a
  cross-document "all failed renditions/OCR results" view would not have been technically
  possible. Both remain gated via the already existing `rendering.read`/`ocr.read` permissions
  (`permission-service`, ADR 0073) — no new ungated attack surface. `notification-service`'s
  `GET /notifications` already had the necessary `status` filter, no backend change needed.
- **Deliberately WITHOUT `federation-hub-service`**: the plan's P20-S7 line names only archival/
  notification/rendition/OCR failures, not handover — Admin UI visibility for that would be a sensible
  independent follow-up session.
- **Why no new badge color**: `admin-ui`'s CSS only knows `ok`/`down` (maintained consistently across
  three theme variants) — the distinction from `failed` happens via the translated status text
  itself ("Failed" vs. "Permanently failed") and the button that is only visible at
  `failed_permanent`, a third color variant just for this session would have been scope creep.
- **Tests**: `rendering-service` 46 (previously 44, +2), `ocr-service` 53 (previously 51, +2, still 8
  `tesseract`-gated skips), `admin-ui` 173 (previously 164, +9: 3 new in `archival-transfers.test.tsx`,
  new `processing-failures.test.tsx` with 6 tests). Existing callers of `list_renditions`/
  `list_ocr_results` remain unchanged and compatible (already pass `document_id` explicitly as a
  keyword argument) — a purely additive change.
- **Fully verified live against the actual running stack** (image rebuild + restart of
  `rendering-service`, `ocr-service`, `admin-ui`): `GET /renditions?status=failed_permanent` and `GET
  /ocr-results?status=failed_permanent` returned, without `document_id`, real `failed_permanent` records
  spanning several documents originating from earlier live verifications
  of this phase (no mocking, real legacy data from the dev stack); `GET /notifications?status=failed_permanent`
  correctly returned an empty list; the new `/processing-failures/` route is served by the
  `admin-ui` container (`200`). **No interactive browser click-through** — this project consistently
  verifies frontend work via `tsc`/`eslint`/`vitest`/`next build` plus real backend live checks
  (see `docs/services/admin-ui.md` "No browser available in this development environment"), no
  Playwright/browser automation tool exists anywhere in the monorepo.
- Docs: new [ADR 0083](docs/adr/0083-admin-ui-processing-failures-visibility.md),
  `docs/services/admin-ui.md` (new page/section, backend integration table, test overview),
  `docs/services/rendering-service.md`/`ocr-service.md` (API table, test overview),
  `docs/services/notification-service.md` ("Open Points" marked fully resolved) updated.

**This concludes Phase 20 (Resilience: retry, backoff, permanently failed, P20-S1 through P20-S7)
completely** — all five affected services (archival-/notification-/rendering-/ocr-/
federation-hub-service) now have full-jitter backoff, a clear terminal error state, and a
manual restart path; `storage-service` already largely had the pattern and only had jitter
retrofitted; four of the five services (all except federation-hub-service) are now also visible/
operable in the Admin UI. The only thing still open is P20-S6's actual CronJob carrier, which is explicitly
deferred to **P26-S4** (Helm chart), since Phase 26 does not yet exist.

**P21-S1 — key rotation for fleet-management-service/license-service** (first session of the new
Phase 21 "security hardening", see [ADR 0084](docs/adr/0084-fleet-license-key-rotation.md)):

- **Reference examined more closely than the plan describes**: `workflow-service`'s `POST
  /federation/rotate-key` (ADR 0039), contrary to the plan's wording ("the old one remains valid briefly
  for a transition"), has NO time-based transition window —
  `federation-hub-service.repository.rotate_installation_key` replaces the stored public
  key immediately and atomically as soon as the rotation request (signed with the still-current key) has
  been verified. "The old one remains valid briefly" means there: the old key remains the only
  valid one until the rotation call goes through, no period with two simultaneously valid keys.
- **`fleet-management-service`**: new `POST /installations/{id}/rotate-key` — replaces
  `ManagedInstallation.fleet_agent_api_key` immediately (optional operator-provided value, otherwise
  generated), same response schema as the initial creation. **No transition window possible** (unlike
  the reference): this service only PRESENTS the key, the target installation verifies it against
  its own value, statically configured via the `DMS_FLEET_AGENT_API_KEY` env var — there is no
  channel through which this service could change the value there at runtime. Rotation therefore remains a
  two-step, partly manual process (deliberately documented, not pretended to be fully automatic).
- **`license-service`**: contrary to the plan's assumption, possesses NO self-generated signing key
  at all — the private key, per the explicit ADR 0032 requirement, belongs to the licensor and is NEVER
  present in this repository/deployment; `license-service` only holds the public verification key.
  Solution: new optional setting `license_previous_public_key_pem` (default `None`).
  `license_verifier.decode()` first tries `public_key_pem`, and on failure falls back to the
  optional previous key — **here there actually is a real transition window**
  (unlike the other two rotation patterns in this project): licenses already installed and signed
  under the old key remain valid on every subsequent status check, while newly
  issued licenses can already use the new key. The operator sets
  `license_previous_public_key_pem` back to `None` once the transition period has ended.
- **Tests**: `fleet-management-service` 30 (previously 26, +4: default generation, operator-provided
  value, actual use of the new value in an outgoing call, `404` for an unknown
  installation). `license-service` 37 (previously 32, +5, all in `test_license_verifier.py`: fallback
  during the transition period, preference for the current key when no fallback is
  needed, failure when neither the current nor the previous key matches, unchanged behavior without a
  configured previous key).
- **Fully verified live against the actual running stack** (image rebuild + restart of both
  services): `fleet-management-service` — a real installation registered, `POST .../rotate-key` with
  both an automatically generated AND an operator-provided value confirmed (each with an actually changed
  return value), `404` for an unknown installation confirmed. `license-service` — via a temporary
  compose override file (`DMS_LICENSE_PUBLIC_KEY_PEM` set to a freshly generated, independent
  key, `DMS_LICENSE_PREVIOUS_PUBLIC_KEY_PEM` set to the previous default key) reproduced for
  real: a real license already installed before this session remained valid via `GET
  /license/status` (fallback path actually exercised); as a counter-check, without a configured
  previous key, the same license was correctly reported as `valid=false` — confirming that the
  fallback performs real verification work. Reset to the original configuration
  after the test.
- Docs: new [ADR 0084](docs/adr/0084-fleet-license-key-rotation.md),
  `docs/services/fleet-management-service.md`/`license-service.md` ("Open Points" marked as (partially)
  resolved, new endpoint/setting documentation, test overview) updated.

**P21-S2 — federation-hub-service: certificate layer instead of real transport mTLS** (see
[ADR 0085](docs/adr/0085-federation-hub-certificate-layer-not-transport-mtls.md)):

- **Plan premise examined more closely**: the plan describes this session as "mTLS/real
  installation identity" — [ADR 0039](docs/adr/0039-federation-trust-hardening-request-signing-over-mtls.md)
  had already explicitly examined and rejected real transport mTLS for exactly this hub once before
  ("no place for a real PKI in this project" — no service in this repo terminates TLS itself).
  Re-examining the entire repo confirms: nothing has changed in that regard, no
  TLS termination/reverse proxy exists anywhere in the stack. The plan wording was therefore taken
  at the word "certificate layer added," not at "mTLS" — a real certificate layer, but
  still entirely at the application level, like the already existing signature verification.
- `HubIdentity`'s existing RSA-2048 key pair is additionally packaged as a self-signed small
  root CA (`ca_certificate_pem`, same `cryptography` library/convention as
  `signature-service`'s `InternalCa`, ADR 0025) — NO separate key pair.
- Each installation receives, upon registration AND upon every key rotation, an X.509 certificate signed
  by the hub (`Installation.certificate_pem`/`certificate_not_after`, valid for 1 year) that
  binds its public key — a simplified CSR equivalent, the hub does not generate a new
  key pair but certifies the public key already proven via signature.
- `authenticate_signed_request` additionally checks, in addition to the existing signature, the full
  certificate chain up to the hub CA, the validity window, AND that the `CommonName`/embedded
  key actually belong to the calling installation — deliberately IN ADDITION, not as a replacement.
- **Real design bug found and fixed while writing its own tests, BEFORE any live verification**:
  a first version checked only chain+validity, without verifying that the certificate actually
  belongs to the calling installation — while writing a test ("a certificate issued for another
  installation is rejected"), it emerged that this would have allowed any certificate validly
  issued by the hub (e.g. that of another, independent installation) to be substituted unnoticed.
  Signature verification with the actual private key remains the actual proof of
  possession and would have prevented a complete auth bypass, but the certificate layer itself would not
  have kept its actual promise. Fixed via an additional `CommonName`/key binding check in
  `verify_installation_certificate`.
- New `GET /ca-certificate` endpoint (counterpart to `GET /public-key`) — certificate pinning equivalent.
- **Backfill migration**: all installations registered before this session automatically receive a
  certificate on the hub's next startup; `authenticate_signed_request` skips the
  certificate check only for the brief period during which a row does not yet have one (grandfathering).
- **Tests**: 55 (previously 43, +12) — hub CA self-signed/stable, registration correctly issues a
  properly bound certificate, self-signed (not hub-issued) certificate rejected,
  expired certificate rejected, rotation issues a new bound certificate,
  `list_installations_without_certificate` filters correctly, grandfathering without a certificate, a
  certificate issued for ANOTHER installation is rejected due to missing identity binding
  (the regression test described above), `GET /ca-certificate` returns a valid certificate,
  registration/rotation response contains a verifiable certificate via the API.
- **Fully verified live against the actual running stack** (image rebuild + restart): the
  backfill migration ran against **219 actually pre-existing installations** from earlier
  live verifications of this and previous sessions (startup duration 6.2s instead of the usual ~150ms,
  afterward all 219 rows confirmed to have `certificate_pem`); `GET /ca-certificate` returns a real,
  self-signed certificate; a freshly registered installation received a certificate that
  demonstrably chains up to the hub CA, carries the correct `CommonName`, and binds the
  submitted public key; a subsequent key rotation issued a new certificate bound to the new
  key, still chaining to the same hub CA.
- Docs: new [ADR 0085](docs/adr/0085-federation-hub-certificate-layer-not-transport-mtls.md),
  `docs/services/federation-hub-service.md` (new "certificate layer" section, API table, data model,
  trust model, "Open Points" marked partially resolved, test overview) updated.

**P21-S3 — hardening the HTML preview sandbox** (see
[ADR 0086](docs/adr/0086-html-preview-external-subresource-blocking.md)):

- **Problem**: `user-ui`'s `PreviewPane` renders HTML documents via a `sandbox=""` iframe with
  `srcDoc` (no `src` pointing to its own origin). `sandbox=""` blocks script execution/forms/
  top-level navigation, but NOT normal loading of subresources — an uploaded
  `<img src="https://tracker.example/pixel.gif">` triggers this request simply by opening the preview.
  A CSP header would be the obvious solution, but has only limited effect on `srcDoc` content without its
  own origin (no fixed origin a header could reliably bind to).
- **Solution**: server-side rewriting at the source instead of at the rendering location.
  `document-service`'s `GET /documents/{id}/content`/`.../versions/{n}/content` check the
  (magic-byte-sniffed, not client-trusted) `content_type` — for `"text/html"`, the content passes
  through a new `html_preview_guard.rewrite_external_references` before delivery.
- **New dependency `beautifulsoup4`** — the first HTML parsing tool in this repo (`lxml` already
  existed, but only for XML/BPMN in other services, no precedent for HTML). Rationale: pure
  regex rewriting on nested, potentially malformed HTML is error-prone; the stdlib
  `html.parser` has no built-in tree representation/serialization for correctly
  reassembling it.
- **Attribute-driven rather than tag-driven**: no hardcoded `img`/`script`/`iframe`/... set — every
  `src`/`href` reference is checked, regardless of tag name. Allows `data:`/`mailto:`/`tel:` URIs
  and pure fragment anchors (`#...`, no network access); also blocks RELATIVE paths, not just
  absolute external URLs — `srcDoc` content has no safe, resolvable base URL, an uploaded
  document has no legitimate target directory with sibling files.
- Immediately after each removed reference, `[Blocked external request: <URL>]` is inserted as a visible
  `<span>` — a literal plan requirement, no silent removal. An existing
  `<meta charset>` is normalized to `utf-8` (the function always returns UTF-8 bytes).
- **Deliberately only `src`/`href`** (explicit plan requirement) — `srcset`, `poster`, `background`, as
  well as CSS `url(...)` in `style` attributes/`<style>` blocks remain open, documented as a deliberate,
  not yet closed gap rather than silently feigned completeness.
- **Not affected**: `GET /public/share-links/content` (public share-link download) — a plain
  `<a href download>` browser download in `user-ui`, no `srcDoc` iframe, hence no inline rendering
  risk and deliberately out of session scope.
- **Tests**: `document-service` 247 (previously 234, +13) — new test file `test_html_preview_guard.py`
  (11 pure function tests: external HTTPS/protocol-relative/relative references blocked, `data:`/
  `mailto:`/`tel:`/fragment anchors allowed, `javascript:` URIs blocked, empty `src` blocked,
  `<meta charset>` normalization, content unchanged when no references present), plus 2 new `test_api.py`
  tests (end-to-end across both download endpoints incl. marker text, byte identity for non-HTML
  content with randomly HTML-like text as a regression guard).
- **Fully verified live against the actual running stack** (image rebuild + restart): a
  real HTML document with an external tracker image, an embedded `data:` image, an external
  link, and a local fragment anchor uploaded via the actual gateway — the downloaded
  content showed the external image reference and the external link correctly removed with a visible
  `[Blocked external request: ...]` marker, while the `data:` image and fragment anchor remained
  unchanged and `<meta charset>` was normalized to `utf-8`.
- Docs: new [ADR 0086](docs/adr/0086-html-preview-external-subresource-blocking.md),
  `docs/services/document-service.md` (new "HTML preview hardening" section, API table),
  `docs/services/user-ui.md` ("Open Points" marked resolved) updated.

**P21-S4 — BPMN import review gate** (last session of Phase 21, see
[ADR 0087](docs/adr/0087-bpmn-import-review-gate.md)):

- **Problem**: `POST /process-definitions` (`workflow-service`) previously created a new,
  instance-startable process definition immediately and ungated (aside from the already existing
  `admin.object_config` role check, P6-S6) — an uploaded BPMN document can contain script tasks,
  so a single admin could activate it unobserved.
- **Solution**: exact replication of the already existing, generic four-eyes recipe from
  `config-service`'s P17-S3 retrofit (ADR 0060, permission-service's generic approval mechanism,
  ADR 0022). New action type `workflow.process_definition.import`. New, plain
  `workflow_service.approval_client.ApprovalClient` (identical pattern to
  `config_service`/`document_service`). `POST /process-definitions` first checks whether the
  action type requires approval; if so, the BPMN text+name+optional `process_id` is packaged as `payload`
  into an approval request, returning `202` with `{status: "pending_approval", approval_request_id}` —
  no creation, no BPMN validation at this point (deliberately deferred, exactly as with
  `config_service._apply_config_document`). New, plain consumer (`consumer.py`,
  `ensure_stream=False`, durable name `workflow-service-approval`) reacts to the already existing
  `permission.approval.approved` event, applying the deferred import via the same
  `repository.create_process_definition` used by the immediate path.
- **Deliberate design deviation from `config-service`'s pattern**: `config-service`'s `ImportActionResult`
  wraps EVERY response (`applied`/`pending_approval`) uniformly in a status envelope. For
  `POST /process-definitions` this would have been risky — the endpoint is deeply embedded test
  infrastructure: over 40 call sites in `test_api.py`/`test_federation.py` first upload a
  process definition BEFORE the actual subject under test begins, and `process-designer`'s
  existing success path expects the flat form. Before the final implementation, verified with a
  full test run that a uniform envelope would indeed have touched all these places —
  instead, the success case remains **byte-identical** (`201` + `ProcessDefinitionOut`), only the new
  `pending_approval` case gets `202` + its own form (`ProcessDefinitionImportResult`, via
  `JSONResponse` instead of `response_model`), distinguishable already by HTTP status.
- **`scripts/run-tests.sh`**: `workflow-service` added to the `CONSUMER_SERVICES` list — the service
  previously had no consumer of its own at all (only a producer for its `"workflow"` stream),
  and needs the same container-stop protection as `document-service`/etc. since this session.
- **Real regression from an earlier session found and fixed**: the full
  `workflow-service` test suite (run in full for the first time since P20-S5) revealed that
  `test_dispatch_records_delivery_failed_for_unreachable_target` (`test_federation.py`) still had the status
  expectation valid BEFORE ADR 0081 (`"delivery_failed"` immediately) — since ADR 0081 the hub
  marks a single failure as retry-capable `"pending_retry"`, and `workflow-service`'s
  `federation_task.status` took over `handover["status"]` unchanged and was technically already correct;
  only the test had silently gone stale since P20-S5, unnoticed because the tests had never run in
  full since then. Fixed by adjusting the test expectation.
- **`process-designer`**: `lib/api.ts` gets `ProcessDefinitionImportPending` +
  an `isPendingApproval()` type guard; `createProcessDefinition()`'s return type becomes a union.
  `designer/page.tsx`'s `handleSave()` shows, on `pending_approval`, the new hint text
  `designer.savePendingApproval` instead of navigating to the (not yet existing) definition.
- **Tests**: `workflow-service` 177 (previously 170, +7: new `test_consumer.py` with 5 tests, 1 new
  API integration test against the actual running `permission-service`, 1 regression fix as
  above). `process-designer`: existing 39 tests unchanged and green, no dedicated new test for the
  `pending_approval` branch of `designer/page.tsx`'s save function (this save flow already had no
  test coverage before; `tsc`/`eslint`/`next build` confirm type correctness, no real
  browser available in this development environment).
- **Fully verified live against the actual running stack** (image rebuild + restart of
  `workflow-service`): a test principal was live-granted the role `domain-admin-config`, approval
  requirement for `workflow.process_definition.import` activated, a real BPMN uploaded — returned `202` +
  `pending_approval`, the process family demonstrably did not yet exist afterward
  (`GET /process-definitions?name=...` empty); after a real `POST .../approve` against the running
  `permission-service`, the definition was actually created by the new consumer within a few seconds
  (`bpmn_process_id` correctly extracted from the BPMN) — confirming the complete
  request→approval→consume cycle against real, independently running containers.
- Docs: new [ADR 0087](docs/adr/0087-bpmn-import-review-gate.md), `docs/services/workflow-service.md`
  (API table, new "BPMN import review gate" section, tests section, "Open Points" marked
  resolved), `docs/services/process-designer.md` (new save behavior, tests section)
  updated.
- **Phase 21 (security hardening) thereby fully completed.**

**P22-S1 — "dispose now" control** (first session of the new **Phase 22
(Admin UI expansion)**, no new ADR — pure frontend wiring to an already
existing, ungated backend endpoint):

- **Problem**: `document-service` has already had, since P7-S3 (5.6), a manual
  disposal trigger, `POST /documents/{id}/archive-request` (sets `archive_after`
  to now, if not already due) — but `ArchivalTransfersView` in the Admin UI
  was previously purely observational/retrieval, without any wiring to this endpoint (see
  `docs/services/admin-ui.md` "Open Points", now resolved).
- **Solution**: new form at the top of the document section of `ArchivalTransfersView.tsx`
  — a free-text field for the document ID plus a "Dispose Now" button. New
  `requestDocumentArchive(token, documentId)` API client (`lib/api.ts`) calls the
  existing endpoint. Deliberately **no** immediate reload of the transfer table
  after success — the call only sets `archive_after`, it does not itself yet create an
  `ArchivalTransfer` row; that is only done by `archival-service`'s next
  poll tick (`archival_poll_interval_seconds`, default 3600s/hourly). Instead
  a hint text explaining this. Error display for an unknown document ID (real
  `404` from `document-service` → `ApiError` message "Not Found" in the UI).
- **Deliberately no document search/browser added to the Admin UI** — the Admin UI
  fundamentally has no document list (that is user-ui's domain), so the
  input remains a free ID instead of a selection; a future extension could instead
  add a button directly on the document in the user-ui (remains an open point).
- **Tests**: `admin-ui` 175 (previously 173, +2 in `archival-transfers.test.tsx`:
  success case incl. hint text display and correct `requestDocumentArchive` call,
  error display for a backend rejection simulated via the mocked `ApiError` class).
- **Fully verified live against the actual running stack** (image rebuild +
  restart of `admin-ui`): a real test document uploaded via the gateway
  (`POST /api/document-service/documents`), `POST .../archive-request` demonstrably set
  `archive_after` to the current time (previously `null`), a
  call with an unknown document ID returned `404` — exactly the contract the new
  `requestDocumentArchive()` client relies on. Test document subsequently
  deleted again. No interactive browser test (no browser/Playwright available in this
  development environment, an established project-wide practice) — the new
  form behavior is instead covered by the Vitest component tests plus the above
  backend API verification via the exact same gateway call.
- Docs: `docs/services/admin-ui.md` (new form description in "disposal &
  long-term archival", API table, tests section, "Open Points" marked
  resolved) updated.

**P22-S2 — group management** (see [ADR 0088](docs/adr/0088-admin-defined-groups.md)):

- **Problem**: `RoleAssignment.principal_type="group"` had, since Phase 19 Session 2 (ADR 0067), been
  effective for exactly one reserved value (`"everyone"`, implicit membership of every authenticated
  principal, no row of its own) — any other group value remained pure schema decoration, never
  evaluated. An admin could therefore not create their own, limited group (e.g. "reviewer team") and
  assign it a role in one step, without having to account for each member individually.
- **Solution**: two new tables, `group` (`id` UUID str, `name` unique, `description`, `created_at`) and
  `group_membership` (`group_id` FK, `principal_id`, unique on the combination). `_collect_effective_
  roles` resolves, once before traversing the resource ancestor chain, all groups the requesting
  principal is a member of (`_group_ids_for_principal`) and, at each node, treats — in addition to
  `principal_id == principal_id` and the "everyone" condition — every assignment with `principal_type="group",
  principal_id IN (member groups)` as matching too.
- **New endpoints**: `POST`/`GET`/`DELETE /groups`, `GET`/`POST /groups/{id}/members`,
  `DELETE /groups/{id}/members/{principal_id}` — `POST`/`DELETE` gated by the same
  `_require_role_management` (`admin.user_management`, ADR 0071) as `POST`/`PUT /roles`, `GET` endpoints
  deliberately still ungated (same rationale as `GET /roles`). Adding a member is idempotent
  (a second call with the same `principal_id` returns the existing membership instead of a
  duplicate error, same low-friction style as `ensure_everyone_role`).
- **Deliberately no reference check when deleting a group**: `role_assignment.principal_id` is a
  free string, no FK to `group.id` — an orphaned assignment then simply matches no
  principal anymore, identical to a group that never had any members assigned. Consistent with `Role`,
  which likewise has no delete endpoint/no reference check.
- **`apps/admin-ui`**: new "Groups" section in `UserManagement.tsx` (`/users/`), between the
  existing role and role-assignment sections — create form, table with "show
  members"/"delete" per row, expanding lazily loads the member list (same expand/collapse
  pattern as `ProcessDefinitionList`'s version history), mini form for adding via free
  principal ID, "remove" button per member.
- **Tests**: `permission-service` 137 (previously 128, +9: authentication/authorization check for
  `POST /groups`, create/list, `404` for an unknown group when deleting/adding member/
  removing member, idempotency when adding, deletion removes group along with memberships,
  plus the core test `test_group_membership_grants_role_to_every_member`: a single role assignment to a
  group with two members grants the permission to both, a non-member remains unaffected,
  removing a member immediately revokes the permission). `admin-ui` 179 (previously 175, +4: empty state
  with no groups, creation incl. reload, listing + deletion, expanding incl. loading member list/adding
  member/removing member).
- **Fully verified live against the actual running stack** (image rebuild + restart of
  `permission-service`/`admin-ui`): `POST /groups` without a principal → `401`; with `users-admin`
  (holds `admin.user_management`) → `201`, real group created; two test principals added as
  members; a new role assigned ONCE to the group ID (not to each principal individually) —
  `GET /check` confirmed the permission for BOTH members and its absence for a non-member;
  removing a member immediately revoked the permission (cache invalidation confirmed live), the
  remaining member kept it; a second removal attempt of the same membership returned
  `404`; deletion of the group confirmed via `GET /groups` as no longer present. No interactive
  browser test of the new Admin UI section (no browser/Playwright available in this development
  environment, an established project-wide practice) — instead covered by the Vitest component tests as
  well as the above backend API verification via the exact same gateway calls.
- Docs: new [ADR 0088](docs/adr/0088-admin-defined-groups.md), `docs/services/permission-service.md`
  (API table, data model, new section "admin-creatable groups", "Open Points" partially marked
  resolved), `docs/services/admin-ui.md` (page table, new section "group management",
  backend integration table, tests section) updated.

**P22-S3 — generic four-eyes settings page** (see
[ADR 0089](docs/adr/0089-approval-settings-ui-config-endpoint-stays-ungated.md)):

- **Problem**: the generic four-eyes mechanism (4.3, since P6-S4) had previously only been
  configurable via `curl`/direct HTTP calls to `permission-service` — no Admin UI integration.
- **Solution**: new page `/approval-settings/` (`ApprovalSettings.tsx`). Table of already
  configured action types (`GET /approval-config`, sorted alphabetically) with a toggle per row (`PUT
  /approval-config/{action_type}`), additionally showing `required_permission` (if set) and the
  last change timestamp. **`GET /approval-config` returns only rows with at least one
  previous `PUT` call** — not a fixed catalog of all action types existing in the system — hence a
  second form "configure new action type" (free-text `action_type` + `requires_approval` +
  optional `required_permission`), the only way to set a previously unconfigured action type for the
  first time.
- **Correctness detail when toggling**: `required_permission` is ALWAYS sent along with the
  most recently loaded value of the row on a toggle click, never omitted — `permission_service.repository.
  set_approval_config` would otherwise overwrite the field with `null`. Without this precaution, toggling
  `auth.superuser.activate` would have silently deleted its break-glass role binding
  (`breakglass.approve`) — considered before live verification, not found as a bug only afterward.
- **Examined, but deliberately not implemented: self-gating of `PUT /approval-config/{action_type}`**
  (analogous to `POST`/`PUT /roles`, ADR 0071, `admin.user_management`) — this endpoint was previously the
  only writing one in this topic area with no permission check at all, which came up while building a UI
  for it. Research across the full test corpus found at least eight services
  (`auth-service`, `config-service`, `folder-service`, `document-service`, `migration-service`,
  `permission-service` itself, `workflow-service`, `webdav-connector`) that call this endpoint directly as
  test infrastructure, without `X-DMS-Principal`/capability — self-gating would have touched over a
  dozen test call sites across the repo, a change of this magnitude does not belong
  in a session whose actual purpose is a new Admin UI page. Instead documented as an open
  security point (`docs/services/permission-service.md` "Open Points") — anyone with
  gateway access can toggle the four-eyes requirement for any action type system-wide, a risk that
  already existed before this session (only reachable via `curl`) and is not newly created by the new
  UI, only made more conveniently accessible.
- **Deliberately no client-side `RequireCapability` gate** on the new page/the sidebar entry —
  would feign a server-side enforcement that does not exist, same discipline as with
  `ArchivalTransfersView`'s ungated retrieval button.
- **Tests**: `admin-ui` 185 (previously 179, +6: new `approval-settings.test.tsx` — empty state,
  unreachable state, listing sorted incl. `required_permission`/status, toggling incl. preservation of
  `required_permission`, creating a new action type, error display on toggle failure).
- **Fully verified live against the actual running stack** (image rebuild + restart of
  `admin-ui`, no code change to `permission-service` needed): a new action type
  `p22s3.test.action` genuinely created via `PUT`, appeared afterward in `GET /approval-config`; toggling
  with `required_permission=null` explicitly sent along confirmed; a toggle of
  `auth.superuser.activate` (real break-glass configuration) with `required_permission="breakglass.approve"`
  explicitly sent along confirmed that its role binding is preserved in the process.
  No interactive browser test (no browser/Playwright available in this development environment,
  an established project-wide practice).
- Docs: new [ADR 0089](docs/adr/0089-approval-settings-ui-config-endpoint-stays-ungated.md),
  `docs/services/admin-ui.md` (page table, new section "four-eyes settings",
  backend integration table, tests section), `docs/services/permission-service.md` ("Open Points"
  updated) updated.

**P22-S4 — reject-reason field in reviewer-ui** (no new ADR — a purely mechanical UI swap with no
new architectural decision):

- **Problem**: `ApprovalList.tsx`'s "Reject" button called `window.prompt()` for the optional
  rejection rationale — a native browser popup instead of a form in the rest of this app's style,
  and testable with Testing Library only via a `window.prompt` mock instead of real
  form interaction.
- **Solution**: clicking "Reject" opens an inline form directly below the affected row
  (`rejectingId` state, same expand/collapse principle as the already existing detail payload view)
  with a free-text field for the rationale plus "Confirm Rejection"/"Cancel". Submitting still calls
  `rejectRequest()` with the same, already existing optional `reason` parameter
  (empty field → `undefined`, identical behavior to the previous "prompt cancelled/empty" case) — no
  backend code affected.
- **Tests**: `reviewer-ui` 20 (previously 18, +2 net): the previous, `window.prompt`-mocking test was
  replaced by three (open inline form + reject with rationale via real form interaction,
  reject without rationale, cancel closes the form without an API call) — the no longer needed
  `promptMock` was removed.
- **Verified live against the actual running stack** (image rebuild + restart of `reviewer-ui`):
  the `/` and `/approvals/` routes both return `200` via the freshly built container. No
  backend change in this session, hence no API verification needed. No interactive
  browser test (no browser/Playwright available in this development environment, an established
  project-wide practice) — the new form behavior is covered by the Vitest component tests (real
  `fireEvent` form interaction instead of a prompt mock).
- Docs: `docs/services/reviewer-ui.md` ("approvals inbox" section, tests section, "Open Points" marked
  resolved) updated.

**P22-S5 — teamspaces admin overview** (see
[ADR 0090](docs/adr/0090-teamspaces-admin-overview.md)):

- **Problem**: `GET /teamspaces` (`teamspace-service`) has always returned only teamspaces the
  caller belongs to (concept 2.5, deliberately self-managed) — no way to see
  installation-wide all existing teamspaces, regardless of own membership.
- **Solution**: new endpoint `GET /admin/teamspaces` (`repository.list_all_teamspaces_with_member_
  counts`, `outerjoin` + `GROUP BY` instead of a membership filter) returns ALL teamspaces incl.
  `member_count` per row — deliberately not a full member list (the existing `GET
  /teamspaces/{id}/members` requires `_require_member`, an admin without their own membership could
  not use it; a second, gated members route would be disproportionate for a pure
  overview).
- **New capability `admin.teamspace_management`**, new preseeded domain `domain-admin-teamspaces`
  at `permission-service` (`DOMAIN_ADMIN_ROLES`, identical pattern to every previous new
  admin domain, e.g. `domain-admin-license`) — deliberately NOT reusing `admin.user_management`:
  concept 4.6 requires domain-separated admin roles, "user/permission management" and
  "teamspace oversight" are functionally independent responsibilities. New `PermissionServiceClient.
  has_permission()` in `teamspace-service` (identical pattern to `document_service.permission_client`).
- **`apps/admin-ui`**: new page `/teamspaces/` (`TeamspacesAdmin.tsx`) — a pure status table (name,
  description, created by, member count, created at). Unlike P22-S3's `ApprovalSettings`
  (deliberately ungated), this endpoint has REAL server-side enforcement — the page is therefore
  consistently protected both via `RequireCapability` and via a gated sidebar entry, like
  `/users/`.
- **Tests**: `teamspace-service` 45 (previously 41, +4: `GET /admin/teamspaces` without a principal/without
  capability → each `403`, an end-to-end test across two teamspaces with different creators
  confirms that a non-member with the capability sees both incl. correct member counts,
  plus a repository unit test). `permission-service` unchanged at 137 (only one new, preseeded
  role, already covered by the existing generic mechanism). `admin-ui` 191 (previously 185,
  +6: four new tests in `teamspaces-admin.test.tsx`, two new visibility tests in
  `admin-sidebar.test.tsx`).
- **Verification detail, not a code bug**: during the live test, a role assignment was initially made
  via username, which had no effect via the gateway — `X-DMS-Principal` (set by the gateway from the
  access token) is the Keycloak `sub` claim, a short numeric ID (`"2"`) for technical accounts like
  `users-admin`, NOT the username. `GET /auth-service/me` returns the actual
  `sub` value. Important reminder for future live verifications via the gateway (earlier
  verifications in this project mostly set `X-DMS-Principal` manually via `curl`, not via
  the gateway, so this difference had not previously been noticed there).
- **Fully verified live against the actual running stack** (image rebuild + restart of
  `teamspace-service`/`permission-service`/`admin-ui`): `GET /admin/teamspaces` without capability → `403`;
  after a real role assignment to the correct `sub` principal → `200`; two real teamspaces with
  different creators created via the gateway, the admin principal (a member of neither
  of the two) saw BOTH in the overview incl. correct member count (`1` resp., after a
  member invite, `2` in the API test) — confirmed by a parallel `403` on `GET /teamspaces/{id}`
  for the same principal (the regular, membership-filtered route remains unchanged). Test data
  subsequently deleted. No interactive browser test (no browser/Playwright available in this
  development environment, an established project-wide practice).
- Docs: new [ADR 0090](docs/adr/0090-teamspaces-admin-overview.md),
  `docs/services/teamspace-service.md` (API table, new section "installation-wide
  admin overview", tests section, "Open Points" marked resolved), `docs/services/
  permission-service.md` ("domain-separated admin roles" table), `docs/services/admin-ui.md`
  (page table, new section "teamspaces admin overview", backend integration table,
  tests section) updated.

**P22-S6 — connector operational config pages for signature-service/storage-service** (see
[ADR 0091](docs/adr/0091-connector-operational-config-live-editable.md)):

- **Background**: the plan wording ("backend work first, new `GET/PUT /config`
  endpoints") left open HOW far "connector configuration" extends — `storage-service`'s target
  set contains real S3 credentials, `object_lock_mode`/`role` are WORM-/disposal-relevant. Before
  implementation, clarified via `AskUserQuestion` instead of assumed: (1) scope = only operational
  parameters, no credentials; (2) only already existing entries editable, no CRUD
  management of the list; (3) live reload without restart. All three recommendations confirmed by the
  user.
- **`storage-service`**: new DB singleton table `operational_config` (`write_strategy`,
  `quorum_count`, `max_replication_attempts`), same get-or-create pattern as `GuardConfig` — read
  freshly from the DB on every affected request (no `app.state` cache). New endpoints `GET`/
  `PUT /operational-config`; `PUT` repeats the same quorum satisfiability check as at
  startup (`_validate_settings`) against the (still structurally fixed) target count, `422` for
  unsatisfiability. `upload_object`/`replication_process_pending` now read the values via the new
  `_get_operational_config()` helper function instead of directly from `settings`.
- **`signature-service`**: new DB singleton table `signature_config` (`provider_levels`, JSON dict
  `{connector_id: [levels]}`). New endpoints `GET`/`PUT /signature-config` — `PUT` accepts a
  list `[{id, levels}]` and updates ONLY the named connector `id`s (partial merge), rejects
  unknown `id`s, empty `levels`, and `qes` at `type=internal` with `422` (the same rule as
  `SignatureProviderConfig._check_levels`, here checked at runtime instead of only at startup).
  `resolve_connector_for_level()` (`connectors/__init__.py`) now takes an already merged
  `list[SignatureProviderConfig]` instead of reading `Settings` directly — the only caller conversion
  in `main.py`'s `create_signature` endpoint.
- **Deliberately NOT part of this session** (see ADR 0091 "rationale" for the
  full trade-off discussion): the target/connector set itself (credentials, `id`/`type`) as well as
  `object_lock_mode`/`role` per storage target — both remain env-var-only, changeable only via restart.
- **New `admin-ui` pages**: `/storage-operational-config/` (`StorageOperationalConfig.tsx`, a simple
  form) and `/signature-config/` (`SignatureConfig.tsx`, a table with level checkboxes per
  connector — **the first Admin UI integration of `signature-service` at all**). Client-side
  blocked (no API call) from deselecting the last remaining level of a connector — purely a
  UX anticipation, `422` remains the server-side enforcement.
- **Two real test infrastructure bugs found and fixed** (both uncovered by running the
  respective test suite twice in a row, exactly the symptom that would otherwise have surfaced only in
  a later, independent run): (1) `signature-service`'s `tests/conftest.py` truncates a fixed
  table list instead of deriving it dynamically from `Base.metadata` — the new
  `signature_config` table was missing from it (identical finding as already in P22-S2 at
  `permission-service`), fixed by adding it to the list. (2) `storage-service` has NO
  truncate fixture between tests at all (existing tests instead rely on per-test-unique
  object keys) — the new tests, which mutate the DB singleton `operational_config`, would otherwise have
  leaked state across multiple `run-tests.sh` runs; fixed via a new, local
  `operational_config_client` fixture that restores the env var defaults after each test, instead of
  rebuilding the whole service's test infrastructure.
- **Tests**: `storage-service` 117 (previously 113, +4: `GET` default, `PUT` persistence,
  quorum rejection, end-to-end proof via a real upload right after `PUT`); `signature-service`
  16 (previously 11, +5: `GET` default, three validation cases, end-to-end proof via a
  failing AES sign attempt after removing AES from the levels, followed by a
  successful SES attempt); `admin-ui` 201 (previously 191, +10: `storage-operational-config.test.tsx` 4
  tests, `signature-config.test.tsx` 6 tests).
- **Fully verified live against the actual running stack** (image rebuild + restart of
  `storage-service`/`signature-service`/`admin-ui`): `GET /operational-config`/`GET /signature-config`
  showed the correct env var starting values; `PUT` with `quorum_count=2` (only 1 regular target in
  the dev stack, the second carries `role=archive`) returned `422`; a satisfiable `PUT` to
  `strategy=quorum` followed by a real object upload ran successfully via the quorum code path
  without a restart between `PUT` and upload; `signature-config`'s `PUT` with `levels=["qes"]` for the
  `internal` connector as well as with an unknown connector `id` both returned `422`. All
  test data/config changes subsequently reset. No interactive browser test of the
  two new Admin UI pages (no browser/Playwright available in this development environment,
  an established project-wide practice).
- Docs: new [ADR 0091](docs/adr/0091-connector-operational-config-live-editable.md),
  `docs/services/storage-service.md`, `docs/services/signature-service.md` (each API table, new
  section, tests section, "Open Points" at signature-service marked partially resolved),
  `docs/services/admin-ui.md` (page table, two new sections, backend integration table,
  tests section) updated.

**P22-S7 — storage target metadata live editable** (last session of Phase 22, see
[ADR 0092](docs/adr/0092-storage-target-metadata-editable.md)):

- **Background**: the plan wording ("make OCR/storage target set editable — extension of the same
  two existing pages from P22-S6") was ambiguous — "target set" is, in the existing code/docs,
  exclusively a `storage-service` concept (the list of backend instances incl. credentials),
  `ocr-service` has no equivalent. Before implementation, clarified via `AskUserQuestion`: scope =
  only `object_lock_mode`/`role` per already configured target made editable (an extension of
  `StorageGuard`, the page P22-S6 referenced as the UI model), no credentials, no
  structural CRUD, no "OCR target set."
- **New, sparse table `target_override`** (`target_id` PK, `object_lock_mode`, `role`, `updated_at`)
  — unlike `OperationalConfig`/`GuardConfig` (singleton, get-or-create), only an actually
  overridden target has a row; if one is missing, the env var value applies unchanged.
- **New endpoint `PUT /guard-status/{target_id}/config`** — `404` for an unknown `target_id` ("only
  edit existing entries," same requirement as P22-S6). **Real crash protection found while
  implementing**: `422` if the change would leave NO regular (non-archived) target remaining
  — without this check, `role="archive"` on the last regular target would have crashed every
  subsequent upload with an unhandled `IndexError` (`app.state.targets[0]` on an empty list).
- **New `_compute_target_state()`** (`main.py`) merges `Settings.targets` with all
  `target_override` rows into an effective target list — called at startup AND on every `PUT`,
  result is immediately written back into `app.state.target_configs`/`.targets`/`.archive_targets`/
  `.lock_target_ids`. Deliberately a DIFFERENT live-reload pattern than P22-S6's
  `OperationalConfig` (there: read freshly from the DB on every affected request) —
  `object_lock_mode`/`role` are read at roughly 15 places in the
  code, a refresh of `app.state` at `PUT` time achieves the same result with a significantly
  smaller, lower-risk diff. Known limitation: with multiple horizontally scaled replicas, a
  replica without its own `PUT`/restart does not see the change (uncritical for the
  current single-replica reality of this project).
- **`resolve_targets()`/`resolve_archive_targets()`** (`backends/__init__.py`) now take a
  `list[BackendTargetConfig]` instead of reading `Settings` directly — callers pass, depending on
  context, the structural env var list (startup/healthz) or the live-merged list.
- **`apps/admin-ui`**: `StorageGuard.tsx` (`/storage-guard/`) — the previously purely read-only "Object
  Lock" column is now a checkbox, plus a new second checkbox column "disposal target"
  (`role=archive`) — a click takes effect immediately, without a restart.
- **Proactive test infrastructure finding** (discovered before any failure, not only afterward):
  unlike the other services in this session, `storage-service`'s `tests/conftest.py` already had a
  teardown `DELETE` list — but it was missing for `operational_config` (P22-S6) AND the new
  `target_override` table. Both added, verified by running three times in a row.
- **Tests**: `storage-service` 122 (previously 117, +5: `404` for an unknown target, `422` for "would
  leave no regular target remaining" with the sole test target, an end-to-end proof via an object
  that was uploaded WITH `retain_until` BEFORE the override and afterward, without a restart, sits
  under governance lock, plus two repository unit tests). `admin-ui` 204 (previously 201, +3:
  `storage-guard.test.tsx` extended with toggling object lock mode/disposal role
  as well as error display).
- **Fully verified live against the actual running stack** (image rebuild + restart of
  `storage-service`/`admin-ui`): `PUT` on an unknown target → `404`; `PUT role=archive` on `local`
  (the only regular target configured in the dev stack) → `422`; a real object with `retain_until`
  uploaded BEFORE activating `object_lock_mode=governance`, then governance mode activated live
  (`GET /guard-status` confirmed it immediately), a deletion attempt without bypass returned `403` —
  confirms the live-reload behavior without a restart between upload and lock activation. Governance mode
  subsequently reset; the test object itself remains, until its retention period naturally expires
  (~24h), locked under the independent application-layer guard (no `dms-admin` realm role
  account for a `bypass_governance` deletion attempt available in this session) — harmless, clearly
  named as a test artifact. No interactive browser test (no browser/Playwright available in this
  development environment, an established project-wide practice).
- Docs: new [ADR 0092](docs/adr/0092-storage-target-metadata-editable.md),
  `docs/services/storage-service.md` (API table, data model, new section, tests section, two new
  "Open Points" bullets), `docs/services/admin-ui.md` ("storage guard" section updated,
  backend integration table, tests section) updated.
- **Phase 22 (Admin UI expansion) thereby fully completed.**

### Phase-boundary `graphify update` (after Phase 22, before Phase 23)

After completing Phase 22 (last session P22-S7), as is standard per `CLAUDE.md`/project convention, the
full `/graphify --update` skill flow was run (not just the plain `graphify update .` CLI, which only
covers the code/AST portion) — 32 document files new/changed since P21-S4 (`IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`,
`PROGRESS.md`, ADRs 0079–0092, 16 edited `docs/services/*.md`) needed fresh semantic
extraction.

- **Two parallel extraction subagents** (16 files each) together delivered 198 new nodes/298 edges;
  merged with the cache of the 164 unchanged document files (574 nodes) and the unchanged
  code nodes from the previous `graphify update .` CLI run (12350 nodes starting basis).

- **Real shrink-guard finding**: the first `build_merge()` attempt (with the FULL cache+new set as
  `new_chunks`) would have deleted 1261 nodes from files that were NOT part of the 32 changed files
  AT ALL (e.g. `auth-service.md`, `archival-service.md`, ~30 more) — cause: `build_merge`'s
  replace-on-re-extract logic deletes every `source_file` that appears ANYWHERE in the passed
  `new_chunks` set from the base graph; since the passed set accidentally contained the ENTIRE cached
  document corpus (not just the actual delta), unchanged files were also "replaced" — with a
  re-extraction result that only covered the 32 files that had actually changed. The built-in
  `to_json` shrink guard (`#479`) correctly blocked this BEFORE `graph.json` was corrupted. Fix:
  pass only the real 32-file delta (from the two chunk files directly, before the cache merge) as
  `new_chunks`.
- **Second, smaller finding**: `PROGRESS.md` (2966 lines/930KB) and `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (465
  lines/214KB) were processed by the first extraction subagent via `grep` sampling instead of full-text
  reading (both files exceeded the read window) — as a result, 82 and 41 existing phase/session nodes
  respectively (Phase 1–19) would have been silently lost on replace. A targeted post-extraction pass
  (paginated reading of the complete files) produced 245 nodes/407 edges with complete Phase-0-through-26
  coverage, deduplicated against the already existing `implementation_plan_
  phase22_admin_ui_expansion` node ID instead of duplicating it.
- **Result after correction**: 12211 nodes/22333 edges/1103 communities (net −139 versus the
  starting baseline — plausible: consolidation from fresh extraction of the 32 files, offset by the
  245 newly gained phase/session nodes, minus 20 genuine dedup collisions). Graph health check
  clean (no dangling/missing/collapsed edges). All 1103 existing community labels remained valid
  (no new community was created).
- **Lesson**: in a `build_merge()` within an `--update` run, ALWAYS pass only the real re-extraction
  delta as `new_chunks`, never the (cache+new) merge — otherwise the replace-on-re-extract logic also
  drops unchanged files. The `to_json` shrink guard is the last line of defense and here prevented
  real data loss before it happened.

### Phase 23 — Frontend UX (User UI) (P23-S1, ...)

**P23-S1 — Version history shows `is_conflict`/`comment` as badge/tooltip** (first session of the new
Phase 23, no new ADR — pure additive visibility of already existing backend fields):

- `DocumentVersion.is_conflict`/`.comment` had existed since P5-S3 in the type (`apps/user-ui/src/lib/api.ts`)
  and were already delivered correctly by the backend, but were completely invisible in the UI (documented
  "Open Point" in `docs/services/user-ui.md`, now closed).
- `PreviewPane.tsx`'s version selector: each `<option>` gets a `⚠` suffix in the label when `is_conflict`
  is set, plus (always) `comment` as a native `title` tooltip. In addition, a `.badge.down` element
  ("Conflict") and visible comment text below the `<select>` show the same fields for the currently
  selected version — deliberately visible not only on hover.
- **Layout trap found and corrected**: the badge/comment `<span>`s were initially nested INSIDE the
  `<label>` of the version selector — as a result, the implicit label association pulled their
  text into the accessible name of the `<select>`, and a `getByLabelText("Version auswählen")` test failed
  immediately. Fix: badge/comment rendered as sibling elements AFTER the `<label>` instead of inside it.
- New `--dms-danger-bg` token (all four theme blocks: light, dark, explicit light/dark, high
  contrast) as well as `.badge`/`.badge.down` classes in `user-ui`'s `globals.css` — adopted 1:1 from
  `admin-ui`'s already existing `.badge.ok`/`.badge.down` pattern, including its
  high-contrast special case (solid instead of transparent surface + `border: 1px solid currentColor`, since
  transparent accent surfaces are hard to read there).
- **No backend code changed** — the underlying fields were already delivered correctly before this
  session, so no curl verification against the real stack was needed. No interactive browser test
  (no browser/Playwright available in this development environment, project-wide established
  practice) — instead the actual DOM structure (badge/tooltip/options markup, including the switch on
  version change) was verified via a new `PreviewPane.test.tsx` case (jsdom).
- **Tests**: `user-ui` 172 (previously 171, +1). `tsc`/`eslint` clean (one pre-existing, unchanged
  `<img>` lint hint elsewhere in the same file).
- Docs: `docs/services/user-ui.md` (added "version selector shows `is_conflict`/`comment`" paragraph,
  closed "Open Points" entry, updated tests section), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P23-S1 ✅).

**P23-S2 — Automatic view switch when opening a search result** (no new ADR, no
code/test effort — pure documentation correction):

- **Found while implementing: the behavior described in the plan already exists since P16-S1.**
  `DocumentWorkspace.tsx`'s `openDocumentTab()` (the ONLY external entry point for opening a
  document — Explorer, search, favorites, teamspaces) already calls `setView("documents")` BEFORE
  delegating to `DockableDocumentArea`; `DockableDocumentArea` itself is `hidden={view !== "documents"}`,
  and the special-area block (`SearchPane` etc.) is completely replaced when `view !== "documents"`. A
  click on a search result therefore already automatically switches the view back to the
  document view.
- **Already proven by an existing test**: `document-workspace.test.tsx`, case "runs a search
  and opens a result as a document tab" — `expect(screen.queryByLabelText("Suche")).not.toBeInTheDocument()`
  directly after clicking a search result, plus a visible `PreviewPane` for the opened
  document. No new test needed.
- **Two stale documentation spots found and corrected** (both still described the pre-P16-S1 behavior,
  apparently not updated during the P16-S1 rework): the "Search" paragraph in `docs/services/user-ui.md`
  incorrectly claimed "deliberately no automatic view switch, since... a switch would unexpectedly hide the
  currently used search view" — now corrected to the actual behavior in effect since P16-S1; the "Open
  Points" entry with the same outdated statement was removed.
- **Lesson**: before starting implementation of a planned session, a brief look at the
  actual code pays off — the plan was created months ago from a consolidated "Open Points" list that
  was already not fully up to date at that time (the underlying implementation had already moved on
  via P16-S1, without the list catching up).
- Docs: `docs/services/user-ui.md` (two spots corrected/removed, see above), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`
  (P23-S2 ✅ with finding explanation).

**P23-S3 — Polling (5–10s) for not-yet-finished renditions/OCR results** (no new ADR — pure
implementation-detail extension of an already existing component, `PreviewPane.tsx`):

- **Important upfront finding**: the backend API has no "pending"/"processing" status at all.
  `RenditionSummary.status` is `"ready" | "failed"` (backend schema additionally `failed_permanent`),
  `OcrResultSummary.status` is `"ready" | "needs_review" | "failed"` (backend schema additionally
  `failed_permanent`/`skipped`) — `rendering-service`'s/`ocr-service`'s `upsert_rendition()`/equivalent
  always already creates the row WITH a completed final status; there is no separate
  "first create as pending, then update" step. "Not yet finished" therefore means **the row is still
  entirely missing**, not "status is pending" — the plan's wording ("as long as status is pending/processing")
  had to be adjusted accordingly; the actual user goal (automatic reload without manual
  reopening) is still achieved unchanged.
- **Two independent poll loops, capped at `RENDITION_POLL_MAX_ATTEMPTS = 8` × `RENDITION_POLL_INTERVAL_MS = 7000`
  (~56s total window)** (deliberate upper bound instead of endless polling — a
  format without a supported rendition or a document with disabled OCR triggering, see
  `ocr-settings`, would otherwise keep asking forever):
  - **Renditions** (office/text formats without their own raw-byte path): the existing
    `pdf_archive`/`substitute_text` search loop now waits BEFORE the final display decision —
    if neither rendition is ready on the first attempt, `listRenditions()` is called again every 7s
    until one is ready or the upper bound is reached (then hint text as before).
  - **OCR overlay** (PDFs/images): deliberately keeps running in the background AFTER the already
    immediately visible native PDF/image view (user sees something immediately, no waiting for OCR) —
    as soon as a result is ready, `setOcrResult(...)` switches the view afterwards to the
    word-overlay display, without interrupting the already visible native view.
- Both loops use the same `cancelled` flag pattern as the rest of the effect (cleanup on
  document/version change or unmount) — no hanging timer after closing a tab.
- **Tests**: `user-ui` 174 (previously 172, +2) — two new `PreviewPane.test.tsx` cases with
  `vi.useFakeTimers()`/`vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync()`, so the test run doesn't actually wait 7s: an
  initially empty rendition response followed by a finished `pdf_archive` rendition after the simulated
  poll tick; a PDF without an initial OCR result immediately shows the native view (`downloadOcrPageImage`
  not yet called) and correctly switches to the
  word-overlay view after the next simulated poll tick. **Test-infrastructure lesson from this session**: the
  first test attempts with `vi.useFakeTimers()` ran into the real 5s test timeout, because Testing Library's
  own `findBy*` queries themselves rely on (frozen by the fake clock) `setTimeout`-based polling —
  Fix: after `render()`/after each `advanceTimersByTimeAsync(N)`, call `advanceTimersByTimeAsync(0)`
  multiple times to manually flush chained effect/promise chains, and use synchronous `getBy*`/
  `querySelector` queries instead of `findBy*` once the state should already be settled.
- No backend code changed, so no curl verification needed — no interactive browser test
  (project-wide established practice).
- Docs: `docs/services/user-ui.md` (new "polling for not-yet-finished renditions/OCR results"
  paragraph, closed "Open Points" entry, updated tests section), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`
  (P23-S3 ✅).

**P23-S4 — Folder moving via drag & drop in `ExplorerPane`/`FolderTree`** (no new ADR —
implementation detail of an existing component, no new endpoint):

- **Backend unchanged**: `PATCH /folders/{id}` already accepted `parent_id` (`FolderUpdate.parent_id`,
  verified before starting implementation) — only a new `moveFolder()` function in `lib/api.ts` (analogous
  to `renameFolder()`, same endpoint, different field) and a new `handleMoveFolder()` handler in
  `DocumentWorkspace.tsx`, passed down through `DockableDocumentArea`'s workspace context to `ExplorerPane`/
  `FolderTree` (same pattern as `onRenameFolder`).
- **Native HTML5 drag & drop** in both views: each folder row is `draggable`, `onDragStart` stores
  the dragged folder ID in local state (deliberately **no** `dataTransfer` payload — that isn't
  readable during the `dragover` event anyway, only in the `drop` event; state is simpler). Drop onto
  another folder row (`ExplorerPane` list) or onto another row OR the root row
  (`FolderTree` tree) calls `moveFolder()`. New `.entry-row-drag-over`/`.tree-row-drag-over` CSS
  classes for visual feedback (same look as dockview's own drag-over indicator).
- **Backend gap found while implementing, confirmed live via curl**: `folder-service`'s
  `repository.update_folder` only checks `new_parent_id == folder_id` ("not its own
  direct parent folder") when moving — **no deeper cycle check**. A forced two-node cycle (folder
  A moved into B via `PATCH`, then B moved into A via `PATCH`) was reproduced live: both
  folders become undeletable afterwards (the non-empty check sees the other one as a child in each
  case) — had to be resolved by a third `PATCH` (moving one of the two back to `root`) before
  cleanup was possible. Client-side protection in `FolderTree` (target must not be any
  descendant ID of the dragged folder currently visible in the tree, checked via the
  ancestor chain already carried during rendering) only prevents this for the loaded/visible part of
  the tree, not for direct
  API calls or invisible tree parts. Deliberately NOT fixed in this session (pure
  frontend session scope per plan) — documented as a new Open Point in `docs/services/folder-service.md`,
  including a solution sketch (traverse the ancestor chain of the new parent folder up to the root
  server-side and reject if the folder to be moved appears in it).
- **Tests**: `user-ui` 177 (previously 174, +3) — `document-workspace.test.tsx` got three new cases with
  `fireEvent.dragStart`/`.dragOver`/`.drop` (jsdom has no native drag & drop, but the component itself
  also only relies on synthetic React events, no `dataTransfer` access needed):
  moving in the list view calls `moveFolder()` with the correct IDs and reloads; dropping on
  itself does NOT call `moveFolder()`; in the tree view, a drop of a parent folder onto its
  own expanded descendant is refused, a drop of the descendant onto the root row works.
  **Test-infrastructure finding**: `.closest("li")` initially failed in the tree view — the drag
  handlers there are attached to the inner `<span class="tree-row">`, not the `<li>` (DOM events don't
  propagate into child elements, so a `fireEvent` on the `<li>` never reaches handlers on the child
  `<span>`); fix: `.closest("span.tree-row")` instead of `"li"` for the tree view (in the list view the
  same handlers are attached directly to the `<li>`, where `.closest("li")` was correct from the start).
- No backend code changed (the discovered gap was deliberately not fixed, see above) — live
  verification made sense nonetheless, since the UI calls the endpoint for the first time from the
  frontend: two test folders created via curl against the real running `folder-service`, `moveFolder()`'s
  exact `PATCH` call confirmed the expected `parent_id` change, the cycle gap reproduced and resolved
  again, both test folders deleted afterwards. No interactive browser test (project-wide established
  practice).
- Docs: `docs/services/user-ui.md` (new "folder moving via drag & drop" paragraph, backend
  integration table, updated tests section), `docs/services/folder-service.md` (new Open Point
  on cycle checking), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P23-S4 ✅).

**P23-S5 — Favorite marker (⭐) also in `FolderTree.tsx`** (no new ADR, no backend code):

- `ExplorerPane`'s already-since-P7-S1d loaded `favoriteKeys` set (`"type:id"` strings) is passed as a new
  prop to `FolderTree` — no second `listFavorites` call, exactly the same pass-through
  pattern as `documentTypeById`/`kennzeichenShowByDefault` for the reference-number prefix.
- `FolderTree` renders the same `⭐ ` prefix in front of folder/document names as the list view, for both
  row types (folder intermediate nodes AND document leaves).
- **Deliberately pure display, no toggle in the tree view** — `FolderTree` doesn't yet have its own
  context menu (the existing toggle path runs exclusively through `ExplorerPane`'s `ContextMenu`), that
  comes only with **P23-S8** (right-click context menu also in `FolderTree.tsx`).
- **Tests**: `user-ui` 178 (previously 177, +1) — a new `document-workspace.test.tsx` case confirms the
  ⭐ prefix for a favorited folder AND a favorited document simultaneously in the tree view,
  with the same `listFavoritesMock` fixture pattern as the existing list-view favorites tests.
- No backend code changed, so no curl verification needed — no interactive browser test
  (project-wide established practice).
- Docs: `docs/services/user-ui.md` (favorites paragraph extended, updated tests section),
  `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P23-S5 ✅).

**P23-S6 — Responsive breakpoint of form layouts switched to CSS container queries instead of window width**
(no new ADR — implementation detail, no new architectural decision):

- **Before**: `useIsBelowBreakpoint()` in `LayoutFormFields.tsx` measured `window.innerWidth` via a
  `resize` event listener — a dockview panel dragged narrow within a wide window remained
  multi-column due to this bug, even though it was visually narrow (documented "Open Point").
- **Now**: `.layout-grid-container` carries `container-type: inline-size`, making this wrapper the
  reference frame for an `@container` rule — it measures the actual width OF THE FORM ITSELF, no
  longer the window width.
- **Dynamic threshold without a CSS variable in the width condition**: `layout.responsive_breakpoint_px`
  is admin-configurable per object type (Layout Designer, P5b-S3) — but an `@container` width condition
  cannot reference a CSS variable (only style queries could, but they don't cover width).
  Solution: `LayoutFormFields` generates an inline `<style>` block with the concrete pixel value directly in
  the JSX, scoped via a per-component-instance unique `data-layout-container` identifier (`useId()`) —
  prevents two simultaneously open panels of different object types (e.g. two
  document tabs) from overwriting each other's threshold.
- The previous `window.innerWidth` `resize` listener (`useIsBelowBreakpoint`) has been completely
  removed — pure CSS solution, no more JS resize handling in this component.
- **`Konzept.md` (2.2b) updated**: the original wording explicitly spoke of "window width" —
  that in fact described the old, undesired behavior (see above), not the actual intent
  ("readability on narrow display areas"). Sentence refined to "width of the form itself", with
  reasoning (dockable workspace allows narrow panels independent of the window) — matching the
  new implementation exactly, no version-number bump for a pure refinement without new functional scope.
- **Tests**: `user-ui` 180 (previously 178, +2) — new standalone `layout-form-fields.test.tsx` (`jsdom`
  does not compute real CSS layout, so no visual verification is possible — checked structurally
  instead): the `data-layout-container` wrapper exists, the `<style>` block contains the correct
  `@container (max-width: ...)` rule with the expected pixel value and references the same scoping ID;
  two simultaneously rendered layout instances with different thresholds get different
  IDs, no collision. `tsc`/`eslint` clean, additionally `next build` (`output: "export"`) run once fully
  through to make sure the raw `<style>` tag in the JSX also compiles correctly in the production-ready
  static export (no styled-jsx special case, pure HTML `<style>`).
- No backend code changed, so no curl verification needed. No interactive browser test
  (no browser/Playwright available in this development environment, project-wide established practice) — for
  a purely visual CSS change like this one, a more noticeable limitation than for most other
  sessions of this phase, but with no alternative in this environment.
- Docs: `docs/services/user-ui.md` ("form layouts & class icons" paragraph rewritten, "Open
  Points" entry closed, updated tests section), `Konzept.md` (2.2b refined),
  `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P23-S6 ✅).

**P23-S7 — `SignaturesPanel` triggers reload of the version list in `PreviewPane` after a successful
signature** (no new ADR, no backend code):

- **Problem**: `SignaturesPanel` (in `MetadataPanel`) and `PreviewPane` are independently mounted
  dockview siblings (`MetadataPanelContent`/`DocumentPreviewContent` in `DockableDocumentArea.tsx`) —
  a successful signature creates a new document version server-side (PAdES modifies the
  PDF bytes, ADR 0025), but `PreviewPane` knew nothing about it until the document was reopened.
- **New "version bump counter per document ID"** at `DocumentWorkspace` level:
  `versionBumps: Record<string, number>` + `bumpDocumentVersion(documentId)` (increments the counter for exactly
  this ID). Deliberately a counter instead of a boolean — a boolean that is set to `true` once
  does not trigger another state change on a SECOND signature (React compares by
  reference/value equality, `true → true` is not a change).
- **Passed through via `WorkspaceContext`** (same pattern as `onRenameFolder`/`onMoveFolder` in the
  previous sessions of this phase): `DocumentWorkspace` → `DockableDocumentArea` props →
  `WorkspaceContextValue` → `MetadataPanelContent` (`onSigned={ctx.onDocumentVersionBump}`) AND
  `DocumentPreviewContent` (`versionBump={ctx.versionBumps[doc.id] ?? 0}`).
- **`SignaturesPanel.handleSign()`** calls `onSigned?.(activeDocument.id)` after a successful `createSignature()`
  (new optional prop, default `undefined` — no breakage for call sites without bump wiring).
- **`PreviewPane`** gets a new `versionBump?: number` prop (default 0) and added it to the
  dependency list of its already existing version-loading effect (`[accessToken,
  activeDocument?.id, versionBump]`) — a bump triggers the same `listDocumentVersions()` call that
  also runs when the document is first opened, no separate second code path.
- **Tests**: `user-ui` 181 (previously 180, +1) — a new `document-workspace.test.tsx` case: open a document
  (only one version, version selector not yet visible) → sign via `SignaturesPanel` →
  version selector automatically appears with the new version 2 selectable, without the document being
  reopened. Staged `listDocumentVersionsMock` responses (first two calls — `PreviewPane`'s
  initial load + `SignaturesPanel`'s `isSignable` check — only version 1, every call afterwards both
  versions) prove that a real reload happens, not just a re-render.
- No backend code changed, so no curl verification needed. No interactive browser test
  (project-wide established practice).
- Docs: `docs/services/user-ui.md` ("signatures" paragraph updated, "Open Points" entry
  closed, updated tests section), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P23-S7 ✅).

**P23-S8 — Right-click context menu also in `FolderTree.tsx`** (last session of Phase 23, no new
ADR, no backend code):

- **No logic duplication**: `ExplorerPane.openFolderContextMenu()`/`openDocumentContextMenu()`
  (delete with/without approval requirement, favorite/unfavorite, share link for documents)
  depend on several `ExplorerPane`-local state values (`folderDeleteRequiresApproval`,
  `documentDeleteRequiresApproval`, `favoriteKeys`, `shareLinkEnabled`, ...) that `FolderTree` doesn't
  have. Instead of duplicating this logic into `FolderTree` or passing down all these states
  additionally, `ExplorerPane` passes the two already existing functions themselves down as new props
  (`onFolderContextMenu`/`onDocumentContextMenu`) — `FolderTree` only wires
  `onContextMenu={(e) => onFolderContextMenu(e, folder)}` (analogously for documents) onto its rows.
- **A single `ContextMenu` instance for both views**: `ExplorerPane`'s already existing
  `contextMenu` state + the one `<ContextMenu>` instance at the end of `ExplorerPane`'s JSX handle the
  display unchanged — the click coordinates (`event.clientX`/`clientY`) are screen-relative,
  independent of which nested component (list or tree) the call came from. No
  second menu, no portal mechanism needed.
- **Tests**: `user-ui` 183 (previously 181, +2) — two new `document-workspace.test.tsx` cases: right-click
  on a folder row in the tree view opens the same menu as in the list view, "delete" correctly calls
  `trashFolder()` (after `window.confirm` confirmation, `vi.spyOn` like the
  list-view counterparts); right-click on a document row + "add to favorites" calls
  `addFavorite()`, the ⭐ prefix appears afterwards (builds on the P23-S5 favorite marker).
- No backend code changed, so no curl verification needed. No interactive browser test
  (project-wide established practice).
- Docs: `docs/services/user-ui.md` (`FolderTree` paragraph extended, updated tests section),
  `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (P23-S8 ✅ with "Phase 23 fully completed" note).

**Phase 23 (Frontend UX User UI) thereby fully completed** — all eight sessions (P23-S1 through
P23-S8) implemented. Notable about this phase: two of the eight sessions (P23-S2, P23-S3-like) turned
out during implementation to already be done or modeled differently than the plan wording assumed
(P23-S2 already implemented since P16-S1; P23-S3's "pending/processing" status doesn't exist in the
backend API at all, yet the actual user goal was still achieved) — both were found through brief
code research before implementation rather than through subsequent failures.

### Phase 24 — Feature completion (P24-S1, ...)

**Four mutually independent sessions, processed in parallel via agent dispatch at the user's suggestion**
instead of sequentially — each subagent got a complete, self-contained task description (including
