pre-researched context on the respective affected interfaces/files), was allowed to touch ONLY its own
service/app, was NOT allowed to edit `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`/`PROGRESS.md`, and was NOT allowed to commit — the
integration into the shared documents as well as all four commits only happened afterwards, sequentially, to
avoid race conditions on the same files. After all four were done: test suites of all four
services independently re-verified (`scripts/run-tests.sh auth-service mail-connector
plugin-orchestration-service storage-service`) — all four counts confirmed exactly
(105/41/43/134), `ruff check` clean repo-wide; the `ruff format` warning concerned
exclusively pre-existing, unchanged files in `federation-hub-service`/`license-service` (none of the four
sessions).

**P24-S1 — Azure Blob backend for `storage-service`** (Konzept 1a, no new ADR):

- `AzureBlobBackend` (`azure-storage-blob`, `aio` client, connection-string auth instead of `azure-identity`/
  AAD) as a third `StorageBackend` implementation alongside `local`/`s3`, `build_backend()`/
  `BackendTargetConfig` (`type="azure"`, `connection_string`+`container`) extended analogously to `s3`.
- Azurite (Microsoft's local Azure Storage emulator) newly added to `infra/docker-compose.yml`
  (`mcr.microsoft.com/azure-storage/azurite:3.30.0`, `--skipApiVersionCheck` against SDK/image
  version drift on `x-ms-version`).
- **`lock_until`/`bypass_governance` deliberately documented as a no-op**: Azurite doesn't support
  immutability policies — an untested protection would have been faked. Same stance as with the
  `local` backend (ADR 0030), so no new ADR, just a cross-reference in the code docstring/settings
  comment/`docs/services/storage-service.md`.
- **Tests**: `storage-service` 134 (previously 122, +12) against real Azurite, no mocking — roundtrip,
  checksum, `exists`, idempotent deletion, `ObjectNotFoundError`, `lock_until`'s no-op behavior.
- **Live verification**: real upload/download/fixity check/deletion via the regular HTTP API against
  a test `type=azure` target. **Risk found and avoided during this**: adding the target first as a
  SECONDARY target triggered the existing rebalancing feature and seeded 30,410
  `pending` rows for EVERY already existing object in the shared dev database (real data from
  parallel sessions) — `process-pending` was deliberately NOT run (would have tried to copy real
  production data into the throwaway Azurite container) — instead, the target was configured as
  PRIMARY for a single new test object, after which the 30,410 falsely seeded rows were removed
  again via SQL and verified by counting against the prior state. Concrete, lived proof of
  the already known gap "no clean way to remove a target" (now documented in `docs/services/
  storage-service.md` "Open Points" with this incident as evidence).
- Docs: `docs/services/storage-service.md` (new backend section, WORM section, tests, open points).

**P24-S2 — AD group→role mapping** (4.4, `auth-service`, see
[ADR 0093](docs/adr/0093-ad-group-role-mapping-simple-1to1-scope-cut.md)):

- New, dedicated, lean table `ad_group_role_mapping` — deliberately SEPARATE from permission-service's
  admin-created groups (ADR 0088/Phase 22 — that's about admin-managed membership, this is about
  external Keycloak/AD group claims, read dynamically from the token instead of synced into a
  membership table). `GET`/`POST`/`DELETE /ad-group-mappings`, gated behind `admin.user_management`.
- **Keycloak previously never included group memberships in the access token at all** — new
  `bootstrap._ensure_groups_mapper()` idempotently adds an
  `oidc-group-membership-mapper` (`full.path=false`) on EVERY start, the same pattern as the already existing
  `_ensure_client_updated` (necessary because `skip_exists=True` at client creation would never retrofit
  an already existing client).
- `GET /me` dynamically merges the roles derived from the `groups` claim (no caching, no
  membership synchronization) into the same `realm_roles` field as Keycloak's directly assigned
  roles. Audit runs via the existing `auth.>` event stream (`audit-service` has already consumed it
  since P6-S5), no new mechanism.
- **Deliberate scope cut, documented in ADR 0093**, versus the full Konzept-4.4 scope: only
  simple 1:1 mapping (one AD group name → one internal role), NO composite rules
  (group AND attribute, multiple groups → one role via AND logic), no approval step before
  taking effect (saving = immediately live, but remains gated + audited), no sync interval, not yet
  part of config-service's JSON configuration export (7.3) — a mapping therefore cannot yet be transferred
  between installations. All four points explicitly documented as "Open Points".
- **Tests**: `auth-service` 105 (previously 96, +9), run three times in a row (once with `--build`),
  new table correctly added to `conftest.py`'s truncate list (no repeat finding of the
  project-wide test-isolation bug, since it was proactively considered when writing the tests this time).
- **Fully verified live against the real running stack**: `auth-service` rebuilt/restarted,
  mapping created via curl, a REAL Keycloak group + real user created and assigned to the group,
  `GET /me` showed the mapped role alongside the Keycloak-native roles; deleting the mapping removed
  it on the next `/me` call; audit events (creation + deletion) confirmed in `audit-service`'s hash chain with
  the correct actor/payload. All test artifacts (Keycloak user, group, mapping row)
  cleaned up.
- Docs: `docs/services/auth-service.md` (new API rows, new section, bootstrap/events, open points,
  tests section), new [ADR 0093](docs/adr/0093-ad-group-role-mapping-simple-1to1-scope-cut.md).

**P24-S3 — IMAP mailbox backend + outbound attachments** (`mail-connector`, see
[ADR 0095](docs/adr/0095-imap-backend-mocked-imaplib.md)):

- **Scope decision**: the plan title mentioned "IMAP/Graph API" — Microsoft Graph (Exchange/O365) is a
  standalone, considerably larger undertaking (external OAuth2 app registration, token refresh,
  Graph REST instead of IMAP/POP3 semantics) and doesn't fit into the same session as IMAP + outbound
  attachments. Deliberately built only IMAP, documented Graph as an "Open Point" — the
  `MailboxBackend` interface already supports it, following the same pattern as `Pop3Backend`/`ImapBackend`.
- **`ImapBackend`** (`backends/imap_backend.py`): `imaplib` (standard library), same
  `asyncio.to_thread` pattern as `Pop3Backend`. UID-based command set (`UID SEARCH`/`UID FETCH` with
  `BODY.PEEK[]`, RFC 3501) instead of sequence numbers; `select(..., readonly=True)` + `BODY.PEEK[]` avoid
  server-side `\Seen` side effects (same restraint as POP3's deliberately omitted `dele()`).
  Since a bare IMAP UID is only stable within the same `UIDVALIDITY` epoch, the
  `source_uid` passed on to `repository.get_by_source_uid` is composite (`{uidvalidity}:{uid}`).
  New settings `DMS_IMAP_HOST`/`_PORT`/`_USERNAME`/`_PASSWORD`/`_USE_TLS`/`_MAILBOX` (default `INBOX`).
- **No self-loopback IMAP in mailpit**: `docker run axllent/mailpit:v1.30.6 --help` confirmed that
  mailpit, unlike in the POP3 case, doesn't come with its own IMAP server. `test_imap_backend.py`
  therefore mocks at the `imaplib` boundary (RFC-3501-faithful fake responses) instead of running
  against a real server — full reasoning + rejected alternatives in [ADR 0095](docs/adr/0095-imap-backend-mocked-imaplib.md).
- **Outbound attachments**: `POST /outbound` attaches the current content of the referenced
  document when `related_document_id` is set. New `DocumentClient.get_current_version()` — `DocumentOut`
  itself doesn't carry file metadata (that lives on document-service per version on
  `DocumentVersionOut`), hence two internal calls. Unknown `related_document_id` → `400`, checked BEFORE
  the SMTP send attempt.
- **Tests**: `mail-connector` 41 (previously 30, +11), run twice in a row, no
  isolation leaks.
- **Verified live against the real stack** (image rebuild + restart): outbound attachment with an
  actually uploaded test document sent, mailpit confirmed filename/content type/bytes exactly;
  IMAP receiving against a temporary `greenmail` container NOT entered into `infra/docker-compose.yml`
  (`docker run`, attached to `dms-net`, removed afterwards) — a test message delivered via SMTP was
  picked up by the connector (started via `docker compose run` with `DMS_INBOUND_PROTOCOL=imap`)
  over a real IMAP connection and correctly ingested. All test data cleaned up, the regular
  `mail-connector-1` container continues running unchanged with the POP3 default.
- Docs: `docs/services/mail-connector.md` (fetch-protocol section, new outbound-attachment section,
  tests section, open points), new [ADR 0095](docs/adr/0095-imap-backend-mocked-imaplib.md).

**P24-S4 — Container orchestration adapter for `plugin-orchestration-service`** (last session of
Phase 24, see [ADR 0094](docs/adr/0094-plugin-orchestration-kubernetes-scheduler-adapter.md)):

- **Scoping correction versus the plan wording**: the already existing interface (`SchedulerAdapter`,
  `platform_scheduler.py`) is purely placement-ADVISORY (`try_place(cpu_cores, ram_mb) -> node_id |
  None`), not a container lifecycle API (no start/stop/scale) — this service has never done anything
  like that itself; its job is to decide WHICH node would be suitable, not to take over actually running
  it there. `KubernetesSchedulerAdapter` therefore implements exactly this
  placement advice, no lifecycle management.
- Lists cluster nodes via `CoreV1Api.list_node()` (in-cluster config — the only sensible
  authentication method, since the adapter, per `detect_platform_scheduler()`'s already existing
  `KUBERNETES_SERVICE_HOST` detection, is only activated inside a real pod anyway), optional
  label scoping via new settings `kubernetes_node_label_selector`, filters cordoned/not-ready nodes,
  checks `cpu_cores`/`ram_mb` against `status.allocatable`.
- **Deliberate simplification, documented in ADR 0094**: NO accounting of capacity actually consumed
  by already running pods — only the static `allocatable` capacity per node. Since
  `decide_placement()` trusts the return value without its own re-check, this is a genuinely documented
  risk for this first version, not a silently glossed-over gap. Tie-break when several
  matching nodes exist: most free allocatable RAM capacity wins (spreads load, reduces the risk
  from the gap above), `node_id` as a deterministic second tie-break.
- **Tests**: `plugin-orchestration-service` 43 (previously 33, +10) — new test file mocking the
  `kubernetes` client directly at the API boundary (real `V1Node` model objects, not the
  `FakeSchedulerAdapter` already existing for `decide_placement()` — that would have proven nothing about
  the real implementation).
- **Honestly named live-verification gap**: no real Kubernetes cluster available in this
  development environment (confirmed — this project currently has only Docker Compose
  as a real deployment target; Phase 26 will explicitly not require a real cluster deployment even with
  finished Helm charts). As the best possible substitute: a Compose regression test (service keeps
  starting error-free, `NullSchedulerAdapter` remains active when `KUBERNETES_SERVICE_HOST` is missing, no
  behavioral difference from the prior state) plus a standalone, non-pytest
  sanity script against a mocked client (4 test scenarios: capacity hit with tie-break,
  capacity failure → `None`, cordoned/not-ready correctly excluded, label-selector
  passthrough) — all five scenarios ran as expected.
- Docs: `docs/services/plugin-orchestration-service.md` (new adapter, activation condition,
  simplifications, honestly named test limitation), new
  [ADR 0094](docs/adr/0094-plugin-orchestration-kubernetes-scheduler-adapter.md).

**Phase 24 (Feature completion) thereby fully completed** — all four sessions implemented,
three of them with a new ADR (0093/0094/0095 — the three parallel subagents collision-free hit the same
"highest existing number was 0092" starting point and, without coordinating with each other,
independently picked the next free numbers in the order of their respective completion — verified during
integration that no real collision/duplicate remained on disk in the end).

### Phase 25 — Workflow/gateway hardening (P25-S1, ...)

**Four sessions, processed dependency-aware in two parallel tracks** (user hint: parallelize
phases/sessions whenever they don't need to access the same resource): P25-S1/P25-S2 share
`workflow-service/repository.py` and build directly on each other (sequential), P25-S3/P25-S4 share
`gateway-service/main.py` but at different, non-adjacent spots (classified as
"safe-with-care" and dispatched in parallel) — both tracks ran simultaneously, S2 started right
after S1 finished, S4 right after S3.

**P25-S1 — Race-condition-safe version assignment** (`workflow-service`, no plan-literal
`SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`, see [ADR 0096](docs/adr/0096-workflow-definition-version-race-advisory-lock.md)):

- `create_process_definition`/`create_dmn_definition` previously guarded version assignment via an
  unlocked `MAX(version)+1` — a `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on existing rows (as already established
  in `object-type-service`/`case-service`) doesn't protect the critical case of "first two concurrent
  uploads of a brand-new process family", though, since no row to lock exists yet in that case.
- Instead a transaction-bound `pg_advisory_xact_lock`, keyed on a hash of the logical
  identifier (`name` or DMN equivalent) instead of a physical row — covers the new-creation
  case too. Deliberate, ADR-documented deviation from the two previous precedents.
- **Found live during this, not fixed**: `permission-service` has its own cache-insert race — outside
  the session scope, documented as an open point.
- Tests: `workflow-service` +2 (concurrency tests, real parallel inserts against the same new
  process family).

**P25-S2 — Rollback endpoint** `POST /process-definitions/{id}/restore`:

- Reads an arbitrary (also no-longer-current) historical version and creates, via
  `repository.create_process_definition` (P25-S1, race-safe), a BRAND-NEW, now-current version with
  its `bpmn_xml`/`bpmn_process_id` — append-only, no in-place edit, `process_id` is explicitly passed
  through as `source.bpmn_process_id` instead of `None` (avoids a repeat auto-resolution).
- Gated like `POST /process-definitions` (`admin.object_config` + license gate), deliberately NOT
  additionally routed through the BPMN import review gate (ADR 0087) — reasoning: no new, unreviewed
  content, pure reuse of an already existing version.
- Restoring the already-current version is not a special case/no-op — like every other call it simply
  creates another, content-identical version (consistent with the append-only model).
- No own ADR (pure reuse of ADR 0096 via a new entry point).
- Tests: `workflow-service` 183 (previously 179, +4: 403 without permission, 404 for unknown ID,
  content/version-history check, restore-of-the-current-version).
- **Live verification**: v1 uploaded, v2 (modified) uploaded, restore from v1 produced v3 with
  byte-identical `bpmn_xml`/`bpmn_process_id`; 404 case against unknown ID confirmed; test data
  cleaned up.

**P25-S3 — Gateway rate limiting with a shared store**
(see [ADR 0097](docs/adr/0097-gateway-rate-limiting-redis-sliding-window.md)):

- `RateLimiter` switched from an in-process `dict` to a shared Redis sliding-window store
  (`ZADD`/`ZREMRANGEBYSCORE`/`ZCARD`), enabling horizontal gateway scaling.
- **Deliberately sliding-window instead of the simpler fixed-window `INCR`+`EXPIRE`**: fixed-window allows
  up to 2x the configured limit at window edges — relevant since the limiter also gates login/
  brute-force-sensitive endpoints.
- New `redis` service in `infra/docker-compose.yml` — the first Redis dependency in the entire project.

**P25-S4 — Workload-aware instance selection**
(see [ADR 0098](docs/adr/0098-gateway-workload-aware-instance-selection-per-replica.md)):

- `InstanceResolver.pick()` has, since this session, selected the instance with the fewest currently
  open requests among several healthy candidates of a `service_type` instead of, as before
  (ADR 0005), purely at random — an in-process `dict[str, int]` counter per instance address, reserved/released
  via the new async context manager `resolver.reserved_instance(instances)` (also covers
  failing upstream calls via `finally`, no leaked reserved slots).
- Tie-break among several instances with the same minimum: random among the minimum
  candidates, not always the first in the list (otherwise, at rest/startup, when all counters are 0,
  the same instance would be systematically favored).
- **Deliberately purely per gateway replica, no cluster-wide value** — unlike the directly adjacent P25-S3
  (Redis rate limiting), since here there's no incentive to circumvent it and an additional
  Redis round trip per proxied request would be out of all proportion to the benefit.
- Tests: `test_upstream.py` extended by 5 cases (selection by load, tie-break randomness, full
  reserve/release cycle, release on success AND on exception) — `gateway-service` thereby at
  29 tests (previously 24 after P25-S3).
- **Live verification**: two temporary test instances (one responding immediately, one artificially
  delayed by 1.5s) registered via the real registry, real parallel load bursts (120 and
  150 concurrent requests respectively) fired at the running gateway — the fast instance
  reproducibly received significantly more requests (77/43 and 105/45 respectively) instead of the ~50/50
  distribution expected under random selection (4-5
  standard deviations from the uniform-distribution expectation).
- Open: selection is still not latency-aware, only the count of open requests is counted.

Test suites independently re-verified (`scripts/run-tests.sh workflow-service gateway-service`):
workflow-service 183/183, gateway-service 29/29, both counts confirmed exactly. `ruff check`
clean repo-wide; the `ruff format` warning concerns exclusively pre-existing, unchanged files in
`federation-hub-service`/`license-service` (none of the four sessions). ADR numbers 0096/0097/0098 checked
for collision-freedom (file system, `docs/adr/`) — no duplicates.

**Phase 25 (Workflow/gateway hardening) thereby fully completed.**

### Phase 26 — Helm charts for k8s/OCP (P26-S1, ...)

**One** Helm chart (`infra/k8s/dms/`, chart name `dms`) for the entire stack from `infra/docker-compose.yml`, values-driven instead of 34+ nearly identical individual charts ([ADR 0099](docs/adr/0099-helm-single-chart-values-driven-service-map.md)). Verification via `helm lint --strict`/`helm template` per session — no real cluster deployment required in this phase (the `helm` CLI wasn't pre-installed, no root in this environment — loaded as a static binary from get.helm.sh, v3.15.4, into the session scratchpad, reused for each session).

**P26-S1 — Base skeleton**: `resources.baseline` (memory/cpu per requests/limits) as default, `services.<name>.resources` selectively overrides via `mergeOverwrite`. Generic `services:` map + `templates/deployment.yaml`/`service.yaml`/`hpa.yaml`/`pdb.yaml` that iterate via `range`, `templates/_helpers.tpl` with `dms.postgresDsn`/`dms.keycloakBaseUrl`/`dms.natsUrl` helpers. HPA (`autoscaling/v2`) and PodDisruptionBudget individually activatable per service via `autoscaling.enabled`/`podDisruptionBudget.enabled`. `postgresql`/`keycloak`/`minio` (only these three, explicit user requirement) with `enabled`/`external.*` switches for bundled-vs-external, `nats`/`redis` deliberately bundled-only (no exception for these two). `storageService.targets` as a native YAML list, field names adopted 1:1 from `BackendTargetConfig` (`services/storage-service/src/storage_service/settings.py`) instead of guessed, serialized at runtime via `toJson` into `DMS_TARGETS` (**not** `STORAGE_SERVICE_TARGETS` — that's only the Compose host variable name, see ADR). Four real example services (`registry-service`/`gateway-service`/`document-service`/`storage-service`) exercise all paths (pure baseline fallback, selective override, full override, HPA active, PDB active, storage-targets JSON) — verified end-to-end via `helm template`, not just visually checked. `helm lint --strict` error-free, `helm template` renders 11 valid manifests, resource-merge cases individually re-checked in the rendered output.

**P26-S2 — remaining 28 FastAPI services**: `services:` extended to 32 entries (audit-, auth-, permission-, virus-scan-, rendering-, ocr-, search-, workflow-, notification-, case-, signature-, federation-hub-, favorite-, reporting-, archival-, query-, license-, plugin-orchestration-, monitoring-service, webdav-connector, cmis-connector, mail-connector, migration-, config-, fleet-management-, teamspace-, object-type-, folder-service) — no new template needed, exactly the P26-S1 pattern. Ports/cross-service `DMS_*_BASE_URL` dependencies adopted 1:1 from the `environment:` blocks in `infra/docker-compose.yml` plus the respective `settings.py` defaults, instead of guessed. `templates/_helpers.tpl`'s `dms.dependsOnServicesEnv` extended with a `{name, envVar}` form for the small minority of services whose Compose env-var name deviates from the standard pattern `DMS_<KEY>_BASE_URL` (`auth-service`/`workflow-service` → `federation-hub-service`/`gateway-service`, actually `DMS_FEDERATION_HUB_BASE_URL`/`DMS_INSTALLATION_GATEWAY_BASE_URL`). Autoscaling additionally activated, beyond `document-service` (P26-S1), for four hot-path services (`virus-scan-`/`rendering-`/`ocr-`/`search-service` — LibreOffice/Tesseract subprocesses and the Postgres FTS read path respectively), PDB additionally for three of them (not `ocr-service`, since `minReplicas:1`). `helm lint --strict`/`helm template` clean, 32/32 Deployments+Services, 5 HPA, 5 PDB rendered (kind counts verified), several manifests (auth-, ocr-, folder-service) spot-checked for ports/computed `DMS_*_BASE_URL` values. Open, documented points: `audit-service`'s deletion-register ledger volume without a PVC equivalent (P26-S3), `mailpit` hostname as well as some addresses conceived as external remain Compose-parity placeholders, `postgres-wal-archive-init` deliberately outside this chart (Job instead of Deployment). No new ADR (pure values population per ADR 0099).

**P26-S3 — stateful infrastructure**: Postgres/Keycloak/MinIO (bundled/external switches, as envisaged in ADR 0099) as well as NATS/Redis (bundled-only) get real Deployment+Service(+PVC) manifests in their own templates (`templates/postgresql.yaml`/`keycloak.yaml`/`minio.yaml`/`nats.yaml`/`redis.yaml`) instead of just connection helpers — structurally too different for the generic `services:` mechanism (volumes, different image sources/health checks, secret references). New `existingSecret`-if-set-else-generate mechanism ([ADR 0100](docs/adr/0100-helm-secrets-existing-secret-pattern.md)) for Postgres/Keycloak/MinIO admin passwords — fixes the plaintext-password gap documented since ADR 0099; `DMS_POSTGRES_DSN` since then references the password via native k8s `$(VAR_NAME)` env substitution instead of a never-resolved `${...}` placeholder. In the process, a real bug latent since P26-S1 was found: `storageService.targets`'s secondary-s3 target pointed fixedly to the Compose hostname `http://minio:9000`, which never existed in this chart — now dynamically resolved to the bundled/external MinIO address via a placeholder. PVCs only for Postgres/MinIO/NATS (Keycloak uses Postgres as its DB, Redis is deliberately purely transient, both documented). `helm lint --strict` clean, `helm template` delivers 37 Deployments/37 Services/3 PVCs/3 Secrets in the default case; the bundled-vs-external toggle (Postgres/Keycloak/MinIO) and the `existingSecret` path both verified end-to-end against the 32 stateless services (including a special case: bundled Keycloak correctly follows external Postgres when only Postgres is switched external). Open points: `storageService.targets` credentials remain plaintext (only the endpoint was made secret-/toggle-aware), no WAL archiving in bundled Postgres (documented scope cut, OpenShift's restricted SCC is incompatible with the Compose fixed-UID trick anyway), audit-service ledger PVC still open (from P26-S2).

**P26-S4 — storage replication CronJob**: `templates/storage-cronjob.yaml` triggers `POST /replication/process-pending` (`main.py` line 561-564, `limit: int = 100`, no auth gate) every 15 minutes against storage-service — the external carrier for the full-jitter backoff retry queue (ADR 0082) announced since ADR 0004/P20-S6. **Session briefing assumed two periodically callable endpoints, the actual code disproves that for one of them**: `GET /object-verify/{key:path}/all` (line 523-542) requires a concrete object `key` as a path parameter and only verifies the redundancy targets of THIS ONE object, not "all objects" — storage-service has no endpoint that lists object keys or delivers a batch of unverified objects (no fixity counterpart to `list_pending_copies`). **Decision ([ADR 0101](docs/adr/0101-storage-cronjob-single-job-no-bulk-verify.md))**: only ONE CronJob, for replication — a second CronJob blindly running against the per-key endpoint would only fake Konzept 3.6's "regular fixity check across all copies" without actually delivering it; `storageCronJob.verification.enabled: false` remains a deliberately unwired placeholder, ADR 0101 sketches a `next_verify_at` field plus bulk endpoint for a future `storage-service` session, mirroring the replication retry queue. `curlimages/curl:8.10.1` (no full storage-service image needed), `concurrencyPolicy: Forbid`, `activeDeadlineSeconds: 180`, `backoffLimit: 2`, schedule configurable (default `*/15 * * * *`). New `dms.storageServiceBaseUrl` helper (same URL formula as `dms.dependsOnServicesEnv`). Sends `X-DMS-Principal: system:storage-replication-cronjob` (same pattern as `archival-service`'s `_SYSTEM_PRINCIPAL_HEADERS`), even though storage-service currently doesn't require an auth header — future-proofed at no cost. `helm lint --strict` green, `helm template` renders 1 additional CronJob (37 Deployments/Services unchanged), `storageCronJob.enabled=false` renders zero CronJobs, manifest re-parsed via PyYAML (no `kubectl` available).

**P26-S5 — frontend apps + ingress** (last session): the 6 Next.js static-export apps (`user-ui`/`admin-ui`/`process-designer`/`reviewer-ui`/`migration-console`/`office-addin`) as further `services:` entries — structurally "one container on one port" like any backend service (all six are built identically: `next.config.mjs` with `output: "export"`, two-stage Dockerfile with `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` `ARG`s BEFORE `npm run build`, then `nginx:1.27-alpine` for the static `out/` folder, no Node process at runtime, no `/config.json` runtime-fetch path), but with two new optional fields: `healthCheckPath` (default `/healthz`, frontends `/`, since nginx instead of FastAPI — `nginx.conf` is `try_files $uri $uri.html $uri/ =404`, no `/healthz`) and `staticFrontend` (activates a `fail` guard against pointless `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` entries in `env:` — these Next.js build-time variables are baked in via Docker `--build-arg`, not read at runtime, see [ADR 0102](docs/adr/0102-helm-frontend-apps-buildtime-env-limitation.md); the guard was actually triggered via `--set services.user-ui.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_GATEWAY_BASE_URL=...`, not just claimed). New generic `templates/ingress.yaml` (vanilla `networking.k8s.io/v1` Ingress, no OpenShift `Route` object — OCP's standard router accepts Ingress natively, a Route template is documented as a deliberate v1 scope cut instead of silently missing, see [ADR 0103](docs/adr/0103-helm-ingress-not-openshift-route.md)) for `gateway-service` + the 6 apps, host-based instead of path-prefix routing (no `next.config.mjs` sets `basePath`/`assetPrefix`, path prefixes would cause the root-relative `/_next/...` asset URLs to collide). `office-addin` with `tls.enabled: true` as the sole exception (HTTPS required per `docs/services/office-addin.md`; its `manifest.xml` additionally has fixed, baked-in `https://localhost:3006` URLs — baked in deeper than a Docker `ARG`, a committed XML document that this chart cannot rewrite). Bug found and fixed live: `templates/deployment.yaml` had had NO liveness/readiness probes at all since P26-S1, despite `/healthz` in every FastAPI service (65 hits verified across all `services/*/main.py`) — now default `healthCheckPath: "/healthz"` for all 43 Deployments, without having to touch existing `values.yaml` entries. `helm lint --strict` green, `helm template` renders 113 manifests (43 Deployment/Service, 7 PodDisruptionBudget, 7 Ingress, 5 HPA, 1 CronJob, 3 PVC, 3 Secret; baseline before this session: 92 → +6 Deployment/+6 Service/+2 PDB/+7 Ingress), no duplicate resource names across all 113 manifests (Python check), external Postgres/Keycloak/MinIO toggles from P26-S3 re-confirmed via regression test (101 manifests with all three switched external).

**Open points for a hypothetical future session** (all documented, none silently skipped): (1) no runtime mechanism for `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` values after image build (structural Next.js limitation, ADR 0102), (2) no native OpenShift `Route` template (deliberate scope cut, ADR 0103), (3) `office-addin`'s `manifest.xml` with fixed baked-in `localhost:3006` URLs, (4) `gateway-service.env.DMS_CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` must be kept in sync by hand with the 6 `ingress.host` values, (5) bulk fixity verification remains on-demand/manual (ADR 0101), (6) `storageService.targets` credentials remain plaintext, (7) `audit-service`'s deletion-register ledger volume without a PVC equivalent, (8) no WAL archiving in bundled Postgres.

**Phase 26 (Helm charts for k8s/OCP) thereby fully completed — the entire roadmap arising from the Open-Points triage since P17-S3 (Phases 18–26) is thereby also fully implemented.**

### Post-Roadmap: Documentation English translation (2026-08-15/16)

User request: translate the entire documentation corpus and all code docstrings/comments from German to English (the German UI locale, `de.json` per app, deliberately excluded — it stays the live, active locale, only prose/comments moved). Done in three large batch passes, each via parallel subagents with a shared glossary (Vier-Augen-Prinzip→four-eyes principle, Aktenzeichen→file reference number, Poststelle→mail room, Aussonderung→records disposal, Papierkorb→trash, Freigabelink→share link, Bereichssperre(n)→scope lock(s), etc.) and a strict "prose/comments only, functional strings/identifiers untouched" boundary: (1) ~200 Markdown files (104 ADRs, 40 service docs, 43 READMEs, `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`, `PROGRESS.md` itself, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `README.md`, `docs/`), (2) ~280 Python files (docstrings/comments), (3) 151 TypeScript/TSX files (comments only, JSX/UI text/test data preserved). Recovered cleanly from a simultaneous multi-agent API rate-limit failure mid-pass by verifying on-disk state directly per file rather than trusting agent self-reports (some files already complete despite a "failed" notification, others needed precise boundary-finding for a scoped re-dispatch). `.gitignore`'s own remaining German comments translated by hand. Three commits (`5c53fd3`, `dd91d4b`, `d10e687`).

### Post-Roadmap: k6 load testing + full HTTP-sensor rollout (2026-08-16)

User request: an automated load test (ramping load, response times, goodput) analyzable via a notebook, followed by "the monitoring sensors are still thin" → extend per-service HTTP metrics to every service.

**Load testing** (`loadtest/`): k6 (chosen over locust for its built-in Prometheus remote-write output, confirmed integrating with the existing Grafana) drives a mixed realistic workload (login/list/search/upload/download) through the gateway with a 10min ramp-up to 200 VUs / 5min hold / 5min ramp-down profile. Per-VU token caching (the gateway rate-limits pre-auth `/login` by client IP, not by user) and a 50-account test-user pool created/torn down in `setup()`/`teardown()`. "Goodput" = 2xx **and** under an SLA threshold (`SLA_MS`, default 1000ms) — stricter than k6's own `http_req_failed`. **Two real bugs found and fixed via the load test itself**: (1) `teardown()` reused the `setup()`-time admin token, which expires (300s) long before a 20-minute run ends — fixed via a `SensorConfigProxy`-style indirection isn't needed here, just a fresh re-login inside teardown; (2) the original per-document delete loop couldn't finish within k6's default 60s teardown timeout or the gateway's rate limit at realistic upload volumes (tens of thousands of documents) — replaced with a single cascading `POST /folders/{id}/trash` call (folder-service already cascades to every contained document server-side), cutting teardown from a timeout to ~2 seconds regardless of volume. The run itself surfaced a genuine ceiling: the dev stack's license caps documents at 5000 — for local testing only, an unlimited license was minted with the dev signing key (`services/license-service/tests/fixtures/dev_private_key.pem`) and installed as local Postgres state, never committed. Analysis notebook (`loadtest/notebook/analysis.ipynb`) reads k6's JSON output plus, via a live Prometheus query, per-service CPU/memory from a newly added `cadvisor` container (pinned to v0.52.1 — v0.49.1's bundled Docker client speaks an API version too old for Docker 29.x and silently loses container labels).

**Full HTTP-sensor rollout** ([ADR 0104](docs/adr/0104-http-sensor-full-rollout.md)): the original sensor concept (10.1) covered only two pilots (`registry-service`/`document-service`) with bespoke metrics. Extended `libs/dms-metrics-client` with a generic `http.requests`/`http.request.duration_seconds` sensor pair (labeled method/route/status, route *template* not raw path to avoid cardinality explosion) that alone covers request count, avg/p95/p99 response time, and error/success rate via PromQL — installed via a new `bootstrap_http_sensors()` one-call helper, which **must** run at module level right after `app = FastAPI(...)`, not inside `lifespan` (Starlette forbids adding middleware once the ASGI lifespan protocol begins). A `SensorConfigProxy` indirection decouples the module-level `SensorRegistry` from the actual network-connected `SensorConfigClient`, which still has to be constructed fresh on every `lifespan` startup (its `httpx.AsyncClient` can't survive across independent event loops, e.g. separate `TestClient` runs in the same pytest session). Rolled out to all 28 installation-internal services (deliberately excluding `federation-hub-service`/`fleet-management-service`, per ADR 0028 not part of an installation at all, and `monitoring-service` itself, to avoid a self-referential scrape) — `gateway-service` newly self-registers with `registry-service` for the first time, purely so it can be scraped. `monitoring_service.scraper.merge_metric_families` now injects a `service` label (from `RegistryInstance.service_type`) so dashboards group by service name, not opaque instance IDs. New Grafana dashboard `dms-service-http-overview.json` (a `$service` dropdown plus an all-services overview table). Rolled out via parallel subagents following a manually-proven reference pattern (document-service/registry-service); one batch independently discovered a real, separate bug (`DMS_MONITORING_SERVICE_BASE_URL` missing from `infra/docker-compose.yml` for most services, defaulting to the wrong `localhost:8026` inside the container network, silently breaking sensor-config polling — fixed for all remaining services directly).

### Post-Roadmap: Playwright end-to-end browser test automation (2026-08-16)

User request: automate real-browser tests via Playwright — a deliberate reversal of an earlier, explicit decision recorded throughout this file ("no Chrome/Chromium installed, Playwright installation explicitly declined by the user"); flagged for visibility rather than silently overridden, then proceeded on explicit confirmation. Scope, chosen by the user over the alternative of starting with just user-ui/admin-ui: all 6 Next.js apps, "key user flows" (not just page-load smoke tests), local-only for now (no CI wiring).

No Node.js on the host - all `npm`/`playwright` commands run via the already-cached `mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.48.0-jammy` image (the same one this project already used for one-off manual browser verification), `--network host`, against the real running `infra/docker-compose.yml` stack. Test code is a real, permanent, committed artifact per app (`apps/<name>/e2e/`); only the execution *environment* stays ephemeral, preserving the spirit of the original "no permanent browser on this machine" decision.

**Three infrastructure bugs found and fixed while building the reference implementation (user-ui), then applied to all 6 apps**: (1) **nginx**: `try_files ... $uri/` (all 6 `nginx.conf`s) triggers nginx's automatic trailing-slash redirect with an *absolute* `Location` header built from `listen`/`server_name` (port 80, the container-internal port) - correct inside the container, wrong for any client reaching the app through this stack's mapped host ports (i.e. every real user of this dev stack). A real browser follows that redirect and fails to connect; `curl`/`fetch` never notices since neither follows redirects by default - a genuine, previously invisible, user-facing bug, not a test-only artifact. Fixed with `absolute_redirect off;` in all 6 `nginx.conf`s. (2) **npm peer conflict**: Next.js declares an optional peer dependency on a newer `@playwright/test` than the version pinned to match the docker image's browser binaries; `--legacy-peer-deps` "fixes" the resulting `ERESOLVE` error but disables npm's automatic peer-dependency installation *entirely*, including for `@testing-library/dom` (an actual, unlisted peer of `@testing-library/react` that every existing Vitest test relies on transitively) - silently breaking `tsc --noEmit` across the whole `tests/` directory with a failure nowhere near its real cause. Fixed with a `package.json` `"overrides"` entry instead, which resolves the one real conflict without touching peer installation anywhere else. (3) **tsconfig/eslint**: Playwright's own globals (and its fixture API's conventional `use` callback parameter, which `react-hooks/rules-of-hooks` mistakes for React's `use()` hook by name alone) don't belong in the same compilation/lint unit as the Vitest `tests/` directory - each app's `e2e/` now has its own `tsconfig.json` (excluded from the root one) and an `eslint.config.mjs` override.

**Permission model gotcha discovered along the way**: `document.read` (needed for `search-service`'s results to ever be non-empty - it filters through a permission check that `facet_counts` doesn't, so facets looked populated while results stayed empty, with no error) turned out to be a genuinely gated permission never granted to any broadly-applicable role, including the `users-admin` technical account used for all E2E logins - not a regression, just never previously noticed empirically. Also surfaced, unrelated to this task: hundreds of leftover one-off `search-test-role-*`/`share-link-test-role-*` permission-service roles from existing backend test suites that create a new role per run and never clean up - left alone (pre-existing, out of scope), but flagged in `apps/user-ui/e2e/README.md` so future E2E work doesn't repeat the pattern; one reusable `e2e-playwright-reader` role was granted once instead.

**Per-app flows** (all verified via the full local quality gate - `lint`/`typecheck`/`e2e/tsconfig.json` typecheck/`vitest`/`build`/`playwright test`, zero regressions, zero leftover test data after every run): user-ui (login, create subfolder → upload → search, in an isolated per-run root folder cascading-trashed in teardown), admin-ui (login, user management CRUD, object-type editor CRUD, registry overview), migration-console (login, paired-installation CRUD with the one-time API key notice, transfers status-filter smoke), reviewer-ui (login, task inbox edit-form validation, approvals status-filter smoke - both inboxes' deeper flows scoped down since this dev stack currently has no four-eyes-gated action types configured), process-designer (login, real `bpmn-js` SVG canvas interaction - place a task via the palette, name it, save via a real `POST /process-definitions` round-trip, delete again), office-addin (Office.js mocked via `page.addInitScript()` since `OfficeGate` blocks on a real `window.Office`/`Office.onReady()` that doesn't exist in a plain browser - also had to block the real Microsoft-hosted `office.js` CDN script via `page.route()`, since the dev network can actually reach it and it would silently overwrite the injected mock mid-test). 21 tests total across 6 apps.

### Post-Roadmap: user-ui version selector + metadata panel scrollbar visibility (2026-08-17)

User request: the document version selector was hidden whenever a document had only one version (`versions.length > 1` gate) - user wants it always shown, even for a single version. Fixed in `PreviewPane.tsx` by relaxing the condition to `versions.length > 0`. Separately, the metadata panel was reported as "not scrollable" - live investigation with the new Playwright infrastructure (DOM measurement + simulated mouse-wheel scroll) showed the underlying `overflow-y: auto` mechanism already worked correctly (`scrollHeight` > `clientHeight`, content does scroll into view); the real issue was OS/browser "overlay scrollbar" default behavior (invisible except while actively scrolling, common on macOS/GTK) making a genuinely-scrollable panel look broken. Fixed with persistent scrollbar styling (`scrollbar-gutter: stable`, `scrollbar-width: thin`, `scrollbar-color`, plus WebKit `::-webkit-scrollbar`/`::-webkit-scrollbar-thumb` rules) on the shared `.explorer-pane`/`.metadata-panel`/`.preview-pane`/`.search-pane` rule in `globals.css`. Both fixes verified live via Playwright against the rebuilt container. One test (`document-workspace.test.tsx`, P23-S7) updated - it asserted the now-obsolete hidden-with-one-version behavior as incidental test setup.

### Post-Roadmap: Phase 27 — public frontend base URLs & login `returnTo` (2026-08-17)

User request (three new features together): a PDF "Export" option alongside "Download" with export history and a combined folder export (Phase 28); authenticated direct links to documents/Akten/Vorgänge/folders for email notifications (Phase 29); configurable email templates by use case/recipient domain (Phase 30). Grounded via three parallel Explore agents plus a Plan agent against the real codebase before any code was written, then split into **Phase 27–30** in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (Phase 27 as a shared prerequisite for 29 and 30). During design, one mapping mistake was caught by the user before implementation: "Vorgang" was initially mis-mapped to case-service's `Case` — corrected to `workflow-service`'s `ProcessInstance` (a running process users actively work on) after the user clarified.

**P27-S1**: three new nullable settings in `notification-service` (`user_ui_public_base_url`/`reviewer_ui_public_base_url`/`admin_ui_public_base_url`, `DMS_*_PUBLIC_BASE_URL`), mirroring auth-service's `keycloak_public_base_url` (ADR 0062). New `notification_service/links.py` (`build_resource_link`), already implementing Phase 29's final URL scheme (`?document=`/`?folder=`/`?instance=`) rather than a throwaway stub, since the scheme was already fixed at plan time - not yet called from any `consumer.py` handler (Phase 29 Session 5). See [ADR 0105](docs/adr/0105-notification-service-public-frontend-base-urls.md).

**P27-S2/S3**: `returnTo`/`next` query parameter through the login flow, all 6 apps. `RequireAuth.tsx` (6 near-identical copies, deliberately left duplicated rather than extracted into a shared lib - same rationale as this project's poll-loop precedent) now redirects to `/login/?returnTo=<encoded current path+query>` instead of a bare `/login/`; `login/page.tsx` reads it back and redirects there after a successful login instead of hardcoding `/`, with a `sanitizeReturnTo()` open-redirect guard (rejects `//host/...`/absolute URLs, same-origin-relative only). Deliberately reads `window.location` directly rather than `useSearchParams()`, avoiding a `<Suspense>` wrapper requirement under `output: "export"` that would otherwise be needed almost everywhere (`RequireAuth` wraps nearly every page). `user-ui` additionally carries `returnTo` through the SSO/`state` round trip via a second `sessionStorage` key (`dms.sso.returnTo`) alongside the existing CSRF `state`, since Keycloak's `redirect_uri` allow-list (ADR 0062) can't carry an arbitrary query string. See [ADR 0106](docs/adr/0106-login-returnto-redirect-parameter.md).

Verified live via Playwright against the rebuilt `notification-service`/`user-ui`/`admin-ui` containers: an unauthenticated deep link to `admin-ui`'s `/users/` redirects to `/login/?returnTo=%2Fusers%2F`, login as `users-admin` lands back on `/users/` (not `/`), and a plain `/login/` visit with no `returnTo` still defaults to `/`. New automated tests: `test_links.py` (notification-service, 6), `login-page.test.tsx`/`require-auth.test.tsx` (user-ui, admin-ui, reviewer-ui) plus `login-callback-page.test.tsx` (user-ui, SSO round trip) - 21 new frontend tests across 3 apps. Full regression green across all 6 apps (typecheck/lint/vitest/build) and notification-service (pytest/ruff).

### Post-Roadmap: Phase 28 — PDF export with export history & combined folder export (2026-08-17)

User request (see Phase 27 entry above for full context): "Export" alongside "Download" - a document's current version plus its export history in a configurable order, and a combined folder export (all documents, table of contents, bookmarks, per-document sub-numbering stable against later export-history changes). All six sessions (P28-S1..S6) completed in one continuous pass.

**Mechanism** ([ADR 0107](docs/adr/0107-pdf-export-two-pass-merge-subnumbering.md)): `rendering-service` owns all PDF mechanics, reusing `watermark.py`'s existing reportlab-overlay-merged-via-pypdf idiom (new `export_pdf.py`). Pass A (`build_document_export`, per document): document PDF + export-history PDF concatenated in the configured order, then a **local** "i/N" footer - final and stable the moment Pass A runs, regardless of later folder-export composition. Pass B (`build_folder_export`, folder export only): TOC section rendered first (a throwaway probe determines its own page count), cumulative offsets computed, then a **second, independent** global "j/total" footer plus `pypdf` outline items (bookmarks). New rendering-service endpoints: `POST /render/convert-to-pdf` (reuses `PdfArchiveRenderer`'s dispatch, no persisted `Rendition`), `POST /render/export/document`, `POST /render/export/folder`.

**Export history** ([ADR 0108](docs/adr/0108-export-history-as-audit-service-query.md)): no new storage - `document-service` publishes `document.exported` (unconditionally, unlike the `AuditTraceConfig`-gated `document.downloaded`, since the history section IS the feature) and a new `AuditServiceClient` queries `audit-service`'s already hash-chained `document.>` event log at export time (fail-open to `[]` if unreachable).

**document-service**: `POST /documents/{id}/export` (sync, `?history_position=` override), `GET`/`PUT /export-config` (`ExportConfig` singleton, default `"after"`). `POST /folders/{id}/export` creates an async `FolderExportJob` (`202`, same `attempts`/`next_retry_at`/`failed_permanent` resilience shape as `ArchivalTransfer`, ADR 0078) - `_folder_export_poll_loop`/`_run_folder_export_tick` process due jobs, `GET /folder-exports/{id}` + `.../content` for polling/download. New `RenderingClient`/`AuditServiceClient` HTTP clients; `_build_document_export_pdf`/`_run_folder_export_tick` take their clients as explicit parameters instead of `app.state` (discovered during testing: httpx clients constructed on one event loop can't be reused from another - matches archival-service's existing `pipeline.run_active_transfers_tick` dependency-injection shape).

**Frontend** (user-ui): "Exportieren" button + a small order-override select next to "Herunterladen" in `PreviewPane.tsx`. "Ordner exportieren" folder context-menu entry in `ExplorerPane.tsx`, with 3s polling until the job completes, then an automatic browser download.

**Real bug found and fixed during live verification**: `DMS_RENDERING_SERVICE_BASE_URL`/`DMS_AUDIT_SERVICE_BASE_URL` were missing from `document-service`'s `docker-compose.yml` environment - the `Settings` class defaults (`http://localhost:8011`/`:8002`) resolve to the *document-service* container's own loopback, not the actual other services, since Docker containers don't share `localhost`. Manifested as a `502` with no matching request ever reaching rendering-service's own logs - same class of gap as the `DMS_MONITORING_SERVICE_BASE_URL` omission found during the earlier HTTP-sensor rollout.

Tests: rendering-service 59 (previously 46, +13: `test_export_pdf.py` unit tests for the merge/footer/TOC/bookmark mechanics, `test_api.py` for the three new endpoints). document-service 258 (previously 247, +11: `test_export.py`, permission checks, config, job lifecycle, full tick-to-completion with real cross-service calls). user-ui: PreviewPane export button (+3), ExplorerPane folder-export context menu + polling via `document-workspace.test.tsx` (+2), 197 total. Full regression green (backend pytest/ruff for document-service and rendering-service; frontend typecheck/lint/vitest/build for user-ui). Verified live end to end via Playwright against the rebuilt stack: single-document export downloads a real merged PDF; folder export via context menu completes asynchronously and auto-downloads.

### Post-Roadmap: Phase 29 — authenticated direct links (2026-08-17)

User request (see Phase 27 entry above for full context): direct links to documents/Akten/Vorgänge/folders, an authenticated user with access lands immediately on the resource, important for email notifications. All five sessions (P29-S1..S5) completed in one continuous pass.

**URL scheme** ([ADR 0109](docs/adr/0109-direct-link-url-scheme.md)): `<app-origin>/?<kind>=<id>` on each app's already-existing single route (`?document=`/`?folder=` in user-ui, `?instance=` in reviewer-ui) - no new backend endpoint, no token, resolved via the already-existing `GET /documents/{id}`/`GET /folders/{id}`/`GET /instances/{id}` and the already-existing client-side navigation functions (`openDocumentTab`/`openFolderPath` in `DocumentWorkspace.tsx`). Deliberately structurally opposite to the anonymous, token-bearing public share link (ADR 0047) - access control is exactly the normal permission-service check, surfaced visibly on `403`/`404` instead of a silent fallback to root. "Copy link" in `ExplorerPane.tsx`'s context menus and `PreviewPane.tsx` uses `navigator.clipboard.writeText()` - the first use of the Clipboard API in this project.

**"Vorgang" correction** ([ADR 0110](docs/adr/0110-vorgang-instance-detail-reviewer-ui.md)): mapped during Phase 27+ planning to case-service's `Case`, corrected by the user mid-session to `workflow-service`'s `ProcessInstance` - "ein im System laufender Prozess, der von den usern zu bearbeiten ist". Its first-ever per-instance detail view (`InstanceDetail.tsx`) is placed in reviewer-ui (not admin-ui as first drafted) - it's the app regular users already complete tasks in, no admin-only gate. Shows `GET /instances/{id}` status + `GET /instances/{id}/tasks` open tasks, deliberately no task history (workflow-service persists none, ADR 0019). `TaskList.tsx` gained a "Vorgang öffnen" button surfacing the previously-hidden `instance_id`.

**Email wiring** (P29-S5): `notification-service`'s `build_resource_link()` (from Phase 27) is now called from the three consumer handlers that already have a concrete resource reference - `_handle_task_escalated` (instance link), `_handle_deletion_reminder` (document link), `_handle_folder_deletion_reminder` (folder link) - each appends the link only when the relevant public base URL is configured, unconfigured installations see an unchanged body.

Tests: notification-service 50 (previously 46, +4: link presence/absence per handler with/without configured base URL). user-ui: deep-link resolution + copy-link tests in `document-workspace.test.tsx`/`PreviewPane.test.tsx`, 201 total (up from 197). reviewer-ui: new `instance-detail.test.tsx` (5) + `TaskList` "Vorgang öffnen" test, 31 total (up from 25). Full regression green across all touched services/apps. Verified live via Playwright against the rebuilt stack: document/folder deep links open the right resource, an unknown document shows a visible error, "Link kopieren" writes the correct URL to the clipboard.

### Post-Roadmap: Phase 30 — configurable email templates (2026-08-17)

User request (see Phase 27 entry above for full context): email content configurable by use case and/or recipient domain. All five sessions (P30-S1..S5) completed in one continuous pass. See [ADR 0111](docs/adr/0111-configurable-email-templates.md).

**Model + resolution** (P30-S1/S2): new `EmailTemplate` in `notification-service`, mirroring `permission-service`'s `ApprovalActionConfig` keyed-discriminator + no-row-fallback shape (already reused once for `ExportConfig` in Phase 28) — `(use_case, recipient_domain_pattern)`, `recipient_domain_pattern IS NULL` as the catch-all row. `use_case` is a **fixed, closed catalog** (`EMAIL_TEMPLATE_USE_CASES`, one entry per `consumer.py` dispatch branch) rather than `ApprovalActionConfig`'s open free text — deliberate, since `consumer.py`'s handlers are a fixed set of branches, not an open-ended list of callers. `subject_template`/`body_template` reuse `object-type-service`'s `kennzeichen_format` `{placeholder}`/`str.format()` convention (`templates.render_template`), failing loudly (`UnknownPlaceholderError`) on a typo'd placeholder rather than sending broken text. Five new endpoints (`GET /email-template-use-cases`, `GET /email-templates`, `PUT /email-templates/{use_case}[/by-domain/{domain}]`, `DELETE /email-templates/{id}`), deliberately ungated like `ApprovalActionConfig`'s own precedent (ADR 0089).

**Handler migration** (P30-S3): all 9 pre-existing `consumer.py` handlers now call a new `_render_or_fallback()` helper before sending — a resolved template renders in place of the hardcoded subject/body, `None` (no configured row) keeps the exact original f-string, guaranteeing zero behavior change for every installation that has never configured Phase 30. Resolution runs **per notification, not per event**: an event producing both an in-app and an email notification resolves separately per channel's own recipient, so a domain override can change only the email wording (the in-app recipient, a lane name/`"unassigned"`, never matches a domain row, only the catch-all).

**New use case: locked-document reminder** (P30-S4) — the first notification hook `document-service`'s editing lock (ADR 0002) has ever had. New `DocumentLock.reminder_sent_at` (reset to `NULL` on every `acquire_lock` call — a fresh lock and a same-holder renewal both restart `locked_at`), new `_lock_reminder_poll_loop` (mirrors `_retention_poll_loop`'s idiom exactly, `lock_reminder_poll_interval_seconds`=900s default) publishes `document.lock.reminder` once a lock has sat unrenewed past `lock_reminder_threshold_seconds` (14400s/4h default, deliberately far above the unrelated `default_lock_timeout_seconds`=1800s, so active editing never triggers it). Unlike the deletion reminders (no natural owner, fall back to `"unassigned"`), a lock always has a known holder — `notification-service`'s new tenth `consumer.py` branch sends the in-app notification directly to `locked_by`.

**Admin UI** (P30-S5): new `/email-templates/` page (`EmailTemplates.tsx`), structurally mirroring `ApprovalSettings.tsx` — table of configured rows (use case, domain or "Alle", subject, edit/delete), a form keyed by a closed-catalog `<select>` of use cases with a live placeholder hint, editing locks the use-case/domain fields (identity of the row being edited).

Tests: notification-service 78 (previously 50, +28: `test_templates.py` new 8, `test_repository.py` +6 `EmailTemplate` CRUD, `test_consumer.py` +14 covering catch-all/domain-precedence/unknown-placeholder-fallback/lock-reminder across all 10 use cases). document-service 261 (previously 258, +3: `list_locks_due_for_reminder` threshold/dedup, `acquire_lock` resets `reminder_sent_at` on renewal). admin-ui: new `email-templates.test.tsx` (8), 219 total (up from 211); new `e2e/email-templates.spec.ts` live create/edit/delete round trip. Full regression green (backend pytest/ruff for document-service and notification-service; frontend typecheck/lint/vitest/build/playwright for admin-ui). Verified live: rebuilt `document-service`/`notification-service`/`admin-ui` containers started cleanly (ad-hoc `ALTER TABLE` migrations applied without error), `GET /email-template-use-cases` confirmed to include all 10 use cases including the new `document.lock.reminder`, and the full admin-ui create/edit/delete flow verified via a real Playwright browser session against the running stack.

**Phase 27+ (the full user request from that session) is now fully complete** — all four phases (27, 28, 29, 30) done.

### Post-Roadmap: bugfix round after Phase 30 (2026-08-20)

User-reported bugs, four fixed plus one scope-reduction request, all in one continuous pass:

1. **PDF preview showed plain text instead of the PDF viewer when reopened later** (worked correctly right after upload) — `PreviewPane.tsx`'s rendition-polling loop computed `substitute` (the `substitute_text` rendition, meant only for Office/text formats without a native PDF view) by checking only whether `pdfArchive` was falsy, not whether the document actually `expectsOfficeRendition` — so a PDF whose `substitute_text` rendition (a side effect of `rendering-service`'s OCR-text-extraction pipeline, created for *every* PDF including ones with a real text layer, not just scans) happened to already exist by the time of a later re-open would incorrectly win over the native `<embed>` branch. Fixed by scoping the `substitute` lookup by `expectsOfficeRendition` too, matching the existing `pdfArchive` scoping. Regression test added.
2. **Light-mode UI bugs (checkboxes/buttons/inputs rendering as solid black boxes)** — root cause: `color-scheme: light dark` was hardcoded on bare `:root` in all six frontend apps' `globals.css` and never overridden per `[data-theme]`, so native form-control chrome (checkboxes, selects, buttons, date pickers) followed the OS's actual dark/light preference regardless of the app's own explicit theme choice — when the OS was dark but the user picked "Hell," native controls rendered dark while custom-styled elements rendered light, producing exactly this symptom. Fixed in five apps (user-ui, admin-ui, process-designer, reviewer-ui, migration-console; `office-addin` has no theme switcher and was unaffected, `libreoffice-addin` has no `globals.css`) by adding `color-scheme: light`/`color-scheme: dark` inside the respective `[data-theme="light"]`/`[data-theme="dark"]`/`[data-theme="high-contrast"]` blocks. Verified live via Playwright with `colorScheme: "dark"` forced at the browser level while the app's own theme was set to light — confirmed `getComputedStyle(document.documentElement).colorScheme === "light"` and a clean screenshot.
3. **OCR crash noise for non-OCR-able uploads (e.g. `.xlsx`), logged as `MsgAlreadyAckdError`/"Task exception was never retrieved"** — the user's hypothesis (OCR incorrectly running on xlsx) was wrong: `ocr-service`'s `select_engine()` already correctly excludes non-PDF/non-image content types, xlsx never reaches an OCR engine. The real bug: `libs/dms-eventbus-client/src/dms_eventbus_client/nats_backend.py`'s concurrent `_run()` path called `await msg.ack()` in an `else:` clause outside its surrounding `try/except`, inside a fire-and-forget `asyncio.create_task()` — if `ack()` itself raised (confirmed live: a real, still-not-fully-root-caused but now harmless redelivery race on the `document.>` wildcard subject), the exception surfaced only as an unhandled "Task exception was never retrieved," crashing nothing visibly but polluting logs and, per the user's report, apparently correlating with visible failures. Fixed by wrapping the ack call in its own try/except (`_ack()` helper), logging instead of crashing, applied to both the concurrent and sequential dispatch paths for consistency. New regression test mocks `Msg.ack` to raise and asserts no unhandled loop exception. Rebuilt and verified live in `ocr-service` — the underlying race still occurs under load (real documents rapidly created by concurrent test suites), but now degrades to a clean log line instead of an orphaned task exception.
4. **Export-history-position selector removed from the user-facing UI** (explicit user request: "die Position der Exporthistorie soll serverseitig eingestellt werden, nicht in der User-UI") — `PreviewPane.tsx`'s "Exportieren" button previously offered a `before`/`after`/default `<select>` letting end users override the Phase 28 `ExportConfig` installation default per export. Removed entirely (state, JSX, CSS rule, `exportDocument()`'s now-unused parameter, two i18n keys) — the position remains configurable, but only via `document-service`'s existing `GET`/`PUT /export-config` (server-side, no admin-ui page for it yet).

**Testing gotcha hit during verification, not a regression**: the full backend regression (`scripts/run-tests.sh` with no args) showed mass failures (`storage-service`, `signature-service`, `webdav-connector`, `workflow-service`, `rendering-service`) with connection-refused/timeout errors across completely unrelated services — root cause turned out to be the user manually stopping Docker containers mid-run while investigating the same issue independently ("ich hatte die Container gestoppt"). Re-verified clean once the stack stabilized: `signature-service`/`workflow-service`/`storage-service`/`ocr-service` all pass fully in isolation; `webdav-connector`'s pre-existing PROPFIND-timeout gap (documented since a prior session, "438 accumulated root test artifacts") is unrelated and untouched; `rendering-service`'s `test_consumer_integration.py` (2 tests) reproduced a genuine ~10s-timeout flakiness even in a fully isolated, resource-healthy re-run — confirmed unrelated to any of the four fixes above (git-stash causation test: reverting the `nats_backend.py` change and re-running still failed identically) and the test file itself is untouched since P19-S8, long before this session — flagged as a pre-existing, real-but-separate flakiness worth a dedicated look, not chased further here.

Tests: `libs/dms-eventbus-client` 8 (previously 7, +1 ack-failure-doesn't-crash regression). user-ui: `PreviewPane.test.tsx` 18 (net unchanged — two export-position tests merged into one, one new substitute_text regression added), 201 total unchanged. All five touched frontend apps' typecheck/lint clean. Rebuilt and restarted: `user-ui`, `admin-ui`, `process-designer`, `reviewer-ui`, `migration-console`, `ocr-service`. The other ~30 services share the same `dms-eventbus-client` fix but were deliberately not rebuilt in this pass (out of scope for a targeted bugfix round) — they pick it up automatically at their next regular rebuild.

### Post-Roadmap: Phase 31 planning — eGov feature gap analysis (2026-08-20)

User provided the release-notes documentation of a mature, long-established commercial German
government-sector (eGov) document management add-on — referred to in all project documentation only as
"the reference system," per explicit user instruction to never name it, and with the explicit framing
that this project's own government focus ("OG Doc") is intentional, not incidental, so eGov-specific
capabilities are in scope rather than something to filter out. Five parallel research passes read the
full ~230-page document (roughly seven years of release history) and extracted a ~300-entry feature
inventory; a sixth verification pass checked each candidate gap against this codebase's actual current
state (exact file/ADR citations, not assumptions) before it was allowed onto the final list — several
initially-plausible gaps turned out to already exist (delegation/deputy access, redaction *provenance
linking* though not the actual redaction mechanism, a first Aussonderung-scoped xdomea implementation)
and were excluded or downgraded to "extend" rather than "build new" accordingly.

Result: [`docs/egov-feature-gap-analysis.md`](docs/egov-feature-gap-analysis.md) (the full inventory,
citations, and explicit triage of what was deliberately not carried forward) and a new **Phase 31**
in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` — 13 sessions covering draft/pre-registration objects, per-document
multi-level classification with a dedicated gating role, document redaction with burn-in, deletion
reason codes, a records-management quarantine/retention-hold mechanism, output stamping (barcode/QR),
hand folders & work trays, an accessibility compliance pass, an org-hierarchy foundation plus the
dynamic access-grant and supervisor-oversight-view sessions that depend on it, a central/decentralized
mail-inbox model, and general xdomea/XJustiz exchange. Pure planning — no code changed, no ADR written
yet (each session writes its own once actually implemented). Per explicit user instruction, no git
commit was made for this round; the user will commit it themselves.

### Roadmap look-ahead planning after P6-S2
- **bpmn.io license (watermark) accepted**: `bpmn-js` (Process Designer, P6-S8) is under the "bpmn.io License" — free commercial use, but a non-removable watermark on every rendered diagram. Decision: accept (same pattern as ADR 0018), see [ADR 0021](docs/adr/0021-bpmn-io-license-watermark.md). To be revisited on future white-label need. **`bpmn-js-spiffworkflow` itself was in the end not used during the actual P6-S8 implementation** (not published on npm since 2022, license inconsistency npm vs. GitHub) — see [ADR 0026](docs/adr/0026-process-designer-bpmn-js-without-spiffworkflow-addon.md), deviating from the original ADR-0021 assumption.
- **P6-S4 split into P6-S4/S5/S6**: originally one collective session for the four-eyes mechanism (4.3) + break-glass (4.6) + emergency shutdown (4.8) AND a catch-up appointment for six already-unsecured spots — too much for one session. New scoping and **provisional** assignment of the six legacy cases, see `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` Phase 6; to be confirmed/adjusted at the respective session start. Old P6-S5 (Signature)→P6-S7, old P6-S6 (Process Designer)→P6-S8.
- **`PN-S0` convention starting at Phase 7**: every phase from Phase 7 onward gets its own kickoff planning session (`P7-S0`, `P8-S0`, ...), which at session start clarifies concept sections/licenses/session scoping of the respective phase — deliberately no deep research for this yet (would become stale before implementation). Phase 6 didn't get its own `P6-S0`, since this look-ahead planning round retroactively took on exactly that role.

### Process Designer / process versioning (follow-up question at P6-S8 plan approval)
- **Plan follow-up question instead of silent implementation**: the first P6-S8 plan (only BPMN modeling + signature-task properties panel) was rejected by the user at plan approval — two additional requirements: (1) real process versioning should already be retrofitted now, not remain an open point any longer; (2) cross-installation external swimlanes via the address book of connected installations, with automatic handover upon reaching a foreign swimlane and notification/shared design of the target installation (Federation Hub, 7.4) — **explicitly still no multi-installation** of the Process Designer itself ("whoever wants to use the UI must have the container in their own environment").
- **Split via `AskUserQuestion` instead of attempting both in one session**: the Federation Hub is an independently operated, cross-installation mediation service with its own trust/security model — technically a completely new service, not an appendage to a designer UI session. The user chose the recommended option "versioning now, Federation Hub as its own session afterward": versioning (ADR 0027) was still implemented in P6-S8, the Federation Hub base skeleton was inserted into the roadmap as new session **P6-S9** (moved up from P13-S3/S4, see `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`) — same splitting pattern as the P6-S4→S4/S5/S6 split above.
- **P6-S9 has meanwhile implemented the Federation Hub** (see own section "Federation Hub" below) — the split has thus proven itself as planned: versioning ran unremarkably in P6-S8, the Federation Hub got the room that a standalone, cross-installation mediation service actually needs.

### Registry & Gateway (3.2a/3.5)
- **No cleanup of permanently unreachable registry entries**: `docker compose down` without clean deregistration leaves `healthy=false` rows unbounded — harmless for routing, but without periodic cleanup.
- **Registry has also registered itself with itself since P4-S3** — otherwise `service_type=registry-service` would not be resolvable via the Gateway (needed for the Admin UI registry overview). Uses the same self-healing mechanism as any other self-registration.
- **Gateway rate limiting without a shared store** (ADR 0005): purely in-process, correct only for a single Gateway instance.
- **Backend service ports remain directly published**: no real network perimeter that makes backends reachable exclusively via the Gateway — a later deployment step.

### Frontend (Konzept 8)
- **Frontend apps under `apps/`, not `services/`** (ADR 0006): its own pattern (static export, `nginx`), since the Python service template doesn't fit. Two instances (`user-ui`, `admin-ui`) now confirm the pattern; `docs/frontend-template.md` might make sense with a third app.
- **Tokens in `localStorage` instead of an httpOnly cookie** (ADR 0006): deliberate XSS risk for both frontend apps — a harder model (session cookie via the Gateway) is a later, standalone step.
- **i18n only structurally prepared** (ADR 0007, since P4-S3): both apps have a dictionary/context skeleton, only German is active, no language switch in the UI.
- **No browser available in this development environment** (since P4-S2): no Chrome/Chromium installed, Playwright installation explicitly declined by the user. Every UI session instead verifies all Gateway calls individually via `curl` against the real running stack. **Confirmed to have occurred**: the first real browser test by the user after P4-S3 immediately uncovered a bug impossible to find with curl (missing CORS preflight handling in the Gateway, see below) as well as substantial UX/feature-scope feedback (→ P4-S4/S5/S6). Relevant for all future UI sessions (P4-S4, P4-S5, P4-S6, P6-S8, P14-S2). **P14-S2 addendum**: the limitation applied to the previous practice of not installing anything permanently in the repo/environment - for P14-S2, a purely **ephemeral** `mcr.microsoft.com/playwright` Docker container was used instead (no `npm install`, no new `package.json` dependency, no repo state) to actually click through both new frontend apps in a real browser - the same class of bug (Gateway CORS, this time a stale `infra/.env` origin list instead of missing preflight handling) was thereby found again before session completion instead of only at the next real user login.
- **User UI: folder management and document metadata editing added (P4-S4)**: `DocumentWorkspace` replaces the earlier flat `FolderBrowser` with the three-part layout (explorer with document tabs top left, metadata panel bottom left, preview right, all resizable via `Splitter`). Folder CRUD (create/rename/delete) uses the backend endpoints available since P3-S3, but had not previously been wired into the UI. Document metadata can now be edited via a new `PATCH /documents/{id}` (Document Service), form fields are generated dynamically from the object-type schema. Folder move (new parent folder) deliberately not wired into the UI — rename only, see `docs/services/user-ui.md`. **Discovered during this, not fixed**: `DELETE /folders/{id}` (Folder Service) only checks for subfolders, not contained documents — a folder containing only documents can be deleted without their `folder_id` being updated or the deletion being blocked. Python workspace now **263 tests** (Document Service: 6 new for the metadata endpoint), user-ui Vitest suite **17 tests**.
- **Admin UI: dashboard layout and multi-installation added (P4-S5)**: `AdminShell` now shows a left, grouped/collapsible navigation sidebar (`AdminSidebar`) instead of the earlier flat top-nav links. New installation management (`/installations/`, `InstallationProvider`) allows multiple installations each with its own gateway endpoint and its own session — switching without re-login as long as the respective session remains valid, no single sign-on across installation boundaries (see **ADR 0008**). Installation list is purely client-side (`localStorage`), no new backend service needed for it.
- **Cross-UI theming added (P4-S6)**: light/dark/high-contrast/auto in both apps, switchable via `ThemeSwitcher` (user-ui: popover in the `IconRail`, admin-ui: header). Stored across devices on the user account — as suspected, a Keycloak user attribute (`dms_theme`) via the Auth Service's admin client available since P4-S3, no new backend service needed (see **ADR 0009**). Found a Keycloak-25 trap along the way: the Declarative User Profile silently discards undeclared attributes on `update_user` — fixed via idempotent attribute declaration in the realm bootstrap.
- **Process Designer (BPMN) has been a standalone UI since P6-S8** (`apps/process-designer`, port 3002), not part of the Admin UI as originally described in the concept. **Single-installation like user-ui, deliberately not multi-installation like admin-ui** — explicit user decision at plan approval, see "Process Designer / process versioning" above. Save/delete are button-gated behind `admin.object_config` (read/open remains open to every authenticated principal), details in `docs/services/process-designer.md`. **Since P6-S9 additionally a federation properties-panel group** (visible only when the Federation Hub knows at least one installation, see "Federation Hub" above).
- **CORS gap in the Gateway found and fixed** (immediately after P4-S3, via a real browser login attempt by the user): the Gateway had no `CORSMiddleware`, preflight `OPTIONS` requests from the browser frontends failed with `405` before the actual request could even be sent — curl-based tests never trigger a preflight and missed this. Fix: `CORSMiddleware` with `cors_allowed_origins` (default: both frontend ports), 2 new tests. Python workspace now **257 tests**.

### Processing (Phase 5, Konzept 2.4/3.7/3.9/10.3) — found while deepening research before Phase 5 began
- **OCR confidence/BPMN dependency** (P5-S3, implemented, but still without a real workflow connection): 3.9 optionally envisages a manual review as a BPMN process step for low OCR confidence — the Workflow Engine only exists starting at P6-S1. `ocr-service` therefore only builds a confidence value + `needs_review` status/event (threshold `average_confidence < 70.0`), no real workflow connection; **still open**, to be caught up once the Workflow Engine (and the generic approval mechanism already planned for P6-S4 anyway) exists.
- **Renditions are persistent objects, not a cache** (2.4, implemented since P5-S2): the Rendering Service permanently stores every generated rendition/preview via the Storage Service (`renditions/{document_id}/{version_number}/{rendition_type}`), not as a Redis/in-memory cache.
- **Pipeline order vs. session order — caught up since P5-S3**: 3.9 envisages OCR before rendering/preview, but the sessions built rendering (P5-S2) before OCR (P5-S3). P5-S2 therefore deliberately limited itself to formats that don't need OCR (raster images, `.docx`, `.pptx`, `.pdf`); the catch-up effect already announced in P5-S2 was implemented in P5-S3 — rendering-service consumes `ocr.completed` and subsequently generates a `substitute_text` rendition from it for scanned/image-based documents (and, as a side effect, also for PDFs with a real text layer).
- **Rendering Service deliberately deviates from the concept's example text on two rules** (P5-S2, the same trade-off as ClamdEngine vs. EicarSignatureEngine in P5-S1): `.pptx` produces a `.txt` text extraction instead of the example-mentioned `.pdf` conversion (would need an external office-rendering component like LibreOffice headless, not available here); the PDF/A conversion is best-effort metadata tagging via `pypdf`, not an ISO-19005-validated conversion (would need Ghostscript/veraPDF). Both documented in `docs/services/rendering-service.md`.
- **Permission Service has had a batch-check endpoint since P5-S4** (`POST /check/batch`, see `docs/services/permission-service.md`): resolves exactly the necessity predicted in P5-S4 (filtering search results across many hits instead of many individual `GET /check` round trips). Search Service is so far the only caller and the first service in the whole system to actually enforce real read permissions (via the `X-DMS-Principal` header injected by the Gateway) — the overarching limitation "Gateway only checks token validity, no role check" continues to apply unchanged to all other services.
- **Object-Type Service still doesn't publish any events**: practically relevant since P5-S4, no longer just background fact — Search Service queries the attribute schema for facets/attribute filters synchronously over HTTP (`GET /object-types`/`GET /object-types/{id}`), a confirmed, already known limitation (see `docs/services/object-type-service.md`).
- **Audit-service coverage grows with Phase 5**: `virus_scan.>` since P5-S1, `rendering.>` since P5-S2, `ocr.>` since P5-S3 added — for OCR the auditing is even explicitly required in 3.9/5.3, not just "nice to have".
- **Virus-Scan Service: `ClamdEngine` implemented, but not wired in for production** (P5-S1, ADR 0010): the default engine is `EicarSignatureEngine` (detects only the standardized EICAR test signature, no real malware protection) — no `clamd` container in `infra/docker-compose.yml`, since the initial signature-database download takes minutes and requires reliable internet access. `ClamdEngine` can be activated via `DMS_SCAN_ENGINE=clamd` once a `clamd` is operated separately.
- **No release/delete workflow for quarantined objects** (P5-S1): infected files land permanently under `quarantine/{scan_id}` in the Storage Service, with no way to restore or permanently delete them via the API.
- **No video transcription plugin** (2.4, P5-S2): per the concept itself optional ("if available") — the Rendering Service has no rule/engine for this (yet), since no transcription engine exists.
- **No notification to the uploader on a virus finding** (P5-S1): `notification-service` has existed since P6-S2, but deliberately only consumes `workflow.task.escalated` (see "No retrofit of existing 'logs only' alerting spots" above) — `virus_scan.completed` is already published and could be wired up there without any change to the Virus-Scan Service, once that gets prioritized.

### Document / Folder / Object-Type Services
- **Force-unlock without a "monitored" lock state** (ADR 0002): optimistic version checking at check-in instead of a third lock state — covers the same guarantee more generally.
