For a later notification-service correlation between force-unlock and any resulting conflict copy, both `document.>` events would need to be linked client-side via `subject=document_id`.
- **Folder deletion checks only subfolders, not documents** (Folder Service, discovered at P4-S4): `DELETE /folders/{id}` only rejects when child folders exist (`FolderNotEmptyError`) — a folder that only contains documents can be deleted without their `folder_id` being handled. Not fixed, since outside the P4-S4 scope (user-ui layout); to be caught up once folder deletion is actively used from the UI.
- **No Alembic, ad-hoc schema migration**: additive, default-bearing columns via `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` in the lifespan startup. Works only additively — the next session with a breaking schema change should introduce Alembic instead of repeating this trick.
- **Constraint Engine as a lib, not a service** (ADR 0003): `type: reference` attributes are only formally checked (non-empty string), no existence check against the referenced service.
- **Granular cache invalidation Permission Service**: entire `effective_permission_cache` instead of just the affected subtree — deliberate simplification.
- **Audit-service coverage**: consumes `registry.>`, `document.>`, `permission.>`, `virus_scan.>` (P5-S1), `rendering.>` (P5-S2), `ocr.>` (P5-S3). Neither auth/storage nor folder/object-type events are part of the audit trail.

### Tooling & Testing (lessons from operations)
- **⚠️ CRITICAL: `uv run pytest` against a running, in-use Docker Compose stack deletes its real data** (discovered after P5-S2, direct data-loss incident): each service's `conftest.py` truncates its own tables before every test run (`TRUNCATE`/`DELETE FROM <schema>.<table>`) — by default against the same Postgres instance (`localhost:5432`) that the running Compose stack also uses, **not** against a separate test database. A `uv run pytest` (whether the full workspace or just a single service like `document-service`/`storage-service`) while the stack is actively in use (user is testing in the UI, "real" documents exist) **irrecoverably deletes this data** — the associated storage objects do remain on disk as anonymous, content-addressed files, but without the deleted metadata rows (title, folder, filename, object type) they can no longer be reconstructed. This is exactly what happened during the verification of P5-S2: several full `uv run pytest -q` runs against the live running stack emptied `document.document`/`document.document_version` and `storage.object_metadata`, irrecoverably losing all documents previously uploaded via the UI/curl. **Rule from now on**: before every `uv run pytest` invocation (whether the full workspace or a single service), make sure the stack is either (a) not currently in use with real/valuable data, or (b) explicitly check with the user when in doubt, or (c) `TEST_POSTGRES_DSN` explicitly points to an isolated throwaway database. **Partially fixed since P5c-S1**: every `conftest.py` now enforces `os.environ["DMS_POSTGRES_DSN"] = DSN` (where `DSN` is read from `TEST_POSTGRES_DSN`), so that `TestClient(app)`-based tests (e.g. `test_api.py`) are guaranteed to use the same DB as the other test fixtures — exactly the gap that caused the P5b-S6 incident (only `TEST_POSTGRES_DSN` set, `DMS_POSTGRES_DSN` unnoticed still pointing at the live DB) — is thereby closed and verified via a real test run against an isolated throwaway DB (76/76 `storage-service` tests green, live `dms` database demonstrably unchanged). **Still not fixed**: without an explicitly set `TEST_POSTGRES_DSN`, both variables still default to the same DB as the running stack — the P5-S2 failure case (test run without any isolation) remains possible, rules (a)/(b)/(c) above still apply unchanged.
- **`graphify --update` can trigger the shrink guard when file-change detection deviates strongly** (first observed at P5-S2): `detect_incremental` classified 252 of 320 files as changed, although only a fraction of them had actually been touched in this session — the graph variant merged from that had net fewer nodes than the existing graph; graphify correctly refused the write instead of losing content. Not overridden with `force=True`, since per `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` a `graphify --update` is mandatory only at phase end anyway — instead deferred to the next phase completion (full rebuild instead of incremental). On future occurrence: react first with a full, non-incremental run instead of forcing the guard.
- **Three interlocking graphify pitfalls at the P5d-S2 phase boundary, all solved**: (1) `detect_incremental` again reported an implausible mass change (392 of 392 files) — `check_semantic_cache` was consulted instead, which correctly identified only 8 actually changed documents (content-hash-based, not mtime-based). (2) Semantic subagents wrote `source_file` per the standard prompt as an **absolute** path (`/E/Workspaces/.../dms/...`), while `extract()`'s AST path already delivers **relative**, `dms/`-prefixed paths (via `cache_root`) — `build_from_json(root='.')` doesn't retroactively relativize `source_file`, it takes over the value unchanged from the extraction. Without manual correction, two unmerged node sets arise for every doc file (old `dms/...` nodes + new absolute-path nodes). **Rule from now on**: after every subagent dispatch round, programmatically rewrite `source_file` in nodes/edges/hyperedges to the `dms/`-relative path (strip the prefix `/E/Workspaces/VSCode/mini-Files-Experiments/`) before merging. (3) **The actual core error**: a `build_from_json()` (complete rebuild) on a fresh single-pass full extraction of all 66 documents can structurally never match a graph accumulated over 15+ sessions, even with an explicit "full thoroughness, no diff extraction" instruction — every single file ended up at 20–80% of the historical node count (e.g. every ADR file uniformly 5→1), because a single subagent extraction never reaches the same cumulative depth as many independent sessions each discovering new facets of the same file. **The shrink guard thereby correctly prevented a categorically wrong strategy, not an extraction detail.** The correct solution: **`build_merge()`** (as in the normal `--update` flow) with the full AST re-extraction (deterministic, no accumulation problem) **plus** a targeted re-extraction limited to only the 8 actually changed documents as the `new_extraction` list, **without** `prune_sources` — `build_merge`'s automatic replace-on-re-extract replaces the old nodes per `source_file` in the new extraction, all 58 untouched documents as well as their accumulated historical depth remain untouched. Result: net +342 nodes (3031→3373) instead of a net loss. **Side effect/open point**: the first (discarded) full extraction was accidentally written into the cache via `save_semantic_cache` and thereby overwrote the cache entries for 58 unchanged documents with thinner versions — doesn't affect `graph.json` (unchanged, correct), but a future `--update` for one of these 58 documents might be served from the now-thinner cache instead of a fresh extraction. Not fixed (rebuilding the cache for all 58 would be disproportionately expensive) — keep this cause in mind for visibly thin community labels for older doc files in a future session.
- **Package manager `uv`**: `uv sync --all-packages` as the standard command for the entire workspace (single-package sync via `--package` otherwise removes dev dependencies from the venv).
- **pytest `--import-mode=importlib`**: identically named test modules (`test_repository.py`) across multiple services otherwise collide. Consequence: no test helpers via `from conftest import x` (doesn't work with importlib mode) — define as fixtures instead.
- **Pattern lesson `session.delete()` without `session.flush()`**: a subsequent `session.get()` on the same primary key still returns the object despite `delete()`, if no flush happened in between (identity-map fast path) — check this for future repository functions with delete+re-read in the same test.
- **Recurring NATS hang on `uv run pytest` after Docker Compose restarts**: observed across several sessions (P3-S3, P4-S1, P4-S3) — a full test run occasionally hangs indefinitely. Reliable fix: kill the hanging process, then `docker compose restart nats` (drops active client bindings, JetStream state on the named volume is preserved), after which the test run proceeds normally. Not a root-cause fix, but an established, repeatedly successful procedure.
- **Related but different NATS trap (newly observed at P4-S4)**: if the full `uv run pytest` runs while the Docker Compose stack is running (e.g. for parallel curl verification), `permission-service` and `audit-service` consumer tests fail with `nats: JetStream.Error consumer is already bound to a subscription` — the respective container already holds the same durable consumer name that the local test run also claims at its own app lifespan start. Not a bug in the services themselves (isolated per-service, the tests run green, see P4-S4 verification of `document-service`/`folder-service`/`object-type-service`) — workaround: stop the affected containers before the full test run, or only test the actually changed services specifically.
- **NATS is shared infrastructure across all services/test runs — independent of `TEST_POSTGRES_DSN` isolation** (discovered at P6-S4): every test run connects to the same real NATS server (`localhost:4222`) as the running Docker Compose stack, even when the associated Postgres database is cleanly isolated. A test run that actually publishes an event (not just feeds it to a handler in a simulated way) publishes it **for real** — any other live-running container at that time that consumes the same subject actually processes it, with test data/payloads that aren't necessarily sensible for the live container. Concretely at P6-S4: `permission-service`'s own API test for the generic approval endpoint (`action_type="document.force_unlock"`, test payload `{"x": 1}`) published a real `permission.approval.approved`, which the concurrently running `document-service` container consumed and crashed on with a `KeyError` at a naive `payload["document_id"]` access — the NATS message thereby stayed unacknowledged and would have been redelivered endlessly. **Rule from now on**: (a) every event consumer must read its payload defensively with `.get(...)` instead of `[...]` and log missing/malformed fields instead of throwing an exception (otherwise the message stays stuck in the retry loop forever); (b) when testing a service that publishes real events on a subject that another *live-running* service consumes, either temporarily stop that consumer container or explicitly plan for the result of the cross-effect — the same caution as with the already known Postgres data-loss case above, just for the event bus instead of the database.
- **Keycloak is also shared infrastructure across all services/test runs — just like NATS above, but with an additional side-effect chain** (discovered at P6-S5): there's no separate test realm; every test run of `auth-service`/`permission-service` talks to the same real Keycloak realm `dms` as the running Docker Compose stack. A test that plays through a real break-glass approval (`permission-service`'s `test_break_glass_activation_via_api_with_two_group_members`) thereby publishes a real `permission.approval.approved` (same NATS trap as above) — the concurrently running `auth-service` container consumes it and thereby **actually** activates the real Keycloak superuser account, which in turn triggers **real** email notifications to `notification-service` and produces **real** `auth.>` entries in the audit trail. Ten real "superuser break-glass activated" notifications from previous test runs were found during this session's live verification run (account was already `enabled=true` before the actual smoke test had even started). **No correction needed** (no security gap, since the only prerequisite "two test principals equipped with `breakglass.approve`" only exists because of the test run itself and is deleted again afterward) — but the same rule as with NATS applies analogously: whoever tests an action type with real side effects (account activation, email sending) via the approval mechanism should be aware that this *really* happens, not just gets simulated, as soon as a live-running consumer is running along.
- **`scripts/run-tests.sh` now coordinates all the above steps automatically** (built after P6-S4, at the user's request, to reduce the manual testing effort per session): brings up the full Docker stack, creates `dms_test` if needed, **always** explicitly exports `TEST_POSTGRES_DSN` to `dms_test` (closes the above-described default-to-live-DB gap for every test run going through the script), stops each of the nine services with its own NATS consumer (`audit-`, `auth-` [since P6-S5], `case-`, `document-`, `notification-`, `ocr-`, `permission-`, `rendering-`, `search-service`) specifically before the test run and restarts it afterward — all other containers stay live for cross-service HTTP calls —, and at the end summarizes all service results plus `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` in a table (logs under `.test-results/`). On the first full run across all 16 services (428 tests), directly found real, pre-existing lint drift in 8 files (unsorted imports, unformatted lines) — fixed with `ruff check --fix`/`ruff format`, clean throughout since then. Invocation: `scripts/run-tests.sh [service ...] [--build] [--no-ruff] [--down]`, without arguments all services with `tests/` run. Doesn't fix the fundamental NATS cross-service trap directly above (that remains architecturally in place, see the defensive `.get(...)` rule), but reduces the risk of accidentally triggering it, since the script already temporarily stops the respective affected consumer container anyway.
- **Root cause of the "related but different NATS trap" workaround from P4-S4 (above) finally found and fixed (P15-S1)**: the workaround there (stop the affected container before the test run) has been automated since `scripts/run-tests.sh` (P6-S4) (`CONSUMER_SERVICES`), but `consumer is already bound to a subscription` still occurred intermittently nonetheless (most recently at P6-S8's full test run, see below, and again at P15-S1's `--build` run against `permission-service`). Actual cause: `dms_eventbus_client.NatsEventBusClient.close()` called `nc.close()` instead of `nc.drain()` — the former disconnects immediately, without cleanly unsubscribing active durable-consumer subscriptions server-side, so the binding briefly remains in place server-side even after a successful `docker compose stop` (a purely NATS-server-side timing issue, not a bug in the stop mechanism itself). Fixed in the shared library (`close()` now calls `drain()`, explicitly unsubscribes every subscription before the connection closes, 30s timeout against a hanging shutdown) — fixes the race at the root for all `CONSUMER_SERVICES` alike, not just a workaround around it. Verified via two consecutive, cleanly green runs of all 13 consumer services together.
- **`webdav-connector` tests react sensitively to general system load, not just root object count (again observed at P14-S10)**: already documented as a "pure performance finding" in an earlier session (accumulated root test data → `httpx.ReadTimeout`, see above). Recurred at P14-S10, this time WITHOUT a notable root object count (only 1 document/1 folder under `root` at the time of checking) — occurred directly after a full `scripts/run-tests.sh --build` run across all 32 services and worsened on repeated isolated re-runs (4→5→6 failed tests), all exclusively `ReadTimeout` (no assertion failures). `webdav-connector` itself was **not** touched in this session (verified via `git diff`) — no code connection to the public share link (4.2a). Not fixed (outside the session scope) — on future occurrence: test `webdav-connector` in isolation (not as part of a `--build` full regression run) and after a brief system settling pause before assuming a real regression bug.
- **`graphify` respects `.gitignore` during file scanning** (discovered during the `graphify --update` after P5-S4): `.gitignore` deliberately excludes `Business__DMS-Konzept.md`/`CLAUDE.md` from git tracking (section "Plan") — the same rule, however, also causes graphify's `detect()`/`detect_incremental()` to never see these two files, and an `--update` run wrongly reports them as "deleted", even though they remain unchanged, central reference documents of the project (the very first graph in P0-S1 was built exactly around `Business__DMS-Konzept.md`). **Rule from now on**: for every future `graphify --update`, check the reported `deleted_files` list against the actual file-system state before `prune_sources` is set — a file excluded via `.gitignore` but still present must not be pruned from the graph. Open point: evaluate a permanent solution (e.g. a graphify include-override option) so this manual check isn't needed on every future update.

## Changelog

- **P0-S1**: Git repo initialized in `dms/` (branch `main`). Monorepo skeleton created (`services/`, `libs/`, `infra/`, `tools/cli/`, `docs/adr/`, `docs/services/`). `README.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `.gitignore` written. `infra/docker-compose.yml` with Postgres 16, NATS 2.10 (JetStream), Keycloak 25.0, MinIO created and **verified** (`docker compose up` → all four containers healthy, Keycloak successfully migrated against its own Postgres schema), then shut down again. Ran `graphify` over the concept document, ran `graphify claude install` + `graphify hook install`. Next step: P0-S2 (shared libs + service template + CI).
- **P0-S2**: uv workspace root (`pyproject.toml`, `.python-version`, Ruff config including `ignore = ["B008"]` for FastAPI's `Depends(...)` idiom) created. Four shared libs built, each with a README + **real** tests (16 tests total, all green):
  - `dms-common`: `BaseServiceSettings`, JSON logging, OTel tracing base.
  - `dms-db-base`: async engine, `make_declarative_base(schema)`, session scope — integration test against real Postgres (Docker).
  - `dms-eventbus-client`: `EventBusClient` interface + `NatsEventBusClient` — integration test against real NATS JetStream (Docker), including a publish/subscribe roundtrip.
  - `dms-auth-client`: `TokenValidator` (JWKS/RS256) + FastAPI dependency — unit tests with a self-signed test key (no real Keycloak needed).
  - Docker test infrastructure shut down again after every lib test.
  `docs/service-template.md` written (layout, `pyproject.toml`, `main.py`, Dockerfile) — **convention established**: Docker images copy `libs/` from the monorepo and install locally via `uv sync --frozen --package <name>` (no internal package index, full reproducibility via `uv.lock`), build context is the repo root. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (Postgres as a service container, NATS started separately due to the JetStream flag, `ruff check .` + `pytest` across the whole workspace) — run through locally, not verified on real GitHub Actions. `graphify dms/ --update`: 167 nodes/209 edges/31 communities (concept-document nodes pruned as planned, since `Business__DMS-Konzept.md`/`CLAUDE.md` are now gitignored). A self-loop edge in the graph noted as a minor health warning, non-critical. Next step: P1-S1 (Registry Service).
- **P1-S1**: `services/registry-service` built (first real service, following the `docs/service-template.md` pattern): FastAPI app, own Postgres schema `registry` (table `service_instance`), endpoints `POST /instances` (register/upsert), `POST /instances/{id}/heartbeat`, `DELETE /instances/{id}`, `GET /instances/{service_type}` (active routing table), `GET /instances`, `/healthz`. **Design decision**: failure detection is computed on read (`last_heartbeat_at` vs. `heartbeat_timeout_seconds`, default 15s) instead of via a mutating background sweep — avoids race conditions, easier to test, functionally equivalent to "drops out of the routing table". License brokering (3.2b) deliberately not yet included, follows with the License Service (Phase 9). 14 tests (repository + API, against real Postgres) green. Dockerfile built following the service template and **verified**: image built, brought up in the Compose stack with Postgres, end-to-end smoke test via `curl` (register → routing-table query) successful, then shut down. `docs/services/registry-service.md` created. Entire workspace (30 tests across 4 libs + Registry Service) runs green, lint clean. `graphify --update` deliberately not yet run (per plan only mandatory at the end of the phase, i.e. after P1-S2). Next step: P1-S2 (event bus in production + Audit Service).
- **P1-S2**: `dms-eventbus-client` extended: `Event` envelope (shared wire format, `to_bytes()`/`from_bytes()`) as well as **ADR 0001** — `NatsEventBusClient` now distinguishes producers (`ensure_stream=True`, default) from pure consumers (`ensure_stream=False`, no own stream needed, JetStream resolves the stream via the subject), plus a `deliver_new` flag for subscriptions that don't want message history (needed for clean test isolation). Registry Service now publishes `registry.instance.registered`/`registry.instance.deregistered` after commit (no event per heartbeat). New **Audit Service** (`services/audit-service`): hash-chained event log (`sha256(prev_hash + canonical_json(...))`, genesis hash at the first entry), idempotent by `event_id` (at-least-once redelivery via JetStream), consumes configurable subjects (default `["registry.>"]`) via a durable consumer without its own stream, API `GET /events`, `GET /events/verify` (chain check, detects tampering, tested to prove it). 33 new tests (hash-chain unit tests, repository including tamper detection, consumer integration against real NATS+Postgres, registry event-publishing tests) — entire workspace now **48 tests, all green**. **Incident & fix**: `pytest` collision between identically named test modules (`test_repository.py`) across multiple services → `--import-mode=importlib` set in the root `pyproject.toml`, for which `dms-auth-client`'s test helpers had to be switched from module import to fixtures. **Incident & fix**: end-to-end smoke test in the Compose stack initially showed no events, since `docker compose up -d` had not rebuilt the Registry Service image from P1-S1 (old code without event publishing was still running) — lesson recorded in `CONTRIBUTING.md`: after code changes, always explicitly `docker compose build <service>` before the smoke test. After the fix: end-to-end verified in the Compose stack (registration → event published → Audit Service consumes and chains it → `/events/verify` → `ok: true`). `docs/services/audit-service.md`, READMEs updated. `graphify --update` (phase completion P1, mandatory): 327 nodes/454 edges/47 communities. → **Phase 1 (control-flow foundation) completed.** Next step: P2-S1 (Keycloak + Auth Service).
- **P2-S1**: New **Auth Service** (`services/auth-service`) as a lean OIDC broker in front of Keycloak (4.4) — no own Postgres schema, no own user table (Keycloak already natively covers local + AD-federated accounts). `ensure_realm_and_client()` idempotently creates realm `dms` and confidential client `dms-api` on every start (`python-keycloak`/`KeycloakAdmin`), including an audience mapper (without it, the access token only carries `aud: "account"`, not the app's own client name — Keycloak default). Endpoints `POST /login` (password grant), `POST /refresh`, `GET /me` (token validation via `dms-auth-client`'s `TokenValidator` against Keycloak's JWKS, stateless), `/healthz`. **Two Keycloak traps found through interactive probing** (verified against real Keycloak directly before writing the tests, not guessed): (1) new users without `firstName`/`lastName` trigger "Account is not fully set up" at login, since Keycloak 25's default User Profile requires these fields (`VERIFY_PROFILE`); (2) access tokens carry only `aud: "account"` instead of the app's own client name without an explicit audience mapper. Both traps documented in `docs/services/auth-service.md` and `README.md`. Keycloak got a real health check (no curl/wget in the image → raw HTTP check via `bash /dev/tcp` against `/realms/master`), needed for the Auth Service's `depends_on: condition: service_healthy`. 9 new tests (bootstrap idempotency, login/refresh/me flows against real Keycloak) — entire workspace now **57 tests, all green**. End-to-end verified in the Compose stack (login → access token → `/me` returns correct identity including Keycloak roles). AD group→internal-role mapping deliberately not implemented, deferred to the Permission Service (P2-S2). `graphify --update` deliberately not yet run (Phase 2 not yet complete). Next step: P2-S2 (Permission Service).
- **P2-S2**: New **Permission Service** (`services/permission-service`, Konzept 4.1): RBAC with `Role` (name + `permissions` capability list), `RoleAssignment` (principal × role × resource), `ResourceNode` (generic hierarchy, `parent_id` + `inherit` flag), `EffectivePermissionCache` (materialized). **Inheritance algorithm**: ascends from the requested resource via `parent_id` up to the root (`root`, automatically created at start), collecting assignments at each node; `inherit=false` ends the ascent *after* evaluating the node's own assignments (SharePoint/Alfresco default behavior). Cache invalidation deliberately coarse-grained (entire cache instead of just the affected subtree) — simpler, correct, documented simplification. **Architecture challenge**: the Folder Service (P3-S3), which will actually own the resource hierarchy, doesn't exist yet — Permission Service therefore defines a **provisional structural-event contract** (`folder.resource.created/.moved/.deleted`, documented in `docs/services/permission-service.md`) and already consumes it via `structure_consumer.py`, verified via synthetically published events (simulating the role of a non-existent "Folder Service"). **Incident & fix (lib extension)**: the service initially failed to start with `NotFoundError`, because `ensure_stream=False` consumers (ADR 0001) fail on subscribing if no producer has ever created the stream (no Folder Service has ever run) — `dms-eventbus-client` got its own, backend-independent `SubjectNotFoundError` for this (translated from NATS' `NotFoundError`), and `start_consuming()` skips affected subjects instead of blocking service startup (no retry loop, restart needed once the stream appears later — documented limitation). 24 new tests (repository including inheritance/override/cache-invalidation, structure-consumer integration against real NATS, API) plus 1 new test in `dms-eventbus-client` for the new exception — entire workspace now **76 tests, all green**. End-to-end verified in the Compose stack: synthetic `folder.resource.created` event → resource created → role assigned at root → correctly inherited at the sub-resource → `/check` returns `allowed: true`. `graphify --update` (phase completion P2, mandatory): 480 nodes/749 edges/50 communities. → **Phase 2 (Auth & Permissions) completed.** Next step: P3-S1 (Storage Service + backend plugin interface).
- **P3-S1**: New **Storage Service** (`services/storage-service`, Konzept 3.6): `StorageBackend` interface (`write`/`read`/`delete`/`exists`/`checksum`) with two implementations. **`LocalFilesystemBackend`** deliberately covers both "local filesystem" and **NFS** — in Kubernetes the configured path is simply the mount point of a PVC, the backend only sees a folder regardless of what lies beneath it (user hint picked up in this session, no separate NFS backend built). Writes atomically (temp file + `os.replace`) instead of `fcntl` locking, whose semantics are inconsistent across NFS implementations. **`S3Backend`** (`aioboto3`) tested against MinIO, works identically against any S3-compatible provider. Metadata (`object_key`, `backend`, `checksum_sha256`, `size_bytes`, `content_type`) in its own schema `storage`; content itself never in the DB. API: `PUT`/`GET`/`DELETE /objects/{key:path}`, `GET /object-metadata/{key:path}`, `GET /object-verify/{key:path}` (fixity-check basis: re-read the checksum from the backend, compare with the DB reference value). Storage redundancy (multiple targets, quorum writes) deliberately not part of this session, follows in P3-S4. **Incident & fix**: `repository.delete_metadata` was missing a `session.flush()` after `session.delete()` — without a flush, a subsequent `session.get()` on the same primary key still returned the object (identity-map fast path bypasses the query), a test uncovered this. 46 new tests (local backend against real filesystem, S3 backend against real MinIO, repository against real Postgres, API end-to-end) — entire workspace now **106 tests, all green**. **End-to-end verified in the Compose stack for BOTH backends** (explicitly required by the user): upload/download/verify via the real HTTP API first with the `local` backend (file confirmed in the mounted volume), then the same container restarted with `DMS_BACKEND=s3` and the same flow repeated against MinIO (object confirmed in the MinIO data directory). `docs/services/storage-service.md` created. `graphify --update` deliberately not yet run (Phase 3 not yet complete). Next step: P3-S2 (Document Service).
- **P3-S2**: New **Document Service** (`services/document-service`, Konzept 2.1/2.1a/4.2): `Document` (metadata + `current_version_number` pointer), `DocumentVersion` (every version remains retrievable forever, 2.1a — including conflict copies), `DocumentLock` (bound to user+session, expiry is evaluated on access instead of via a background sweep, analogous to the Registry Service). Holds no file content itself — talks to the Storage Service (P3-S1) exclusively via its HTTP API, object keys are content-addressed (`documents/{id}/{sha256}`), which avoids the chicken-and-egg ordering "key needs a version number, version number needs a completed DB write" and deduplicates identical content. **Architecture decision (ADR 0002)**: the Konzept describes force-unlock via a third lock state ("released, but monitored"); instead, force-unlock deletes the lock entirely, and the same protection against silent data loss arises from an always-active **optimistic conflict detection** at check-in (`expected_base_version_number`): if the given base version deviates from the current main version, a standalone conflict copy is created (`<name>_conflict_<user>_<timestamp>`) instead of a silent overwrite — covers the same and more general cases (e.g. a race even without any lock) with a single mechanism instead of two. API: upload/get/delete, version list/content, lock/unlock/force-release, publishes `document.created`/`.version.created`/`.lock.force_released`/`.deleted`. **4.2 explicitly requires full auditing of force-unlock** — the Audit Service therefore consumes `document.>` from this session on (previously only `registry.>`). **Incident & fix (Audit Service)**: the second consumed subject uncovered a real concurrency bug — NATS callbacks for `registry.>` and `document.>` can run simultaneously, but `append_event` read the chain head (`prev_hash`) without serialization, so two parallel appends could read the same predecessor hash and corrupt the chain (uncovered by a previously stable test that suddenly failed, because already-accumulated `document.*` messages were processed in parallel with the injected test event during the test run). Fix: `audit_service/consumer.py` serializes all `append_event` calls via an in-process `asyncio.Lock` per handler instance (Audit Service is designed as a single writer for its own chain, no horizontal scaling planned). 25 new tests in the Document Service (repository including lock-conflict/force-unlock/conflict-copy scenarios against real Postgres, API end-to-end against real Storage Service over HTTP, event publishing against real NATS) — entire workspace now **131 tests, all green**. End-to-end verified in the Compose stack: upload → lock → force-unlock (admin) → Bob's regular check-in becomes the new main version → Alice's check-in based on the superseded version visibly lands as a conflict copy, main version stays Bob's state → Audit Service shows both events (`lock.force_released` + `version.created` with `is_conflict: true`) in the `/events` trail, `/events/verify` confirms `ok: true` across the mixed, concurrently arrived event history. `docs/services/document-service.md`, `docs/adr/0002-document-locking-optimistic-conflict-detection.md`, READMEs (Document Service + Audit Service) created/updated. `graphify --update` deliberately not yet run (Phase 3 not yet complete). Next step: P3-S3 (Folder Service + Object-Type Service + Constraint Engine).
- **P3-S4**: Last session of Phase 3 (Konzept 3.6/4.7). **Storage Service extended** with redundancy: `object_copy` table (one row per object × configured target, status `pending`/`ok`/`failed`/`failed_permanent`), new `replication.py` orchestration, backend-agnostic (`dict[str, StorageBackend]` + `list[str]` target priority), testable independently of the FastAPI singleton configuration. Two write strategies: `quorum` (synchronous to all targets, success only once `quorum_count` confirmations are reached; if not reached, already successful partial copies are best-effort rolled back, failed targets remain as a diagnostic entry) and `primary_async` (default setting for production operation: only the primary target synchronous, secondary target `pending`, caught up via the new endpoint `POST /replication/process-pending` — a retry queue instead of an in-process background task). Read fallback (`GET /objects/{key}` tries targets in priority order, skips non-`ok` copies) and fixity check across all copies (`GET /object-verify/{key}/all`, updates `object_copy` on checksum mismatch) round out the base required by the concept. **Architecture decision (ADR 0004)**: redundancy deliberately limited to exactly two targets (`local`+`s3`, the only existing backend types) instead of a generic target set, and the retry queue deliberately via an explicit, deterministically testable endpoint instead of a timing-dependent in-process background task — both extensively reasoned including a migration path to real multi-target configuration. **Permission Service extended** with scope locks (4.7): new `ScopeLock` table (never hard-deleted, release is documented), `repository.get_active_scope_locks_for_resource` walks the same ancestor chain as the RBAC evaluation, but does **not** stop at `inherit=false` (scope locks always apply to the entire subtree, independent of the RBAC inheritance control). `GET /check` got an `access_type` parameter (`read`|`write`, default `write`) and blocks on an active lock regardless of the actually assigned rights (`blocked_by_scope_lock`, `scope_lock_reason`, `scope_lock_expires_at` in the response) — except for a principal that has the newly introduced capability `scope_lock.bypass`. Setting/releasing publishes `permission.scope_lock.created`/`.released` via a new producer client separate from the existing structure consumer (own stream `permission`) — the **Audit Service consumes `permission.>` additionally from this session on** (4.7 explicitly requires full auditing). As with the Document Service force-unlock (P3-S2), the management endpoints themselves remain ungated, real authorization follows at API Gateway level (P4-S1). 51 new tests (storage-service repository/replication/API including real error paths via a deliberately always-failing but real backend-interface implementation instead of mocking; permission-service repository/API/events against real Postgres+NATS) — entire workspace now **228 tests, all green**. End-to-end verified in the Compose stack: quorum writes over a real local filesystem **and** real MinIO simultaneously (both copies `ok`, fixity check confirms both checksums) → switch to `primary_async` → secondary copy initially `pending`, download already works via the primary copy, `POST /replication/process-pending` catches up the MinIO copy (`succeeded: 1`) → Document Service continues working transparently against the redundant Storage Service, no code change needed → scope lock at the root node blocks write access with reason in the response, read access remains allowed (since `blocks_read=false`), immediately back to the original state after release → Audit Service (freshly started, without `deliver_new`) catches up the complete `permission.>` history without gaps, `/events/verify` confirms `ok: true` across 55 mixed events from live testing and previous pytest runs. `docs/services/{storage,permission,audit}-service.md`, `docs/adr/0004-storage-redundancy-scope.md`, all affected READMEs, `infra/docker-compose.yml`/`.env.example` updated. → **Phase 3 (Storage & document core) completed.** `graphify dms/ --update` (phase completion, mandatory) run afterward. Next step: P4-S1 (API Gateway/BFF) — first session of Phase 4, ending with the MVP milestone (P4-S3).
- **P3-S3**: Three new building blocks (Konzept 2.1/2.2/4.5). **`libs/dms-constraint-engine`**: pure, stateless validation function (`schema, name, attributes -> errors`) — required fields, conditional required fields (restrictive `attr op literal` parser, no `eval()`), pattern checking (regex) for values and names (placeholders like `{Rechnungsnummer}` are replaced by the actual attribute value), value ranges (`min`/`max`) — the minimum rule types required in 4.5. **New Object-Type Service**: persists object-type definitions (2.2) and offers `POST /object-types/{id}/validate` as the only consumer of the lib. **Architecture decision (ADR 0003)**: the "Constraint Engine" was deliberately not built as its own microservice, but embedded as a lib in the Object-Type Service — a pure function without its own state/scaling needs, a service just for stateless validation would have offered no architectural advantage. **New Folder Service**: folder hierarchy (2.1) with CRUD, move (with self-reference protection), delete only if empty (409 otherwise), optional object-type validation on creation (calls the Object-Type Service over HTTP). Publishes exactly the structural event contract that the Permission Service had provisionally expected since P2-S2 (`folder.resource.created/.moved/.deleted`) — **no adjustment needed**, verified live end-to-end: a folder created via the real Folder Service API appears immediately in the Permission Service's `resource_node` tree with the correct `parent_id`. Permission-service documentation updated from "provisional" to "confirmed". **Document Service extended**: new column `attributes` (JSON, custom fields per object type); `folder_id` is now checked for existence against the Folder Service on creation (400 for unknown ID), `object_type_id`+`attributes` validated against the Object-Type Service (400 with error list on violation) — both checks are skipped if the respective field isn't set. Object keys remain content-addressed, no change to the storage integration. **Incident & fix (schema evolution without Alembic)**: the new `attributes` column on the already existing `document` table (from P3-S2, Postgres volume persisted between sessions) wasn't created by `create_all` (only creates missing tables, not missing columns) — fixed with an idempotent `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS ... DEFAULT ... NOT NULL` in the lifespan startup; recorded as a limitation of this ad-hoc approach in "Open Decisions" (Alembic will become necessary once a change is more complex than additive+default-bearing). 61 new tests (constraint-engine lib, Object-Type Service repository+API, Folder Service repository+API+events+object-type integration against a real Object-Type Service, Document Service metadata integration against real Folder/Object-Type Services) — entire workspace now **187 tests, all green**. End-to-end verified in the Compose stack: object type with a conditional required field created → document upload without the required field correctly rejected with 400 → accepted with complete attributes → folder appears live in the Permission Service → audit trail shows all `document.>` events, chain remains verified across 60 entries (`ok: true`). `docs/services/{object-type,folder}-service.md`, `docs/adr/0003-constraint-engine-as-library.md`, all affected READMEs updated. `graphify --update` deliberately not yet run (Phase 3 not yet complete, follows after P3-S4). Next step: P3-S4 (storage redundancy + scope locks) — **last session before Phase-3 completion and the mandatory `graphify --update`**.
- **P4-S1**: First session of Phase 4 (Konzept 3.5, MVP vertical). New **gateway-service**: stateless API gateway/BFF — central bearer-token validation (JWT against Keycloak JWKS, as in the Auth Service), in-process sliding-window rate limiting per client (`sub` claim or IP), and dynamic routing (`ANY /api/{service_type}/{path:path}`) via the Registry instead of static address lists. On successful auth check, identity claims are forwarded as `X-DMS-Principal`/`X-DMS-Username`/`X-DMS-Roles` headers to the downstream (not yet consumed by any backend service). Public routes (`auth-service:login`/`auth-service:refresh`) are exempt from the auth check, but still subject to rate limiting. **Prerequisite for this**: the Registry (built since P1-S1, but until now without a single caller) got its first real purpose — a new shared lib **`libs/dms-registry-client`** (register-on-start, periodic heartbeat, deregister-on-shutdown, errors reaching the Registry are logged instead of blocking service startup) was wired into seven backend services (auth-/permission-/storage-/document-/object-type-/folder-/audit-service). **Architecture decision (ADR 0005)**: two deliberate scope boundaries documented — rate limiting purely in-process (no Redis, since only one Gateway instance is planned) and backend services still don't validate tokens themselves (implicit trust that they're only reachable via the Gateway; a real network perimeter is a later deployment step). **Incident & fix (self-healing of a failed initial registration)**: in the first Docker Compose smoke test, `object-type-service` started faster than `registry-service` was reachable — the very first registration failed, and the service then heartbeated permanently against a never-existing instance (`404`), never appearing in the routing table. Fix in `dms-registry-client`: a heartbeat that gets back a `404` immediately re-registers itself fully (upsert) — covers both "never successfully registered" and "Registry has forgotten the instance in the meantime". New test (`test_heartbeat_reregisters_after_registry_forgot_instance`) covers exactly this scenario. 17 new tests (`dms-registry-client`: 7, including the self-healing test; `gateway-service`: 10, including rate-limiter unit tests without infrastructure needs and API tests against a real running Registry/real running Audit Service as a real proxy target, JWT check via real but locally generated test keys instead of a real Keycloak, analogous to `dms-auth-client`'s own test strategy) — entire workspace now **245 tests, all green**. End-to-end verified in the Compose stack with all 13 containers (including a real Keycloak test user): login via `POST /api/auth-service/login` (public route, no token needed) → real access token → `GET /api/permission-service/healthz` with token returns `200` (Registry resolution + proxy work live) → without/with an invalid token `401` → unknown `service_type` `503` → rate limit lowered to 3 requests/minute for testing, fourth request returns `429` live, then the limit was reset. `docs/services/gateway-service.md`, `docs/adr/0005-gateway-registry-routing-and-inprocess-rate-limiting.md`, all seven affected service docs/READMEs (new "self-registration" section), `libs/README.md` (added missing entries for `dms-constraint-engine`/`dms-registry-client`), `infra/docker-compose.yml`/`.env.example` updated. `graphify --update` deliberately not yet run (Phase 4 not yet complete, follows after P4-S3). Next step: P4-S2 (user-ui base skeleton: login, navigation, upload/download, preview stub).
- **P4-S2**: Second session of Phase 4 (Konzept 8). First frontend application of the project: **`apps/user-ui`** (Next.js/TypeScript, `output: "export"` — pure client-side rendering as fixed in the concept, no Node process at runtime, delivery via `nginx` in the production image). **Architecture decision (ADR 0006)**: frontend apps live under a new `apps/` folder instead of `services/` (the Python service template doesn't fit Node/React), tokens live in `localStorage` (deliberate, documented XSS trade-off decision for a serverless SPA). Built: login page (`POST /api/auth-service/login`), `AuthProvider` context with proactive token refresh before expiry and session restoration from `localStorage`, `RequireAuth` route protection (client-side, since static export has no middleware equivalent), `FolderBrowser` (breadcrumb navigation via `folder-service`, upload/download via `document-service`, each via the Gateway), `PreviewStub` (placeholder, since the Rendering/Preview Service only comes in P5-S2). **Backend gap closed**: the Document Service had no endpoint to list documents of a folder (only individual retrieval by ID) — mandatory for a working navigation, so `GET /documents?folder_id=...` added (opaque foreign reference as everywhere else in this service: unknown folder returns `[]`, no 404). **Known limitation of this environment**: no browser available (no Chrome/Chromium installed, user has explicitly declined a Playwright installation) — real browser E2E was thus not feasible. Instead, every Gateway call used by the UI was individually retraced via `curl` against the real running Compose stack: login with a real Keycloak test user → `/me` returns identity → create a subfolder and see it in the navigation → upload a document → the list shows it → download returns exactly the uploaded bytes back — every step successful, but without visual confirmation in the browser (communicated to the user as an open point). 3 new Python tests (document listing endpoint) — Python workspace now **248 tests, all green**. Additionally 12 new Vitest component/unit tests (`AuthProvider`, API client, `FolderBrowser`) — the network layer (`fetch`) mocked, since it's the boundary to external infrastructure (analogous to `dms-auth-client`'s local test keys). `.github/workflows/ci.yml` extended by a standalone `user-ui` job (typecheck/lint/test/build). `docs/services/user-ui.md`, `docs/adr/0006-user-ui-static-export-spa.md`, `apps/user-ui/README.md`, `docs/services/document-service.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md` (note on the deviating frontend pattern), root `README.md`, `infra/docker-compose.yml`/`.env.example` (new `user-ui` container, port 3000) updated. `graphify --update` deliberately not yet run (Phase 4 not yet complete, follows after P4-S3). Next step: P4-S3 (admin-ui base skeleton + doc/ADR pass — **MVP milestone**).
- **P4-S3**: Last session of Phase 4 (Konzept 8) — **MVP milestone: runnable core DMS**. New **`apps/admin-ui`**, identical pattern to user-ui (Next.js/static export/nginx, ADR 0006): three areas — user/role management (`/users/`, including role assignments), object-type editor (`/object-types/`, attributes as a JSON list), registry overview (`/registry/`, all registered instances including health status). **Two backend gaps closed**, which became visible while building the admin-ui pages (same pattern as the document-listing gap from P4-S2): (1) Permission Service had no listing for role assignments (only create/delete individually) — `GET /role-assignments` (optionally filtered by `principal_id`/`resource_id`) added; (2) Auth Service had no user management at all — `GET/POST /users`, `DELETE /users/{id}` added, directly against Keycloak's admin API via `python-keycloak` (already existing dependency from the realm bootstrap). **Registry now also registers itself with itself** (`DMS_REGISTRY_SERVICE_BASE_URL`/`DMS_SELF_ADDRESS` point to its own address) — without this, `service_type=registry-service` would never have been resolvable via the Gateway, the Admin UI's registry overview couldn't have shown anything. The self-healing fix from `dms-registry-client` (P4-S1: re-registration on `404`) immediately kicked in for the in-practice unavoidable case "self-registration at own startup" (the server itself only accepts connections after lifespan completion) — observed as expected in the first live smoke test and self-healed without manual intervention. **Additionally added at explicit user request**: i18n base skeleton for both frontend apps (**ADR 0007**) — JSON dictionary (`src/i18n/de.json`) + React context (`useI18n()`/`t("bereich.schlüssel")`), currently only German active, but extensible to more languages without component changes. None of the established Next.js i18n libraries fit, since they rely on routing/middleware, which is incompatible with the static export (ADR 0006). **Incident & fix**: `export const metadata` in `layout.tsx` broke the build (`_not-found` page crashed while collecting data), because it was re-exported from the i18n module marked as `"use client"` — Next.js evaluates `metadata` server-side at build time, before the client context exists; fixed by direct JSON import in `layout.tsx` instead of via the hook. **Doc/ADR pass** (second part of this session): new `docs/architecture.md` with a Mermaid architecture diagram of the overall system at the MVP stage; `PROGRESS.md`'s "Open Decisions" consolidated from 26 chronologically appended points into thematically sorted sections (authorization, auth/identity, storage, registry & gateway, frontend, document/folder/object-type, tooling & testing), in the process removing one meanwhile resolved point (provisional structural event contract, confirmed since P3-S3) and recording a recurring operational finding (NATS hang on `uv run pytest`, observed for three sessions, reliable fix documented). 7 new Python tests (Auth Service user management: 5, Permission Service role-assignment listing: 2) — Python workspace now **255 tests, all green**. Additionally 29 new Vitest tests for the admin-ui (API client, auth context, three feature components) as well as adjustments to the existing user-ui tests for the `I18nProvider`. **No browser available in this environment** (as P4-S2, Playwright installation declined again by the user) — every Gateway call of both admin-ui pages was instead individually retraced via `curl` against the real running 15-container stack: login → create user → create role → create/see/remove role assignment → delete user → create/see/delete object type → registry overview shows all eight running backend services as `healthy` (including the Registry itself). `docs/services/admin-ui.md`, `docs/adr/0006...md` (user-ui docs extended with an i18n section), `docs/adr/0007-frontend-i18n-preparation.md`, `docs/services/{auth,permission,registry,document}-service.md`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (new `admin-ui` job), `infra/docker-compose.yml`/`.env.example` (new `admin-ui` container, port 3001) updated. → **Phase 4 (vertical MVP slice) completed — MVP milestone: runnable core DMS.** `graphify dms/ --update` (phase completion, mandatory) run afterward. Next step: P5-S1 (Virus-Scan Service + upload pipeline wiring) — first session of Phase 5 (Processing: scan, rendering, OCR, search).
  - **Addendum (real browser test by the user)**: exactly the feared case occurred — the first real login in a browser failed at the preflight `OPTIONS` request with `405`, since the Gateway had no CORS clearance (curl never triggers a preflight, hence it stayed undetected). Fix: `CORSMiddleware` in the Gateway (`cors_allowed_origins`, default the two frontend ports 3000/3001, `DMS_CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` env-configurable), 2 new tests (allowed vs. unknown origin) — Python workspace now **257 tests**. Confirms exactly the limitation of curl-based verification recorded in "Open Decisions" for this session.
- **P4-S4**: First of the three sessions newly scheduled from user feedback after the MVP milestone (Konzept 8). **User-UI v2**: `DocumentWorkspace` replaces the earlier flat `FolderBrowser` (P4-S2) with the three-part layout sketched by the user — far left an icon-based `IconRail` outside the main content (currently only "documents" active, "search"/"settings" visible placeholders for P5-S4/P4-S6), top left `ExplorerPane` (Windows-Explorer-like navigation with folder CRUD and a tab bar for opened documents instead of the previous modal preview dialog), bottom left `MetadataPanel` (attribute form fields generated from the object-type schema), right `PreviewPane` (still a stub, but now fixed in the layout instead of an overlay). All three columns resizable via a new, dependency-free `Splitter` component (pointer events, sizes remembered per browser in `localStorage`) — deliberately no external layout library introduced. **Backend gap closed**: the Document Service had no way to change `title`/`attributes` of an existing document (only at creation) — new `PATCH /documents/{id}` (re-validates object type if one is set; publishes `document.metadata.updated`, automatically logged by the Audit Service via the existing `document.>` wildcard). Folder CRUD in the UI, however, uses exclusively Folder Service endpoints already existing since P3-S3 (`POST/PATCH/DELETE /folders`) — only the UI wiring was missing here. **Deliberately not wired into the UI**: folder move (new parent folder) — rename only, a drag & drop move is a later UX refinement. **Discovered during this, not fixed**: `DELETE /folders/{id}` only checks for subfolders, not contained documents (see "Open Decisions"). 6 new Python tests (Document Service: 3 repository, 3 API tests for the metadata endpoint) — Python workspace now **263 tests**, of which in this session **242 individually verified green** (`document-service`/`folder-service`/`object-type-service` each run individually: 39/37 tests green; part of the `permission-service`/`audit-service` tests failed when running the *entire* workspace with the Docker Compose stack running in parallel with `consumer is already bound to a subscription` — a NATS trap independent of this session's changes, newly documented, see "Tooling & Testing"). 17 new/adjusted Vitest tests for the user-ui (API client extensions, `DocumentWorkspace`: navigation, folder CRUD, tab-opening including preview synchronization, metadata saving including tab-title update, upload reload). **No browser available in this environment** (as P4-S2/S3) — every Gateway call retraced via `curl` against the real running stack: create folder → rename → upload document → change metadata via `PATCH` → list shows the new title/new attributes → object-type schema retrievable via the Gateway → delete folder; the new resizable/tab interaction itself thereby remained **visually unverified** (only covered via Vitest), which is explicitly communicated to the user. `docs/services/document-service.md` and `docs/services/user-ui.md` updated. Next step: P4-S5 (admin-ui v2: dashboard sidebar navigation, multi-installation).
- **P4-S5**: Second of the three user-feedback sessions (Konzept 3a/8), **pure frontend session without backend changes**. **Dashboard layout**: `AdminSidebar` (new) replaces the earlier flat top-nav bar of the admin-ui with a left, grouped navigation sidebar — groups ("management", "installations") individually collapsible/expandable, state remembered per browser in `localStorage`; `AdminShell` was rebuilt accordingly into a header+sidebar+main-area layout. **Multi-installation** (Konzept 3a, **ADR 0008**): new `InstallationProvider`/`useInstallation()` manage a purely client-side list of installations (`{id, name, gatewayBaseUrl}`, `localStorage`), `InstallationSwitcher` in the header switches the active installation, `InstallationManager` (`/installations/`) creates new ones/removes some (at least one always remains). **Architecture trick**: `lib/api.ts`'s previously fixed-imported `GATEWAY_BASE_URL` constant was replaced by a mutable module variable plus a `setGatewayBaseUrl()` setter, so that no existing caller (`UserManagement`, `ObjectTypeEditor`, `RegistryOverview`) had to be touched; `AuthProvider` got its own `localStorage` key per installation (`dms.tokens.<id>` instead of a global key), so sessions are isolated per installation — switching to an already once-logged-in installation doesn't require re-login as long as its session remains valid, but no single sign-on across installation boundaries (deliberate, matches the isolation from Konzept 3a). `InstallationProvider` sets the gateway address synchronously during render instead of in a `useEffect` (reasoning: effect ordering, see ADR 0008) — a deliberate, documented deviation from the "no side effects in render" convention. Python workspace unchanged at **263 tests** (no backend change in this session, confirmed via regression curl against object-type/registry endpoints). 15 new/adjusted Vitest tests for the admin-ui (`InstallationProvider`: bootstrap/add/switch/remove/protection of the last installation/persistence; `AuthProvider`: new test for cross-installation session isolation; `AdminSidebar`: collapse/expand including persistence; `InstallationManager`/`InstallationSwitcher`; API client test for the actively set gateway address) — admin-ui Vitest suite now **33 tests**. **No browser available in this environment** (as with all previous UI sessions) — existing backend flows regression-checked via `curl` (login, object types, registry still reachable unchanged), the actual new behavior (dropdown switch, sidebar collapse/expand) is purely client-side and **only verified via Vitest, not visually in a browser** — explicitly communicated to the user. `docs/services/admin-ui.md`, `docs/adr/0008-admin-ui-multi-installation-sessions.md` (new) updated. Next step: P4-S6 (cross-UI theming: light/dark/high-contrast/auto + profile persistence).
- **P4-S6**: Third and last of the three user-feedback sessions (Konzept 8). **Cross-UI theming** for both frontend apps: four levels (light/dark/high-contrast/automatic), switchable via a new `ThemeSwitcher` (user-ui: in the previously deactivated settings popover of the `IconRail`, now functional; admin-ui: header next to `InstallationSwitcher`). **Cross-device profile persistence** (core requirement of this session), as suspected in the "Open Decisions" point, resolved via a Keycloak user attribute instead of a new persistence building block — Auth Service got two new endpoints `GET/PUT /me/preferences`, behind them `admin_users.get_theme_preference`/`set_theme_preference` via the admin client available since P4-S3 (**ADR 0009**). **Stumbling block found and fixed along the way**: Keycloak's Declarative User Profile (default since Keycloak 25, in use here) silently discards every attribute not declared in the realm profile on `update_user` — an initial test run showed `PUT` returning status 200, but a subsequent `GET` still returned `"auto"` anyway. Fixed via a new, idempotent `_ensure_theme_attribute` declaration in the realm bootstrap (`ensure_realm_and_client`). Both frontends got their own, deliberately duplicated `theme-context.tsx` (ADR 0006) — `localStorage` (`dms.theme`) as an immediately available cache (relevant e.g. for the login page without a session), synced with the server attribute once an access token is available; `data-theme` on `<html>` (via `useLayoutEffect`, no flash) controls both stylesheets previously written with hardcoded colors via new CSS variables (`--dms-bg`/`--dms-fg`/`--dms-border`/`--dms-accent`/...). 4 new Python tests (Auth Service: default value, persistence, 422 for invalid theme value, missing token) — Python workspace now **267 tests**, `auth-service` isolated verified 18/18 green. 3 new Vitest tests per frontend app (`ThemeProvider`: default `auto`, `localStorage` cache restoration, `setTheme` persistence) — user-ui suite now **20 tests**, admin-ui suite now **36 tests**; `matchMedia` polyfilled in both `tests/setup.ts`, since jsdom doesn't know it. **No browser available in this environment** (as with all previous UI sessions) — login → `GET /me/preferences` (default `auto`) → `PUT` (`dark`) → repeated `GET` confirms `dark` → `PUT` with an invalid value returns 422, all retraced via `curl` against the real running stack (including rebuild of both UI containers, both reachable); the actual switching (popover, dropdown, flash-free switch) is **only verified via Vitest, not visually in a browser** — explicitly communicated to the user. `docs/services/auth-service.md`, `docs/services/user-ui.md`, `docs/services/admin-ui.md`, `docs/adr/0009-cross-ui-theming-profile-persistence.md` (new) updated. → **All three user-feedback sessions (P4-S4/S5/S6) completed.** `graphify dms/ --update` run afterward. Next step: P5-S1 (Virus-Scan Service + upload pipeline wiring) — first session of Phase 5.
- **P5-S1**: First session of Phase 5 (Processing), planned in depth against the spec beforehand (see `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`/"Open Decisions"). New **`services/virus-scan-service`** (Konzept 10.3): `POST /scan` (multipart, optional `document_id`/`created_by`) performs the scan, on a hit stores a quarantine copy via the Storage Service under `quarantine/{scan_id}` (no automatic deletion — traceability/evidentiary value) and persists the result in its own schema `virus_scan`; `GET /scans/{id}`/`GET /scans?document_id=...` for lookup. **Swappable engine** following the same plugin principle as the storage backends (3.3/3.8): the default is a new `EicarSignatureEngine` (detects the standardized EICAR test signature — industry-standard for integration tests, but no real malware protection), plus a complete `ClamdEngine` (INSTREAM protocol against a `clamd` daemon) implemented, but **not** the default engine and **no** `clamd` container in `infra/docker-compose.yml` — the initial signature-database download (`freshclam`) takes minutes and requires reliable internet access, not suitable for a reproducible `docker compose up` in this environment (activatable via `DMS_SCAN_ENGINE=clamd` once a `clamd` is operated separately). **Core decision, documented as ADR 0010**: the Document Service calls `/scan` **synchronously before** persisting content/metadata of an upload (`POST /documents` *and* `POST /documents/{id}/versions`) — not asynchronously via `document.version.created`, since 10.3 requires a scan *before* release and the existing upload path makes content immediately retrievable as soon as it's written. Follows the same, already established pattern as the synchronous object-type validation in the same service, instead of introducing a new `scan_status` gating state on `document_version`. On a hit: `422` (`{"error": "virus_detected", "threat_name": ...}`), no document/version is created. If the Virus-Scan Service is unreachable: `503` (**fail-closed** instead of silently letting it through). **Audit extension**: `audit-service` consumes `virus_scan.>` additionally from this session on (10.3/5.3 explicitly require auditing of scan results) — confirmed via live verification: the hash chain stays intact after all test runs (`GET /events/verify` → `ok: true`). 19 new Python tests (Virus-Scan Service: 17 — engines including EICAR detection/factory selection/`ClamdEngine` error behavior with an unreachable daemon, repository, API; Document Service: 2 new integration tests for scan rejection at creation/check-in) — Python workspace now **286 tests**, all green (`virus-scan-service`+`document-service` together verified in isolation: 58/58). **Live E2E over the real Gateway stack**: clean upload → `201`; EICAR upload → `422` with the correct `threat_name`; EICAR check-in onto an existing document → `422`, `GET /versions` still shows only the one clean version; quarantine bytes retrievable directly via the Storage Service; all 16 containers ran error-free (`docker compose up -d --build`). `docs/services/virus-scan-service.md` (new), `docs/adr/0010-virus-scan-synchronous-gating.md` (new), `docs/services/document-service.md`, `docs/services/audit-service.md`, `docs/architecture.md` (diagram/reading notes extended with `virus-scan-service`) updated. `graphify dms/ --update` run afterward. Next step: P5-S2 (Rendering/Preview Service + renditions) — hooks onto `document.version.created`, which is now only published *after* a clean scan.
- **P5-S2**: Second session of Phase 5. New **`services/rendering-service`** (Konzept 3.7/2.4): consumes `document.created`/`document.version.created` from the Document Service (both hook on after the scan gating from P5-S1) and automatically produces **renditions as permanently persisted objects via the Storage Service** — deliberately no cache, as 2.4 explicitly requires. **Rule-based plugin architecture** (`renderers/`, the same principle as the storage backends): `ThumbnailRenderer` (raster images → PNG thumbnail, Pillow), `DocxTextExtractionRenderer`/`PptxTextExtractionRenderer` (`.docx`/`.pptx` → `.txt` text extraction, python-docx/python-pptx), `PdfArchiveRenderer` (`.pdf` → archive copy with best-effort metadata tagging, pypdf). Each rule is applied independently and recorded separately as `ready`/`failed` (natural primary key `{document_id}:{version_number}:{rendition_type}`, making reprocessing idempotent) — a failing renderer doesn't block the others. **Two deliberate deviations from the concept's example text, the same trade-off as ClamdEngine vs. EicarSignatureEngine in P5-S1**: `.pptx` produces `.txt` instead of the example-mentioned `.pdf` conversion (would need an external office-rendering component like LibreOffice headless, not available here); the PDF/A conversion is best-effort tagging, not an ISO-19005-validated conversion (would need Ghostscript/veraPDF). Image-based/scanned documents are deliberately not yet served (OCR dependency, catch-up effect from P5-S3) — the concept pipeline envisages OCR before rendering, the sessions build it in reverse order. Additionally `POST /render/watermark` (reportlab + pypdf) as an **on-demand function, not automatic/not persisted** — a watermark is a deliberate single action, not a default step for every upload. **Consumer architecture**: a single broad `document.>` subscription with in-handler dispatch by `event_type` (same pattern as `permission-service/structure_consumer.py`), since a JetStream durable consumer name would be reserved per subject; since no `deliver_new` is set, a fresh consumer at its first start retroactively processes the complete previous event history (intentional backfill behavior, observed live). Metadata/original content are obtained exclusively via the Document Service's already existing HTTP API (`GET .../versions/{n}` or `.../content`) — **the Document Service itself didn't have to be changed for this integration**. **Audit extension**: `audit-service` consumes `rendering.>` additionally from this session on (including `status="failed"` events, so failures are visible without gaps). 22 new Python tests (renderers against real, in-memory generated files; repository; pipeline `process_version` directly against the real running Document/Storage Service including error isolation for a corrupt PDF; API; consumer integration with a real NATS event) — Python workspace now **308 tests**, all green (`rendering-service` isolated 22/22, together with `audit-service`+`document-service` 75/75 with the consumer containers stopped due to the known NATS durable-consumer collision, see "Tooling & Testing"). **Live E2E over the real Gateway stack**: real user created/logged in, PNG upload → automatically generated, correctly downscaled thumbnail retrievable via `/renditions`, `POST /render/watermark` returns a genuinely stamped PDF, audit trail shows both `document.created` and `rendering.completed`, hash chain stays intact (`GET /events/verify` → `ok: true`, 115 entries checked). `docs/services/rendering-service.md` (new), `docs/services/document-service.md`, `docs/services/audit-service.md`, `docs/architecture.md` (diagram/reading notes extended with `rendering-service`) updated. **`graphify --update` deliberately deferred**: `detect_incremental` classified 252 of 320 files as "changed" (notably more than this session's actual changes — presumably a cache/manifest mismatch issue of the tool, not attributable to this session), the graph variant merged from that had net 99 fewer nodes than the existing graph — graphify's own shrink guard correctly refused the write instead of silently losing content. Since per `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` `graphify --update` isn't mandatory after every session but only at phase end (P5-S4), it wasn't forced here (`force=True`) — `graphify-out/graph.json` remains at the P5-S1 state (1970 nodes), a full rebuild is planned for the Phase-5 completion. `manifest.json` was already updated with the current file hashes in the process, so a future run won't report the same large diff again. Next step: P5-S3 (OCR Service) — PaddleOCR/Tesseract, feeds both Search (P5-S4) and the image-based renditions left out in P5-S2.
- **Addendum to P5-S2 (user feedback + data-loss incident)**: while testing the preview, the user reported a `500` error retrieving document content. Investigation revealed two separate findings: (1) **real bug**: `document-service`'s `/documents/{id}/content` and `.../versions/{n}/content` endpoints crashed with an unhandled `500` instead of returning `404` when the Storage Service no longer knows the object — fixed via a new `ObjectNotFoundError` in `storage_client.py`, both endpoints now catch it (1 new regression test, deliberately deletes the object via the real Storage Service API and checks `404` instead of a crash). (2) The `rendering-service` could have run into an **endless NATS redelivery loop** in exactly this failure scenario (version known, content missing), since `pipeline.process_version()` only guarded the `get_version()` call against `DocumentNotFoundError`, not `download_content()` — the message would never have been acked. Fixed (both calls now in a shared `try`, 1 new regression test). During the investigation it turned out that all containers had been idle for 2 hours (Docker restart) — brought back up with `docker compose up -d`. **Discovered during this, serious**: several full `uv run pytest` runs of this session against the live running stack completely emptied `document.document`/`document.document_version` (Document Service) and `storage.object_metadata` (Storage Service) (`TRUNCATE`/`DELETE` in the respective `conftest.py` fixtures, default DSN identical to the Postgres instance used by the stack) — all documents that had previously existed (including everything the user had uploaded via the UI) were thereby irrecoverably lost; the raw data still lies on the storage volume as anonymous, content-addressed files, but without the deleted metadata can no longer be associated with a document/title/folder. Not fixable, communicated transparently to the user. See the new ⚠️ rule above and in "Tooling & Testing". **Additionally at the user's request**: `PreviewPane.tsx` (user-ui) was still the old stub from P4-S2 (own code comment: "follows in P5-S2") — now connected to the Rendering Service: loads the document's renditions when opened (`GET /api/rendering-service/renditions?document_id=...`), shows a `ready` thumbnail rendition as an `<img>` via a blob URL (`URL.revokeObjectURL` on change/unmount), otherwise falls back to hint text — renditions are, per 2.4, an added benefit, not a blocker, the download button remains always usable. 2 new/adjusted Vitest tests (no rendition present → hint text; `ready` thumbnail → `<img>` with correct blob URL) — user-ui suite now **21 tests**, `npm run typecheck`/`npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean (a harmless `next/image` optimization hint, deliberately ignored: blob URLs aren't suitable for the `next/image` loader). **No browser available** (as always) — the underlying Gateway calls had already been verified live via `curl` previously (real thumbnail correctly retrievable), the actual image display in the DOM only via Vitest. `docs/services/user-ui.md`, `docs/services/document-service.md` (implicitly via the code reference) updated.
- **P5-S3**: Third session of Phase 5. User request at session start expanded the scope by two UI requirements: being able to access old document versions in user-ui, and being able to highlight text in the preview — the latter was, upon clarification, fixed on the **position-accurate overlay variant** (text lies invisibly exactly on top of the image, as with pdf.js) instead of a simple text view, which fixed the new service's data model on word bounding boxes instead of plain text. New **`services/ocr-service`** (Konzept 3.9): consumes `document.>` (same pattern as rendering-service), automatically detects whether a document needs OCR at all — for PDFs, the existing text layer is read directly first (`NativeTextLayerEngine`, PyMuPDF `get_text("words")`, confidence always `100.0`, no image-recognition run needed), `TesseractEngine` only runs (`pytesseract` against `tesseract-ocr`/`tesseract-ocr-deu`) for scanned PDFs without a usable text layer or for raster images directly. **Deliberate deviation from the concept, documented as ADR 0011, the same trade-off as ClamdEngine vs. EicarSignatureEngine in P5-S1**: PaddleOCR (the concept's default) is not implemented — unlike ClamdEngine (a thin, fully built protocol client), there's no lightweight partial implementation of PaddleOCR that avoids the several-hundred-MB-heavy ML framework; only the plugin interface allows for a future addition. **Data model** (natural key `{document_id}:{version_number}`, one authoritative result per version instead of several rules as with `Rendition`): `status` (`ready`/`needs_review` from `average_confidence < 70.0`/`failed`), `engine`, `full_text`, `pages` (JSON: page dimensions + word list with pixel coordinates), `page_image_storage_key` (PDFs only — the OCR Service rasters its own page image for PDFs, since `ThumbnailRenderer` from P5-S2 only supports raster images and there had so far been **no** PDF preview in the system). `needs_review` is, as envisaged in 3.9, purely informational and blocks nothing — real BPMN review remains open until the Workflow Engine (P6-S1). **Catch-up effect in rendering-service**: new second subscription on `ocr.completed` (own durable name `rendering-service-ocr`), fetches the full text via HTTP on `ready`/`needs_review` (`GET /ocr-results/{id}` — events deliberately carry only status fields, no potentially large full text, to keep NATS payloads/audit hash chain small) and creates a `substitute_text` rendition, unless one already exists — closes the gap left open in P5-S2 for scanned/image-based documents and, as a side effect, also for PDFs with a real text layer. **Audit extension**: `audit-service` consumes `ocr.>` additionally from this session on (3.9/5.3 explicitly require auditing of OCR results). **User-UI**: `PreviewPane.tsx` got a version selector (`<select>`, already existing backend-side via document-service's version endpoints, only the UI was missing) as well as the desired text overlay — a `<span>` per recognized word, invisible and positioned percentage-exact over the display image (`left/top/width/height` as `%` of the OCR page dimensions), font size adapted to the actually rendered image height via `ResizeObserver`, so that highlighting/double-click word selection stays accurate at any splitter width/zoom; download now also uses the selected version instead of always the current one. 23 new Python tests for `ocr-service` (engines against real in-memory generated PDFs/images including `select_engine()` dispatch; repository; pipeline against the real running Document/Storage Service including the `DocumentNotFoundError` path; API; consumer integration) plus 6 new/extended Python tests in `rendering-service` (catch-up pipeline, consumer dispatch including duplicate check with a fake `OcrServiceClient`) — Python workspace now **338 tests**. Two `TesseractEngine` tests are marked `pytest.mark.skipif`, since this development environment itself has no `tesseract` system binary (only the Docker container) — verification for these was done via live E2E. **Safety lesson from the P5-S2 data-loss incident followed**: before the test run, an isolated throwaway database (`dms_test_p5s3`) was created in the same Postgres instance and referenced via `TEST_POSTGRES_DSN`, instead of testing against the live schemas — deleted again after the run, no live data touched; `ocr-service`/`rendering-service` containers were stopped for the duration of their respective API/consumer integration tests (known NATS durable-consumer collision, see "Tooling & Testing") and started again afterward. 4 new/adjusted Vitest tests (OCR word spans with correct percentage positions, no overlay without a ready OCR result, version switch triggers reload with the chosen version number, download uses the selected version) — user-ui suite now **25 tests**; `ResizeObserver` (in addition to `matchMedia`) polyfilled in `tests/setup.ts`, since jsdom doesn't implement it. **Live E2E over the real Gateway stack**: real PDF with a text layer uploaded → `native_text_layer` result with correct word bounding boxes and a matching 834×625 page image, `average_confidence=100.0` → rendering-service automatically produced a `substitute_text` rendition with exactly the same full text ("Rechnung Nr 4711 ueber 543,21 EUR") → audit trail shows both `ocr.completed` and `rendering.completed`, hash chain stays intact (`GET /events/verify` → `ok: true`, 436 entries checked); Gateway routing enforces auth for `ocr-service` as for every other service (401 without/with an invalid token). `docs/services/ocr-service.md` (new), `docs/adr/0011-ocr-tesseract-over-paddleocr.md` (new), `docs/services/rendering-service.md`, `docs/services/audit-service.md`, `docs/services/user-ui.md`, `docs/architecture.md` (diagram/reading notes extended with `ocr-service`) updated. **`graphify --update` deliberately deferred**, for the same reason documented in P5-S2: per `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`, mandatory only at phase end (P5-S4). Next step: P5-S4 (Search Service) — backend decision (Postgres FTS vs. dedicated index) deliberately left open, to be decided as an ADR within the session; full-text indexing can now draw on both the OCR text layer (P5-S3) and `substitute_text` renditions (P5-S2/P5-S3).
- **P5-S4**: Fourth and last session of Phase 5.
