New **`services/search-service`** (Konzept 3.7): full-text index + facet search over **Postgres' built-in full-text search** instead of a dedicated search index (`tsvector` column `search_vector`, `setweight(to_tsvector('german', title), 'A') || setweight(to_tsvector('german', full_text), 'B')` — title hits are weighted higher, GIN-indexed, queried via `websearch_to_tsquery('german', ...)`/`ts_rank`) — **documented as ADR 0012, the same class of trade-off as ADR 0010/0011**: a dedicated search index (Elasticsearch/Meilisearch) would have been another container/operational overhead that an already available native alternative makes unnecessary; `pg_trgm`/`unaccent` are available but deliberately not wired in (the concept only requires a full-text index + facets, no typo tolerance). **Data model**: one row per **document** (not per version as with `Rendition`/`OcrResult`) with `postgresql.JSONB` for `attributes` (deliberately not the generic `JSON` of the other services, since JSONB cleanly supports the `->>` operations needed for attribute filters); soft-deleted documents are hard-removed from the index instead of just marked. **Two critical findings verified before implementation**: (1) documents are **not their own permission resources** — `permission-service` only registers folders as `ResourceNode` (`structure_subjects=["folder.>"]`), `document-service` never calls `permission-service` — the permission check for a search result therefore runs via its **`folder_id`** (or `"root"` without a folder), not via `document_id`. (2) document events are deliberately thin (`document.created` delivers only `{title, created_by}`, no `folder_id`/`attributes`/`object_type_id`) — `reindex_document()` loads the full record via `GET /documents/{id}` for every event. **Indexing pipeline**: two separate consumer subscriptions on the same `event_bus` client (no own publisher — pure consumer role like audit-service): `document.>` (durable `search-service`) for metadata, `ocr.>`+`rendering.>` (durable `search-service-text`) for subsequent full-text enrichment (OCR preferred, `substitute_text` rendition as fallback). **Cross-stream backfill race deliberately solved via a single code path**: since `document.>` and `ocr.>`/`rendering.>` are separate streams without ordering guarantees, an `ocr.completed` can arrive before the corresponding `document.created` — both consumers call the same `reindex_document()`, which always reloads the full state via HTTP instead of relying on an already existing index row. **Permission Service** got the **`POST /check/batch`** endpoint already predicted in P5-S4 (body: `{principal_id, permission, access_type, resource_ids}`, response `{results: {resource_id: bool}}`) — repeats the existing `/check` logic per resource ID in a loop against the already existing `effective_permission_cache`, no expensive recomputation despite the loop. `GET /search` reads `principal_id` from the **`X-DMS-Principal` header** injected by the Gateway (JWT `sub`) — no query parameter, to rule out forgery; Search Service is thereby the **first service in the system to evaluate this header at all and enforce real read permissions**. Results are loaded with an overfetch margin, filtered via batch check (unreadable hits disappear without comment, no "locked" marker) and only then paginated — deep paging under heavy permission filtering is thereby deliberately not paging-stable (documented open point, a SQL join across service boundaries isn't possible due to 3.1). **Object-Type Service/Folder Service unchanged** (already sufficient endpoints, same zero-change precedent as rendering-service↔document-service in P5-S2). **User-UI**: the "search" button in `IconRail`, deliberately left a disabled placeholder since P4-S4, was activated (`activeView`/`onSelectView` props, no longer a placeholder) — new `SearchPane.tsx` replaces `ExplorerPane`/`MetadataPanel` in search mode (object-type selection reveals matching attribute filters, range fields for `date`/`decimal`/`integer`, exact match otherwise), clicking a result uses the same `openDocumentTab()` function as a document from the Explorer (a search result is a structural extension of `DocumentSummary`); `PreviewPane` deliberately stays coupled to `activeTabId` independent of the active view, no automatic view switch when opening a hit. 21 new Python tests for `search-service` (repository including attribute filters per type and facet grouping; pipeline against the real running Document/Folder Service; consumer integration including an explicit regression test for the cross-stream backfill race; API including real permission filtering against the running Permission Service) plus 4 new Python tests for the permission-service batch check — Python workspace now **363 tests**. **Important test finding of this session**: `TestClient(app)`-based tests read the real app's Postgres connection via `DMS_POSTGRES_DSN`, not via `TEST_POSTGRES_DSN` (which only affects the test engines built directly in `conftest.py`) — an initial test run without `DMS_POSTGRES_DSN` thereby unnoticed wrote a (harmless, non-destructive) test row into the live `dms` database instead of the isolated throwaway database and caused an assertion to fail; fixed (row cleaned up, both environment variables set together, then green) and recorded in `docs/services/search-service.md` as a lesson for future sessions. Isolated throwaway database (`dms_test_p5s4`) used as in P5-S3 and deleted afterward; `search-service`/`permission-service` containers stopped for the duration of their respective `TestClient` tests (known NATS durable-consumer collision) and restarted afterward. 4 new Vitest tests for the user-ui (view switch documents↔search, search+result click opens a tab, attribute-filter controls per type, empty state without hits) — user-ui suite now **29 tests**. **Live E2E over the real Gateway stack**: a PDF with a text layer from P5-S3 found via `GET /search?q=Rechnung` (correct ranking/snippet); `401` without an `X-DMS-Principal` header; a principal without folder read permission doesn't see the document, after a real role assignment on `"root"` via the running Permission Service it appears; Gateway routing enforces auth for `search-service` as for every other service. `docs/services/search-service.md` (new), `docs/adr/0012-search-postgres-fts.md` (new), `docs/services/permission-service.md`, `docs/services/user-ui.md`, `docs/architecture.md` (diagram/reading notes extended with `search-service`) updated. **`graphify dms/ --update` started as phase completion, but not finished**: detection/AST extraction ran through (72 files changed since the last successful manifest state, 61 of them code — 596 new AST nodes/1385 edges —, 11 documentation files for the semantic extraction), but the subagent started for this aborted due to an environment session usage limit ("hit your session limit", reset per the error message at 21:50 Europe/Berlin) — an external constraint, not an error in graphify or this session. Intermediate state (`.graphify_detect.json`/`.graphify_ast.json`/`.graphify_incremental.json`) sits ready in `graphify-out/`; `graph.json` itself was **not** changed, no shrink-guard risk. **To be caught up in a follow-up session**, once the usage limit resets — until then `graphify-out/graph.json` remains at the P5-S1 state (1970 nodes), as already after P5-S2/S3. → **Phase 5 (Processing: scan, rendering, OCR, search) content-wise completed** (code/docs/tests fully done).
- **Addendum to P5-S4 (Phase 5b inserted, pure concept/planning session, no code)**: right after P5-S4 completed, new user request following the same pattern as P4-S4/S5/S6 (there: feedback after the first browser test; here: six new functional requirements before starting Phase 6). **Concept (`Business__DMS-Konzept.md`, v0.16 → v0.17) extended**: (1) new section **2.2a** — object types can, via `allowedParentTypes` (multiple values, special value `"$ROOT"`), enforce under which folder classes they may be created (example from the requirement: `meinTopLevelOrd` only under root, `meinSecondLevelOrd` only under `meinTopLevelOrd`, `meinDoc` only under `meinSecondLevelOrd`), enforced via the same rule engine as the other object-type constraints (4.5), automatically ex-/importable, since it's an ordinary schema field; folder classes additionally get an `icon` field for the explorer display. (2) new section **2.2b** — form layouts (row/column grid, responsive fallback to single-column below a breakpoint window width) aren't authored as text/JSON, but generatively derived in the admin-ui from the attribute selection/display names/required fields of an object type ("smart layout") and uniformly control three use cases: metadata display, search mask, upload mask — each individually adjustable afterward via the same Layout Designer. (3) **3.6 extended** with "storage-device-swap sensitivity" (identity file with device ID per backend instance, startup refusal on a missing/deviating device, admin override with mandatory background replication) as well as an explicit clarification that any number of instances of the same backend type can be configured in the same target set. (4) **3.9 extended** with OCR configurability (`ocrEnabled`, word upper bound, batch size). (5) **section 8** extended with the GUI object-type/layout designer in the admin-ui and the user-selectable explorer tree view including class icons in the user-ui. (6) six new open points added to section 13 (retroactivity question on `allowedParentTypes` changes, icon format/origin, layout JSON detail format, identity-file format/replication prioritization, OCR word-upper-bound estimation method). **`IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` extended**: new **Phase 5b** (six sessions **P5b-S1–S6**, see session table above) inserted between Phase 5 and Phase 6 — deliberately as its own intermediate phase instead of renumbering the already-referenced Phases 6–14 (same precedent pattern as concept section "3a", which was likewise inserted afterward between 3 and 4). Total session count thereby **53** (previously 47). **Deliberately no implementation in this session** — only concept/plan/progress tracker updated, so the next session (P5b-S1) starts with an already thought-through specification instead of a vague requirement (same approach as the in-depth advance planning of Phase 5 before P5-S1). **`graphify dms/ --update` successfully caught up afterward** (the blocker open since P5-S4 was only the external session usage limit, which had meanwhile reset): detection found 365 files changed since the last successful manifest state (P5-S1) (305 code, 60 docs, of which 12 actually new for the semantic extraction — the rest already cached), AST extraction produced 596 new nodes/1385 edges, a single subagent (general-purpose) semantically extracted the 12 changed documentation files (59 nodes/104 edges/3 hyperedges, including one hyperedge over the recurring "avoided instead of implemented a heavy external dependency" pattern of ADRs 0010/0011/0012). **Important finding during this**: `detect_incremental` reported `Business__DMS-Konzept.md` and `CLAUDE.md` as "deleted" — both files, however, exist unchanged and remain unchanged central reference documents; the cause is that graphify respects `.gitignore` rules during the file scan, and exactly these two files are on the git-tracking exclusion list there (section "Plan"). This is a git-tracking decision, not a statement about whether they should stay in the knowledge graph — since the very first graphify run (P0-S1) originally built the entire graph exactly around `Business__DMS-Konzept.md`, pruning these two files would have been a silent, large content loss. **Deliberately not pruned** (`prune_sources=None` instead of the deletion list reported by `detect_incremental`) — all other steps followed `references/update.md` in order. Graph afterward **2357 nodes/3959 edges in 233 communities** (previously 1970 nodes, P5-S1 state) — no shrink-guard trigger, since real growth. All 233 communities renamed (size distribution: 61 down to 1 node, 68 singleton communities); graph health check ran clean (no dangling/missing/collapsed edges). `graphify-out/graph.html`/`GRAPH_REPORT.md`/`manifest.json`/`cost.json` updated (cumulative 528,523 input tokens across 5 runs). **Open point for a follow-up session**: check whether `Business__DMS-Konzept.md`/`CLAUDE.md` should be permanently exempted from the `.gitignore`-based graphify exclusion (e.g. via a future graphify include-override option), so future `--update` runs don't repeatedly report them as "deleted". → **Phase 5 thereby definitively and fully completed** (Definition of Done met). Next step: P5b-S1 (object-type hierarchy + class icons).
- **P5b-S1**: First session of Phase 5b (Konzept 2.2a). **Object-Type Service**: two new columns on `object_type` — `allowed_parent_types` (JSON list of object-type names or the sentinel `"$ROOT"` for "directly under the root", nullable = placeable anywhere) and `icon` (string, only for `applies_to="folder"`), added additively via `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` in the lifespan startup routine (same pattern as document-service's `attributes` column from P3-S3). New validation when creating/changing an object type: `allowed_parent_types` entries must be either `"$ROOT"` or an already existing **folder class** (only folders can be parent objects) — `422` otherwise; `icon` on a document class likewise `422`. **`dms-constraint-engine`** (ADR 0003, pure library) got a new check integrated into `validate()`: `parent_type_name` (resolved name of the parent class, `ROOT_PARENT_TYPE="$ROOT"` sentinel for the root, `None` for "parent folder without its own object type" — deliberately **not** an alias of the root, but its own, third state) checked against an optional `allowedParentTypes` field in the schema. **Core decision, documented as ADR 0013**: Folder Service/Document Service don't pass the already-resolved name of the parent class to `POST /object-types/{id}/validate`, only `parent_object_type_id`/`parent_is_root` (which they know anyway from their own folder table) — the Object-Type Service resolves the name itself and thereby remains the single source of object-type names, no additional round trip needed on the caller side. **Folder Service**: `create_folder` now resolves the parent folder in advance (`object_type_id` + `parent_id == "root"` flag) and validates before creating; **`update_folder` (move) validates against object-type constraints for the first time ever in this session** — previously a move (present in the backend since P3-S3, never wired into the UI, see P4-S4) re-validated nothing at all. **Document Service**: `FolderClient.exists(folder_id) -> bool` was extended to `FolderClient.get(folder_id) -> dict | None` (now returns the full folder body instead of just discarding a boolean), `create_document` passes `parent_object_type_id`/`parent_is_root` (`folder_id is None or folder_id == "root"`) to validation — documents have no move operation, so this only matters at creation. The same distinction "real root (`\"$ROOT\"`) vs. simply an untyped intermediate folder (`None`)" applies consistently to the Folder and Document Services. 12 new tests in `dms-constraint-engine` (pure function logic), 15 new tests in `object-type-service` (repository+API: valid/invalid `allowed_parent_types`/`icon` combinations, `/validate` resolution of `parent_object_type_id`/`parent_is_root`), 9 new tests in `folder-service` (create/move under/outside allowed parent classes, untyped folders remain unaffected), 3 new tests in `document-service` (correct/incorrect parent class, missing `folder_id` when root is excluded) — all run safely against an isolated throwaway database (`dms_test_p5b_s1`, set jointly via `DMS_POSTGRES_DSN`+`TEST_POSTGRES_DSN`, lesson from P5-S4) instead of the live `dms` instance, deleted afterward; all green. **Live E2E against the real, newly built containers** (directly against the service ports, since authorization sits only at the Gateway anyway, no service checks tokens itself): the concept example `meinTopLevelOrd` (only `"$ROOT"`) → `meinSecondLevelOrd` (only `meinTopLevelOrd`) → `meinDoc` (only `meinSecondLevelOrd`) recreated 1:1 — creation at the right/wrong parent location, document creation at the right/wrong parent location including missing `folder_id`, as well as a folder move onto an allowed and a forbidden new parent folder, all 13 individual cases behaved exactly as intended; all test artifacts afterward removed via the real API endpoints (no trace in the live database). `docs/adr/0013-object-hierarchy-parent-type-constraints.md` (new), `docs/services/object-type-service.md`, `docs/services/folder-service.md`, `docs/services/document-service.md` updated. **Deliberately not part of this session**: admin-ui handling of `allowed_parent_types`/`icon` (follows P5b-S3, GUI object-type/layout designer) as well as any icon display in the user-ui (follows P5b-S4) — this session is pure backend data model + enforcement, verified via curl/pytest instead of UI. `graphify --update` deliberately deferred (not Phase 5b's end). Next step: P5b-S2 (form-layout data model: smart-layout generation, row/column grid, Konzept 2.2b).
- **P5b-S2**: Second session of Phase 5b (Konzept 2.2b). **Object-Type Service**: new table `object_type_layout` (PK `(object_type_id, purpose)`, `purpose` ∈ `display`/`search`/`upload`, foreign key on `object_type.id` with `ON DELETE CASCADE`) — unlike the additive columns from P5b-S1, a completely new table, so `create_all` in the lifespan startup routine suffices without an additional `ALTER TABLE`. New module `object_type_service.layout.generate_smart_layout()`: packs an object type's attributes in creation order into groups of two fields per row (`COLUMNS_PER_ROW=2`), initially adopts the technical attribute name as `label` (a dedicated display-name assignment only comes with the GUI editor in P5b-S3) and mirrors the attribute's `required` flag as a snapshot. The same generation applies to all three use cases — differences only arise later through individual adjustment in the future Layout Designer, per the concept. **Core decision, documented as ADR 0014**: generated smart layouts are **not** persisted — `GET /object-types/{id}/layouts/{purpose}` is computed fresh from the current attribute list on every call without a stored override (`is_custom: false`), so a not-yet-adjusted layout never automatically goes stale on later attribute changes. Only a `PUT` explicitly freezes a state as an override (`is_custom: true`, deliberately a snapshot without live rebinding to later attribute changes — same non-retroactivity logic as with `allowedParentTypes`, ADR 0013). `PUT` validates that all referenced attributes actually belong to the object type (`422` otherwise, analogous to `allowedParentTypes`'s reference check). `DELETE` resets a single use case specifically back to the generated layout — idempotent, no error without an existing override. New `LayoutPurpose` enum (`StrEnum`, FastAPI automatically validates the path parameter against the three allowed values, `422` otherwise). 4 new tests for the pure smart-layout generation function (`test_layout.py`), 7 new repository tests (upsert/overwrite/reset/unknown attribute/unknown object type) and 8 new API tests (generated vs. stored, 422/404 cases, reset behavior) in `object-type-service` — service test suite now **48 tests**, all run safely against an isolated throwaway database (`dms_test_p5b_s2`, same procedure as P5b-S1), deleted afterward; `ruff check`/`ruff format` clean (one `E501` and one `UP042` hint, switched to `StrEnum` instead of `class X(str, Enum)`, fixed in the process). **Live E2E against the newly built container**: object type with three attributes created, generated display/search layout checked via curl (correct 2-column packaging, third row with the remaining attribute), upload layout overwritten and persistence as well as independence from the other use cases confirmed, `422` for an unknown attribute/unknown `purpose` path segment, `404` for an unknown `object_type_id`, `DELETE` resets back to the generated layout — all 8 individual cases behaved as intended; test artifact afterward removed via the real `DELETE /object-types/{id}` endpoint (no trace in the live database). `docs/adr/0014-form-layout-generated-defaults-not-persisted.md` (new), `docs/services/object-type-service.md` (API table, data model, new "form layouts" section, tests, open points) updated. **Deliberately not part of this session**: GUI editor/Layout Designer in the admin-ui (follows P5b-S3), any user-ui consumption of the layouts in `MetadataPanel`/`SearchPane`/upload dialog (follows P5b-S4) — this session is pure backend data model + generation + read/write/reset API, verified via curl/pytest instead of UI. `graphify --update` deliberately deferred (not Phase 5b's end). Next step: P5b-S3 (admin-ui: GUI object-type editor + layout designer).
- **P5b-S3**: Third session of Phase 5b (Konzept 2.2/2.2a/2.2b, admin-ui part). **`ObjectTypeEditor` fundamentally rebuilt**: replaces the JSON free-text attribute editor from P4-S3 with a structured attribute builder (row per attribute: technical name, type selection, required-field checkbox, plus pattern/minimum/maximum depending on type), extended with checkboxes for `allowedParentTypes` (2.2a: sentinel `"$ROOT"` + all existing folder classes except the one currently being edited) and an `icon` selection field (only for `applies_to="folder"`) from a deliberately **curated, hard-wired seven-icon set in the frontend** (`folder`/`folder-open`/`folder-star`/`archive`/`briefcase`/`invoice`/`contract`) instead of free SVG upload — resolves the icon-origin point left open in Konzept 13 pragmatically, thereby fully avoiding the security question raised there of active content in uploaded SVGs. **For the first time, the editor also supports editing existing object types** (`PUT /object-types/{id}`, previously only create/delete) — `name`/`applies_to` stay locked in the form (server-side immutable anyway), `naming_constraints`/`conditions` are passed through unchanged on save, so that saving via the new editor doesn't silently reset them to their default (neither has its own form in this session). **Core decision on display-name assignment**: since labels, per ADR 0014, live in the form layout, not in the attribute schema, the attribute builder shows a "display name" field per attribute **only when creating** — if at least one display name deviates from the technical name, an initial smart layout (2 attributes per row, the same packaging as `object_type_service.layout.generate_smart_layout`) with these labels is persisted for all three use cases right after creation (`PUT .../layouts/{purpose}` three times); without deviating labels it stays with the server-generated default. When **editing**, the display-name field is deliberately absent and the editor doesn't touch existing layouts — later label adjustments run exclusively via the new Layout Designer, otherwise every save in the object-type editor would overwrite already-made, targeted layout adjustments there. **New `LayoutDesigner` component** (its own area below the object-type editor on the same page): object-type and use-case selection, shows the current layout including an `is_custom` badge ("automatically generated"/"customized"). **Deliberate UX decision**: editing exclusively via unambiguous **row operations** instead of free dragging of individual fields between rows (move row up/down, remove row, add a new empty row, move a field within a row left/right, remove a field from the row, add a not-yet-used attribute deliberately into a specific row) — moving a field between two rows thereby needs two steps (remove, then add in the target row) instead of a single gesture, but without a drag-and-drop library and without the ambiguity of a flatten/regroup heuristic; explicitly decided this way since this environment has no browser for visual UX verification and every row operation stays individually testable with Vitest this way. "Save" (`PUT`) and "reset to generated layout" (`DELETE`, then automatic reload) act independently per object type and use case. **`lib/api.ts`** extended: `ObjectType`/`ObjectTypeAttribute` types with `allowed_parent_types`/`icon`, `ROOT_PARENT_TYPE` constant (must match the backend sentinel `"$ROOT"`), new `updateObjectType()` as well as `getObjectTypeLayout()`/`putObjectTypeLayout()`/`resetObjectTypeLayout()`. 15 new Vitest tests (7 `ObjectTypeEditor`: structured attribute capture without/with label-driven layout persistence, validation error for a missing technical name, edit mode including preservation of `naming_constraints`/`conditions`, icon field only for folder classes, delete; 8 `LayoutDesigner`: empty object-type stock, load generated layout, switch use case, add attribute to a row, remove field including automatic row removal, add a new row, save including badge change, reset) — admin-ui suite now **47 tests**, `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` clean. **Live verification without a browser** (as with all previous UI sessions in this environment): admin-ui container rebuilt, `GET /object-types/` returns `200` after redirect (static export, client-side rendered, so no text visible in the raw HTML — expected behavior); the request payloads produced by the new code retraced directly against the running Object-Type Service: creation with a deviating display name followed by three `PUT .../layouts/{purpose}` calls (all `200`, `GET` confirms the stored label), a `PUT /object-types/{id}` with unchanged pass-through of `naming_constraints`/`conditions`, as well as a folder type with `allowedParentTypes: ["$ROOT"]` + `icon`. All test artifacts afterward removed via the real `DELETE` endpoints (no trace in the live database). `docs/services/admin-ui.md` (new section on the guided editor/layout designer, API table, tests, open points) updated. **Deliberately not part of this session**: `naming_constraints`/`conditions` editing in the UI, admin-ui authorization (still the oldest open point), icon upload/larger icon set, any user-ui display of the icons/layouts (follows P5b-S4). `graphify --update` deliberately deferred (not Phase 5b's end). Next step: P5b-S4 (user-ui: tree view, class icons, layout-driven forms).
- **P5b-S4**: Fourth session of Phase 5b (Konzept 2.2a/2.2b/8, user-ui part), last of the three pure frontend/data-model sessions originally sketched for Phase 5b before the two retrofit sessions P5b-S5/S6. **New `FolderTree` component** (tree view, alternative to the existing list view in `ExplorerPane`, chosen by the user via a toolbar toggle and remembered in `localStorage`, `dms.explorer.viewMode`) — builds the folder hierarchy lazily starting from the root (children only loaded on expand via `listChildFolders`/`listDocumentsInFolder`). **Core decision, documented as ADR 0015**: the breadcrumb path when clicking any, possibly deeply nested, tree node is **reconstructed client-side from the already-expanded ancestors** instead of via a new Folder Service endpoint for the full path (open point since P3-S3) — a tree node is only visible once all its ancestors have been expanded, so the component already knows the full chain. New callback `onNavigateToFolder(path)` in `DocumentWorkspace` replaces the entire `trail` for this, unlike the existing `onOpenFolder` (list view), which only appends one level. **Class icons** (2.2a/2.2b): new `lib/icons.ts` with the same curated seven-icon set as the admin-ui editor (P5b-S3, deliberately duplicated rather than shared, ADR 0006) — `ExplorerPane` loads `GET /object-types?applies_to=folder` once and shows the respective class icon in front of every folder name in **both** views; documents always keep showing the fixed 📄 (icon is only allowed server-side for folder classes). **Layout-driven forms** (2.2b): new shared `LayoutFormFields` component renders the row/column grid of a form layout (including responsive switching to single-column below the window width stored in the layout, measured via a `resize` listener on `window.innerWidth` — deliberately not the actual, freely-resizable-via-`Splitter` panel width, since the concept explicitly requires "window width") and is used by all three consumers: `MetadataPanel` (now reads the object type's "display" layout instead of rendering attributes in fixed order, `required` comes from the layout field instead of the raw attribute), `SearchPane` (reads the "search" layout, falls back on load error to a one-attribute-per-row layout, so search doesn't become completely unusable) and the **fundamentally extended `UploadForm`** (previously didn't know about object types at all — now selection from `GET /object-types?applies_to=document`, attribute fields via the "upload" layout, `uploadDocument()` extended with `objectTypeId`/`attributes`, appended to the already existing `POST /documents` as `object_type_id`/`attributes` form fields). The object-type hierarchy check (2.2a) deliberately remains server-side at the Document Service, no pre-filtering/duplication in the UI. `lib/api.ts` extended with `listObjectTypes()`, `getObjectTypeLayout()`, `ObjectType.icon` as well as the `uploadDocument()` extension. 5 new Vitest tests (class icon + list/tree toggle, tree navigation to a nested folder with correctly reconstructed breadcrumb, layout-driven metadata fields with deviating label/required, search-mask field selection/labeling per layout instead of raw attribute order, upload with a chosen document class including `object_type_id`/`attributes`) — user-ui suite now **34 tests**, all existing 16 tests continued to run green unchanged (the default mock for `getObjectTypeLayout` deliberately fails, so existing scenarios without a layout reference automatically hit their previous fallback behavior); `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` clean. **Live verification without a browser** (as with all previous UI sessions): user-ui container rebuilt and reachable (static export, client-side rendered). `docs/adr/0015-user-ui-folder-tree-client-side-breadcrumb.md` (new), `docs/services/user-ui.md` (layout section, backend integration table, tests, open points) updated. **Deliberately not part of this session**: cross-device sync of the tree/list-view preference (only `localStorage`, unlike the color scheme), container-query-based instead of window-width-based responsive breakpoint, deep-link navigation to a folder never expanded in the tree (see ADR 0015). `graphify --update` deliberately deferred (Phase 5b only ends with P5b-S6). Next step: P5b-S5 (OCR configurability: on/off, word upper bound, batch size).
- **P5b-S5**: Fifth session of Phase 5b (Konzept 3.9), first of the two closing retrofit sessions before the phase end (P5b-S6). **Core decision, documented as ADR 0016**: 3.9 requires "all three settings are admin-ui configuration, no code/deployment change" but thereby contradicts itself against the likewise required property of `ocrEnabled: false` ("the OCR Service itself doesn't have to be deployed") — a container not deployed can't accept an admin-ui request. Since `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` already concretely fixes "Docker-Compose profile opt-out" for `ocrEnabled`, this part was implemented literally that way (`ocr-service` now carries `profiles: ["ocr"]`, activated via `COMPOSE_PROFILES` — default still on); the remaining two settings, in return, get the full "no deployment change" property. **New `OcrConfig` table** (`ocr_config`, fixed `id=1`, first service-wide DB-backed runtime setting in this repo — previous configuration was always either a restart-bound `Settings` environment variable or a personal Keycloak attribute): `max_word_count` (`null` = no upper bound) and `batch_size` (default `4`), administered via new `GET`/`PUT /config` routes, read fresh from the DB **for every processed document** instead of once at startup. **Word upper bound**: new `engines.estimate_word_count()` estimates the word count before the actual engine call (`PDF page count × 250`, PyMuPDF's `page_count` is available without rendering anyway; raster images always count as one page) — if the estimate exceeds the configured limit, **no** engine runs; instead a new `"skipped"` status is persisted and `ocr.skipped` is published (visible in the audit trail instead of silently vanishing). The concrete estimation method was, per the concept footnote, explicitly an implementation detail of this session. **Batch size as a concurrency limit**: the event bus so far delivers purely sequentially (a NATS callback is fully worked off before the next is delivered) — there's no pull/batch-fetch pattern whose size could be configured. `NatsEventBusClient.subscribe()` (`dms-eventbus-client`) therefore got a new **optional** `max_concurrency` parameter (an `int` or an async callable for live-re-readable values; default `1` = exactly the previous behavior, unchanged for all other consumers); `ocr-service` passes a callable that re-reads `OcrConfig.batch_size` from the DB on every message. A failing handler still releases its capacity but does **not** acknowledge (ack) the message — the same redelivery semantics as in the unchanged sequential path. **Cross-service consequence discovered and fixed**: since `ocr-service` can now genuinely be absent, `rendering-service`/`search-service` no longer depend on it via `depends_on` (otherwise a disabled profile would prevent their Compose startup); `search-service` already caught an `ocr-service` unreachability via a generic `except Exception`, `rendering-service`'s `consumer.py` didn't yet have this protection for the OCR catch-up handler (an old `ocr.completed` event would otherwise have redelivered endlessly after a later opt-out, without ever being processable) — now caught up, with its own regression test. **Admin-UI**: new page `/ocr-settings/` (`OcrSettings` component, new sidebar group "processing") — the first admin-ui page of this project to edit a backend runtime configuration instead of a business object; deliberately shows an explanatory empty state instead of an error when `ocr-service` is unreachable (this is the only visibility of `ocrEnabled=false` in the UI, no toggle). Backend test suites: `ocr-service` **34 tests** (previously 24, 3 tesseract-conditional skips — new tests for `OcrConfig` repository, word-count estimation, `skipped` pipeline path, `/config` API including validation), `dms-eventbus-client` **9 tests** (previously 6 — `max_concurrency` default/increase/error case), `rendering-service` still green (**29 tests**, 1 new for the connection-error tolerance) — all run safely against an isolated throwaway database (`dms_test_p5bs5`), `ocr-service`/`rendering-service` containers stopped meanwhile (known NATS durable-consumer collision, same procedure as previous sessions), restarted afterward; admin-ui suite now **50 tests** (3 new for `OcrSettings`), `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` clean. `docs/adr/0016-ocr-configurability-compose-profile-and-live-settings.md` (new), `docs/services/ocr-service.md`, `docs/services/rendering-service.md`, `docs/services/admin-ui.md` updated. `docs/architecture.md` deliberately not touched (per its own stated policy, only updated at phase boundaries, Phase 5b only ends with P5b-S6). `graphify --update` likewise deliberately deferred. Next step: P5b-S6 (Storage Service: storage-device identity & redundancy guard + multi-device) — **last session of Phase 5b**, `graphify dms/ --update` mandatory afterward.
- **P5b-S6**: Sixth and last session of Phase 5b (Konzept 3.6). **Core decision, documented as ADR 0017**: 3.6, taken literally, contradicts itself — "`ocrEnabled`-style" admin-ui configurability for the redundancy override on one hand, but a service that was already refused startup due to a storage-device swap somehow has to be able to accept that clearance on the other hand, even though it isn't running right now. Resolved as with `ocrEnabled` (ADR 0016): the override (`GuardConfig.allow_degraded_start`) is a **proactively set standing policy**, not a clearance at the moment of refusal — Postgres (where the setting lives) is independent of a broken storage backend and already available before the backends themselves in the lifespan. **The target set becomes a real list** (`Settings.targets: list[BackendTargetConfig]`, key `id` instead of `type`) and replaces the fixed `backend`/`secondary_backend`/`redundancy_enabled` structure from ADR 0004 outright — `replication.py` was, per ADR 0004, already fully generic against arbitrary target `id` strings (its own tests always used freely chosen IDs instead of type names), the restriction to "exactly two targets" sat exclusively in `Settings`/`backends/__init__.py` — resolved there now, which immediately also handles "any number of like-kind backend instances" (concept requirement), without touching the replication logic. **Storage-device-swap guard**: every target gets a generated device ID on first use as a marker object under the reserved key `__dms_storage_identity__` (via the existing `StorageBackend.write`/`read`, no new backend method); the reference value additionally lives in a new `backend_identity` table, since a genuinely swapped target would currently have *no* marker file — a pure self-comparison of the backend would be ineffective. Default: startup refusal (`RuntimeError` before `yield`, new `_run_startup_guard()` function in `main.py`, same fail-fast pattern as the existing `_validate_settings`); admin override allows a degraded start only if at least one target is demonstrably unchanged, and afterward automatically marks all copies of the affected targets as `pending` (`repository.reset_copies_for_backend`) — the existing retry queue (`POST /replication/process-pending`, ADR 0004) catches them up, no new background task. New `GET`/`PUT /guard-config` and `GET /guard-status` (device ID + open copies per target) round out the API. **Admin-UI**: new page `/storage-guard/` (`StorageGuard` component, new sidebar group "storage") following the same deployment-vs-admin-ui pattern as `OcrSettings` — no editor for the target set itself, only a status table + override toggle. **Important methodology finding during test execution**: `storage-service/tests/test_api.py` uses `TestClient(app)` against the real, module-wide `Settings()` singleton, which reads `DMS_POSTGRES_DSN` — **not** the separate `TEST_POSTGRES_DSN`, which only the `conftest.py`-own `engine`/`session` fixtures know. An initial "isolated" test run that had only set `TEST_POSTGRES_DSN` thereby unnoticed wrote into the real live `dms` database (seven `test-*` objects, one `backend_identity` row, `guard_config.allow_degraded_start=true`) — discovered because the subsequent real container restart falsely reported a storage-device swap exactly because of this. Cleaned up and the test run repeated cleanly with **both** variables (`DMS_POSTGRES_DSN`+`TEST_POSTGRES_DSN` on the same throwaway database `dms_test_p5bs6`) (76 tests green). The same pattern presumably also applies retroactively to earlier sessions with a `test_api.py` built this way (e.g. P5b-S5) — there it happened to remain harmless due to the final live cleanup, but here it was recognized as the cause for the first time and documented, so future sessions set both variables. Storage-service suite now **76 tests** (previously 54, new: `test_identity_guard.py` with 6 tests against a real `LocalFilesystemBackend`/a real, always-failing test double — first-start bootstrap, confirmed match, missing/deviating marker file after a known identity, unreachable backend with/without a known identity; new `test_backend_factory.py` with 6 tests for multiple instances of the same type and `BackendTargetConfig` required-field validation; 10 new repository tests). Admin-ui suite now **53 tests** (3 new for `StorageGuard`), `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` clean. **Full live verification without mocking** against the newly built containers: two-local-targets configuration brought up (both device IDs automatically stamped), a test object uploaded (primary target immediately `ok`, secondary target `pending`), the secondary target's identity file manually overwritten with invalid content (simulated storage-device swap), restart → **startup refusal** with exactly the expected error message; admin override set directly in Postgres (simulating a standing policy previously set via the admin-ui), another restart → **degraded start** succeeds, log shows the automatic re-replication scheduling, `/guard-status` shows `pending_copies: 1` for the affected target; marker file repaired, `POST /replication/process-pending` called → copy successfully caught up, `pending_copies` back to `0`. All test artifacts (object, additional target directories, `backend_identity` rows, `guard_config` override) removed afterward, service restarted with the normal single-target configuration and confirmed healthy; admin-ui container also rebuilt, `/storage-guard/` returns `200`, Gateway routing still enforces auth (`401` without a token) as for every other service. `docker-compose.yml`/`.env`/`.env.example` switched to `DMS_TARGETS`/`STORAGE_SERVICE_TARGETS` as a JSON list (default unchanged: a single local target, no redundancy). `docs/adr/0017-storage-device-identity-guard.md` (new), `docs/services/storage-service.md`, `docs/services/admin-ui.md`, `services/storage-service/README.md` updated. **`docs/architecture.md` updated this time** (header, storage-node label, new reading-note paragraph) — Phase 5b is fully completed with this session, no new service nodes arose. **Deliberately not part of this session**: rebalancing for newly added targets (they get no automatic copy of existing objects), no correction mechanism for an intended, legitimate device change in `backend_identity` (direct DB access would still be needed), no automatic periodic execution of `/replication/process-pending` (still waiting on the Plugin Orchestration Service, Phase 10). → **Phase 5b thereby fully completed** (all six sessions done). `graphify dms/ --update` runs afterward (mandatory this time, see below). Next step: P6-S1 (Workflow Engine base skeleton: SpiffWorkflow, BPMN import/execution, manual/automatic tasks, LGPLv3 license check first).
- **P5c-S1**: First session of a newly inserted **Phase 5c** — not a regular concept session, but a consolidation round initiated by the user over the points accumulated in "Open Decisions" after Phase 5b's completion, with four concrete decisions: (1) **SpiffWorkflow license** (LGPLv3, was a gate before P6-S1) accepted — as an unmodified dependency the copyleft obligation doesn't propagate to the overall codebase, documented in **ADR 0018**, P6-S1 thereby unblocked. (2) **Test-DB isolation gap** fixed immediately (this point, see below). (3) **Authorization gap** (Gateway only checks authentication, never permissions) deliberately not fixed in isolation, but bundled with P6-S4, since a generic permission/approval mechanism arises there anyway. (4) **Storage rebalancing + device-swap correction mechanism** (ADR 0017 follow-up points) moved up from the Phase-10 backlog, new session **P5c-S2**. **Test-DB fix in detail**: root-caused by the same bug type that had already occurred for real at P5-S2 (data loss) and P5b-S6 (live-DB leak) — every service's `conftest.py` reads `TEST_POSTGRES_DSN` only for its own `engine`/`session` fixtures, while `TestClient(app)`-based tests (`test_api.py`) import the app's own, module-wide `Settings()` singleton, which independently reads `DMS_POSTGRES_DSN` (`dms_common.BaseServiceSettings`, `env_prefix="DMS_"`) — without manually setting both variables in sync, `TestClient` tests unnoticed against the live DB. Fix: all 11 DB-backed service `conftest.py` files (`audit-`, `document-`, `folder-`, `object-type-`, `ocr-`, `permission-`, `registry-`, `rendering-`, `search-`, `storage-`, `virus-scan-service` — `auth-service` uses Keycloak instead of its own DB, `gateway-service` has no DB, both unchanged) now set `os.environ["DMS_POSTGRES_DSN"] = DSN` directly after resolving the `DSN`, so that both variables necessarily match from conftest load time on, regardless of what the caller otherwise set. **Verified via a real reproduction test**: isolated throwaway database `dms_test_verify_conftest` created, `storage-service` suite run with `DMS_POSTGRES_DSN` deliberately removed from the shell and only `TEST_POSTGRES_DSN` set (exactly the original failure case) — 76/76 tests green, live `dms` database row counts (`object_metadata`/`backend_identity`/`guard_config`) identical before/after the run (223/1/1), throwaway database deleted afterward. `ruff check` clean on all 11 changed files. **Deliberately not fixed**: the more general case "no `TEST_POSTGRES_DSN` set at all" (both variables then still default to the same DB as the running stack, the P5-S2 failure case) remains possible — that would be a separate, larger change (e.g. a required field without a fallback) and wasn't part of the decision made in this round. `docs/adr/0018-spiffworkflow-lgpl-license.md` (new), `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (new Phase 5c with P5c-S1/S2, P6-S1 license gate removed, session total 53→55) updated. No `graphify dms/ --update` (not a phase end, follows at the end of Phase 5c with P5c-S2). Next step: P5c-S2 (storage rebalancing + device-swap correction mechanism).
- **P5c-S2**: Second and last session of Phase 5c — resolves the two follow-up points documented in ADR 0017 "consequences", both moved up from the Phase-10 backlog on the user's decision. **Rebalancing on a newly added target**: new `repository.seed_pending_copies_for_new_target(session, backend_id)` creates a `pending` row for every already existing object without a copy on the new target; called exactly in the "known is None" branch of `identity_guard.check_target_identity` — the same place where a target's first-start bootstrap already happens. **No new trigger mechanism needed**: a target-set change (`DMS_TARGETS`) requires a restart anyway, since `Settings` is only read at startup, and exactly this restart triggers the bootstrap. On a completely fresh installation the function is a cheap no-op (no objects present). **Device-swap correction mechanism**: new `identity_guard.reidentify_target(session, target_id, backend)` + `POST /guard-status/{target_id}/reidentify` — adopts an already existing marker file of the new device as the reference, otherwise (as at first-start bootstrap) a new one is written; afterward resets all previous copies of the target back to `pending` via the already existing `reset_copies_for_backend`, since they referred to the now-swapped physical device — the same retry queue (`POST /replication/process-pending`) catches them up, exactly the same recovery logic as the automatic degraded start, only explicitly triggered by the admin and **without a restart**. Replaces the previously necessary direct correction in the `backend_identity` table. Both mechanisms are deliberately compositions of already existing building blocks (retry queue from ADR 0004, reset function and bootstrap logic from ADR 0017) instead of new infrastructure — hence no own ADR, only an addition to ADR 0017 "consequences". **Admin-UI**: `StorageGuard.tsx` got an "accept device swap" button per target row with a `window.confirm` confirmation (same pattern as `ExplorerPane`'s folder deletion), new `reidentifyTarget()` API function. Storage-service suite now **86 tests** (10 new: 2 repository, 6 identity-guard including another `session.get()` identity-map trap while writing the tests themselves — immediately recognized and fixed with a plain string instead of an ORM object reference, same lesson as documented in "Tooling & Testing" —, 3 API), admin-ui suite now **55 tests** (2 new), `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` clean. **Full live verification without mocking, with special care around the 223 real, productively used objects in the running stack**: containers rebuilt/restarted with the unchanged single target as a regression check (same device ID, `pending_copies: 0`), then a second, purely test-only target (`local2-p5cs2-test`) added via a `STORAGE_SERVICE_TARGETS` override — restart automatically stamped it and **live marked all 223 real objects** as `pending` for this new target, without touching the existing `local` copies; `POST /replication/process-pending?limit=5` actually replicated five real files onto the new target. The device-swap correction was deliberately performed **only against the purely test-only `local2` target**, not against the real `local` target (a reset to `pending` there would have instantly made all 223 real document downloads fail with 404 until every single copy was re-verified — a deliberately avoided, unnecessary risk to real data). Marker file of the test target removed (simulated swap), `POST .../reidentify` returned a new device ID + correctly reset copies; unknown target → `404`. Afterward fully cleaned up: containers rebuilt back to the single target, orphaned `object_copy`/`backend_identity` rows of the test target deleted directly via SQL — final check confirms exactly **223 real objects, all `status=ok` on `local`**, unchanged from the starting state. Gateway auth check confirmed: `POST /api/storage-service/guard-status/{id}/reidentify` still returns `401` without a token, like every other endpoint. ADR 0017 "consequences" (both points marked resolved), `docs/services/storage-service.md`, `services/storage-service/README.md`, `docs/services/admin-ui.md` updated. → **Phase 5c thereby fully completed** (both sessions done). `graphify dms/ --update` runs afterward (phase completion, mandatory). Next step: P6-S1 (Workflow Engine base skeleton: SpiffWorkflow, BPMN import/execution, manual/automatic tasks) — license question already clarified (ADR 0018).
- **P5d-S1**: First session of the newly inserted Phase 5d (real user feedback after Phase 5c's completion: missing preview for `.txt`/`.json`, unvalidated client content type taken over as-is, no format/OCR control at upload). **Document Service**: new `content_type_sniffer.py` determines each version's `content_type` server-side from the actual magic bytes (`python-magic`/`libmagic`, system package `libmagic1` added to the Dockerfile) instead of from the unvalidated `file.content_type` header sent by the client — runs for both creation *and* check-in, before the virus scan. New single-row `UploadConfig` table (`GET`/`PUT /upload-config`, same pattern as `OcrConfig`/`GuardConfig`) allows an admin-editable format whitelist; an unlisted detected content type is rejected with `400` before virus scan/storage run — an empty list (default) = no restriction, identical behavior to before. **OCR Service**: `OcrConfig` extended with `allowed_content_types` (additive `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`, same ad-hoc migration pattern as with `document.attributes`) — applies in addition to the existing technical `select_engine()` selection. **Correction still within the same session**: initially built as a negative list (`excluded_content_types`), switched at the user's request directly to a **positive list** (empty = no restriction, otherwise only a listed content type triggers OCR) — model field/migration/repository/schema/pipeline condition/admin-ui/tests uniformly renamed and the skip condition inverted (`config.allowed_content_types and metadata.content_type not in config.allowed_content_types`), the already-running session's DB column cleaned up via `DROP`+`ADD COLUMN` (no real admin data affected, since only created minutes before). Produces the same `status="skipped"` path on a missing list match as the existing word upper bound, only with `content_type` instead of `estimated_words` in the `ocr.skipped` payload. **Admin-UI**: `OcrSettings.tsx` extended with a comma-separated positive-list field, new page `/upload-settings/` (`UploadSettings.tsx`, same load/save/empty-state pattern as `OcrSettings`) under the existing "processing" sidebar group. Document-service suite now **57 tests** (11 new: 4 pure sniffer unit tests, 6 API tests for sniffing at create/check-in + whitelist acceptance/rejection/default + config persistence, 2 repository tests), OCR-service suite **34 tests + 3 tesseract skips** (4 new: one pipeline test each for skip-when-unlisted and continue-when-listed, 2 API tests for `allowed_content_types` persistence/default — plus adjustment of all existing `update_config()` call sites to the new required parameter), admin-ui suite now **58 tests** (3 new for `UploadSettings`, 3 existing `OcrSettings` tests extended with the new field), all `ruff check`/`ruff format`/`npm run typecheck`/`lint` clean. `uv lock` updated (new dependency `python-magic`). **Full live verification against the real, newly built stack** (Postgres/NATS/MinIO/Storage/Virus-Scan/Document/OCR Service): curl upload of a text file with a deliberately wrong `application/octet-stream` client header → correctly stored server-side as `text/plain`; whitelist set to `["application/pdf"]` → text upload `400`, reset afterward; OCR positive list set to `["image/png"]` → uploaded text-PDF returns `status="skipped"` with a matching error message, reset afterward. Test runs followed the established safety procedure (isolated throwaway database, two passes due to the subsequent correction, deleted afterward each time; `ocr-service` container stopped/restarted for its own test suite due to the known NATS durable-consumer collision); the test documents produced along the way via curl as well as via `ocr-service`'s own pipeline tests running against the real Document Service were fully soft-deleted afterward, the 223 real production objects remained untouched. **Deliberately not verified via browser**: P5d-S1 isn't among the sessions listed as browser-mandatory in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (pure configuration forms following the already established `OcrSettings`/`StorageGuard` pattern) — curl + Vitest are considered sufficient here, real browser testing of the new upload popup follows with P5d-S2. Next step: P5d-S2 (user-ui: upload as a popup with drag & drop + client-side text preview) — last session of Phase 5d, `graphify dms/ --update` mandatory afterward.
- **P5d-S2**: Second and last session of Phase 5d. **`UploadForm`** is now a real modal dialog (`role="dialog"`, reuses the previously unused `.modal-backdrop`/`.modal-content` classes from `globals.css`) instead of a form shown inline in `ExplorerPane` — new `onClose` prop, triggered via the same "upload" button as before as well as via a new ×-close button/cancel button/backdrop click. **Drag and drop**: `onDragOver`/`onDragLeave`/`onDrop` handlers on the entire dialog surface (not just the `<input type="file">`) take over the first dropped file via the same `setFile()` logic as the file picker, with visual feedback (`upload-dropzone-active`, accent color from the existing theme variables). **`PreviewPane`**: new branch `previewKind === "text"` checks the `content_type` of the selected version (from the version list already loaded anyway, since P5d-S1 reliably server-side sniffed) — for `text/*`/`application/json` the content is loaded directly via `downloadDocumentVersion()`, decoded via `Blob.text()` and shown in a `<pre class="preview-text">` block (truncation hint from 200,000 characters on), **without** requesting renditions/OCR — no new Rendering Service renderer, no rendition detour for content that's already text-based. The existing image/OCR overlay branch runs unchanged for all other content types. **TS narrowing trap found and fixed along the way**: `versions.find(...)` inside a nested `async function load()` was still classified by TypeScript as `possibly undefined` despite a preceding guard — fixed by extracting `content_type` in advance into its own `const` instead of carrying the object reference into the closure. **Test-infrastructure gap found and fixed along the way**: jsdom's `Blob` implementation has no `.text()` — led to a silent but incorrect `catch` path (`previewKind` stayed at `"none"`); localized via debug logging, fixed with a new `Blob.prototype.text` polyfill via `FileReader` in `tests/setup.ts` (same pattern as the existing `matchMedia`/`ResizeObserver` polyfills). User-ui suite now **37 tests** (3 new: drag-and-drop upload, cancel button closes without uploading, direct text preview without a renditions/OCR call), `npm run typecheck`/`lint`/`build` clean. **Full verification**: `docker compose build user-ui` + `up -d` — container starts, returns `HTTP 200`/correct `<title>`. **Deliberately not verified via browser** (as already P5d-S1): no Chrome/Chromium in this environment, Playwright declined by the user — `fireEvent.drop()` only simulates the DOM event, not the native OS drag gesture; a human should click through modal/drag-and-drop/text preview in a real browser before production use (see `docs/services/user-ui.md`). → **Phase 5d thereby fully completed** (both sessions done). `docs/services/user-ui.md` and `docs/architecture.md` updated (new Phase-5d completion paragraph, no new node/edge). `graphify dms/ --update` run afterward (phase completion, mandatory) — three interlocking pitfalls found and solved in the process (see "Tooling & Testing" above): implausible `detect_incremental` mass change (bypassed via `check_semantic_cache`, correctly identified only 8 changed documents), a `source_file` path-convention mismatch between AST (relative) and subagent nodes (absolute), and an initially attempted complete rebuild from a single-pass full extraction of all 66 documents that structurally could never match the graph accumulated over 15+ sessions (shrink guard correctly triggered) — fixed via `build_merge()` with a full AST re-extraction plus a targeted re-extraction of only the 8 actually changed documents, all 58 unchanged documents remained untouched with their accumulated depth. Result: **3373 nodes, 5472 edges, 507 communities** (previously 3031/5222/320, net +342 nodes), health check clean (only 1 non-critical self-loop). Right afterward, a new user request voiced during the P5d-S2 planning (reference-number generator) was registered as **Phase 5e** (see above, "Reference-number generator / file reference number") — Phase 6 moves back accordingly. Next step: P5e-S1 (Object-Type Service: reference-number generator configuration + atomic yearly counter).
